Its central pieces have the actual rational anticanonical toric presentations used in the local virtual calculation. No total normality, CM, Gorensteinness or outer Cartier property is silently assumed; these are derived below. Arbitrary cyclic and outer-path vertices and disconnected smooth generic outer divisor are included.

The local cohomology exact sequence gives depth two for the vertex kernel. Thus the central ring is Cohen-Macaulay. Oriented toric logarithmic two-forms have opposite primitive ray residues; the quotient index is included by dlog(v^r), not dropped as 1/r. **Construction step 3.1.** Compute the maximal-ideal order in each normal toric semigroup ring from its Hilbert-basis sail. Adjacent basis vectors have determinant one; on each straight sail edge the integral supporting height is the maximal decomposition length. Products across a bend vanish in the summed graded degree. ### Inline construction 2

#### Actual A, D and pinch trait normal forms by finite elimination

Research construction. This is an algebraic finite procedure over the complete trait; it replaces the earlier merely formal D4 comparison. The finite implicit contact-correction lemma is the complementary theta-lane construction. No Artin approximation or abstract versal-deformation theorem is used.

Let R=C[[t]], and work on an actual smooth etale ambient chart over R with relative coordinates x,y,z. Let F be an actual regular function defining a hypersurface family with smooth generic fibre. Suppose that, on an actual central etale chart, its central equation is one of

    A_k: x²+y²+z^(k+1),            k>=1;
    D_n: x²+z y²+z^(n−1),         n>=4;
    pinch: x²−z y².

The given central coordinate change is lifted to the ambient by finite etale equations; t remains fixed. The generic smoothness assumption gives an actual finite integer M with

    t^M in J_F=(F,F_x,F_y,F_z).

Indeed this ideal becomes the unit ideal after t is inverted. Its central support need not be isolated in the pinch case.

#### 1. Finite algebraic central divisions

For A_k, the central derivative ideal is (x,y,z^k). Its residue basis is 1,z,...,z^(k−1). Finite Taylor division leaves a polynomial of degree at most k−1 in z; the remainder is in the displayed ideal.

For D_n, discard x, and use the relations

    zy=0,       y²=−(n−1)z^(n−2).

Multiplying the second relation by z gives z^(n−1)=0; multiplying by y gives y³=0. Thus a residue basis is

    1,z,...,z^(n−2),y.

Every monomial of total degree at least n−1 belongs to the derivative ideal, because a mixed y,z monomial is divisible by yz, a pure z monomial is divisible by z^(n−1), and a pure y monomial of that degree is divisible by y³. Each of these relations is an explicit combination of 2x,2zy,y²+(n−1)z^(n−2). Consequently a FINITE Taylor jet of any actual regular coefficient gives its residue polynomial and actual regular derivative-ideal quotients. This is ordinary ideal membership in the local ring, not an infinite formal division. Passing to an etale chart with the same residue field preserves the finite jet quotient, so the same calculation applies there.

For the pinch equation, there is an equally finite division, although its residue space is infinite in z:

    h(x,y,z)=c(z)+b y+(2x)A+(−2zy)B+(−y²)C.

Subtract h(0,y,z) and divide by x. Subtract its value at y=0 and divide by y. The remaining linear-y coefficient is its scalar value at z=0 plus z times a regular function. The residual y-dependent quotient is divisible by y. The function c is an actual regular function on an etale conductor-coordinate line, and b∈C. To lift c independently of x,y, take the product of that finite etale line chart with the x,y-plane and refine by its fibre product with the existing ambient chart. The diagonal component along the conductor makes the two restrictions agree. There are only finitely many such refinements in the procedure below.

#### 2. Finite t-order normalization

Fix N. Starting at order t, remove one t-order at a time. At step r<N, reduce the coefficient of the error t^r modulo t and apply the appropriate central division. Retain its residue-basis coefficients, and remove its three derivative terms by subtracting t^r times the corresponding three coordinate quotients.

Every such coordinate change is actual etale, with relative Jacobian the identity modulo t. Taylor terms quadratic in the change have t-order at least 2r>=r+1. The retained deformation coefficients vanish modulo t, so their derivatives gain another t. Hence the induction improves the equality by one order. It is performed only N−1 times.

The resulting actual equation F_N differs by t^N H from one of the ACTUAL normal-form functions

    G_A=x²+y²+z^(k+1)+sum_(i=0)^(k−1) a_i,N(t) z^i;
    G_D=x²+z y²+z^(n−1)+2b_N(t)y+sum_(i=0)^(n−2) a_i,N(t)z^i;
    G_pinch=x²−z y²+2b_N(t)y+c_N(z,t).

Here all a_i,N and b_N are finite polynomials in t with complex coefficients. In the pinch case c_N is a finite polynomial in t with actual regular functions on the conductor line as coefficients. Therefore every G is an actual regular function over an actual finite-type etale R-chart. No convergent infinite coordinate series is being called algebraic.

#### 3. The same generic Jacobian bound holds for G

The original containment t^M∈J_F persists under the finite etale coordinate changes, since they fix t and have invertible relative Jacobian. Relative derivatives of F_N−G=t^N H still have factor t^N. Thus

    J_{F_N} subset J_G+(t^N).

For N>M this gives t^M=j+t^N a with j∈J_G, hence t^M(1−t^(N−M)a)∈J_G. The parenthesis is a unit, so t^M∈J_G. In particular G has no hidden generic critical locus at this local chart.

Choose N>=2M+1. Then F_N−G∈m J_G². One explicit coefficient decomposition is obtained from t^M=Σr_iJ_i:

    F_N−G=sum_ij J_iJ_j A_ij,
    A_ij=t^(N−2M)H r_i r_j in m.

This argument needs neither maximal-ideal primary J nor isolated central critical support. That is exactly what makes it work for the pinch case.

#### 4. A finite etale implicit system corrects the remaining error exactly

Use the complementary finite contact-correction construction. For J0=G,J1=G_x,J2=G_y,J3=G_z set δx_i=Σ_j J_jY_ij and δu=Σ_jJ_jY_0j. Finite Taylor expansion gives

    (1+δu)G(x+δx)−G(x)=sum_ij J_iJ_j(Y_ij+Q_ij(Y)),

with Q of order at least two in the sixteen Y variables. The equations Y+Q(Y)=A have identity Jacobian at the closed point. Their actual etale implicit solution has Y∈m. The coordinate correction has identity relative Jacobian, the multiplier is a unit, and t is fixed. It gives an exact actual etale contact equivalence F_N=unit times G in the new coordinates.

Composing with the finitely many earlier coordinate changes proves the actual normal-form claim. It is an etale common-neighborhood statement, not a claim that the whole generic fibres of two compactifications are globally isomorphic. Local modifications and their proper supported virtual contributions must be descended using the independently proved isolated-support construction.

#### 5. Geometric consumers and limits

The A_k normal form is an actual polynomial in z; its finite root extension and the explicit double-model charts give the known simultaneous local resolution. The D_n normal form is exactly the polynomial family consumed by the explicit D recursion, with constant-in-z coefficient b_N(t); no additional formal versality is needed. For the pinch form, the conductor blowup/finite-weight procedure is still a geometric argument to be supplied, but the actual quadratic normal form itself is now proved.

In every use the invertible contact multiplier belongs to the normal-frame data and is retained in the gluing/virtual comparison. The procedure makes no assertion about unchosen corner cases, global compatibility, or a theta-period identity by itself. It is the bounded actual-normal-form supplier needed by those subsequent constructions.

### Inline construction 3

#### Anticanonical outer cycles: Hodge vanishing, rational presentation and conductor degree two

Research prerequisite for the new ordinary/nonordinary toric-vertex lane. It does NOT assert that a central monoid refinement lifts to an arbitrary family. The input is an ACTUAL reduced projective Cohen–Macaulay Gorenstein surface E with O_E(1)=H ample, omega_E=O_E(-1), and a reduced Cartier hyperplane D which is a nodal cycle of rational curves. The ordinary PBW exception has D a cycle of lines; the arguments allow a polarization of positive degree on each rational component. At normalization/resolution all maps and complete linear systems below are algebraic. Work over C.

#### 1. A positive-metric vanishing argument

Let S be a smooth projective resolution of a normalization component, let H also denote the pullback of H, and assume the Cartier pullback D_S has only rational components. Then H¹(O_S)=0. Indeed a holomorphic one-form alpha restricts to zero on every P¹ component of D_S. Consequently

    integral_S i alpha wedge conjugate(alpha) wedge c1(H)
    = sum_B mult_B(D_S) integral_B i alpha|B wedge conjugate(alpha|B) = 0.

On the other hand choose the metric on H pulled back from the Fubini–Study metric of the given projective embedding. Its curvature is semipositive everywhere and strictly positive on a dense open set where the map is an immersion. A nonzero holomorphic alpha is nonzero on such an open set, making this integral strictly positive. Thus alpha=0. The published compact Kähler Hodge theorem identifies H¹(O_S) with the conjugate of holomorphic one-forms, proving the claim. Exact supplier read in full: root item `thm-compact-kahler-hodge-decomposition-via-elliptic-harmonic-forms`, status published. This is a positivity proof, not an assumption that all irregular surfaces are classified.

For a normalization component V with conductor divisor C, dualizing/conductor adjunction is

    omega_V(C)=O_V(-H).

It gives K_S.H=-H²-C.H<0; discrepancies over the finite singular points have zero H-intersection. Thus K_S is not effective, p_g(S)=0 and chi(O_S)=1. These assertions do not assume that V is already rational or smooth.

#### 2. Normal exception: rational Gorenstein resolution and actual ruling

If E is normal, D pulls back to a rational cycle after resolving the boundary nodes. Locally at a point of D the completed normal surface ring has a smooth or nodal Cartier quotient. A smooth quotient forces the surface regular. A nodal quotient gives an actual hypersurface xy=t g, and relative Morse fixing t gives XY=Phi(t); normality makes Phi nonzero, so these boundary points are A-type. The explicit toric A resolution has reduced pullback of t=0 and inserts only P¹ components.

The cohomology of the PBW Gorenstein exceptional surface is H¹(O_E)=H²(O_E)=0: its explicit pure free resolution has twists 0,2,3,...,n-2,n in P^n; none has top projective cohomology, and the intervening cohomology vanishes by the monomial Cech calculation. Equivalently use its arithmetic Cohen–Macaulay ring and a-invariant -1. The preceding Hodge argument gives H¹(O_S)=0. Leray and q_*O_S=O_E then force R¹q_*O_S=0. Thus the normal singularities are rational Gorenstein.

The PUBLISHED root theorem `thm-rational-gorenstein-normal-surface-singularity-resolved-by-point-blowups` (read in full) gives an actual finite PROJECTIVE point resolution, normal/rational at every stage, and canonical pullback is an isomorphism at each step. In particular it is crepant. Therefore on the resulting S, K_S+D_S=0 and D_S is the actual reduced anticanonical cycle, with no exceptional discrepancy silently discarded.

In the ordinary n-line case each original line has H-degree one and hence self-intersection -1 (its two boundary intersections contribute two); inserted A-boundary curves have square -2. Contract an original (-1)-line and then the consecutive boundary (-1)-curves created along one adjacent inserted chain. The next original line becomes a smooth rational curve F with F²=0. Every contraction is actual projective. H remains nef and big: at a contraction of L set pi*Hnew=H+(H.L)L, which has zero intersection with L and nonnegative intersection with every other curve. Also Hnew=-Knew and remains supported on the boundary cycle.

Riemann–Roch now gives h⁰(O(F))≥2, since chi(O)=1, K.F=-2 and h²(O(F))=0 by intersection with the nef big H. Since F is irreducible and square zero the system is a base-point-free rational pencil. Its generic conic has a C(t) point by the explicit polynomial coefficient-count lemma already used in the degree-three packet. Extend the section by properness, then contract vertical (-1)-components until the smooth P¹-bundle F_m is reached. This is an ACTUAL finite blowdown presentation, not mere rationality.

For a nonordinary polarized cycle, a component of nonnegative square gives rationality directly: use the complete system of that P¹ component, whose restriction sections lift because H¹(O)=0, and blow its finitely many transverse pencil basepoints on the component. Alternatively apply the general rational-presentation supplier after the rationality argument in §4. The actual family lift at the rational Gorenstein points is a SEPARATE surrounding-family input; this paragraph does not infer it from a central resolution alone.

#### 3. Nonnormal normalization: an actual rational chain and complete embedding

At a point of D the same hypersurface/Morse calculation shows that a nonnormal E is an ordinary double surface there: XY=0. Its normalization separates that node. Every conductor curve meets the ample D, so every conductor branch is generically ordinary double. The normalization conductor is therefore generically reduced. Its ideal is omega_V tensor O(H), a reflexive rank-one sheaf; on the normal Cohen–Macaulay surface V it has depth two, making its quotient pure Cohen–Macaulay of dimension one. Generic reducedness therefore gives an ACTUAL reduced conductor scheme, with no embedded correction silently omitted.

The pullback D_V is reduced: it is a Cartier divisor on the normal surface V, hence Cohen–Macaulay, and is generically reduced on each rational component. Finite birational maps to a node can either retain it or separate its two branches; this follows immediately from the normalization quotient of C[[x,y]]/(xy), which has length one. Thus its dual graph is a chain or cycle cut from the original cycle. It is connected. If it split as disjoint effective A+B, then on a resolution A²=H.A>0 and B²=H.B>0, contradicting the one-positive-direction Hodge index theorem. Since a conductor curve meets D and separates at least one node, the graph on a nonnormal component is a CHAIN. Its boundary singularities are only the A-type normal points described above; their resolution inserts reduced chains of P¹'s. Hence D_S is an ACTUAL rational tree, and pa(D_S)=0.

We already proved H¹(O_S)=0 and chi(O_S)=1. The normalization matching description of a rational tree with a line bundle of nonnegative multidegrees gives H¹(D_S,mH)=0 for m≥0. The exact sequences

    0→O_S((m-1)H)→O_S(mH)→O_D(mH)→0

give H¹(S,mH)=0 for m≥0 and h⁰(S,H)=d+2, d=H². The complete section ring on the polarized rational chain is generated in degree one: remove a leaf, use the elementary surjectivity of multiplication of polynomials on P¹, and match their endpoint values; repeat. Degree-zero inserted chains simply transfer an endpoint value. Lift the generators with the divisor section as in the degree-two cycle proof. Thus the complete H section ring of V is generated in degree one. Since H is ample on V, it gives an ACTUAL closed embedding

    V → P^(d+1),       degree(V)=d.

No classification of surfaces of minimal degree is needed for this embedding statement.

#### 4. Rationality and actual presentation without a classification shortcut

Choose a general second hyperplane H1. It avoids all nodes and singular points of D_V and meets the rational tree D_V transversely in d simple points. The pencil generated by D and H1 has exactly these d basepoints. Blow them up once. The proper transform of a general member has square zero and arithmetic genus zero. General members are smooth (the base-point-free projective map is birational on a dense open and avoids the finitely many singular points) and connected: two disjoint effective components with positive H-degree would again give two positive orthogonal directions in the Hodge form. Hence they are P¹. Each exceptional curve over a simple basepoint is a section of the pencil. The existing vertical (-1)-contraction argument reaches an actual F_m. This proves S rational, without using a bare p_g=q=0 criterion.

For the original S, use the exact general presentation supplier already constructed by theta_families at
`physics/research/conformal-field-theory-string-theory-all-97-2026-10-26-owner-step1-hms-nonfano-closure/log-cy/four-dimensional/rational-surface-algebra/presentation/inline construction 31`, SHA256 cffc32ef7113dbf8fde3696fe79fcc5d8270c927c8a7df0da2406c983e6e3ba7. Its scope is ANY smooth integral rational projective complex surface and its conclusion is a finite point-blowup presentation of the ORIGINAL surface from P² or F_m, not merely a common roof. It is a research draft, not an acceptance stamp. `inline construction 46` consumes such a genuine presentation after an actual reduced log-CY boundary cycle has been constructed.

#### 5. The normalization conductor has degree exactly two

Adjunction on a general member of |H|, whose arithmetic genus is that of the rational tree, gives (K_S+H).H=-2. Combining this with omega_V(C)=O_V(-H) gives

    C.H=2.

Because the complete H system is an ACTUAL embedding of V and C is ACTUALLY reduced, C is a reduced projective degree-two curve: a smooth plane conic, two intersecting lines, or two disjoint lines. This can be checked by taking its linear span: an irreducible degree-two curve spans at most a plane and is its smooth conic; reducible degree two has exactly two degree-one line components. Double lines were excluded by the conductor depth/generic-reduced argument. There is no cuspidal, elliptic or higher-degree conductor curve hidden in the normalization.

At conductor smooth points the ordinary double/pinch family suppliers apply. At a node of two conductor lines the Gorenstein anti-residue/equalizer construction is the separate `inline construction 28`. If the NORMALIZATION itself has a cyclic toric cone at that node, this file does not assert that its fan resolution lifts to the family; that is the explicit remaining monoid/weighted-PBW interface. The existence of a central rational normalization is not substituted for such an actual family lift.

#### 6. Every singular minimal-degree normalization is the explicit cyclic toric cone

The complete embedding proved above also controls its singularities, without a classification of scrolls. Let V be a NORMAL nondegenerate surface of degree d in P^(d+1), and let p be singular. Projection from p has image Y in P^d. If that image had dimension two and generic degree e, intersection with two general hyperplanes through p gives the exact degree formula

    d - mult_p(V) = e degree(Y).

The left side is at most d-2. The image is nondegenerate. Any nondegenerate surface in P^d has degree at least d-1: a general hyperplane gives a nondegenerate integral curve in P^(d-1), whose degree is at least d-1 since normalization of a degree-a curve has at most a+1 independent sections of its hyperplane line bundle. This contradiction shows that the projection image is a CURVE. Hence V is the projective cone over that curve. Its nondegenerate base lies in P^d and has degree d, so equality in the same curve bound makes it the rational normal curve. One elementary equality proof is to impose d-1 general point conditions on the normalized degree-d linear system; its remaining two-dimensional system has degree one after removal of those points, giving an isomorphism to P¹, and the full system is O_P1(d).

Thus a singular normalization is exactly the cone over the rational normal curve. Its vertex local ring is the degree-d Veronese subring of C[u,v], equivalently A²/mu_d with scalar action (u,v)→(zeta u,zeta v). Away from the vertex the normalization is smooth. There is no unknown non-toric normalizer singularity in the degree-two conductor classification.

In the ORDINARY n-line hyperplane case even these cone vertices cannot occur on a nonnormal component. A hyperplane section of the rational normal cone is either a smooth rational normal curve avoiding its vertex, or the union of d ruling lines through the vertex. Our D_V is a chain of actual degree-one lines, so it must be the latter. If d≥3 it has more than two branches at one point, contradicting its finite birational nodal-chain description. If d=2, its vertex is an A1 point of V and the conductor, also a degree-two union of rulings, passes there; but E is ordinary double at every conductor intersection with the nodal outer D, whose normalization is REGULAR there, another contradiction. Degree one is P² and is smooth. Consequently ordinary-cycle PBW exceptions have SMOOTH normalization components; their conductor singularities are precisely the smooth-point/node pushouts in the separate Gorenstein residue packet. For nonordinary polarizations, the explicit Veronese cone is retained as a legitimate central monoid input to the generalized sail/PBW lane; its fan alone is still not claimed an actual family modification.

### Inline construction 4

#### Conductor conic vertices: explicit blowdowns and ordinary relative gluing

Constructive research supplement, 2026-10-28. This supplies the actual surface-presentation input required by `inline construction 46` for the conductor components created by the pinch resolution. It then records their exact interface with the already constructed relative cone/diagonal operation. It asserts no quotient-invariance or degree-two division formula.

#### 1. The actual conic and a rational section

The final conductor component, before its isolated A/D resolutions, is the projective conic bundle over the original line L=P1:

    x²-Zv²+2beta v w+gamma(Z)w²=0.                       (1)

After the displayed isolated resolutions and outer-vertex modifications, let Q be its smooth projective strict transform with f:Q->L. The generic fibre is the same smooth conic. All fibre modifications made so far are the actual point/fibre charts, not an unspecified birational rationalization. In particular its fibres are connected: the original plane conics are connected, and the explicit A/D resolution and point-blowup maps replace points by connected rational trees. The other conductor components are already ruled surfaces over the actual double cover C=P1, so only this conic component needs the following construction.

Here is an explicit proof of a C(Z)-point without importing a draft Tsen theorem. Clear denominators of the three-variable homogeneous quadratic equation so its coefficients have degree at most M. Substitute three polynomials in Z of degree at most N. Vanishing gives at most 2N+M+1 homogeneous quadratic equations in the 3N+3 complex coefficients. Choose N large enough that the number of equations is smaller. Krull's height theorem and the Nullstellensatz then give a common nonzero coefficient vector. The resulting polynomials give a point of the generic conic over C(Z). That conic is smooth, so projection from the point identifies it with P1 over C(Z).

The rational point extends to an actual section S of f. At every closed point of the nonsingular proper base curve, the valuative criterion of properness extends its rational map uniquely; separatedness glues the extensions. Its composition with f is the identity on a dense open, hence everywhere. A section of a separated morphism is a closed immersion, so S is a smooth rational curve and F.S=1 for every scheme fibre F.

#### 2. Every reducible fibre contains a vertical minus-one curve

The map f is flat: a nonzero parameter of the smooth base acts injectively in each local ring of the integral smooth Q, and a torsion-free module over a DVR is flat. A fibre F has F²=0 and K_Q.F=-2, computed on the unchanged smooth generic conic. Surface RR in the exact divisor sequence gives chi(O_F)=1, so its arithmetic genus is zero.

If F=mA is irreducible as support, the section equality 1=F.S=m(A.S) forces m=1. Arithmetic genus zero then makes the reduced integral curve A a smooth P1: normalization genus and delta are both nonnegative and sum to that arithmetic genus. Thus every nonsmooth fibre is reducible.

For a connected reducible fibre F=sum m_i A_i, each A_i² is strictly negative. Indeed 0=F.A_i and at least one intersection with another component is positive. Since sum m_i K_Q.A_i=-2, some component has K_Q.A_i<0. Adjunction gives

    2p_a(A_i)-2=A_i²+K_Q.A_i.

Both terms on the right are negative integers and the left is at least -2. Therefore both equal -1 and p_a(A_i)=0. The curve is a smooth rational minus-one curve, constructed from an actual fibre. Contract it by the previously constructed projective minus-one contraction supplier; because it is vertical, f factors through the smooth contracted surface. The section descends as a section. No general surface MMP or classification theorem is used.

Each contraction strictly decreases the finite numerical Picard rank: its exceptional class has square -1 and is orthogonal to the pullback of the new numerical divisor space. Consequently the process terminates after finitely many actual contractions. At that point all fibres are irreducible, hence smooth P1s by the preceding paragraph. This is an actual finite blowdown presentation of Q, not just the assertion that its function field is rational.

#### 3. The terminal ruled surface is an explicit Hirzebruch surface

On the resulting smooth P1 fibration with section S, the line O(S) restricts to O_P1(1). Its fibre H0 is two-dimensional and H1 vanishes. The actual cohomology-and-base-change supplier therefore makes E=f_*O(S) locally free of rank two and makes evaluation surjective. The induced proper morphism to P(E) is an isomorphism on each fibre, hence is quasi-finite and birational with normal target; the existing proper-birational normal-target isomorphism criterion makes it an isomorphism.

For completeness E splits by an elementary calculation. Degrees of line subbundles are bounded above by embedding E in a sum of sufficiently positive line bundles. Choose maximal a with H0(E(-a)) nonzero and saturate a section. Maximality makes the quotient a line O(b). If b>a, curve RR gives chi(E(-a-1))=b-a>0 and hence a nonzero section, a contradiction. Thus a>=b, and the extension splits because H1(P1,O(a-b))=0. Up to twisting, P(E)=F_(a-b), with its actual base and fibre coordinates. Reversing the preceding finite contractions writes Q as a finite sequence of ordinary point blowups of this Hirzebruch surface.

This proves the exact input of the toric-model supplier. The same argument is immediate for the intermediate components, already projective line bundles over C=P1; only any actual subsequent point modifications need be reversed. All contracted curve classes and their centres can be retained from this construction for the decorated relative vertices.

#### 4. The complete reduced boundary is a rational anticanonical cycle

Let B_Q be the boundary supplied by the completed regular-SNC target: its horizontal curves are the actual conductor copies of C=P1, and its vertical curves come from the displayed conic/toric fibres. The blowdown sequence just constructed shows that all vertical components are rational as well: reverse it from the smooth P1 fibres, adding only exceptional P1s. The incoming cubic identity K_E+D=0 and the actual log-crepant modifications give K_Q+B_Q=0 by adjunction. This uses the complete outer-vertex family join, not only an interior affine chart.

There is no omitted connectedness condition. The explicit blowdowns give H1(Q,O_Q)=0, since each smooth point blowup has R1O=0 and a P1 bundle over P1 has H1O=0. In 0->K_Q->O_Q->O_(B_Q)->0, Serre duality gives H1(K_Q)=H1(O_Q)^*=0, so H0(O_(B_Q))=C. The nonempty reduced boundary is connected. Adjunction to each smooth rational component then makes its total intersection with the other boundary branches equal to two. Since the boundary is reduced nodal, its dual graph is a connected cycle, including the usual two-edge convention. Thus (Q,B_Q), with the actual blowdown presentation above, satisfies every input of `inline construction 46`.

That supplier now gives actual corner blowups followed by interior boundary blowdowns to a smooth toric pair. Its existing relative cone and exceptional-cover calculations therefore compute these conductor vertices without a Fano/nef restriction. The contact orders, normal-frame units and all exceptional total-transform classes are those of the constructed morphisms; no arbitrary cubic classification is inserted.

#### 5. The conductor glues as an ordinary new-target double curve

After the actual conductor blowups, the original self-conductor is replaced by ordinary double curves between **normal** projective component surfaces. Their double curve is C, not L. Relative evaluation maps therefore land in C, and the existing virtual normalization/gluing operation uses the actual diagonal

    Delta_C:C -> C times C.

Its normal enhancement and primitive Cartier factors are the ordinary integral node factors of the already constructed regular-SNC target. The degree-two map C->L remains part of the actual proper pushforward of curve classes and evaluation coupons. It is not an automorphism of a map to the new target and produces no universal factor 1/2. For example the pushforward of Delta_C along C times C->L times L is 2 Delta_L, whereas using Delta_L before gluing would also admit the two different-sheet pairs; those are different operations. The correct operation is gluing on C first and then proper pushforward, exactly as the actual model supplies.

Apply the already constructed projective-DF proper support and its closure of the original generic cone on this regular-SNC algebraic-space target. This argument includes vanishing c3 or d3.

The projective exceptional cubic is

If it is reducible, a plane factor must involve b: a plane independent of b would divide both t and VW, which is impossible. Normalize that plane to b+L(V,W,t). Polynomial division forces the second factor to be t b-VW-tL. Hence the constant term is -L VW-tL². Because g3 has no V²W or VW² monomials, L must be λt. Thus the ONLY reducible alternative is

    E=(b+λt)(t b-VW-λt²).

The second component is a smooth quadric. For λ≠0 their intersection is the smooth conic VW+2λt²=0, so this is the ordinary smooth-double-component case. #### Normal irreducible cubic singularities: finite local proof

For an irreducible normal cubic surface, the singular points are isolated. At any singular point its actual affine equation is Q2+Q3. Rank-three Q2 gives A1; rank-two Q2 gives an A-type block by Morse in two variables. Isolation makes the residual one-variable power finite, and its unit is removed by an actual étale root.

If rank(Q2)=one, choose its square variable x. Absorb the x² and x³ cubic terms into its unit; write the remaining cubic as x q2(y,z)+h3(y,z). Morse in x gives x'²+h(y,z), with leading h3 and next term -q2²/4.

The binary cubic h3 cannot vanish identically, for then the original equation has factor x. If h3 has three distinct roots, the standard D4 leading form has Jacobian quotient of weights strictly less than its degree. Higher-weight terms are removed successively by its explicit Jacobian division; the actual finite contact matrix lemma algebraizes this central equivalence. If h3 has a double root, choose h3=y²z. Normality forces a nonzero z² coefficient in q2: otherwise q2 is divisible by y and the original cubic is singular along x=y=0. With that coefficient nonzero the weighted leading equation is D5, x'²+y²z+κz⁴, κ≠0; all remaining terms have greater D5 weight. If h3 has a triple root, choose h3=y³. The same singular-line argument forces a nonzero z² coefficient in q2, and the weighted leading equation is E6, x'²+y³+κz⁴. All remaining terms have greater E6 weight. For D5 and E6 the explicit Jacobian quotient bases have maximal weight below the equation degree, so the same division kills every higher-weight term. Finite contact determinacy gives an ACTUAL étale central normal form.

Finally Q2=0 is impossible for a normal cubic containing the outer plane curve described below: the singular point would be the vertex of a cubic cone. Any plane section avoiding the vertex of a cone over a smooth cubic is smooth elliptic, whereas our outer plane curve is nodal or reducible. A plane containing the vertex gives concurrent lines, whereas the outer curve is a nodal cubic, a transverse line/conic pair, or the nonconcurrent triangle. A cone over a singular cubic is nonnormal. These alternatives exhaust cones.

Thus this particular normal exceptional cubic needs only A, D4, D5 and E6 family blocks, all now constructed. This argument does not invoke cubic-surface ADE classification or an abstract simultaneous-resolution theorem.

#### Outer boundary and exact scope

The outer plane t=0 cuts E in bVW+c3 V³+d3 W³. When c3d3≠0 this is a rational nodal cubic, whose node [b:V:W]=[1:0:0] lies in the SMOOTH locus of E because ∂F/∂t=b² there. Smooth points of a plane section automatically lie in the smooth locus of the surface. Its node may be blown up as an ordinary corner, producing the rational cycle used by the toric-model packet.

If exactly one of c3,d3 vanishes, the outer curve is a transverse line and conic. A normal E is smooth at their intersections except possibly their common point with b=0; that point has rank-two tangent form and is an ordinary toric boundary double block, handled by the existing two-base Morse construction. Irreducible NONNORMAL E is not inferred to be resolved by this inventory; use its actual conductor-family packet and check the outer labels. ### Inline construction 11

#### Actual divisorial toric gluing: local links, S2 and the log dualizing module

Research hypothesis bridge. This file spells out the SCHEME-THEORETIC meaning required of “toric surfaces glued along toric divisors.” Its input is an actual finite toric normalization diagram with divisor isomorphisms whose pushout IS the central surface. Merely listing normalization components and generic codimension-one intersections does not state this input; if the record uses only that weaker meaning, the pushout identity remains a prerequisite. No extra isolated point identifications or finite-length subring modifications are silently allowed.

#### Actual completed local gluing

Fix a point p. The normalization has finitely many preimages p_i, with completed normal toric surface rings B_i. The only incident toric boundary curves at p_i are its two cone rays. Generic strict semistability means each glued divisor has exactly two normalization branches, and the actual gluing is an isomorphism of their normal toric curve rings. Mark the unglued ray branches as outer boundary. The actual pushout assumption says the completed ring is EXACTLY

    A = { (f_i) in product B_i : f_i|edge = f_j|edge at every glued edge }.

It includes all constant-term matching; this is an equality of rings, not just a bijection of geometric points.

Build the graph whose vertices are p_i and whose edges are the glued ray branches. Every vertex has at most two incident edges. Its graph is CONNECTED: otherwise tuples equal to one on one graph component and zero on all others would be nontrivial idempotents of the LOCAL ring A. Thus it is a cycle when no outer ray is present, and a path when outer rays occur (including the single-vertex cases). This derives the connected link from actual gluing/locality, without assuming that a generic affine pseudomanifold has connected links.

The map delta:product B_i→product_edge C[[z_e]] takes the difference of the two restrictions. Its kernel is A. Its image K is a submodule of the product of the edge DVRs and has no finite-length submodule. Each B_i has depth two. In the exact sequence 0→A→product B_i→K→0, local cohomology H⁰(K)=0 and H⁰(B_i)=H¹(B_i)=0 give H⁰(A)=H¹(A)=0. Hence A is Cohen–Macaulay of dimension two, in particular S2. This proof works for paths as well as cycles and for arbitrary cyclic quotient toric normalizers.

If instead a record identifies additional isolated points across disconnected graph components, its local ring is a proper finite-colength subring of this equalizer. It can have depth one. Such a record is NOT the stated divisorial pushout, and cannot be passed through this proof by saying only that the normalization is toric.

#### Canonical and log canonical calculation

On each toric B_i choose an oriented lattice basis. Its invariant rational two-form Omega_i has a simple logarithmic pole on each ray boundary. In primitive toric coordinates its residues are dlog(z) and -dlog(z) on the two rays. This remains true for a quotient cone: if its lattice has index r, the invariant volume is r dlog(u) wedge dlog(v), while the boundary primitive coordinate is v^r (or u^r), so the residue is exactly dlog(v^r), not an unaccounted 1/r.

Dualizing the finite normalization gluing sequence gives the following explicit residue description: a dualizing form is a tuple of normalizer forms with at most simple conductor poles and opposite residues on a glued pair. One may verify this directly with the monomial Cech pairing: the coefficient of u^-1v^-1 gives the top local duality functional, and on an edge its boundary is the coefficient of z^-1; the two boundary orientations have opposite signs. Coefficients with nonnegative monomial exponents give the regular normalizer module, and exactness of the preceding depth-two gluing sequence leaves precisely the opposite-residue kernel. This proves the description for these toric pushouts, not a general duality assertion inferred from points.

Choose the signs of Omega_i successively along the path or cycle. Compatibility around a cycle holds because at each normalizer its two oriented residues have opposite signs and each edge imposes one further opposite sign. A tuple f_i Omega_i satisfies the residue conditions exactly when the coefficients f_i match under all divisor gluings, i.e. when (f_i) belongs to A. Thus, for an INTERIOR cycle,

    omega_A = A·Omega.

The central local ring is Gorenstein. For a path the same calculation keeps the logarithmic poles on the unpaired outer rays and gives

    omega_A(D_outer)=A·Omega

as a reflexive log canonical module. It does NOT claim omega_A itself invertible or D_outer Cartier. This distinction is essential at outer quotient vertices.

#### Actual total family consequences and precise boundary

If A is an interior central ring and the total family is flat over the trait, its parameter is a non-zero-divisor and its quotient is the Cohen–Macaulay Gorenstein A. The total local ring is therefore Cohen–Macaulay and Gorenstein (lift the canonical module or the minimal self-dual free resolution). Generic smoothness and strict semistability outside the finite exceptional set give regularity in codimension one of the total threefold. S2 then makes it normal. These are the exact hypotheses needed for the cyclic toric sail/pushout, generalized PBW and crepant point-extraction suppliers; they are not merely asserted from the word “fan.”

For an outer path, flatness still gives a Cohen–Macaulay total ring, but this proof gives only a LOG canonical statement on the central pair. An actual boundary Cartier/log-module lifting or equivalent boundary capping construction is still required before applying the interior Gorenstein PBW lemma. If the TOTAL outer divisor is already effective Cartier and its central curve is nodal, the initial outer target is automatically a hypersurface: quotient by its regular Cartier equation has embedding dimension two, so the surface ring has embedding dimension at most three; relative Morse gives the actual double/hypersurface blocks already constructed. Without that Cartier input it is not legitimate to use this last sentence.

This file therefore resolves the S2/connected-link/Gorenstein INTERIOR bridge for genuine divisorial pushouts and records the exact remaining outer-path distinction. It does not strengthen an underspecified record into a pushout by fiat and does not resolve the non-Cartier outer family.

### Inline construction 12

#### An actual E6 family reduces to D5 by a relative line

Research construction. This gives an algebraic simultaneous-resolution step for the E6 family, with no formal algebraization or Weyl-cover theorem. It consumes the finite contact-normal-form lemma and the already constructed D-family resolution.

#### 1. Actual E6 normal form and a cubic compactification

For f0=X²+Y³+Z⁴, the derivative ideal is (X,Y²,Z³), with residue basis

    1,Y,Z,YZ,Z²,YZ².

The finite t-order elimination and finite implicit contact correction used for A,D and pinch apply verbatim with this basis: finite Taylor division is algebraic, generic smoothness supplies t^M in the relative contact Jacobian, and choosing N>=2M+1 corrects the remaining error by an actual etale implicit system fixing t. Thus an actual E6 deformation with smooth generic fibre is etale-contact-equivalent to

    X²+Y³+Z⁴+a5(t)YZ²+a4(t)YZ+a3(t)Z²
       +a2(t)Y+a1(t)Z+a0(t),

where all ai vanish modulo t and are actual elements of R=C[[t]] (finite polynomials in t suffice).

Replace X by x+iZ², with i²=−1, so X²+Z⁴=x²+2ixZ². After a constant rescaling, homogenize to the actual cubic family in P3_R whose central member is

    F0=x²w+xz²+y³=0.                                      (1)

The general lower-degree deformation terms are homogenized by the appropriate powers of w. Central (1) has only the E6 point p=[0:0:0:1]; its other points are smooth. The auxiliary cubic generic fibre is smooth: any generic singular locus in this projective family would have a central specialization by properness; smooth central points exclude it there, and the actual local bound t^M in the Jacobian excludes it near p. The unit in the contact equivalence is retained in the normal-frame data.

#### 2. A relative line specializing to the central line exists after a finite trait extension

The central line L={x=y=0} lies on (1). Lines in its Grassmannian affine chart have equations

    x=a z+b w,       y=c z+d w.

Restricting (1) to such a line gives the four coefficient equations

    a+c³=0,
    a²+b+3c²d=0,
    2ab+3cd²=0,
    b²+d³=0.                                              (2)

The point a=b=c=d=0 is isolated in this central line scheme. Indeed a=−c³ and b=−c⁶−3c²d. If c=0, the last equation gives d=0. If c≠0, put d=lambda c⁴; the last two equations give

    3lambda²+6lambda+2=0,
    lambda³+9lambda²+6lambda+1=0.

They have no common root. Reducing the second by the first forces lambda=−11/26, which does not satisfy the first (its value is −1/676). Thus c=0 and all four coordinates vanish.

For the deformed cubic, the same restriction gives four actual equations over R in the regular five-dimensional local ring R[a,b,c,d]. Their central fibre at this point is zero-dimensional by (2). The four equations consequently have height four; their quotient is a one-dimensional complete intersection, and t is a nonzerodivisor because a vertical one-dimensional component would contradict the zero-dimensional central fibre. The relative line scheme is therefore flat and has a component through L dominating the trait.

The global line incidence is closed in the Grassmannian times the cubic parameter space and is projective over R. Take the closure of this dominating local component and normalize one branch. Its generic point is finite over C((t)); properness extends it over the integral closure of R in that finite field extension. That integral closure is a finite complete DVR with residue C. After this finite trait extension, we obtain an ACTUAL relative line L_R, a P1 bundle over the trait, specializing to L. No formal root or infinitesimal line is being substituted for a line on the family.

#### 3. Blow up the relative line; its generic ideal is Cartier

A Grassmannian coordinate change over the extended DVR takes L_R to x=y=0 and is the identity on the central member. Because the line is actually contained in every cubic, its equation has the form xA2+yB2. Blow up its actual coherent ideal (x,y) on the cubic family.

On the generic smooth surface a line is a Cartier divisor, so this blowup is the identity on the entire generic fibre. Along the central line away from p the central surface is smooth, hence the total family is smooth there and its line ideal is Cartier. Thus the modification is supported over the one central E6 point.

The central x-chart y=xa gives

    xw+z²+x²a³=0,

which is smooth near p (w=1). The central y-chart x=yb gives

    y²+y b²+b z²=0.                                       (3)

Completing the square y'=y+b²/2 turns (3) into

    y'²+b z²−b⁴/4=0,

an actual D5 equation after constant unit roots. All displayed changes are finite algebraic etale operations.

The corresponding charts of the full family are flat over the trait: each is a hypersurface whose central defining equation is nonzero, and hence t remains a nonzerodivisor. There is no extra vertical component hidden by an unjustified base change of a Rees algebra. Their local central equations are respectively smooth and the integral D5 equation just computed. The central exceptional fibre is a projective line; the singular successor is the unique D5 point on it.

#### 4. Canonical comparison and finite completion

The modification is crepant. In each central chart the ambient determinant contributes the same factor x or y that is removed from the defining equation, so the hypersurface residue has factor one. Equivalently, the ambient relative-line blowup has discrepancy one and the family hypersurface has order one along the relative line. The base parameter is unchanged. Hence the central partial resolution has the expected one exceptional curve and a D5 remainder, rather than an uncontrolled exceptional surface.

The transformed D5 family is an actual deformation with smooth generic fibre. Apply the finite actual D5 contact-normal-form lemma and the explicit D-family small-blowup recursion. The latter terminates at A-type and regular charts after the prescribed finite trait extension. This completes an actual etale-local simultaneous-resolution construction of the E6 family. The isolated-supported proper etale descent then globalizes this modification at its exact separately proved scope; no global formal centre has been assumed.

The construction does not prove E7 or E8 simultaneous resolution, and does not claim that arbitrary broad corner reduction avoids those types. It supplies the bounded E6 step which can occur in the weighted del-Pezzo-two cross analysis.

### Inline construction 13

#### Actual E7 family resolution by a relative bitangent curve

Research construction, not production acceptance. Work over a complete characteristic-zero trait R, after finite extension when stated. The input is an actual E7 family whose central local equation is w²=z³+y³z and whose generic fiber is smooth. The finite relative-Jacobian/contact lemma in `inline construction 49`, with the finite division described below, gives an actual étale, parameter-fixed contact equivalence to the polynomial family used here. No formal coordinate change is silently algebraized.

#### 1. Actual polynomial and projective family

For f=z³+y³z the Jacobian ideal is (3z²+y³,3y²z). Its quotient has basis 1,y,y²,y³,y⁴,z,yz: first reduce z², then y²z; multiplying these relations gives y⁵=0. All higher monomials have actual polynomial division by those two generators. Including w eliminates w. Finite t-adic elimination leaves actual polynomial coefficients in this seven-dimensional span; generic smoothness supplies t^M in the relative contact Jacobian, and the finite matrix correction gives exact étale contact equivalence, as in the cited lemma. All changes fix t.

Homogenize the resulting equation to weighted degree four in P_R(1_X,1_Y,1_Z,2_W). The central surface is

    S0: W²=Z³X+Y³Z.

Its only singularity is p=[1:0:0:0]. In X=1 it is E7; at the two points of X=0 with W=0, either the X derivative or the Z derivative is nonzero. The whole central surface avoids the singular point of the ambient weighted projective space. Therefore the total family is a projective Gorenstein surface family, and its generic fiber is smooth: any generic singularity would specialize, by properness, to p or to the smooth central locus; the latter is impossible by openness of smoothness, and the former by the relative Jacobian t-power identity.

Write H=O(1) on this surface. It is an actual line bundle here, -K=H, H²=2. The central curve C={Z=W=0} is a reduced P¹ and H.C=1.

#### 2. An actual relative exceptional curve exists

On any smooth degree-two double-plane surface, the Hilbert scheme locus of smooth rational H-degree-one curves is finite étale. Adjunction gives C²=-1, so its normal bundle is O_P¹(-1); both H⁰ and H¹ vanish, proving étaleness and relative dimension zero.

This locus is proper over the parameter space of smooth weighted quartics. For a DVR family of smooth surfaces, the closure of a generic such curve in the regular total threefold is a Cartier divisor. It has no vertical components, hence is flat over the DVR. Its special divisor has H-degree one, so ampleness makes it integral and reduced. Its arithmetic genus remains zero, so it is a smooth P¹. Adjunction then gives self-intersection -1. Thus it remains in the same Hilbert locus; no embedded-point limit was assumed away.

This finite étale locus is nonempty over the connected smooth-quartic parameter space. Indeed W²=Y⁴+Z(X³+Z³) has smooth branch quartic (its three partial derivatives have no common projective zero), and Z=0, W=Y² is one such curve. The smooth parameter space is a nonempty open subset of the irreducible coefficient affine space, hence connected; a nonempty finite étale proper image is open and closed. Consequently every smooth generic member has such a curve after a finite field extension.

Take its closure in the proper relative Hilbert scheme over R, and normalize the horizontal curve through that generic point. This gives a finite trait extension and a flat closed family E_R of curves with Hilbert polynomial n+1.

#### 3. The special curve and its actual two generators

The affine complement Z≠0 in S0 is A²: set Z=1, then X=W²-Y³. Thus the Weil divisor class group is generated by the single prime C, because the complement has trivial class group and Z has divisor 2C. As H=2C and H.C=1, every effective cycle of H-degree one is linearly equivalent to C.

There is only one effective divisor in the class C. Explicitly O(C) has exactly one global section: its square embeds in O(2C)=H⁰(O(1))=span(X,Y,Z), and a square rational section with divisor supported by a degree-one prime forces the associated line in the plane to have its double-cover inverse image equal to twice that prime. Equivalently, direct restriction of W²=Z(Z²X+Y³) to a line shows that a doubled inverse image requires the line to divide the branch quartic, and its only linear factor is Z. This also proves directly that the unique degree-one prime in class C is C.

For completeness the class-to-linear-system assertion can be checked without square-root choices: if div(h)+C is effective then h²Z is a regular section of H, so is a linear form L. The equality h²=L/Z in the function field forces the divisor of L/Z to be even. Away from C the cover is unramified at the generic point of any non-branch line, where that divisor has multiplicity one. Hence L must be the branch-line Z, giving h constant.

The special fundamental cycle of E_R is therefore C. Away from p, the total surface is regular, so the curve closure is Cartier and has no embedded points there. Any extra structure is zero-dimensional at p. But C has Hilbert polynomial n+1, exactly the flat Hilbert polynomial of E_R; hence there is no extra structure at p either. Its special ideal is exactly (Z,W).

The relative line bundle H|E_R has h⁰=2,h¹=0 on the special P¹ and on the generic P¹. Cohomology and base change gives a rank-two bundle of sections, and the three coordinate sections X,Y,Z generate it. Their kernel is a rank-one direct summand, giving an actual relative linear form l, reducing to Z, which vanishes on E_R. The map E_R→{l=0}=P¹_R induced by H is an isomorphism: it is a proper morphism inducing isomorphisms on both geometric fibers, and its structure-sheaf map is an isomorphism by coherent Nakayama. W on E_R is a section of O_P¹(2), so it lifts to an actual homogeneous quadratic q in X,Y,Z. Consequently

    I_E=(l,W-q).

This follows first on the special fiber and then from Nakayama for the quotient ideal; flatness of O_E gives the required exact reduction. The defining quartic satisfies F4-q²=l B with B cubic, because it vanishes identically on the relative line. Thus the family equation is

    (W-q)(W+q)=l B.

Here q reduces to zero and B reduces to Z²X+Y³.

#### 4. The actual blowup and its central charts

Blow up the actual coherent ideal (l,W-q). It is Cartier on the smooth generic surface (the curve E is a Cartier divisor there), so the blowup is generically the identity. Its two saturated charts have equations

    l-chart: l a²+2qa=B,       W=q+la;
    (W-q)-chart: W+q=bB,      l=b(W-q).

These are actual equations over R, not just central formal equations: the canceled factors are non-zero-divisors, and each displayed hypersurface is flat over R because its central equation is nonzero. They are therefore the schematic closure of their generic blowup charts and equal the blowup charts.

On the central fiber in X=1 the first chart is

    z a²=z²+y³.

Completing the square in z gives an E6 singularity (a nonzero unit multiple of z'²+y³-a⁴/4). The other chart is

    W=b(W²b²+Y³),

which is smooth near the fiber over p by its W derivative. The remaining central points were already smooth. The blowup is small: its exceptional fiber over p is a curve, while it is the identity over the smooth central locus, where C is Cartier. Both source and target are Gorenstein; the residue canonical form agrees off codimension two, so the modification is crepant. The old trait parameter is unchanged.

Apply the actual E6-family construction in `inline construction 12`, then the actual D-family recursion and A-type ordered-root resolution. This provides an actual simultaneous resolution after finite trait extension, with no new central surface component and with the central minimal E7 exceptional curves retained. Global assembly uses the isolated-fiber étale descent packet; no blanket scheme-descent assertion is made.

#### Interface and limit

This closes an actual E7 family block, conditional only on the explicitly constructed finite contact lemma and the existing E6/D/A packets. It does not itself prove that every nonunit reducible-cross family reduces to finitely many ADE blocks; that inventory/termination remains a separate prerequisite of the broad degeneration theorem.

### Inline construction 14

#### Actual E8 trait resolution by a Hilbert limit and all-power flatness

Research construction only; no production admission or assertion that every broad corner has reached an ADE block. Work over an excellent complete characteristic-zero trait R with algebraically closed residue field C; finite trait extensions are allowed. The input is an actual algebraic/étale hypersurface family with central germ E8 and smooth generic fibre. The finite-contact construction in inline construction 2 applies verbatim to the Jacobian basis below. The output is an actual projective modification, generic identity, whose sole nonregular central germ is E7. The subsequent supplier is inline construction 13.

#### Actual compactification

Use weights (X,Y,Z,W)=(1,1,2,3). The central surface is

    S0: W²=Z³+Y⁵X in P(1,1,2,3).

Its only singularity is p=[1:0:0:0], an E8 germ. The weight-two and weight-three orbifold points are absent. H=O(1)|S0 is Cartier, ample, -K=H and H²=1. The central affine equation at p has Jacobian ideal (W,Z²,Y⁴), with quotient basis Z^aY^b, a=0,1 and 0≤b≤3. Finite t-order elimination followed by the exact finite implicit contact correction therefore gives an ACTUAL normal form with these eight coefficients in R. Homogenize its terms by powers X^(6-2a-b). This yields a projective flat weighted sextic S/R with the prescribed actual étale germ, central S0, and smooth generic fibre. Properness and the fact that the central surface is smooth away from p exclude new generic singularities away from this germ. The ambient orbifold locus remains disjoint after shrinking the trait. All uses of H below are therefore uses of a genuine line bundle.

The 6-Veronese embeds this family in ordinary projective space: monomials of weights 1,2,3 and total weight a multiple of 6 partition into weight-six monomials (first pair weight-three variables, group weight-two variables in triples, then handle the remaining bounded residues using weight-one variables). Thus relative Hilbert schemes apply with polarization 6H.

#### A generic exceptional curve exists

For completeness the needed existence concerns smooth weighted sextics, and does not assume a line count or a Weyl cover. In the irreducible coefficient space of equations

    W²=Z³+A4(X,Y)Z+B6(X,Y),

the smooth locus is a nonempty connected open set. The locus of curves with Hilbert polynomial 6n+1 is proper, being a closed part of the projective Hilbert scheme. On a smooth fibre any such curve has H-degree one. Its cycle is one reduced irreducible curve; if it is a smooth rational curve, adjunction gives self-intersection -1 and normal bundle O_P1(-1). Consequently its embedded deformation is unique and unobstructed: H0(N)=H1(N)=0, so its relative Hilbert locus is étale and quasi-finite at that point.

The exceptional-curve locus is also proper OVER THE SMOOTH SURFACE LOCUS. To verify the valuative assertion, take a trait of smooth surfaces. Its total space is regular. The schematic closure of an integral generic exceptional curve is a prime Weil divisor, hence Cartier. Its quotient is trait-flat and has no vertical component. It is the Hilbert limit, by uniqueness of the flat closure. Its central cycle has degree one, hence one reduced prime; Cartierness excludes embedded points. Its arithmetic genus is zero by flatness, so the central curve is smooth rational and adjunction again gives square -1. This proves properness, without supposing that arbitrary singular-fibre limits are Cartier.

Nonemptiness follows from B6=Y⁶ and A4=X⁴: Z=0, W=Y³ is a rational curve of H-degree one. The surface is smooth along this curve (the W derivative works at Y≠0 and the Z derivative works at Y=0), and the explicit affine-cone Jacobian proves smoothness everywhere: a singular nonzero cone point would have W=Y=0, 3Z²+X⁴=0 and 4X³Z=0, forcing X=Z=0. The surface avoids the two ambient orbifold points. Hence the proper étale locus has nonempty image, both open and closed in the connected smooth coefficient locus. Every smooth weighted sextic has an exceptional curve after an algebraic field extension. Apply this to S_K, and make a finite trait extension to define one such curve E_K.

#### The special Hilbert ideal is uniquely Y times m_p

Let C/R be its projective flat Hilbert closure and I its ideal in S. The ideal is the t-saturation of its generic prime ideal; hence C is integral. Its special cycle has H-degree one and is one prime with multiplicity one.

Since S0 minus (Y=0) is the affine plane (set Y=1 and eliminate X), the divisor class group is generated by D=(Y=0). This generator has infinite order because H·D=1. Thus every degree-one prime is linearly equivalent to H and is a member of |H|=P(span(X,Y)). In particular a prime through p must be D.

Here is the support detail needed to rule out a cycle avoiding p. The integral flat curve C over the excellent trait is pure two-dimensional; minimal primes over its nonzero parameter have height one. The dimension formula therefore makes every irreducible component of its special SUPPORT one-dimensional. There cannot be an isolated support point outside the special cycle. Away from p the ambient total S is regular, so the prime ideal of C is Cartier there, and its special fibre has no embedded point there. If the degree-one cycle avoided p, the entire special support would avoid p. The closure would then lie in the regular locus near all of its proper support; it would be a relative Cartier divisor. The degree of its normal line bundle is constant in this proper flat curve family, giving C0²=E_K²=-1. But its central class is H, of square +1, a contradiction. Thus the cycle is D.

The special ideal I0 is contained in (Y), with quotient (Y)/I0 supported only at p. The Cartier curve D has arithmetic genus one, hence χ(O_D)=0; the flat exceptional curve has χ(O_C0)=1. Therefore this quotient has length one. Multiplication by Y identifies it with O_S0/J for an ideal J of colength one at p, necessarily m_p. We have proved the SCHEME-THEORETIC equality

    I0 = Y m_p.

This includes the necessary embedded length-one correction; replacing the limit by its cycle alone would be incorrect.

#### Every Rees power commutes with the special fibre

For k≥1 set J_k=(I^k:t^infinity). This is a coherent ideal, and O_S/J_k is trait-flat. Its generic fibre is the k-fold thickening of E_K. With polarization H its Hilbert polynomial is

    k n + k(k+1)/2,

because the successive conormal quotients on E_K=P1 are O(j), 0≤j<k.

The image of I^k in O_S0 is exactly I0^k=Y^k m_p^k. The special image J_k,0 contains it. The ordinary initial form of the local E8 equation W²-Z³-Y⁵ is W², so

    gr_m O_S0,p = C[W,Z,Y]/(W²),
    dim m^j/m^(j+1)=2j+1,
    length O/m^k=k².

This computation is direct and does not assume rationality of the singularity. The Cartier thickening kD has polynomial k n-k(k-1)/2 (successive quotients O_D(-jD), degree -j and χ(O_D)=0). Multiplication by Y^k identifies (Y^k)/(Y^k m^k) with O/m^k. Hence O_S0/I0^k has polynomial

    k n-k(k-1)/2+k² = k n+k(k+1)/2.

Flatness gives the SAME polynomial for O_S0/J_k,0. The quotient J_k,0/I0^k consequently has zero Hilbert polynomial and is zero. Thus J_k=I^k+tJ_k locally along the central fibre: if j=i+t a, saturation forces a∈J_k. Nakayama applied to the coherent module J_k/I^k, supported centrally, gives J_k=I^k. In particular O_S/I^k is flat, and the inclusion of I^k has no special-fibre kernel. Degree by degree,

    (⊕ I^k) ⊗R C = ⊕ I0^k.

This proves the required Rees BASE CHANGE, rather than merely the automatic torsion-freeness of each ideal as an R-module.

#### The actual central blowup and its family

Take Bl_I S. It is projective and generic identity because E_K is Cartier. The established Rees identity identifies its central fibre with

    Bl_(Y m_p) S0 = Bl_m_p S0;

the Cartier factor Y does not change Proj. In affine E8 coordinates x²+y³+z⁵=0 the point blowup has charts:

* z-chart: x=z a,y=z b, giving a²+z b³+z³=0, an E7 germ at the origin;
* y-chart: x=y a,z=y b, giving a²+y+y³b⁵=0, regular;
* x-chart: 1+x a³+x³b⁵=0, with no exceptional point.

The first chart is smooth elsewhere on its exceptional line. Thus the central fibre is normal Gorenstein, with one E7 point. The family blowup is flat (its Rees ring and its charts have no trait torsion). Flatness and the Gorenstein central fibre make its total space Gorenstein near the central fibre; it is normal by the fibrewise normality criterion. The exceptional set of the family map is only the central exceptional curve, so it has codimension two. Canonical forms agree on the complement and extend across codimension two on the normal Gorenstein total space. The modification is consequently crepant. There is no divisorial generic modification hidden in the construction.

Apply the actual E7-to-E6 supplier, then the actual E6-to-D5 line blowup and the finite D recursion. Every auxiliary choice was made on an actual proper Hilbert scheme after finite trait extension, and every modification is an actual blowup. Global use still requires the separately constructed proper étale local descent/common-domination interface; this note does not infer that interface from a formal isomorphism.

### Inline construction 15

#### Coherent comparison for projective curves and surfaces by finite projection

Research binding lemma, using the ACTUAL P² projective-coherent GAGA supplier rather than extending its public dimension scope by assertion. Exact published item read in full: `physics/items/thm-qftx-projective-gaga-for-monad-data.md`, whose absolute Statement is for P²/P³. Its exact local flatness/coherence supplier applies to all finite-type complex local rings. We need only cohomology and Hom comparison for algebraic coherent sheaves on an algebraic projective curve or surface, not essential surjectivity for arbitrary analytic sheaves.

Embed an integral projective variety Z of dimension r≤2 in P^N. Choose r+1 general linear forms with no common zero on Z; the incidence dimension for a codimension-r-1 linear center gives this choice. They define a morphism f:Z→P^r with f*O(1)=O_Z(1) ample. No fiber contains a curve, because its pullback O(1) would have degree zero on it. Thus f is proper quasi-finite and hence FINITE. The same argument on finitely many components handles any projective coherent sheaf support needed here. For r=1 include P¹ as a line in P² after taking finite projection; for r=0 include its finite support in P². Consequently a finite map followed by a closed inclusion reduces every required cohomology comparison to a coherent sheaf on P².

For a finite algebraic map, pushforward of quasi-coherent modules is exact on each affine inverse image, higher direct images vanish, and H^q(Z,F)=H^q(P^r,f_*F). Analytification commutes with this FINITE pushforward. To check this last assertion without a general relative-GAGA claim, take a finite affine algebra presentation and a finite module presentation. The natural comparison (f_*F)^an→f^an_*F^an has, at a complex base point, the completed stalk map equal to the finite product of the completed module maps over its finitely many preimages. The local analytic/algebraic comparison gives exactly these common completions. Both sides are coherent analytic modules: the finite algebra/module matrices give their finite analytic presentations. Faithful flatness of completion of a Noetherian analytic local ring therefore makes the map an isomorphism.

Analytic finite pushforward is exact even on underlying abelian sheaves. The map is closed with finite fibers in Hausdorff analytic spaces. For disjoint neighborhoods of the finitely many preimages of a point, properness shrinks a base neighborhood whose whole inverse image lies in their union. These neighborhoods are cofinal, so the pushforward stalk is the finite product of the source stalks. Finite products are exact. Pushforward also preserves flasque sheaves (restriction is restriction on inverse-image opens). Pushing a flasque resolution thus proves H^q(Z^an,F^an)=H^q((P^r)^an,f^an_*F^an) directly. Closed inclusions are treated the same way, or by their exact extension-by-zero pushforward.

Apply the published P² comparison to the coherent pushforward (and its closed inclusion if r<2). This gives the canonical isomorphism

    H^q(Z,F) → H^q(Z^an,F^an)

for EVERY q and every algebraic coherent F on the projective curve or surface Z. The construction is natural, since it is the same finite-presentation and cohomology pullback comparison on both sides. For coherent F,G, exact analytification and finite presentation identify Hom-sheaves; the q=0 comparison gives full faithfulness on algebraic coherent objects. In particular an algebraic line bundle on a projective curve which is holomorphically trivial is algebraically trivial: its holomorphic nowhere-vanishing section algebraizes, and its zero locus is empty on the algebraic scheme as well.

This supplies the algebraic/analytic H¹(O_S) comparison used in the outer-cycle Hodge argument and the holomorphic-to-algebraic line-bundle step used in the elliptic evaluation symmetry. It invokes neither GAGA on arbitrary P^N outside the published supplier's Statement nor algebraization of holomorphic families over a nonproper base. Published status is the actual item metadata, not a new acceptance receipt; its scoped verification remains Root-owned.

### Inline construction 16

#### Statement

Assume AC. Let R=C[[t]], and let F=0 be an actual pointed algebraic/étale hypersurface family in three spatial variables over R, with smooth generic fibre and central equation f=x²-v³-v²w². Then after the single fixed cover t=s^60 there is a finite sequence of actual normalized weighted blowup charts, generic identity and retaining the parameter, that reduces its unique tangential-conductor branch to one of six simultaneous weighted degree-six faces described in the proof. Every exceptional face is either a normal weighted degree-one surface with boundary X²=V²(V+W²), or has actual normalization P² with a smooth conductor conic and a transverse outer line. No tangential-conductor crossing recurs on a nonzero terminal face. This is a finite reduction lemma: resolutions of the remaining normal surface singularities, ordinary-double and ordinary-pinch family blocks, their global descent and virtual comparisons are separate supplier obligations. No claim about an arbitrary non-complete-intersection toric central vertex is included.

#### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** Exactly the actual pointed family, complete complex trait, central equation and smooth generic fibre in the Statement. All finite implicit systems and blowup ideals in the proof are defined on actual pointed étale neighborhoods; a formal isomorphism is not supplied as a modification.

[F1] [[def-axiom-of-choice]] supplies the finitely many selections and compatible algebraic coefficients used here. [[def-etale-morphism-schemes]] fixes the meaning of the actual pointed étale neighborhoods; finite implicit systems below have invertible coordinate Jacobian and relative dimension zero.

[F2] [[def-rees-algebra-ideal-sheaf]] fixes the actual Rees construction. A normalized weighted blowup means relative Proj of the integral closure of the finite monomial Rees algebra for the specified weights; its charts are the explicitly displayed substitutions, with the finite cyclic quotient charts on non-unit weights retained.

[F3] [[thm-serre-normality-criterion]] applies to the Noetherian hypersurface rings used below. They are Cohen–Macaulay, hence S2; their codimension-one regularity is checked on the explicit double-cover DVR equations.

#### Proof

**Proof technique:** direct.

**Construction step 1.1.** The spatial derivatives of f generate (2x,-2vw²-3v²,-2v²w). Modulo this ideal, x=0, v²=-(2/3)vw² and vw³=0. Exact division of an actual function h therefore has remainder c(w)+v(b0+b1w+b2w²). This division is algebraic: first evaluate x=0; next evaluate v=0 and v=-(2/3)w², divide their difference by -(2/3)w², and subtract its linear-v interpolation. The remainder is divisible by v(v+(2/3)w²). Finally divide its linear-v coefficient by w³. Each division is by a regular graph equation on a common pointed étale refinement. [given, F1, construct, algebra]

**Construction step 1.2.** Generic smoothness implies that J_F=(F,F_x,F_v,F_w) becomes the unit ideal after inverting t. Clearing the finitely many denominators gives t^M in J_F for some integer M. Apply the preceding exact division at finitely many t-orders to remove Jacobian multiples by coordinate and contact-unit changes fixing t. For arbitrary finite N this gives an actual G=f+c(w,t)+v(b0(t)+b1(t)w+b2(t)w²), with F-G divisible by t^N, c(w,0)=b_i(0)=0, and finitely many actual axis functions as coefficients. [given, F1, step 1.1, construct, algebra]

**Construction step 1.3.** Choose N>=2M+1. The relative derivative ideals of F and G differ modulo t^N; their finite certificate gives t^M(1-t^(N-M)a) in J_G for an actual a. The bracket is a unit, so t^M is in J_G. Consequently F-G belongs to (t)J_G². [given, F1, step 1.2, algebra]

**Construction step 1.4.** Write the error as sum J_i J_j A_ij with A_ij in (t), where J_i are G and its spatial derivatives. Seek coordinate corrections delta x_i=sum J_j Y_ij and a contact-unit correction delta u=sum J_j Y_0j. Exact Taylor expansion factors the corrected equation as sum J_i J_j(Y_ij+Q_ij(Y)), with Q at least quadratic in Y. The finite system Y+Q(Y)=A has identity Jacobian at Y=0. Its pointed standard étale solution gives an ACTUAL contact equivalence fixing t. Thus the displayed G is an exact normal form, not a formal approximation. Rename G as F. [given, F1, step 1.3, construct, algebra]

**Construction step 2.1.** Make the one fixed extension t=s^60 and assign weights (x,v,w,s)=(3,2,1,1). When all error terms have weight greater than six, the normalized weighted blowup has a continuing s-chart x=s³X,v=s²V,w=sW,F=s⁶F_1. It is actual and generic identity because its monomial centre is supported centrally. The residue/volume-form discrepancy is (3+2+1+1-1)-6=0. Non-unit weight charts retain their finite cyclic quotients. [given, F1, F2, step 1.4, construct, algebra]

**Construction step 2.2.** After r such continuing charts at their unique tangential origins, the substitutions are x=s^(3r)X,v=s^(2r)V,w=s^rW,F=s^(6r)F_r. A normal-form error monomial t^k of spatial weighted degree j has s exponent a_r=60k-r(6-j). Terms of spatial weight at least six never cause a smaller face. For j<6, the positive integer 6-j divides 60. Thus the first weighted degree at most six is EXACTLY six; no lower-weight monomial is skipped over. [given, F2, step 2.1, algebra]

**Construction step 2.3.** A first nonzero terminal face occurs in finitely many steps. Otherwise the constant coefficient c(0,t), the coefficient of w in c, and b0(t) would all be identically zero. The axis x=v=w=0 would then lie in the generic hypersurface with all relative derivatives zero. This contradicts generic smoothness. At least one finite-order monomial of spatial weight zero, one or two bounds r. Only these finitely many relevant spatial weights are needed, even for an infinite actual axis series. [given, step 1.4, step 2.2, algebra]

**Construction step 2.4.** On the first degree-six face, 60k=(r+1)(6-j). Put q=60/gcd(60,r+1). Every appearing 6-j is divisible by q and lies between one and six, so q is one of 1,2,3,4,5,6. The central weighted polynomial always includes X²-V³-V²W². The remaining simultaneous terms are: q6, S⁶; q5, S⁵W; q4, S⁴W² and S⁴V; q3, S³W³,S³VW,S⁶; q2, S²W⁴,S²VW²,S⁴W²,S⁴V,S⁶; q1, S⁶,S⁵W,S⁴W²,S³W³,S²W⁴,SW⁵ and V(S⁴,S³W,S²W²). At least one displayed face coefficient is nonzero. No competing term has been deleted. [given, step 2.2, step 2.3, algebra]

**Construction step 3.1.** Write any exceptional polynomial as X²-B6(V,W,S). Its specialization at S=0 is V²(V+W²), which is not a square, so X²-B6 is irreducible. At a generic branch divisor over a DVR, the equation X²=pi^m u is regular for m=0 or one and nonnormal for m>=2. Away from the branch the cover is étale. By S2 and [F3], nonnormality is exactly the presence of a repeated factor of B6. [given, F1, F3, step 2.4, algebra]

**Construction step 3.2.** Write its full square part as L_d²Q_(6-2d), Q squarefree. No factor S divides L, because B6 at S=0 is nonzero. Hence L(V,W,0)² divides V²(V+W²). Its only possibility is a scalar times V; in particular d=2. Rescaling gives L=V+mu WS+nu S², Q=V+W²-2mu WS-2nu S². The latter identity follows by comparing the V² coefficient, which is exactly W² in the actual normal form. [given, F3, step 3.1, algebra]

**Construction step 3.3.** The coefficient of VW³S in L²Q is 2mu. The normal form of step 1.4 has no vw³ term at ANY trait order, and none of the six faces has VW³S. Thus mu=0. If nu=0 also, B6=V²(V+W²) and every terminal coefficient vanishes, contrary to step 2.4. Therefore nu is nonzero in every nonnormal terminal case. [given, step 1.4, step 2.4, step 3.2, algebra]

**Construction step 3.4.** Adjoin A=X/L. The equation A²=Q eliminates V=A²-W²+2nu S². The ring C[A,W,S], with all variables of degree one, is integral over the original graded ring, has the same fraction field and is normal. It is therefore the actual integral closure. The finite projective normalization is P²_[A:W:S]. Its conductor upstairs is L=0: writing the original ring as R+LRA inside R+RA shows that both the scalar and A coefficients of a conductor element must be divisible by L. Downstairs its ideal is (L,X). [given, F3, step 3.2, step 3.3, construct, algebra]

**Construction step 3.5.** In these coordinates the conductor is the smooth conic A²-W²+3nu S²=0, since nu is nonzero. The strict outer boundary is the line S=0. It meets the conic at two distinct transverse points. Thus the normalized boundary is a rational SNC anticanonical line/conic cycle. At Q=L=0 their gradients are independent because W²=3nu S² and W,S are nonzero. The central germ there is ordinary pinch X²=L²Q. At Q a unit it is an ordinary two-branch double germ on a pointed étale neighborhood. There is no tangential-conductor crossing. [given, F1, step 3.4, algebra]

**Construction step 3.6.** Every nonzero terminal exception is consequently normal or has precisely the preceding ordinary conductor geometry. Normal projective surfaces have finitely many singular points; any such remaining family blocks are passed to their exact normal-surface supplier. Non-t weighted charts and their old parameter products are retained as actual charts, not inferred from a formal central normalization. This proves the finite reduction asserted, leaving exactly the supplier boundaries stated. [given, F1, F2, F3, step 2.3, step 2.4, step 3.1, step 3.5, construct, algebra] ∎

#### Remarks

This is a research candidate. It is not production-installed or natively accepted. Its companion finite-tangential-conductor-model-bindings.json binds the exact research construction bytes for every terminal-face resolution, outer chart, actual ADE/pinch family and toric presentation supplied to the global geometry candidate. Those terminal resolutions are not silently used to strengthen this leaf's reduction Statement. The initial reduction of a general non-complete-intersection toric vertex to the displayed hypersurface models is explicitly outside this lemma.

### Inline construction 17

#### Formal D4 trait normal form and an actual auxiliary polynomial family

Research construction. This proves a formal comparison, with an actual algebraic auxiliary family over the complete trait. It does not assert that the original germ is etale-isomorphic to that family, nor that chosen formal resolution centres algebraize on the original total space.

Let R=C[[t]]. Suppose a hypersurface deformation germ has completed equation f(X,y,z,t)=0 and central surface singularity D4. Here this means that, after an invertible central change, f modulo t is X²+z y²+z³. In the normal cubic-triangle application, the rank-one tangent form and three distinct binary cubic tangent factors give precisely this central form; the ordinary triple plane curve is straightened by its three smooth etale branches. The base coordinate t is fixed.

#### 1. Remove the nonsingular square direction
