The Hessian in X is a unit. The critical equation f_X=0 has a unique formal solution X0(y,z,t). Translate by it, factor the remaining X² coefficient as a unit and adjoin its unit square root, with prescribed constant root. This is the one-variable Morse calculation. It gives exactly

    f=X'²+P(y,z,t),       P modulo t=P0=z y²+z³.

The same construction is actual etale if f and its coefficients are algebraic, but the next infinite iteration is only claimed formally.

#### 2. Explicit Jacobian division, with four residue monomials

The partial derivatives of P0 are 2zy and y²+3z². The quotient of C[[y,z]] by their ideal has basis

    1, y, z, z².

Indeed zy=0 and y²=−3z² there; multiplying the second relation by z and using the first gives z³=0, and then y³=0 as well. Conversely these relations leave those four linearly independent residues. A direct convergent division gives every h∈C[[y,z]] in the form

    h=e0+e1 y+e2 z+e3 z²+(2zy)A+(y²+3z²)B.

To see this for arbitrary series, reduce the degree-two monomials yz and y², and reduce every monomial of degree at least three using z³ and y³ and their displayed Jacobian expressions. The quotients have degree at least the original degree minus two, so the termwise sums converge in the (y,z)-adic topology. No finite-determinacy or versal-deformation theorem is imported.

#### 3. A convergent t-adic elimination

Suppose inductively that modulo t^n the function has been written P0 plus an R-linear combination of the four basis monomials, with all four coefficients in tR. Write its error as t^n H_n. Reduce H_n modulo t and apply the preceding explicit division. Add t^n(e0+e1 y+e2 z+e3 z²) to the four retained coefficient series, and change coordinates

    y -> y−t^n A,       z -> z−t^n B.

Modulo t^(n+1), this removes the Jacobian part of the error. The retained deformation coefficients contribute an additional factor of t to their derivatives, while the second-order coordinate error has order at least 2n>=n+1. Thus the induction improves by one t-order.

Starting at n=1 and iterating gives convergent coefficient series a0,a1,a2,b∈tR and a formal coordinate automorphism fixing t, equal to the identity modulo t. The inverse converges in the same way, since the nth change is the identity modulo t^n. The resulting exact completed equation is

    X²+z y²+2b(t)y+c(z,t)=0,
    c(z,t)=z³+a2(t)z²+a1(t)z+a0(t).

This is a t-adic formal identity, not a finite etale normal-form claim. The construction uses explicit division and completeness, and no approximation theorem is silently invoked.

#### 4. The auxiliary family is algebraic over the actual trait

Although the coordinate automorphism may be formal in y,z, the four coefficients are elements of R itself. Consequently

    Spec R[X,y,z]/(X²+z y²+2b(t)y+c(z,t))

is an actual finite-type algebraic family over Spec R, not a formal scheme masquerading as one. It admits an ordinary projective compactification by homogenization. The polynomial D-family simultaneous-resolution construction applies to this actual auxiliary family after its stated finite trait extension and root splitting.

The formal isomorphism identifies the completed deformation germs and their obstruction complexes. Passing from that identification to equality of proper-supported virtual degrees requires the separately proved formal-neighborhood/POT comparison at its exact scope. It does not follow merely from the existence of the formal coordinate series. Likewise this note does not produce a global modification of the original degeneration or a global theta-algebra identification.

Thus the actual auxiliary polynomial-model step is now explicit, while the remaining algebraic or proper-supported comparison obligation is isolated rather than conflated with a formal-algebraic normal form.

### Inline construction 18

#### Four-component cycle: actual finite family reduction

Research construction of the ORDINARY FOUR-COMPONENT preliminary case. This is a codimension-two complete intersection, not a hypersurface corner. It is not a theorem for arbitrary higher toric-face vertices.

#### Input and actual presentation

Let a normal flat finite-presentation surface family over R=C[[t]] have smooth generic fibre and actual central germ

    ac=0, bd=0

in four regular central coordinates. Flat ideal lifting, Nakayama and the independent ambient Jacobian rows give an ACTUAL etale two-equation presentation

    F1=ac-t f(a,b,c,d,t)=0,
    F2=bd-t g(a,b,c,d,t)=0.                          (1)

The central two equations are a regular sequence. Their actual lifts are a regular sequence and the resulting family is flat. No assertion about a general n>=5 Stanley-Reisner deformation is inferred.

Choose the finite contact-minor certificate

    t^M in I_F=(F1,F2, all 2-by-2 spatial Jacobian minors).

It exists because the generic complete-intersection surface is smooth. The ideal is invariant modulo F1,F2 under actual relative etale coordinates and invertible changes of the two generators. These are the only coordinate/generator operations used in the continuing branch.

#### Component blowup and all noncontinuing outer charts

Blow the ACTUAL vertical component ideal (a,b,t). It is the unit ideal on the generic fibre. In the a-chart b=aB,t=aT the exact strict equations are

    c=T f(a,aB,c,d,aT), Bd=T g(a,aB,c,d,aT), old t=aT.

At T=0 the first has unit c derivative and is solved actually. The residual equation has a hyperbolic (B,d) Hessian at its only possible outer critical point. Relative Morse FIXING (a,T) gives PQ=Phi(a,T), old t=aT, and the complete two-base supplier applies. At the other outer points implicit coordinates are already monomial. The b-chart is symmetric. Their T!=0 loci overlap the continuing t-chart; none is an unlisted extra family branch.

In the t-chart a=tA,b=tB the exact strict equations are

    Ac=f(tA,tB,c,d,t), Bd=g(tA,tB,c,d,t).             (2)

There is no hidden vertical component from dividing by t. The reductions Ac=f0(c,d),Bd=g0(c,d) form a regular sequence: no irreducible factor can divide both, because the first has its independent linear A term Ac and the second its independent B term Bd. Thus the complete-intersection dimension is three and its central dimension is two; Cohen-Macaulayness makes t regular. The displayed ring equals the t-saturated strict transform locally. Its generic central components are regular, so the normal total-family model is unchanged by a spurious normalization.

The extraction is log crepant. Its ambient centre in the five-dimensional smooth ambient space has codimension three, discrepancy two, and each equation has order one along it. The complete-intersection discrepancy is 2-1-1=0; old t has exceptional order one. Component multiplicities and old parameter are retained.

#### Central rank: smooth, hypersurface EXIT, or one continuing point

Over the original vertex the central fibre of (2) has c=d=0 and coordinates A,B. Its spatial Jacobian has its possibly nonzero block

    [ A-f_c(0)   -f_d(0) ]
    [ -g_c(0)    B-g_d(0)].

Rank two makes the central fibre and family smooth. Rank zero can occur at AT MOST ONE point, and only if both off-diagonal constants vanish. Translate A,B by the two diagonal constants. Then f0,g0 have order at least two. The exact triangular divisions of inline construction 19 give

    Ac=p(d), Bd=q(c), ord p,ord q>=2,

including identically zero p or q. This is the continuing rank-zero class. Its normalization and actual finite toric fan are explicitly computed in that supplier, not assumed to be four planes.

At rank one, an actual implicit equation eliminates one variable and leaves ONE hypersurface equation, with t fixed. The following direct algebra classifies its potentially hard central germ; it is not abbreviated to xy=0.

#### Exact rank-one hypersurface classification

It is enough to examine the swapped form AC=F0(A,B),BD=G0(A,B); the other block orientation is a permutation. If a diagonal derivative is a unit, solve A=B phi or its symmetric equation, since the zero axis solves the corresponding equation uniquely. The residual factor is B(D-psi(B,C)), and hence is an ordinary double germ, because its D derivative is one.

Suppose instead the off-diagonal derivative F_B is a unit. Solve the first equation as B=A phi(A,C), with phi_C a unit. Dividing G0(A,Aphi) by A gives the exact residual

    A[phi(A,C)D-psi(A,C)]=0.

If phi is a unit, the bracket is a regular independent coordinate and this is an ordinary double. Otherwise take C'=phi(A,C). Dividing psi(A,C')-psi(A,0) by C' and translating D gives EXACTLY

    A(C'D'-p(A))=0, p(0)=0.                        (3)

At points where the bracket is regular independently of A this is again a double. Its hard point has C'=D'=A=0; it is at most one point of the rank-one exceptional curve. If p=0, (3) is the ordinary triple germ. Otherwise p(A)=A^k unit, k>=1, after its actual unit coordinate operation. The full family is an ACTUAL lift of (3), not merely a completed-ring equation.

#### Generalized-cross EXIT to known finite hypersurface models

For the actual family A(CD-p(A))+tH=0, blow the vertical ideal (A,t). In the A-chart t=AT its strict equation is CD-p(A)+T H=0, old t=AT. Relative Morse in (C,D) FIXING (A,T) gives the existing two-base family at T=0. The T!=0 part overlaps the t-chart.

In the t-chart A=tB the central equation is

    BCD+h(C,D)=0,

because p(0)=0 and H at t=0 has no B dependence. An actual B translation absorbs every mixed CD term of h. The central equation is then BCD-c(C)-d(D)=0.

The COMPLETE lower-rank central inventory is inline construction 33: linear terms give smoothness; spatial quadratic rank two gives an ordinary double; rank one with both axis quadratic coefficients nonzero is an ordinary pinch; rank one with one vanishing axis quadratic coefficient is EXACTLY f_n=x²-v²w²-v^(n+2), for finite n, or the reducible cross when its last axis function is zero. The remaining case has c,d of order at least three.

Every one of these is a finite actual FAMILY EXIT. Double/pinch use their existing arbitrary-deformation suppliers. For f_n use inline construction 23: ONE t=s³ and blow (x,v,s), whose s-chart is the ordinary reducible cross and whose other charts are the exact two-base/monomial models. For c,d of order at least three use inline construction 32: ONE cubic extension and blow (C,D,s), whose s-chart is EXACTLY three planes bUV=0 and whose other charts are again exact two-base models. These consume the independently completed triple/cross algorithm with its n=1 fixed-sixty-cover terminal proof. They DO NOT invoke the present four-component reduction. Thus all rank-one branches exit the four-component class after finite actual constructions; they cannot cause a circular restart.

#### Continuing rank zero: opposite component and strict minor-budget decrease

For the actual rank-zero family Ac=p(d)+tF,Bd=q(c)+tG, blow the opposite component ideal (c,d,t). Its non-t outer charts are solved by ACTUAL relative Morse exactly as in the central-algebra supplier. Its continuing t-chart c=tC,d=tD has central AC=F0(A,B),BD=G0(A,B), the same two-block class with the blocks interchanged. Rank two/rank one are the already constructed exits; rank zero repeats the stated normalization.

After TWO successive continuing t-charts, all four original spatial coordinates have the form

    old z=t U(new z,t),

where the relative Jacobian of U is invertible. The triangular graph divisions used between them have coefficients regular and zero constant term at the selected rank-zero point; hence this is an actual etale coordinate expression, not just equality of valuations. Each original defining equation is t² times an invertible generator combination of the new two defining equations.

Differentiate RELATIVE to the four spatial variables. Modulo the new equations, the old 2-by-4 Jacobian matrix is t times invertible row and column combinations of the new matrix. Therefore every old 2-by-2 minor pulls into t² times the new minor ideal; the old equations also pull into t² times the new equation ideal. Thus

    pullback(I_old) subset t² I_new.

The identity t^M in I_old gives t^(M-2) in I_new by cancellation in the regular ambient chart. This is a strict INTEGER decrease on the ORIGINAL trait. No new root is taken in the continuing rank-zero branch.

At each continuing stage there is at most one rank-zero point. All other exceptional points are the smooth/double or finite special rank-one exit germs above. Noetherian quasi-compactness and their actual finite local atlas give finitely many exit constructions per stage. Consequently after at most floor(M/2)+1 pairs of stages no rank-zero continuation remains. The four-component construction is finite. Auxiliary finite trait extensions are combined only for its now finite exit tree.

#### Output and exact remaining generality

All component extractions are actual projective vertical blowups. Every exit has its independently constructed finite regular-SNC family model and actual rational anticanonical component presentations. The global common exterior refinement/etale algebraic-space descent and original-cone proper-support operations apply with their exact retained coupons and class pushforwards.

This proves finite family reduction for the ORDINARY four-cycle central germ (ac,bd), preserving the entire generic fibre after the recorded finite trait extension. It does NOT prove that every cyclic toric-face central germ of valence at least five has this presentation or reduces to it. The higher-valence ideal and saturated syzygy/lift supplier remains logically distinct.

### Inline construction 19

#### Four-component toric-face central algebra and actual component-blowup charts

Research construction for the newly identified non-CI-vertex preliminary obligation. A four-component central vertex is itself a complete intersection of codimension two, but is NOT one hypersurface xy=0 or xyz=0. This note supplies exact central normalization, its fan, and the non-t outer family charts. It explicitly records what these computations do not yet prove.

#### 1. The starting four-cycle and the actual component extraction

The ordinary four-cycle toric-face ring is

    C[a,b,c,d]/(ac,bd).

Its four minimal components are (a,b),(a,d),(c,b),(c,d), arranged cyclically. A flat actual two-equation lift can be written

    ac=t f(a,b,c,d,t),       bd=t g(a,b,c,d,t).

The equations and the actual parameter are part of this input; this presentation is not claimed for every higher toric-face vertex.

Blow the actual component ideal (a,b,t). Its a-chart b=aB,t=aT has strict equations

    c=T f(a,aB,c,d,aT),
    Bd=T g(a,aB,c,d,aT),      old t=aT.

Near T=0 the first equation solves c by its unit c derivative. The second has its hyperbolic (B,d) Hessian at B=d=T=0, so relative Morse FIXING (a,T) reduces that outer chart to PQ=Phi(a,T), old t=aT. At smooth outer points an implicit coordinate already gives a monomial map. Its b-chart is symmetric. Points where T is nonzero overlap the t-chart, rather than constituting an omitted extra family branch.

The t-chart a=tA,b=tB has exact equations

    Ac=f(tA,tB,c,d,t),       Bd=g(tA,tB,c,d,t).

If f,g vanish at the selected central point and their linear central cross derivatives vanish, translate A,B by the two diagonal derivative constants. The central equations then have the form Ac=f0(c,d), Bd=g0(c,d), with f0,g0 of order at least two.

#### 2. Exact rank-zero central normal form

There are actual algebraic coordinate changes

    A'=A-[f0(c,d)-f0(0,d)]/c,
    B'=B-[g0(c,d)-g0(c,0)]/d.

The quotients are regular by their displayed vanishing, and the coordinate maps are triangular isomorphisms. They give EXACTLY

    A'c=p(d),       B'd=q(c),
    ord p>=2, ord q>=2,

allowing either function to vanish identically. If p=d^m u(d),q=c^n v(c) are nonzero, rescale A' by u(d) and B' by v(c), both units, to obtain

    Ac=d^m,       Bd=c^n,       m,n>=2.                (1)

This is a central coordinate operation. Applied to the full family it leaves an ACTUAL lift Ac=p(d)+tF, Bd=q(c)+tG with t fixed; it does not delete the deformation coefficients.

#### 3. Complete reduced normalization when p,q are nonzero

Put I=(Ac-d^m,Bd-c^n), h=AB-c^(n-1)d^(m-1), P=(c,d), and J=I+(h). Then I=P intersect J. Indeed c h and d h lie in I. If i+rh belongs to P with i in I, reducing modulo P gives r(A,B,0,0)AB=0 and hence r in P; therefore rh lies in I. This proves the asserted intersection.

The quotient by J is the affine toric ring with generators

    A=(-1,m), d=(0,1), c=(1,0), B=(n,-1)

in Z². Its equations are the three displayed binomials. Reduction by Ac=d^m, Bd=c^n and AB=c^(n-1)d^(m-1) leaves only monomials A^r d^s, B^r c^s and c^i d^j; their exponent vectors are distinct. Thus there are no extra polynomial relations and J is prime.

This semigroup is already saturated. An integer vector in its cone has either both coordinates nonnegative, or first coordinate -r<0 and second at least mr, or second -r<0 and first at least nr. These cases are respectively generated by c,d; A,d; and B,c. Therefore the toric main component is NORMAL, not merely an unspecified normalization candidate.

Consequently the normalization of (1) is the disjoint union of the plane Spec C[A,B] and this normal affine toric surface. Their scheme-theoretic intersection before normalization is

    c=d=0, AB=0,

the reduced pair of coordinate axes in the plane. Since I=P intersect J, the original ring is the corresponding fibre product over C[A,B]/(AB). Its conductor is reduced on both components.

The main component has the explicit regular fan with ordered rays

    (-1,m), (0,1), (1,0), (n,-1).

Each consecutive determinant has absolute value one. Its two exceptional curves have self-intersections -m and -n, since neighbouring-ray sums are m(0,1) and n(1,0). This is an actual finite toric resolution, with full toric boundary and K+boundary=0. It does not assert that this central fan operation has already been lifted to a generic-isomorphic total-family operation.

For m=n=2 the projectivized central tangent curve is a line plus a twisted cubic, not four lines. This concrete example disproves a claimed automatic return from every rank-zero chart to the ordinary four-plane ring.

#### 4. Vanishing p or q

If p=0 and q=c^n u(c)!=0, the central ring has three reduced components: the planes (c,B) and (c,d), and the normal toric A_(n-1) surface (A,Bd-c^n). The analogous assertion holds with p and q interchanged. These decompositions follow by factoring Ac and substituting c=0; the complete intersection is unmixed and generically reduced on all listed components, hence reduced. If both p and q vanish, it is the original four-plane cycle. Thus all these central normalizations are explicit toric surfaces with reduced conductor and explicit regular fans. Their actual total-family lifts are a separate obligation.

#### 5. Opposite-component extraction and its COMPLETE outer charts

Take the actual family

    Ac=p(d)+tF(A,B,c,d,t),
    Bd=q(c)+tG(A,B,c,d,t).

Blow (c,d,t). In its c-chart d=cD,t=cT, the exact equations are

    A=p(cD)/c+T F(A,B,c,cD,cT),
    BD=q(c)/c+T G(A,B,c,cD,cT),
    old t=cT.

The displayed quotients are regular, because p,q have order at least two. At T=0 the first equation has unit A derivative; eliminate A. At c=T=0 the second equation is BD=0 with nondegenerate hyperbolic (B,D) Hessian. Hence its possible outer critical point has an ACTUAL relative Morse model PQ=Phi(c,T), fixing c,T; all other outer points are smoothly monomial. The existing two-base-conic construction therefore solves the entire c=T=0 outer intersection. The d-chart is identical after permutation. Their T!=0 loci overlap the continuing t-chart.

The t-chart c=tC,d=tD is

    AC=t^(m-1)D^m unit+F(A,B,tC,tD,t),
    BD=t^(n-1)C^n unit+G(A,B,tC,tD,t),

with the obvious interpretation for identically zero p,q. Its central equations are again AC=F0(A,B), BD=G0(A,B). At rank ZERO, the triangular divisions of section 2 apply with the two variable blocks interchanged. This proves an actual rank-zero continuation rule without claiming it is the whole resolution algorithm.

#### 6. Rank-one issue must be retained

Rank one need not give xy=0. For example F0=B,G0=A^n gives AC=B, BD=A^n. Eliminating B leaves

    A(CD-A^(n-1))=0.

For n=2 this is the known reducible cross; for n>=3 it is a plane and a toric A_(n-2) component meeting along its toric boundary. It is not a supplied normal-crossing central germ. A full family proof must lift the relevant toric refinement or separately resolve this generalized-cross family while preserving old t.

After one further component blowup (A,t), the A-chart is a two-base Morse block; the t-chart can have a lower-multiplicity hypersurface whose quadratic part has rank one. That part can yield central X²-B²D²-D^m, m>=4, with normalization A_(m-3), rather than the already settled m=3 tangential conductor. Therefore neither the phrase rank-one hypersurface nor a cubic-only branch list supplies all the missing family cases.

Two alternating continuing t-charts do scale all four original spatial coordinates by t and divide both equations by t². This suggests the relative two-by-two Jacobian-minor budget decreases by two. To convert it into a COMPLETE termination theorem one must first close every noncontinuing rank-one family branch. The exact central toric computations above do not by themselves provide that closure. This note deliberately leaves that mathematical interface visible.

### Inline construction 20

#### Four generators: exact toric-conductor cyclic complete intersection

Research CENTRAL-algebra supplier. Assume the actual completed toric-conductor pushout is the one constructed by the Hilbert-basis-sail lemma, and its polarized nodal genus-one tangent cycle has total degree four. This proves the exact central equations needed by `inline construction 21`; it is not a claim that arbitrary tangent-cone data alone identifies a surface germ.

#### Hilbert-basis variables and the cyclic equations

A normalization component of polarization degree d has a sail of d+1 monomial generators U0,...,Ud. At interior sail positions,

    U_(i-1) U_(i+1)=U_i^(b_i),       b_i≥2.

Across a component break the corresponding term is zero. Conductor endpoint identification replaces the two endpoint coordinates by their SUM (rescale the actual toric gluing constants first). Products of nonadjacent sail generators reduce by the standard sail identity

    U_i U_j = U_(i+1) U_(j-1) product_(i<k<j) U_k^(b_k-2),

with the repeated middle factor combined when j=i+2. Thus an endpoint times an interior generator is its endpoint-SUM times that generator minus a product of interior generators. Pure matched endpoint powers are likewise polynomials in their sum and interior products. These exact monomial identities prove that the pushout is generated by four cyclic variables X1,...,X4.

Following the four cyclic positions gives EXACTLY

    X1 X3=X2^(b2)+X4^(b4),
    X2 X4=X1^(b1)+X3^(b3),

where an exponent infinity denotes the zero term. There is at least one infinity: each normalization component contributes d-1 finite interior sail positions, so the total number of finite positions is sum(d_i-1)=4-m≤3, where m≥1 is the number of normalization components. No arbitrary extra term of finite exponent is inserted across a conductor break.

The finite positions are completely explicit:

* partition 1+1+1+1: all four exponents are infinite, the ordinary four-plane cycle;
* partition 2+1+1: one finite position, giving one monomial relation and one zero-product relation;
* partition 2+2: two opposite finite positions, giving X1X3=X2^p+X4^q and X2X4=0 after rotation;
* partition 3+1: two adjacent finite positions, giving X1X3=X2^p and X2X4=X3^q after rotation;
* partition 4: three consecutive finite positions, giving zy=x^p+w^q and xw=y^r after rotation.

All p,q,r are at least two. This table is a finite enumeration of ordered compositions of four around a circle; reversal and rotation merely rename variables. It covers a single toric normalization cone as well as multiple components.

For the last row the identities can be checked without the cyclic shorthand. The normalization has five sail generators U0,...,U4. Put z=U0+U4,x=U1,y=U2,w=U3. Then

    zy=U0U2+U4U2=x^p+w^q,       xw=y^r.

The other nonadjacent products express U0x, U4w, and the remaining conductor monomials by these four generators, exactly as above. For partition 2+2, adding the two endpoint products gives the sum of the two interior powers, while the product of the interior generators from different normalization components is zero. Cutting one or both of those sail chains deletes precisely the corresponding power term, giving all remaining rows. This establishes the surjection from the displayed complete intersection onto the ACTUAL pushout algebra.

#### No hidden kernel or higher central correction

The two displayed equations form a regular sequence. Their quadratic initial forms also form a regular sequence: the first is either X1X3 or an irreducible quadratic of rank at least three, while the second contains X2X4 and cannot share a factor with it or be its scalar multiple. Therefore the associated graded complete intersection has two quadrics and multiplicity four; its Koszul complex proves strictness of those initial forms. The complete intersection is Cohen–Macaulay and unmixed of dimension two.

The actual pushout has multiplicity four by the strict Hilbert-basis-sail tangent-cone calculation. Its surjection from this dimension-two complete intersection consequently has ZERO kernel. Indeed any nonzero ideal submodule of an unmixed Cohen–Macaulay dimension-two ring has a height-zero associated prime and positive dimension-two multiplicity. Additivity of Hilbert–Samuel leading coefficients in the kernel sequence would then give multiplicity(source)>multiplicity(target), contrary to four=four. Thus the equations above identify the actual completed central ring, not merely its tangent cone.

Actual toric gluing constants are removed by rescaling sail monomials and taking finitely many nonzero scalar roots over C. If the initial presentation is on an étale chart, pass to the common pointed refinement. Any needed finite-contact presentation of the complete-intersection deformation keeps the ORIGINAL trait parameter; the actual flat-family lifting of a central complete-intersection presentation is obtained by lifting the two regular-sequence generators and Nakayama, not by renaming a hypersurface coordinate as the trait.

#### The actual family consumer

Rotate the guaranteed infinite exponent into b4. The actual central form is then

    zy=x^p+w^q,       xw=y^r,

with any additional omitted power interpreted literally as zero. The source-ready family consumer `inline construction 21` handles arbitrary trait deformations of these two equations after the one stated cubic cover, including all infinity cases; it retains the old parameter in every chart and exits to the already constructed ordinary four-cycle or hypersurface inventories. The table and kernel proof above are its formerly missing central classification input.

This result presupposes the exact toric-conductor sail/pushout description. It does not infer such a description for an arbitrary non-toric surface merely from multiplicity or a four-dimensional cotangent space.

### Inline construction 21

#### A four-generator toric-conductor CI exits to the finite four-cycle class

Research construction. Input is the EXACT displayed central complete intersection, including missing terms; deriving that presentation for every degree-four toric pushout is a separate central-algebra obligation.

Let the actual trait family have equations

    F1=zy-x^p-w^q+t H1=0,
    F2=xw-y^r+t H2=0,

with p,q,r>=2. An exponent infinity means that term is absent. Generic fibre is smooth. These are actual equations, not a tangent-cone identification. Make the ONE extension t=s³ and blow the coherent vertical ideal (x,y,w,s), leaving z as the conductor parameter. The centre has ambient codimension four. F1 has order one along its generic point and F2 has order two; the total CI discrepancy is 3-1-2=0. The generic exceptional parameter order is one, so the reduced central trait log pair is also crepant. All centres are vertical and hence all modifications are generic identity.

In the s-chart put x=sX,y=sY,w=sW. The strict equations are

    zY-s^(p-1)X^p-s^(q-1)W^q+s² H1=0,
    XW-s^(r-2)Y^r+s H2=0.

They are t-saturated actual CI equations: their central restrictions form a regular sequence, so quotient has no s-torsion and equals closure of its generic chart. If r>=3 or infinity, the central equations are zY=XW=0, the ordinary four-cycle. Its full actual finite reduction is inline construction 18.

If r=2, the central equations are zY=0, XW=Y². Blow the actual central-component ideal (X,Y,s) in this current family. In its s-chart X=sA,Y=sB, the strict equations are

    zB-s^(2p-2)A^p-s^(q-2)W^q+s H1=0,
    A W-s B²+H2=0.

Here H1,H2 denote their actual pulled-back functions. At s=0 they take the form

    zB=f0(z,W),
    A W=g0(z,W).

For q=2 the extra W² term is included in f0; for q>2 it disappears. This is the exact bi-affine central four-cycle class Ac=f0(c,d),Bd=g0(c,d), with c,d=(z,W) and coefficient variables (B,A). The rank-two, rank-one and rank-zero analysis and alternating-component quotient-minor budget in inline construction 18 applies directly at its stated internal bi-affine interface; no false rank-one-to-xy shortcut is used.

For completeness the non-s charts of the SECOND blowup are already finite. In its X-chart set Y=XB,s=XT. The second equation, divided by X, solves W near T=0 with unit derivative. Substitute into the first divided equation. Its (z,B) Hessian at X=T=0 is hyperbolic. Actual relative Morse fixes X,T and gives PQ=Phi(X,T), while the exact old parameter is s=XT. The complete two-base conic supplier resolves it. In the Y-chart set X=YA,s=YT. The first equation divided by Y solves z near T=0, and the second has hyperbolic (A,W) Hessian, retaining old s=YT. Points with T nonzero overlap the s-chart. The second blowup has ambient codimension three and orders one,one for the two CI equations, so discrepancy 2-1-1=0.

The non-s charts of the FIRST blowup are just as explicit. In its x-chart y=xB,w=xC,s=xT, the second equation divided by x² is C-x^(r-2)B^r+xT³H2=0, which solves C near T=0. The first divided by x is zB-x^(p-1)-x^(q-1)C^q+x²T³H1=0. Its (z,B) Hessian is hyperbolic along x=T=0. Relative Morse fixes x,T, old s=xT, and the two-base supplier applies. The w-chart is symmetric. In its y-chart x=yA,w=yC,s=yT, the first equation divided by y solves z, and the second divided by y² is AC-y^(r-2)+yT³H2=0. If r=2 it is smooth with AC=1 on the exception and the parameter is monomial; if r>2 its (A,C) Hessian is hyperbolic, fixing y,T and old s=yT. Every point with T nonzero overlaps the s-chart. These calculations retain arbitrary H_i; a trait-linear unit or nonunit is not discarded.

Therefore this actual four-generator CI requires one fixed cubic trait cover and at most two preliminary vertical blowups before it is covered by the already finite bi-affine four-cycle/two-base models. It introduces no new degree-four del Pezzo classification or repeated ramification argument.

The central classification still to bind is precise: a degree-four toric conductor pushout should be expressed as a cyclic CI xy=z^p+w^q, zw=x^r+y^s with at least one exponent infinity, so rotation gives the missing-term presentation above. This note does NOT assume that classification has been proved merely from the Hilbert series. It also does not replace a general nodal-quartic family by four planes without the displayed actual preliminary maps.

### Inline construction 22

#### Generalized tangential conductor: exact finite actual contact normal form

Research mathematical construction for the rank-one family interface of the four-component preliminary reduction. Fix n>=1 and

    f_n=x²-v²w²-v^(n+2).

Let F be an ACTUAL algebraic/etale hypersurface over C[[t]], with F modulo t equal to f_n and smooth generic fibre. Then, after an actual etale change fixing t, its equation is EXACTLY contact-equivalent to

    G=f_n+c(w,t)+b(t)vw+sum_(i=1)^(n+1) a_i(t)v^i,

where every deformation coefficient vanishes modulo t, b and a_i are actual elements of C[[t]], and c is an actual regular function on the w-axis chart. Finite polynomials in t with actual algebraic w-coefficients suffice. This is a normal-form supplier, NOT a simultaneous-resolution theorem or a decreasing-n induction theorem.

#### Exact central algebraic division

Write J=(f_x,f_v,f_w), with

    f_x=2x,
    f_v=-2vw²-(n+2)v^(n+1),
    f_w=-2v²w.

In particular v²w belongs to J, and

    v f_v-w f_w=-(n+2)v^(n+2)

puts v^(n+2) in J. Also

    vw²=-(n+2)v^(n+1)/2 modulo J.

Every ACTUAL regular function h has an exact decomposition

    h=c(w)+b vw+sum_(i=1)^(n+1) a_i v^i
          +A_x f_x+A_v f_v+A_w f_w.                 (1)

Here b,a_i are scalars for a single central coefficient; the same operations work over an actual coefficient base. To construct (1), first subtract h|x=0 and divide by x, removing the difference with f_x. On the remaining plane subtract its v=0 axis value c(w). The remainder is v times an actual regular function. Subtract its w=0 restriction; the remaining mixed part is vw times an actual function H(v,w).

Subtract H(0,0), producing bvw. Decompose H-H(0,0)=vH1+wH2. The first term gives v²wH1 and is in J. In the second use vw²=-(n+2)v^(n+1)/2 modulo J. Split H2(v,w)=H2(v,0)+wH3(v,w). Its w-part is a multiple of v^(n+1)w, hence of v²w, because n+1>=2. The remainder is an actual pure-v function. Finite division by v^(n+2) leaves the polynomial sum in (1), and the remainder is in J by the displayed identity.

All divisions are by functions whose numerators vanish on the indicated coordinate axis, or by a specified monomial after subtracting its finite Taylor jet. They are ACTUAL algebraic divisions on common etale axis charts. No infinite formal division, preparation theorem, or convergence assertion has been used.

#### Finite trait-order elimination

Smooth generic fibre gives a finite integer M with t^M in the relative CONTACT Jacobian J_F=(F,F_x,F_v,F_w). At each t-order use (1): retain its residue terms c(w),bvw and v^i, and remove its derivative terms by the corresponding t-order coordinate corrections in x,v,w, fixing t. Their Jacobians are identity modulo t, so every individual step is an actual etale coordinate operation. Perform only N steps. The result is an actual F_N with

    F_N=G_N+t^N H,
    G_N=f_n+c_N(w,t)+b_N(t)vw+sum a_i,N(t)v^i.

The coefficients are finite polynomials in t; the w-coefficients remain actual regular functions. No infinite coordinate change is asserted.

Because derivatives are relative to x,v,w, the error and its derivatives lie in (t^N). Thus J_F_N is contained in J_G_N+(t^N). For N>M the transported identity t^M in J_F_N yields

    t^M(1-t^(N-M)u) in J_G_N,

and the parenthesis is a unit. Hence t^M in J_G_N. Choosing N>=2M+1 gives

    t^N H in m J_G_N².

#### Actual finite contact correction

Apply the explicitly constructed finite contact-matrix lemma: write the error as sum a_ij J_i J_j with a_ij in m, introduce finitely many correction variables Y_ij, and Taylor-expand a unit multiplication and coordinate translation whose increments are linear combinations of J_i. The exact polynomial/regular equations have the form Y+Q(Y)=a, with Q of order at least two. Their Y-Jacobian at the closed point is the identity. The actual etale implicit theorem solves them, fixing t, and gives an exact contact equivalence between F_N and G_N. This is the same inline construction 49 construction; its proof does not require the CENTRAL Jacobian to be primary.

Combining the finite preliminary steps and this single actual correction proves the stated exact normal form. It preserves the original trait coordinate and its smooth generic fibre. In particular the normal form has NO independent vw² coefficient: that class was retained as v^(n+1), and has NO vw^j with j>=2. A terminal weighted-face proof may use that coefficient restriction, but must not omit the permitted b(t)vw or any pure-v power through n+1.

### Inline construction 24

#### Projective domination preserving the ENTIRE generic projective fiber

Research construction for the supported-cone specialization. This gives the exact refined Chow consequence needed by that route. It uses the family owner's U-admissible flatification construction as its substantive input; the rest is reconstructed below. Ordinary Chow's lemma with an unspecified dense isomorphism open is not substituted for this result.

#### Precise claim and input

Let R be an excellent Noetherian complex trait with parameter t, fraction field K, and let Y be a separated proper finite-type R-scheme whose entire generic fiber X=Y_K is smooth and projective over K. Suppose the following actual flatification input has been reconstructed:

> If g:G->Z is a proper finite-presentation morphism with Z normal excellent integral and U=Z[t^-1] an open over which g is an isomorphism, then there is a projective U-admissible modification b:Z'->Z, obtained by blowups with centers disjoint from U and finite normalizations, such that Z' is normal integral and the strict transform G'->Z' is flat of finite presentation. The strict transform is the schematic closure of G_U=U in G x_Z Z'; it is not an arbitrary reduced replacement. Every modification is an isomorphism on all U. This weaker form suffices; no arbitrary-base single-blowup theorem is required.

Then there exists a projective R-scheme P and a proper morphism P->Y inducing an isomorphism P_K->X on the ENTIRE generic fiber. If Y is the schematic closure of X (in particular if Y is flat with every component meeting the generic fiber), the morphism is surjective. If Y has extraneous vertical components, the construction dominates the schematic generic closure; that is the only part needed for generic-specialization support. It does not claim to dominate unrelated vertical components by an isomorphism generic model.

#### 1. Projective generic closure and graph

Choose a closed immersion i:X->P^N_K. Let Z be its schematic closure in P^N_R. Then Z is projective over R and Z_K=X. For an irreducible generic X, replace Z by its finite normalization (the trait is excellent). This is still projective, and on the entire generic fiber it is the identity because X is smooth and hence normal. Work componentwise and take a finite disjoint union if necessary. The schematic closure before normalization has no t-torsion by its defining kernel into the generic coordinate ring; after normalization this also follows because its local rings are domains and t is nonzero. Thus U=X is schematically dense in Z. The generic fiber need not be replaced by a smaller affine/dense open.

In the separated proper R-scheme Y x_R Z, take the schematic closure G of the graph of the identity of X (using i to identify X with Z_K). Both projections G->Y and G->Z are proper because the ambient product has proper projections and G is closed. Both induce the identity on X. The map g:G->Z is of finite presentation, since every scheme here is Noetherian. Its inverse image over the WHOLE U=Z_K is U, scheme-theoretically, because the generic graph is a closed graph and schematic closure commutes with restriction to this open.

#### 2. Apply only U-admissible proper flatification

Apply the stated flatification input to g. Every blowup center is in the central fiber and every finite normalization is the identity on the generic smooth X, so the resulting Z'->Z is an isomorphism on all X. The modifications are projective (a finite normalization is projective), hence Z' is projective over R. Its normal integral structure sheaf is t-torsion-free; the Rees charts before normalization also retain t-torsion-freeness. Thus X is schematically dense in Z'.

Let G' be the strict transform. It is closed in G x_Z Z', hence g':G'->Z' is proper. By the actual flatification input it is flat and of finite presentation. Its generic restriction is the identity of X. No projectivity of the original g or Y has been assumed.

#### 3. Proper, flat and generically the identity implies the identity here

Consider W=G' x_(Z') G'. It is flat over Z', since each factor is flat and flatness is preserved by base change and composition. Multiplication by t is injective on O_Z'; tensoring that injection with the flat O_W gives injectivity of t on O_W. Therefore W[t^-1] is schematically dense in W.

Because g' is proper, its diagonal Delta:G'->W is a closed immersion. Over t!=0, g' is an isomorphism, so Delta is all of W[t^-1]. Its defining ideal therefore vanishes after inverting t. Every section of that ideal is locally killed by a power of t. The t-torsion-freeness just proved forces the ideal to be zero. Consequently Delta is all of W, and g' is a monomorphism. This argument retains nilpotents: it is equality of defining ideals, not merely density of points.

A finite-type monomorphism has geometric fibers of at most one reduced point, so it is quasi-finite. Proper plus quasi-finite is finite, using the already constructed scheme proper-quasi-finite theorem from the family supplier. Thus g' is finite flat of finite presentation. Its finite algebra is locally free, and its rank is locally constant. Every nonempty open-and-closed rank locus meets the schematically dense X, where the rank is one, so the rank is one everywhere. On every residue fiber its unit map is a nonzero map between one-dimensional vector spaces and hence an isomorphism. Nakayama makes the unit O_Z'->g'_*O_G' an isomorphism. Therefore g' is an isomorphism of schemes.

This avoids invoking a general 'flat birational morphism is an open immersion' theorem or a dimension-of-fibers assertion. The only operations are schematic density, the actual closed diagonal, finite/quasi-finite calculus and finite-flat algebra.

#### 4. The resulting model

Identify P=Z' with G'. Composing its inclusion in G x_Z Z' with G->Y gives P->Y. It is proper by composition. Its generic restriction is exactly X->X, because every modification used was the identity on all X. Its image is closed and contains X, so it contains the entire schematic generic closure of Y; on a flat degeneration that is all Y. P is projective over R.

The map P->Y is also projective if that is useful: choose P->P^M_R a projective closed immersion. Its graph into P^M_R x_R Y is closed because Y/R is separated, so it factors as a closed immersion into P^M_Y followed by projection. Thus it is a projective modification of the actual generic-closure target, with no change to generic curves or any generic point of X.

This is the required input for the projective-target stable-map support P_E used by `inline construction 45`. Generic maps to Y lift uniquely to P because P_K=Y_K globally. They do not merely lift away from an unspecified finite center in X.

#### Reading and dependency boundary

The published root supplier `lem-chow-lemma-proper-noetherian` was read in full. It gives only an unspecified dense isomorphism open, so it is insufficient for this stronger conclusion. Stacks Lemma76.40.2 (tag088R), Lemma76.40.1 (088Q) and Lemma76.39.4 (087F) were fetched and read in full. The first gives the desired specified-open refinement through common U-admissible blowups; the latter two ultimately invoke U-admissible flatification. The proof above uses a shorter proper-graph argument once that same flatification input is genuinely constructed. It does not import Stacks' flatification theorem as a locally proved statement, and it does not claim that clearing poles of an ample generic linear series alone makes the total space projective.

Consequently the remainder of refined Chow is proved here conditional on exactly the proper U-admissible flatification lemma stated above. Once the family owner's actual flatification construction is supplied, no additional generic-entire Chow hypothesis remains in the supported-cone route. No production item or acceptance record is changed.

### Inline construction 25

#### Projectivity of a flat algebra with geometrically integral fibers

Research local reconstruction, 2026-10-28. This supplies assertion (P) in `inline construction 64`; it is not a declaration that all flatification inputs have been completed.

#### 1. Statement and actual elementary suppliers

Let R be a Noetherian ring and let B be a finite-type flat R-algebra with nonempty geometrically integral fibers. Then B is projective as an R-module, without an assertion that its rank is finite.

Use AC as inherited by the actual generic-freeness and prime-filtration suppliers, and for the module splittings below. The actual imported suppliers read here are `lem-generic-freeness-finite-type-algebra-module`, `thm-artin-rees-lemma`, `thm-krull-intersection-theorem`, `thm-exactness-of-inverse-limits-under-mittag-leffler`, and their stated Noetherian/module conventions. No published infinite-module projectivity or flatification theorem is used as an input. Noetherian adic completion is flat; localization times adic completion is faithfully flat. These are the existing completion interfaces already used by the stable-map source. For the latter assertion, flatness is componentwise, and the images of the two spectra contain D(a) and V(a), respectively, hence jointly cover Spec R.

#### 2. A flat countable module criterion, with proof

For a module M over a Noetherian ring define its tensor-product condition to be injectivity of

    M tensor_R product_j Q_j -> product_j (M tensor_R Q_j)    (ML)

for every family of R-modules. We prove the only consequences needed below.

If M is flat, condition (ML) is equivalent to the following minimum-submodule property: for every finite free F and x in `F tensor M`, there is a smallest submodule L of F with `x in L tensor M`. In one direction take the intersection of all such L, and map F to the product of the quotients F/L. Flatness identifies the kernel after tensoring with the tensor of that intersection; (ML) forces x into it. Conversely, given an element of the left-hand side of (ML) mapping to zero on the right, write it using finitely many m_i, regard these as x in `R^n tensor M`, and use the least L. Each coordinate homomorphism `R^n->Q_j` kills L, since its kernel is one of the submodules containing x after tensoring. Therefore their joint map to the product kills L, proving injectivity. All submodules of finite free F are finitely presented by Noetherianity.

A countably generated flat M is a sequential direct limit of finite free modules F_n. Here is a sufficient explicit construction. Map an initial finite free module to finitely many selected generators. Its kernel is finitely generated because R is Noetherian. The finite-equations criterion of flatness factors this finite map through another finite free module in such a way that the finitely many kernel relations become zero; include the next selected generator in the same factorization. Iterate. Every selected generator enters the system, and every kernel relation at a finite stage dies at the next, so the resulting colimit is exactly M.

To verify the finite-equations factorization explicitly, present the finitely generated image N as `R^k --A^t--> R^n ->N->0`, and let m in M^n be the images of the n generators. They satisfy `A m=0`. Flatness gives `ker(A tensor M)=ker(A) tensor M`; thus `m=sum_l v_l tensor m'_l` for finitely many v_l in ker(A). The matrix with columns v_l gives a map `N->R^l` because its transpose kills every relation A^t, and `R^l->M`, sending its generators to m'_l, recovers the original map. This proves the factorization and kills the entire prescribed kernel. It does not assert that N itself is flat.

Assume now (ML). For a fixed n, let x be the tensor in `F_n^* tensor M` corresponding to the structural map F_n->M. Let L be its least submodule. It is finitely generated. Lift a finite expression of x in `L tensor M` to a sufficiently late F_m, and enlarge m so that its equality with the actual transition-map tensor already holds at that stage. Then the image of `F_m^*->F_n^*` is contained in L. Conversely every such image contains L by minimality. Hence these images equal L for all sufficiently large m. After tensoring with any module N, the images of

    Hom(F_m,N) -> Hom(F_n,N)

are therefore stationary as well: both are the image of `L tensor N` in `F_n^* tensor N`. Thus the countable inverse system `Hom(F_n,N)` is Mittag-Leffler for every N.

For any exact sequence `0->N_1->N_2->N_3->0`, the Hom sequences for F_n are exact because F_n is finite free. The actual imported countable Mittag-Leffler inverse-limit theorem makes their inverse-limit sequence exact. Since `Hom(M,N)=lim Hom(F_n,N)`, the functor Hom(M,-) is exact. Hence M is projective.

We will also use faithful-flat descent in this countable setting. If R->R' is faithfully flat, M is countably generated flat, and `M tensor R'` is projective, apply the preceding finite-free system. Over R', projectivity implies (ML), since it is a direct summand of a free module, and free modules satisfy (ML) by their finite-support coordinates. Thus its dual-map images stabilize. Formation of those images commutes with flat base change, and faithful flatness reflects equality of their submodules. The original images stabilize as well. The preceding Hom/inverse-limit proof then makes M projective. This proves the needed descent; it is not an invocation of descent for arbitrary infinite modules without hypotheses.

#### 3. Products and completed free sums

For any set J the product `R^J` is flat when R is Noetherian: for a finitely generated ideal I, finite presentation gives `I tensor R^J=I^J`, and the map to R^J is coordinatewise injective. The ideal criterion for flatness proves the assertion. It also satisfies (ML). Indeed, for finite free F, `F tensor R^J=F^J`; the smallest submodule containing a tuple after tensoring is precisely the submodule of F generated by its coordinate vectors. Every submodule of F is finitely presented, so its tensor with R^J is its coordinate product, proving the minimum-submodule property of Section 2.

Now let R be complete along an ideal I and let `F=direct-sum_J R`. Its completion Fhat is the submodule of R^J consisting of families tending I-adically to zero: for every n only finitely many coordinates are nonzero modulo I^n. The natural map `Fhat->R^J` is universally injective. To prove this, test on a finite module Q, with a finite presentation `R^k ->R^m ->Q->0`. A vector in `Fhat^m` that vanishes in `Q tensor R^J` has each coordinate vector in the image of this relation matrix. Artin–Rees gives one constant c such that a relation vector lying in `(I^n)^m` has a preimage in `(I^(n-c))^k`. Choose these preimages coordinatewise. They still tend I-adically to zero, so lie in `Fhat^k` and show that the original element was already zero in `Q tensor Fhat`. Finite Q suffice because every module over Noetherian R is a filtered union of finite modules and tensor commutes with those unions.

A universally injective submodule of a flat module is flat, by the ideal tensor criterion. A universally injective submodule of an (ML) module also satisfies (ML), by the commutative diagram with the two tensor/product maps and its injective top and bottom horizontal arrows. Consequently Fhat is flat and (ML), being a pure submodule of the flat (ML) module R^J.

#### 4. Completion when the modulo-I module is projective

Let M be flat over an I-adically complete Noetherian R, with M/IM projective over R/I. Then Mhat is a direct summand of a completed free module.

First, for a nilpotent ideal J, a flat module L with L/JL projective is projective. Lift a free-module idempotent presenting L/JL to a column-finite matrix over R. The error `e^2-e` lies in the nilpotent matrix ideal. The polynomial correction `e -> e+(1-2e)(e^2-e)` squares the order of this error; finitely many iterations give an actual idempotent. Its image P is a projective lift. A homomorphism P->L lifting the modulo-J isomorphism exists by projectivity of P. Its cokernel C has C=JC and hence C=0 by nilpotence. Its kernel K has K/JK=0 by flatness of L, and hence K=0 by nilpotence. Thus P=L. Column-finite matrices remain column-finite under these finitely many polynomial operations, so no finite-rank hypothesis is being inserted.

Choose a free surjection F->M with kernel K. Flatness gives exact sequences modulo I^n. The preceding nilpotent lifting makes `M/I^n M` projective over `R/I^n` for every n. Choose compatible splittings of these sequences. To extend a splitting from n to n+1, start with any splitting at n+1. Its discrepancy modulo n is a map to `K/I^n K`; lift that map to `K/I^(n+1)K` using projectivity of the domain over R/I^(n+1), and correct the splitting. The inverse limits then split

    Fhat = Khat direct-sum Mhat.

Section 3 makes Fhat flat and (ML), and these properties pass to its summand Mhat. This proves the required completion assertion.

#### 5. Pure completion for the algebra B in the complete-base case

Suppose R is Noetherian and I-adically complete, B is finite type flat with nonempty geometrically integral fibers, and B/IB is projective over R/I. We prove B is projective.

For a prime p of R, the ring B/pB is a domain. It is flat over the domain R/p, so injects into its generic fiber, which is integral by the fiber hypothesis. For every finite R-module Q, choose a prime filtration. Tensoring with B remains exact, and gives a filtration of `Q tensor B` with factors `B/pB`. Consequently every associated prime of this finite B-module is some pB. Since I lies in the Jacobson radical of the complete ring R, `p+I` is proper. A maximal ideal of R containing it lifts to B because every fiber is nonempty. Hence `pB+IB` is proper as well.

Put N=`Q tensor B` and let T be the kernel of `N->Nhat`. It is `intersection_n I^n N`. At each associated prime q=pB of N choose a prime q' containing q+IB. The local Krull-intersection theorem at q' gives `T_q'=0`, and then `T_q=0`. A finite module injects into the product of its localizations at its associated primes; this follows by taking an associated prime of a hypothetical nonzero kernel. Thus T=0. The map

    Q tensor B -> Q tensor Bhat -> (Q tensor B)hat

has injective composite, so its first arrow is injective. This holds for every finite Q, hence B->Bhat is universally injective.

By Section 4, Bhat is (ML), since B/IB is projective. Therefore its pure submodule B is (ML). B is countably generated over R by the finitely many algebra generators' monomials. Section 2 now proves that B is projective. This is the complete-base purity argument specialized to the algebra actually needed; it avoids importing a general associated-prime theorem for arbitrary flat modules.

#### 6. Noetherian induction gives the general theorem

Suppose the theorem fails. Among ideals J of R for which B/JB is not projective over R/J, choose a maximal one; ascending chains terminate. Replace R,B by these quotients. Now B/JB is projective for every nonzero ideal J of the new R, but B is assumed not projective.

R must be reduced. Otherwise its nonzero nilradical is nilpotent, its corresponding quotient satisfies projectivity by maximality, and the nilpotent lifting argument in Section 4 makes B projective, a contradiction.

Choose a minimal prime of the reduced R and an element g vanishing on all other irreducible components but not on this one. Then R_g is a domain. Apply the actual imported generic-freeness lemma there to find a further denominator; their product a is nonzero in R and B_a is free over R_a. If a is a unit we are already done. Otherwise the nonzero ideal (a) has projective quotient B/aB by maximality.

Let Rhat be the a-adic completion. Its base-changed algebra Bhat_base=`B tensor_R Rhat` is flat finite type with nonempty geometrically integral fibers; the modulo-a module is the same projective B/aB. Section 5 proves Bhat_base projective over Rhat. The other base change B_a is free. Thus B becomes projective over the faithfully flat ring `R_a times Rhat`. It is countably generated and flat over R, so the descent proved in Section 2 makes B projective over R, the final contradiction.

This proves (P). It also supplies the direct-summand-of-free coordinate realization required in the Fitting-coefficient blowup calculation. No finite-rank assumption has been made.

#### 7. Remaining flatification boundary

Assertion (P) is now supplied locally by Sections 1–6. The associated-prime proof in Section 5 also gives the universal-injectivity step needed after generic-fiber freeness: a map `B^r->N` that is an isomorphism at every generic fiber point remains injective after tensoring with every finite base module Q, because the associated primes of `Q tensor B^r` are among pB and the kernel localizes to zero there. This supplies that reduction without importing Stacks 05I3. The later `inline construction 50` supplies the geometric charts and `inline construction 36` completes the sufficient normal excellent integral-base version by an explicit dimension induction. No arbitrary-ring single-blowup version is claimed.

### Inline construction 26

#### Global patching of local models with a toroidal exterior

Research construction resolving the global-center/descent interface. It allows local modifications which change whole double curves away from a bad point; the earlier isolated-identity lemma alone was insufficient for those modifications. The construction gives a proper algebraic-space target with actual etale monomial charts, not an invented globally projective small resolution.

#### Precise input

Let Y be a proper separated normal target over the trait, with a finite set S of bad central points. On Y^o=Y\S the actual central/outer log structure is toroidal. Give a common finite trait extension and, for each p in S, a separated quasi-compact etale scheme neighborhood V_p->Y having exactly one preimage of p and no preimage of the other bad points. Give an ACTUAL finite local model Q_p->V_p, proper and generic-isomorphic, regular with SNC central/outer support. Its restriction to V_p\{p} is a finite toroidal modification of the supplied exterior log structure. The local model includes its actual base parameter, units and component identifications.

The output is a global proper generic-isomorphism algebraic-space model, with regular reduced SNC charts after one final common finite ramification/toric subdivision. Its local models are further TOROIDAL refinements of Q_p. Thus the local toric/boundary-blowup invariant comparisons remain the computed ones; arbitrary non-toric centers are not introduced by gluing.

#### 1. Regularize and label the exterior

The toroidal exterior has a finite atlas of actual monoid charts, with finite central/outer Cartier data after its ordinary finite monomial regularization. Make that regularization first and pull the supplied Q_p models to compatible toroidal refinements. In each chart this is the explicit toric fan subdivision and, when needed, finite ramified normalization described in the semistable packets. There are finitely many charts/components because Y is Noetherian and S is finite. No assertion about the still-singular points in S is made by this step.

On the resulting regular SNC exterior the irreducible central/outer components D_1,...,D_r have actual effective Cartier ideal sheaves. Their characteristic generators are globally labelled; on a chart the components which are absent have invertible sections. This is the finite-DF situation already constructed by the family owner.

#### 2. Construct one GLOBAL exterior common refinement

Each toroidal blowup in Q_p|V_p^o is generated, on finitely many charts, by finitely many monomials in those actual Cartier component generators. A local monomial with exponents a_i extends globally on the exterior as the actual ideal sheaf O(-sum_i a_i D_i), setting missing exponents to zero. Sums of finitely many such ideals give the corresponding globally coherent monomial ideal. A change of chart multiplies generators by units and does not change its ideal. Thus there is an actual extension, not an assumed descent of a local branch curve.

At a stage take the product of all these finitely many extended ideals and perform its normalized/saturated log blowup. On a normal domain, invertibility of that product makes each factor invertible: use the actual regular-generator/product-ideal argument in the family owner's stagewise patching packet. The blowup universal property gives maps to every requested local stage. Continue on the CURRENT refined exterior, whose new exceptional divisors are again actual globally labelled Cartier boundary components, and extend the next local monomials there. Pad shorter local sequences by identity steps. The finite maximum length of the supplied construction trees proves termination of this common-refinement construction.

The resulting R^o->Y^o is one actual global toroidal modification dominating all Q_p|V_p^o. This remains true with intermediate etale refinements: their component labels are pulled back from the same global central components, so the monomial ideals extend by the same exponent vectors. The finite common-fan description gives the same universal maps. No norm ideal with an uncontrolled non-toric complementary factor is used in this exterior step.

#### 3. Extend that exterior refinement across each local exceptional locus

For a fixed Q_p, the map R^o|V_p^o ->Q_p|V_p^o is a projective toric refinement. Its fan subdivision is represented by its finite relative convex support functions, hence by finite monomial ideals on the actual boundary charts. Extend every exterior central/outer component which occurs in these monomials by its closure in Q_p. Since Q_p is regular, that closure is an effective Cartier divisor; it is a union of components of the actual central/outer SNC divisor. Choose coefficient zero on the new components lying wholly above p whenever they are not prescribed by the exterior. This is a genuine extension of the exterior characteristic sections.

Use these extended Cartier divisors to extend the monomial ideals and perform the corresponding finite log blowups on Q_p. Their restriction is EXACTLY the specified refinement R^o|V_p^o, as follows from the fan/blowup universal property. Call the result R_p. Further common toric regularization can be made on both sides with the same fan data. Thus

    R_p|_(V_p^o) = R^o x_(Y^o) V_p^o                                  (1)

with actual monomial and unit maps, not merely equal tropical supports. These are only toroidal refinements of the already proved local model. In particular this step does not require that a Weil divisor or a chosen curve branch on the ORIGINAL singular Y extend globally.

#### 4. Actual etale descent

Use the cover consisting of Y^o and all V_p. Over Y^o use R^o, over V_p use R_p. Their overlaps with Y^o agree by (1). For a self overlap W=V_p x_Y V_p, the diagonal is open and closed because V_p/Y is separated etale. It contains the entire fiber over p since V_p has exactly one point above p. On that diagonal the two pullbacks of R_p agree identically. On its complementary open-and-closed part there is no point over S, so both pullbacks identify with the SAME R^o. These give the actual overlap isomorphism.

For overlaps of neighborhoods of distinct bad points there is no exceptional bad fiber; use R^o. The cocycle holds on the triple overlaps: over S every coordinate is the same selected point and the identifications are diagonal identities; away from S they are the single prescribed exterior identification. These are scheme/algebraic-space isomorphisms on an open cover, so the cocycle is not merely checked on points.

The etale equivalence relation on the disjoint union of the R_p and R^o gives an actual algebraic-space quotient Q. Properness, separatedness and finite presentation of Q->Y are etale-local on Y and hold for each displayed local modification. Every generic restriction is the original generic family after the common field extension. The actual central/outer log structures, their line/section/tensor coupons and all normal units descend by the same overlap maps.

This proves global existence even when Q is not a scheme. No global projectivity or global small-resolution center is inferred from etale equations.

#### 5. Reduced SNC and virtual-comparison join

The finite common refinements have finitely many monomial parameter charts. Take one final ramification degree divisible by all their primitive central orders, normalize, and perform compatible regular subdivisions of their at-most-two-dimensional height-one slices. Empty lattice triangles are unimodular. This gives regular reduced SNC charts on Q and on all overlaps. All these operations are actual finite toric/root/normalization constructions; multiplicities and residual group actions are retained, not erased by taking a reduced fiber.

For the numerical comparison use the family owner's `inline construction 10` and `inline construction 44`: pull Q's actual finite DF data to a projective generic-isomorphic scheme, form its proper finite enhancement stack, close the ORIGINAL generic cone in the pulled-back original obstruction bundle, and apply proper Cartier/zero-section operations. Common projective supports and exact basic log contractions prove choice independence. A full unconstructed Hom stack to Q, or a POT on its chosen support stack, is not assumed.

Hence finite local standard models with a toroidal exterior really do join globally. The ONLY geometric input not supplied by this lemma is those actual LOCAL models. In the current run higher double defects and all nonzero-linear corner branches have such constructions. Arbitrary remaining singular/nonnormal higher-corner blocks must still be supplied by their owners; this patching result does not assert their existence by fiat.

### Inline construction 27

#### Statement

Assume AC and put R=C[[t]]. Let Y->Spec R be a proper separated normal flat finite-presentation surface family with smooth generic fibre. Supply a finite closed set S in its central fibre such that the central and outer divisorial structure is ACTUALLY toroidal on Y minus S. At each point of S supply an actual etale central hypersurface germ xy=0 or xyz=0, with its labelled component/outer Cartier data. The outer data in these charts are the supplied toroidal boundary coupons and their actual units; an arbitrary additional nonmonomial divisor through a corner is not inferred to be such data. Supply projective toric normalization components and their original finite toric boundary gluings. Retain a fixed generic curve class and every original Cartier line, section and tensor identification used to specialize it.

Then a finite trait extension and a proper generic-fibre-isomorphism modification Q of the base-changed Y exist, with Q a regular separated algebraic SPACE whose central fibre and supplied outer divisor have reduced SNC etale charts. Q need not be a scheme or globally projective. The construction is finite and consists of the actual local double/corner modifications described below, compatible toroidal refinements, and effective etale descent. All new central components have actual finite rational-anticanonical presentations by point blowups and smooth (-1)-contractions to toric or Hirzebruch surfaces; the original components acquire the explicitly tracked boundary modifications. The actual Cartier coupons, normal-frame units, specialization pushforwards and ALL exceptional degrees are retained. No connectedness of the outer divisor and no Fano or nef hypothesis on the original smooth pair is required.

In particular Q has finite Deligne-Faltings data: finitely many actual Cartier lines and sections with their actual monoidal tensor identifications, giving its supplied divisorial log structure. For any separately supplied proper support of the ORIGINAL generic obstruction cone with these coupons, the construction permits the existing proper-support specialization operations: pull the coupons to a projective generic-isomorphic scheme support, close that original cone and apply the same proper Cartier/zero-section operations. This last clause supplies a target and its actual coupons; it does not assert a new obstruction theory on a support stack or the algebraicity of an unconstructed full Hom stack.

The hypothesis on central bad-point germs is LOAD BEARING. A bare record of a toric-normalization central fibre can have non-complete-intersection vertices with more than three components. Their preliminary reduction to the supplied hypersurface germs is NOT a conclusion of this lemma and is not supplied by consistency of a scattering diagram.

**Given:** Exactly the surface family, finite toroidal exterior, hypersurface bad-point germs and actual labelled outer/class data in the Statement. #### Proof

**Construction step 1.1.** Flatness lifts the supplied central hypersurface presentations to actual total hypersurfaces. The independent central smooth-coordinate rows remain invertible, so the etale implicit theorem eliminates those rows. The remaining equations are F mod t=xy or F mod t=xyz. All supplied outer labels are retained in these actual charts. [given, F2, construct]

Solve its two critical equations over the remaining coordinate z and t, translate, complete the square and take specified unit square roots by etale equations. **Construction step 4.1.** For higher corner order, use the actual point blowup. Non-t charts have a hyperbolic two-variable Hessian and old parameter uA, so they are the same two-base-conic construction PQ=Phi(u,A). The identity cannot hold for 2k>M. The only new terminal exceptional cubics have the explicitly bound normal, nonnormal or reduced-cross inventory; a reduced cubic is not silently assumed smooth.
