### Inline construction 28

#### Gorenstein degree-two conductors: residue and actual pushout classification

Research construction. The normalization component and degree-two conductor premises are supplied separately by theta's hyperplane-chain argument. The conclusion below is exact when the normalization germs at the point are smooth. Toric cyclic-quotient normalization germs are explicitly retained as a separate monoid/family-lift boundary; resolving their central fan is not declared to resolve an arbitrary actual deformation.

Let Z be a reduced S2 Gorenstein surface, ordinary double in codimension one, with finite normalization nu:Zbar->Z and its reduced conductor Cbar. Assume the actual conductor pushout has its double-cover involution tau; equivalently its local ring is the equalizer of the two conductor restriction maps, with the Gorenstein dualizing residue matching. The conductor components under consideration have projective hyperplane degree two. Thus after the separately constructed complete hyperplane embedding, they are a smooth conic, two meeting lines, or two disjoint lines (with the relevant paired branches on other normalization components included). A reduced degree-two curve has no cuspidal or higher-degree hidden branch. The following gives the precise local algebra at its smooth and nodal points.

#### Residue forbids a branch-preserving fixed conductor node

At a smooth normalization point with conductor uv=0, a generator of omega_Zbar(Cbar) is

    alpha=h(u,v) du/u wedge dv/v,
    h(0,0) nonzero.

Its branch residues have opposite signs: h(u,0)du/u and -h(0,v)dv/v, with one simultaneous sign convention. Gorenstein descent requires each paired residue to be the NEGATIVE pullback of the other. This can also be read directly from the residue exact sequence for the two normalization branches: the sum of their boundary residues must vanish for an actual local dualizing generator.

Suppose tau fixes the node and preserves one branch. It is an actual order-two automorphism of that smooth branch. In a local parameter u it has form tau(u)=lambda u+O(u²), lambda nonzero. Its pullback of du/u has leading term du/u, regardless of lambda (in particular lambda=-1 gives no minus sign). The matching equation would therefore have leading coefficient 2h(0,0), which is nonzero over C. This is impossible for a dualizing GENERATOR. Hence an allowed fixed conductor node MUST exchange the two branches. A ramified self-cover preserving each branch at a node cannot be inserted as another Gorenstein local model.

At a smooth conductor point the residue is instead h(u)du. An order-two fixed point has parameter tau(u)=-u after the actual averaging coordinate u-tau(u); its residue changes sign, so this is allowed and gives the ordinary pinch below.

#### Smooth conductor points

If two distinct smooth normalization branches are paired without ramification, choose the paired conductor parameter identically. The equalizer of C[[u,x]] and C[[u,y]] over C[[u]] is

    C[[u,x,y]]/(xy).

Indeed a compatible pair is its common axis series plus x times an arbitrary series on the first branch and y times one on the second; the products xy vanish. Thus the central germ is the ordinary double surface.

At a ramified fixed smooth conductor point, write the normalization as B=C[[u,v]], conductor v=0 and involution u->-u on it. The exact equalizer is

    A={f in B : f(u,0) is even in u}
      =C[[u²,v,uv]].

Setting Z=u²,Y=v,X=uv gives

    X²=Y² Z.

This is the actual ordinary pinch surface, including its conductor, not just a birational parametrization.

#### One fixed node with swapped branches

At a fixed node the allowed involution exchanges the axes. Choose the two actual axis parameters so it is u<->v; finite-order averaging or choosing one parameter and transporting it along tau makes this exact. Put s=u+v,p=uv. The equalizer condition is f(u,0)=f(0,u). The normalization ring decomposes as

    C[[u,v]]=C[[s,p]] plus (u-v) C[[s,p]].

Compatibility on the two axes forces the antisymmetric coefficient to be divisible by p. Hence the pushout ring is generated by s,p,q=p(u-v), with its ONLY relation

    q²=p²(s²-4p).

Conversely every expression in these generators has matching axis restrictions, so the displayed ring is the full equalizer. After rescaling variables its equation is x²=v²(w²+v), the tangential-conductor hypersurface. Its actual finite-contact and fixed-cover family resolution is the already authored finite-tangential-conductor supplier. No arbitrary non-CI germ is silently identified with this case.

#### Several smooth node preimages

At a glued point suppose the normalization has k smooth surface germs, each with its two conductor axes. Pairing axes gives a finite graph whose vertices are these normalization points and whose edges are the paired axis germs. Every vertex has valence two. Connectedness of the local pushout makes it a single cycle. The residue condition excludes the branch-preserving fixed-axis alternative above. Reparametrize the individual axes so every pairing is identity; the two axis coordinates on one smooth surface are independent, so there is no unproved analytic holonomy constraint.

For k>=3 choose variables z1,...,zk, with component i carrying zi,zi+1 and all other z variables zero. Every compatible tuple of series is its common constant, its shared axis series, and arbitrary zi zi+1 times a series on each component. Thus the actual completed pushout is the Stanley–Reisner face ring of the k-cycle. For k=3 its equation is z1z2z3=0; for k=4 it is the complete intersection z1z3=z2z4=0; for k>=5 its ideal is generated by all nonadjacent zi zj. This is a ring computation, not a fan drawing.

For k=2 there are two normalization planes glued along both axes. Write their common axis coordinates u,v, and x=(uv,-uv) on their disjoint union. The equalizer is C[[u,v,x]]/(x²-u²v²), the reducible cross. For k=1 the swapped-axis computation above applies. These are exactly the ordinary-cycle, four-component, reducible-cross and tangential-conductor supplier interfaces.

#### Actualness and remaining quotient boundary

All displayed central ring maps are polynomial or complete-local equalizers. Their equations are exact. When the surrounding family is actual algebraic/étale, its smooth normalization coordinates and finite conductor involution can be taken on actual pointed étale neighborhoods; the finite actual contact-algebraization suppliers handle the subsequent family normal forms. The argument does not infer a family modification solely from an isomorphism of completed central rings.

If a normalization component has a cyclic toric cone point on its conductor, the preceding smooth-plane identification DOES NOT apply. Its toric index cover has two coordinate rays, and the residue matching still forbids branch-preserving fixed-node gluing. The actual central pushout is the equalizer of the corresponding saturated monoid algebras along the two ray algebras. A Hirzebruch–Jung fan resolves that NORMALIZATION cone, but an actual vertical modification of the whole deformed pushout, retaining generic identity and its log form, must still be supplied. No spatial ramified cover is substituted for such a modification.

Likewise the degree-two conductor premise itself must be obtained from an actual complete hyperplane embedding and the normalization dualizing formula. This note does not assert those hypotheses for every arbitrary toric-face degeneration merely by its name. It supplies the exact smooth-node classification and identifies the monoid-quotient boundary required by the broad original theorem.

### Inline construction 29

#### Higher double-curve defects: actual resolution, global descent and local coefficients

Research construction removing the primitive-zero restriction at an interior double-curve defect. It supplies actual algebraic/etale charts, proper global algebraic-space descent, and projective local relative comparison. It does not assert that a globally projective small resolution exists; that assertion can fail and is unnecessary. The remaining mapping-stack interface for the resulting algebraic-space target is assigned to the family owner.

#### 1. Actual finite branch scheme, rather than formal preparation alone

Use the actual etale hypersurface/Morse presentation from the nodal-model packet. Its critical base B is a smooth relative curve over the excellent complete complex trait R, and the target equation is

    XY=t^l f,  l>=1,

where f on B has a finite-order zero k at p on the special fiber, and f mod t is not identically zero. Shrink around p so its zero divisor Z=V(f) is quasi-finite over R. The isolated-fiber finite-neighborhood construction used by the family owner supplies a finite neighborhood; equivalently apply its explicit quasi-finite finite-model argument and use henselianity to select the finite component meeting p. Indeed, in a finite R-model, the connected components through the selected special point are contained in the quasi-finite open: their complement would be a closed subset containing a generic point but not its specialization, impossible. Remove the other components by shrinking B. Thus Z is actually finite over R here.

The pair t,f is a regular sequence on the smooth relative surface B, since f mod t is nonzero on its regular curve. Hence O_Z has no t-torsion and Z is finite flat. Smoothness of the generic target forces Z_K to be reduced and etale: a repeated root would give X=Y=f=f_z=0, a singular point of the generic surface. Choose a finite extension L/K splitting this finite etale algebra and take the finite normalization R' of R in L. It is a complete DVR after choosing one component, with residue C. Finiteness can be reconstructed from the trace-pairing bound for integral elements; no Puiseux theorem is needed. All generic branch points extend uniquely to actual R'-sections sigma_1,...,sigma_k of Z_R', because Z is finite and proper. They all specialize to p.

Each sigma_i is a Cartier divisor on the smooth relative curve B_R'. There are no vertical components in div(f), since f mod t is nonzero. Its generic divisor is the sum of the k distinct sections, each with multiplicity one. Equality of height-one valuations on the regular B_R' therefore gives the ACTUAL Cartier identity

    div(f)=sum_i sigma_i.

On a Zariski neighborhood of p choose actual generators f_i of those Cartier section ideals. Then f=unit*product_i f_i. Absorb the unit in X. Each f_i has nonzero relative derivative, so it is an etale relative coordinate at p. This is an equality in the actual local algebra, valid on arbitrary nilpotent tests; it is not a formal factorization being declared algebraic.

A common finite trait extension handles the finitely many defects simultaneously. A coordinate on B may write f_i=z-a_i(s) after an etale change, but that notation is not needed for the construction.

#### 2. Actual ordered local resolution and its generic behavior

First make the toric/component modification for the factor t^l as in the primitive model, retaining the toric expansion charts. The only non-toroidal residual chart is

    X Y=product_(i=1)^k f_i.

Blow up (X,f_1). On the first strict chart, f_1=Xu and Y=u product_(i>1) f_i. Because f_1 is an actual etale relative coordinate, this chart is etale over the smooth chart with coordinates X,u,s. On the other, X=f_1 v and

    vY=product_(i>1) f_i.

Repeat in that residual chart. The last equation vY=f_k is smooth because f_k has nonzero derivative along B/R'. This gives a finite actual projective local modification with regular total space and SNC central/outer divisor; the remaining crossings are the explicit toric thickness charts already resolved by monomial component subdivisions.

Every center is Cartier on the ENTIRE generic smooth fiber. Where f_i=0 generically, all the other f_j are units, and the equation gives f_i=X Y/(product_(j!=i) f_j), so (X,f_i)=(X). Elsewhere the ideal is also invertible. The composite is therefore an isomorphism on the whole generic fiber. Its non-isomorphism locus on the original residual chart is only p: a central root collision with X=Y=0. Subsequent exceptional loci stay over p. No arbitrary generic map is forced to avoid an unspecified center.

Centrally, f mod t is z^k times a unit. The modified component is the resolution of the actual weighted boundary-point blowup ideal (X,z^k). Its regular fan inserts the rays (1,1),(2,1),...,(k,1), equivalently k successive ordinary point blowups on the strict boundary. After the first blowup, the next center is the intersection of the strict boundary with the last exceptional curve; that curve is NOT included in the boundary. Thus all k are interior boundary-point blowups, at infinitely near smooth boundary points. Their exceptional chain has k-1 self-intersections -2 and a final -1 curve meeting the strict boundary. The old toric component and its other boundary points are unchanged away from p.

#### 3. Explicit global algebraic-space descent at a finite bad set

A local simultaneous resolution need not extend to a global projective small resolution. The correct unconditional gluing target is a proper algebraic space. The source Gross-Siebert definition allows algebraic spaces; no unproved scheme representability is asserted.

Here is the actual descent construction. Choose a separated etale neighborhood V->Y_1 of p in the already component-modified target, with one selected v over p. Its etale fiber is finite near v; remove the other preimages of p so the ENTIRE fiber over p is the single point v with its residue field C. Shrink V until the constructed modification V'->V is an isomorphism away from v. The previous sections give such a proper projective V' with actual equations.

In W=V x_(Y_1) V, the diagonal Delta_V is open (etale) and closed (separated). It contains the ENTIRE fiber over p, because that fiber is the single ordered pair (v,v). Consequently the complementary open-and-closed subset W\Delta_V has no point over p. On Delta_V the two pullbacks of V' are canonically identical. On W\Delta_V both are canonically W itself, since V' is the identity away from v. These two identifications define an actual isomorphism of pullback schemes on all W. On the triple overlap the cocycle holds: over p every coordinate is v and all identifications are diagonal identities; away from p they are identities of the unchanged target. These loci are an open cover, so equality is scheme-theoretic, not only a check of geometric fibers.

Glue this descent datum with the identity modification of Y_1\{p}. Effective etale descent of schemes as algebraic spaces is explicit here: take the etale equivalence relation on V' disjoint-union (Y_1\{p}) induced by those pullback isomorphisms and form its etale sheaf quotient. The relation is representable and its projections etale, so this is an algebraic space with that actual scheme atlas. Properness, separatedness and finite presentation of the map to Y_1 are checked after the surjective etale cover, where they are the displayed proper modifications and identities. Thus the result is a proper generic-isomorphism algebraic-space modification Y_2->Y_1. Descent of its actual central/outer log structures follows from the same monomial/unit identifications.

For several defects use the complement of the finite bad set and one such neighborhood per point. Overlaps of neighborhoods of distinct defects contain no exceptional point, so their modifications are identities there. The same cocycle argument gives the global space. This avoids assuming a global Weil-divisor center or a global ample line selecting a small resolution. If the particular choices already descend as coherent ideals, the result is the corresponding scheme blowup, but that extra conclusion is unnecessary.

#### 4. Proper central components and the exact higher-order coefficients

Each central modified surface is the canonical minimal resolution of its weighted point-blowup singularity, obtained by the explicit sequence of ordinary point blowups above. This is a projective SCHEME over its original toric surface; the possible nonschematic issue concerns the TOTAL threefold gluing, not these component surfaces. Consequently all local vertex moduli remain those of actual projective toric surfaces with finitely many interior boundary blowups, possibly infinitely near.

The infinitely near configuration has an actual smooth projective deformation separating its centers. Take the original toric surface with its chosen smooth boundary component D. At step i choose a section p_i of D over a parameter base; after every preceding point blowup the strict transform of D is canonically D itself, so p_i lifts to an actual section of that strict boundary, even when it coincides with an earlier p_j. Blow up these sections in order. Each center is a smooth relative codimension-two section, so every fiber is a smooth projective surface and the strict boundary remains smooth relative. Distinct p_i give disjoint ordinary exceptional curves; coincident p_i give exactly the infinitely near chain.

Use the actual proper incidence/relative map stacks in this projective family and their compatible cone operation. The relative degree is constant with the ACTUAL line/curve-class insertions. In particular retain the total-transform exceptional classes E_i as classes of the relative exceptional divisors, not merely their strict central chain classes. The generic separated centers have connected cover coefficients A_w=(-1)^(w+1)/w^2 and their actual normal/gluing units M_i. The initial wall transformation is therefore

    product_i (1+M_i),

with commuting parallel factors and all full curve-class monomials retained. Specializing to the chain preserves that product under the actual coefficient identification. It is not correct to replace a multiplicity-k defect by one primitive factor or to discard the difference between total-transform and strict-chain classes. The toric/blowup-vertex partition formulas then compute its full relative contribution, including non-Fano zero-D-degree classes.

#### 5. Remaining interface, without a simplicity restriction

The higher-order double-curve local resolution and its global proper algebraic-space descent are constructed above. No simple-zero restriction remains in this part of the model. The family owner must extend its actual regular-SNC mapping-stack/finite-DF/cone construction to this explicitly etale-glued algebraic-space target, retaining pullbacks of the target atlas on source curves. One must not declare the total space a scheme or apply the quasiaffine torus-torsor scheme presentation to a nonschematic space.

Arbitrary toric-corner defects are a separate remaining construction owned by the corner agents. The present packet does not silently use the Gross-Siebert no-corner condition, because Root requires the literal broader target. No production or acceptance record is changed.

### Inline construction 30

#### Isolated-point algebraic-space resolutions: exact lifting interface

Research, 2026-10-28. This records what the isolated-point descent proves and what remains for mapping-stack algebraicity. It does not extend the scheme-target torus-torsor atlas by assertion.

#### 1. The isolated-point descent datum is effective

Let Y be a separated scheme, p a closed complex point, and V->Y a separated etale neighborhood with V_p exactly one reduced point v. Let V'->V be proper of finite presentation, an isomorphism away from v. On `W=V times_Y V`, its diagonal copy of V is both open and closed, and it contains the whole fiber over p. The two pullbacks of V' are canonically identical on this diagonal. On its complement there is no point over p, so both are the identity and are canonically identical there as well. These disjoint-open comparisons give the actual isomorphism on W; on the triple overlap the cocycle is either that identical diagonal comparison or the identity away from p. Include Y minus p with its identity model. Effective etale descent gives a separated proper finite-presentation **algebraic space** Y'->Y, isomorphic away from p. Regularity and the stated SNC local target models descend etale locally. This does not prove that Y' is a scheme.

#### 2. Exact source-cover reduction, including nilpotents

For any family of maps `f:C->Y` from proper nodal curves, put

    D = C times_Y p,   C_V = C times_Y V.

The etale C_V->C is a scheme morphism and is an isomorphism after restriction to D. It is also an isomorphism on every finite infinitesimal thickening of D defined by a power of the pulled-back maximal ideal of p: finite etale algebras are invariant under nilpotent thickenings, and the selected residue fiber has rank one.

A lift C->Y' is exactly a section of the projective scheme modification

    C_V times_V V' -> C_V

together with its automatic identity on C_V minus D, when the given local V'->V is projective. No additional descent choices are missing: the diagonal component of `C_V times_C C_V` has identical sections; its off-diagonal component avoids D, where the modification is the identity. Thus every such section has the required descent datum, on all base schemes. This is an exact equivalence of functors, not only a geometric-point classification.

The remaining problem is representing this section functor on the varying quasi-finite etale source C_V. C_V need not be proper, and ordinary projective-source Hom charts cannot simply be applied to it.

#### 3. A coherent-quotient reduction and its precise flatness limitation

Suppose V' is the blowup of a coherent ideal J, with its actual Rees equations; finite iterates can be treated by repeating the following construction. A section over C_V is an invertible quotient of the pulled-back J satisfying the actual Rees relations. Away from D, that quotient is an isomorphism because the blowup is the identity there.

The imported scheme Zariski Main factorization compactifies C_V as an open in a finite scheme bar C_V over C. Thus bar C_V is projective over the ordinary base whenever C is. The proper closed subscheme D remains closed in this compactification, so its boundary is disjoint from D. Extend J to a coherent sheaf Jbar on bar C_V. A section quotient `J->L` glues with the **identity** quotient `Jbar->Jbar` on bar C_V minus D, using its canonical isomorphism on C_V minus D. It gives an actual coherent quotient of Jbar on bar C_V, unique once this exterior extension is fixed. Its kernel is supported on D. All target equations and tensor coupons are retained by this gluing.

However bar C_V and Jbar need not be flat over the ordinary-map parameter scheme. The glued quotient equals Jbar outside D and can therefore be base-nonflat. A usual flat-quotient Hilbert/Quot scheme does not automatically parametrize these objects. Nor is an arbitrary extension of J guaranteed invertible at the added boundary. A proof of a relative Quot construction with fixed possibly nonflat exterior, or an actual flat compactification and compatible exterior extension, is still needed to complete this particular representation route. Replacing the quotient by its reduced support would lose the nilpotent lifting functor.

#### 4. Projective domination extends to an algebraic-space target

There is no corresponding gap in the auxiliary projective domination. In `inline construction 36`, the source X of the flatification can be a quasi-compact separated algebraic space: take a finite etale scheme atlas on that source and apply its finite-projection construction to the pulled-back coherent sheaf on these scheme charts. Flatness is local on the etale source. The normal-base norm/product modifications are made on the same scheme base, so the stagewise global patching applies without change. The strict transforms on the scheme charts are pullbacks of the actual algebraic-space strict transform (torsion quotient commutes with this flat etale pullback). Hence the resulting space is flat over the modified scheme base.

Apply this to the proper algebraic-space graph over the normal projective generic closure. Its flat strict transform is a proper monomorphism by the same parameter-torsion diagonal proof. A proper quasi-finite algebraic space over a scheme is finite and therefore a scheme; rank one makes it that base scheme. Thus a proper algebraic-space target with the projective generic fiber still has the required projective generic-entire dominating **scheme**. This does not by itself supply its ordinary or basic mapping stack.

#### 5. A genuine sufficient criterion for reducing a genus-zero mapping stack to a scheme target

If, in addition, an algebraic-space target Q has an actual finite etale G-torsor `W->Q` with W a scheme and G finite, its genus-zero mapping stack does reduce to a scheme mapping stack. This is a sufficient criterion, not a property asserted for the isolated resolution above.

Here is the family proof. A finite etale cover of a geometric nodal genus-zero tree is a disjoint union of copies of that tree. Normalize its components: a connected finite etale cover of P1 in characteristic zero has degree one by Riemann–Hurwitz. The componentwise trivial sheets are glued at nodes; because the dual graph is a tree, sheet permutations can be removed successively from a root, leaving trivial global sheets.

For a family `pi:C->B` and a finite etale algebra A on C, its geometric fibers are therefore O_C^N. H^1 of those fibers vanishes and H^0 has constant dimension N. The actual proper-flat direct-image complex and base-change criterion make `pi_*A` locally free of rank N with arbitrary base change, and its evaluation pullback to C is an isomorphism by the proper-support/Nakayama argument. Multiplication and unit descend as the corresponding maps of this finite locally free algebra. It is finite etale because that property is checked on its fibers. Consequently every finite etale cover of C is pulled back from B, including on nilpotent bases.

Pull back W->Q along any C->Q. It is a G-torsor pulled back from B and becomes trivial after an etale cover of B. Hence the map lifts to W after that base cover. Isomorphisms of lifts are exactly the G-action. The actual genus-zero map stack is therefore the quotient of the scheme-target map stack by G, with the same finite-type and nilpotent charts. A descended log structure and its finite generator coupons can be retained in the same construction.

An arbitrary nonschematic proper resolution need not come with such a finite etale scheme cover. The separated etale neighborhood in Section 1 is not that cover. Thus Section 5 cannot be used to close the general space-target mapping problem without establishing its additional hypothesis.

#### 6. Current application boundary

For a regular SNC **scheme** model, the preceding family/support packets supply the actual all-schemes logarithmic stack and proper-supported generic cone specialization. Section 1 proves descent of a local isolated resolution to a proper regular algebraic space. Sections 2–3 isolate the remaining actual all-schemes lifting/representation obligation for that space, and Section 4 supplies its auxiliary projective support model. No claim that a target etale atlas is a mapping-stack atlas is made.

### Inline construction 31

#### Statement

Assume AC. Let $Y$ be a smooth integral projective surface over $\mathbb C$ that is rational, meaning birational over $\mathbb C$ to $\mathbb P^2$. There are smooth projective surfaces $Y_0,\ldots,Y_r=Y$ and complex closed points $p_i\in Y_{i-1}$ such that
$$Y_i\cong\operatorname{Bl}_{p_i}Y_{i-1}\quad(1\le i\le r),$$
where either $Y_0=\mathbb P^2$ or
$$Y_0=\mathbb F_a:=\mathbb P_{\mathbb P^1}\bigl(\mathcal O\oplus\mathcal O(a)\bigr),\qquad a\ge0.$$
Here projectivization parametrizes invertible quotients. The points may be infinitely near previous ones. The terminal base can be chosen to have no smooth rational curve of square $-1$; then $a\ne1$. If $\rho$ denotes the rank of the Picard group, which is proved finite below, then $r=\rho(Y)-1$ in the plane case and $r=\rho(Y)-2$ in the Hirzebruch case.

This is a presentation of the original surface $Y$, not merely a common birational roof. No toric boundary or anticanonical divisor hypothesis is required.

#### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** exactly the rational smooth projective surface and AC in the Statement.

[F1] [[thm-principalization-of-ideals]] principalizes the base ideal of a rational map on a smooth projective surface by regular-center blowups. In dimension two the nonidentity centers are points. [[thm-factorization-of-birational-morphisms-of-smooth-surfaces]] factors the resulting proper birational morphisms between regular proper surfaces into point blowups. [[lem-gts-projective-minus-one-contraction-and-crepant-canonical-surface-resolution]] contracts an actual smooth rational minus-one curve to a smooth projective surface.

[F2] [[thm-riemann-roch-for-smooth-projective-surfaces]], [[thm-serre-duality-smooth-projective-variety-locally-free-sheaves]], [[thm-hodge-index-theorem-for-smooth-projective-surfaces]], and [[lem-adjunction-formula-for-effective-divisors-on-smooth-surfaces]] supply exactly their smooth projective complex-surface formulas. [[lem-normalization-lowers-arithmetic-genus-delta]] gives $p_a(C)=g(\widetilde C)+\sum_x\delta_x$ for an integral proper complex curve, with nonnegative terms and $\delta_x=0$ exactly at regular points. Thus $p_a(C)=0$ makes $C$ smooth of genus zero; its complex point and [[thm-genus-zero-point-implies-projective-line]] identify it with $\mathbb P^1$. [[lem-blowup-intersection-matrix-at-smooth-point]] supplies the point-blowup intersections; the Picard, canonical and structure-sheaf formulas are constructed below.

[F3] [[thm-serre-criterion-ampleness]] gives eventual generation of coherent ample twists. [[thm-proper-pushforward-coherent]], [[thm-leray-spectral-sequence-for-sheaf-cohomology]], [[lem-proper-flat-fp-cohomology-perfect-complex]] and [[lem-cohomology-base-change-finite-free-criterion]] supply the exact coherent and flat-family constructions used below. [[thm-cohomology-projective-space-twisting-sheaves]] gives the elementary cohomology of the plane and projective line.

[F4] [[thm-finite-integral-closure-in-a-finite-separable-extension]] constructs the finite normalization of a smooth curve in a finite separable function-field extension. [[lem-gts-complex-function-field-conic-point-and-section]] gives a rational point on the actual generic plane conic over $\mathbb C(t)$ and extends it to a section. [[thm-birkhoff-grothendieck-vector-bundles-p1]] splits the final rank-two bundle. [[thm-proper-quasi-finite-is-finite]] and [[lem-finite-birational-to-normal-is-isomorphism]] identify the resulting finite degree-one maps.

[A1] [[def-axiom-of-choice]] is inherited from these suppliers and used for bases, finite selections and the finite sequence of contractions. No cone theorem, surface MMP, basepoint-free theorem, Tsen theorem or minimal-rational classification is a premise.

#### Proof

**Given:** [F1]–[F4] and [A1]. Write $K$ for the canonical class of whichever smooth surface is under discussion. A class is called nef precisely when its intersection with every integral curve is nonnegative.

**Construction step 1.1.** A common point-blowup roof exists without using a classification of rational surfaces. Resolve a fixed birational map $Y\dashrightarrow\mathbb P^2$ by principalizing its three-section base ideal. The resulting $q:T\to Y$ is a sequence of regular point blowups and $T$ is smooth projective; a regular curve center on a smooth surface is Cartier and its blowup is the identity. The resolved map $p:T\to\mathbb P^2$ is proper birational and is a point-blowup sequence by [F1]. This common roof is used for numerical facts only; it is not asserted to be the required presentation of $Y$. [F1, A1, given, construct]

**Construction step 1.2.** For one point blowup $b:S'\to S$ with exceptional curve $E$, every line bundle has a Cartier divisor representative by the rational-section and Cartier–Weil suppliers. Pushing that divisor to regular $S$ and comparing its pullback shows
$$\operatorname{Pic}(S')=b^*\operatorname{Pic}(S)\oplus\mathbb ZE.$$
The intersection with $E$, whose square is $-1$, proves uniqueness; pullback is injective either by the common function field or by $b_*\mathcal O=\mathcal O$. The latter identity and $R^1b_*\mathcal O=0$ have the following local computation. The blowup of $\mathbb A^2$ has charts $\mathbb C[x,u]$, $\mathbb C[v,y]$, with $y=xu$, $v=u^{-1}$ on their affine overlap. Every overlap monomial belongs to one chart, so its Čech $H^1$ vanishes; their ring intersection is $\mathbb C[x,y]$. Étale local coordinates at a smooth complex point and flat tensor of this two-chart calculation give the same sheaf identities on $S$. Higher direct images vanish by the same two-affine cover. Thus Leray preserves every $H^i(\mathcal O)$ under a point blowup. Finally $dx\wedge dy=x\,dx\wedge du$ gives $K_{S'}=b^*K_S+E$. [F2, F3, A1, construct, algebra]

**Construction step 1.3.** Apply these formulas to the roof in step 1.1. If $p$ and $q$ have respectively $N$ and $M$ point blowups, then $\operatorname{Pic}(Y)$ is a free finite-rank group, its intersection form is unimodular, and
$$\rho(Y)=1+N-M,\qquad K_Y^2=9-N+M=10-\rho(Y),\qquad H^1(Y,\mathcal O)=H^2(Y,\mathcal O)=0.$$
The total transforms of each sequence's exceptional curves are an orthogonal square-minus-one basis. Over $\mathbb Q$ cancel the $M$ such summands from the two decompositions of $\operatorname{Pic}(T)$ to obtain
$$\operatorname{Pic}(Y)_{\mathbb Q}\cong\langle1\rangle\oplus\langle-1\rangle^{\rho(Y)-1}.$$
Here cancellation is explicit: for two rational vectors $v,e$ of square $-1$, reflection in $v-e$ sends $v$ to $e$ if $(v-e)^2\ne0$; otherwise reflection in $v+e$, followed by reflection in $e$, does so. Restrict to their orthogonal complements and repeat. This uses no classification of integral lattices. [F2, F3, step 1.1, step 1.2, algebra]

**Construction step 1.4.** The canonical divisor on a rational surface is not nef. Take the strict transform $L$ of a general plane line on $T$, avoiding the finitely many proper images of the $p$-centers and not contracted by $q$. It is a smooth rational curve with $K_T\cdot L=-3$. If $E_j$ are the effective total exceptional transforms of $q$, then
$$K_Y\cdot q_*L=K_T\cdot L-\sum_jE_j\cdot L\le-3,$$
because distinct effective curves have nonnegative intersection. Thus there is an effective curve with negative canonical intersection. These same roof arguments apply to every smooth rational projective surface obtained later. [F1, F2, step 1.1, step 1.2, algebra]

**Construction step 2.1.** Contract any smooth rational square-minus-one curve using [F1] and continue. Each contraction lowers the Picard rank by one: its inverse is a point blowup by the birational-morphism factorization, since exactly one curve is contracted. Rank is finite by step 1.3 and is at least one, so the procedure stops at a smooth rational projective surface $S$ with no such curve. Reversing this finite sequence will give the required original $Y$ once $S$ is classified. [F1, step 1.3, construct]

**Construction step 2.2.** On this minimal $S$, every integral curve $C$ of negative square satisfies $K\cdot C\ge0$. Indeed adjunction gives $C^2+K\cdot C=2p_a(C)-2\ge-2$. If both terms were negative integers, they would both be $-1$ and $p_a(C)=0$; [F2] would make $C$ a smooth rational minus-one curve, contrary to termination. [F2, step 2.1, algebra]

**Construction step 3.1.** Suppose first $\rho(S)\ge2$. Fix an ample integral $H$ not proportional to $K$, possible because ample twists of each member of a finite Picard basis give ample classes spanning the real space. Let $\mathcal C$ be the closed positive cone $\{v:v^2\ge0,\ H\cdot v\ge0\}$. Every rational class in its interior is effective after a positive integral multiple: for an integral representative $A$ with $A^2>0$, $H\cdot A>0$, Serre duality kills $H^2(mA)$ for large $m$ by $H\cdot(K-mA)<0$, and surface Riemann–Roch makes $\chi(mA)>0$. Thus the closed effective cone contains $\mathcal C$. The Lorentz form supplied by Hodge index makes $\mathcal C$ self-dual, by the ordinary Cauchy–Schwarz inequality in coordinates of signature $(1,\rho-1)$. Consequently every nef class belongs to $\mathcal C$. [F2, F3, step 1.3, algebra]

**Construction step 3.2.** Put $t_0=\inf\{t:K+tH\text{ is nef}\}$. It is finite because $K+nH$ is ample for large $n$, and positive by step 1.4. The closed curve inequalities make $D=K+t_0H$ nef; it is nonzero because $H$ was not proportional to $K$, so $H\cdot D>0$. If $D^2>0$, Lorentz Cauchy–Schwarz on the compact slice $\{C\in\mathcal C:H\cdot C=1\}$ gives $D\cdot C\ge\epsilon H\cdot C$ for every nonnegative-square curve and some $\epsilon>0$. Negative-square curves instead satisfy $D\cdot C=K\cdot C+t_0H\cdot C\ge t_0H\cdot C$ by step 2.2. Subtracting a smaller positive multiple of $H$ would therefore leave all curve intersections nonnegative, contradicting the definition of $t_0$. Hence
$$D^2=0,\qquad K\cdot D=-t_0H\cdot D<0.$$
No real Nakai criterion or contraction theorem is used in this threshold argument. [F2, F3, step 1.4, step 2.2, step 3.1, algebra]

**Construction step 3.3.** Rational nonzero null vectors are dense on the positive null cone: use the rational isometry in step 1.3 and the rational stereographic parametrization of the unit sphere; in rank two its two null rays are already rational. Choose one $F$ sufficiently close to $D$, with $K\cdot F<0$. It can be chosen nef. Indeed, for a fixed Picard basis $B_j$, choose one integer $m$ such that $mH\pm B_j$ are ample. Then $|B_j\cdot C|\le mH\cdot C$ for every integral curve, so all negative-square inequalities retain the uniform margin $t_0H\cdot C/2$ when $F$ is sufficiently close to $D$. For nonnegative-square curves, $F\cdot C\ge0$ follows from Lorentz Cauchy–Schwarz. Clear denominators. We have obtained an integral nef divisor
$$F\ne0,\quad F^2=0,\quad K\cdot F<0.$$
[F2, F3, step 1.3, step 2.2, step 3.2, algebra]

**Construction step 4.1.** Riemann–Roch and $H^2(mF)=0$ for large $m$ give $h^0(mF)\ge1-mK\cdot F/2\ge2$. Remove the fixed divisor $N$ of this linear system and call its moving divisor $M$. It is nef: for any curve choose a member not containing it. Since $F$ is nef and $F\cdot(N+M)=0$, we have $F\cdot M=0$. Hodge index on the orthogonal space of a nonzero positive null vector makes $M^2\le0$, with equality only when $M$ is proportional to $F$. Two members with no common component show $M^2\ge0$. Thus $M^2=0$ and $M\equiv aF$ for $a>0$. Choose two such members without common components; their zero total intersection means they are disjoint. Their sections have no common zero and give a morphism $f_0:S\to\mathbb P^1$ with $M=f_0^*\mathcal O(1)$ and $K\cdot M<0$. [F2, F3, step 3.3, construct, algebra]

**Construction step 4.2.** Construct its connected factor directly. Let $L$ be the algebraic closure of $\mathbb C(f_0)$ in $\mathbb C(S)$. This is a finite extension: choose a transcendence basis for the remaining transcendence degree and a finite field degree above it; the degrees of all finite algebraic constant subextensions are bounded by that field degree, so one maximal finite subextension contains them all. Normalize $\mathbb P^1$ in $L$, using the finite integral-closure supplier, to get a smooth projective curve $B$. The map $f_0$ factors as $S\xrightarrow fB\to\mathbb P^1$: elements integral over an affine base ring pull back into every normal local ring of $S$, so define the factor morphism. Proper coherent pushforward gives $f_*\mathcal O_S=\mathcal O_B$, since its generic algebra consists precisely of the constants $L$ and its finite local algebras are integral subalgebras of the normal curve ring in that field. Leray injects $H^1(B,\mathcal O_B)$ into $H^1(S,\mathcal O_S)=0$, so $B\cong\mathbb P^1$. [F2, F3, F4, step 1.3, step 4.1, construct, algebra]

**Construction step 4.3.** The fibers of $f$ are connected, with no unproved Stein assertion needed. It is flat because the local rings of its integral source are torsion-free over the base curve DVRs. Complete at a base point. Flat base change gives global functions $R$ on the resulting proper flat surface over the complete DVR $R$, since $f_*\mathcal O=\mathcal O_B$. Its finite nonnegative perfect cohomology complex shows that the inverse limit of global functions on the infinitesimal fibers is also $R$: put the degree-zero differential into Smith normal form; nonzero diagonal entries have inverse-limit kernel zero and zero entries contribute $R$. If a fiber were disconnected, its nontrivial idempotent would lift uniquely across every nilpotent thickening and give a nontrivial idempotent in $R$, impossible. The generic fiber is smooth, since it is a localization of the regular surface over a characteristic-zero field. Its global functions are its base field by step 4.2; flat field-extension cohomology then makes it geometrically connected. Properness removes the remaining critical fibers over a base open. For a general smooth connected fiber $G$, the finite map $B\to\mathbb P^1$ has degree $e\ge1$ and $M\equiv eG$, so $G^2=0$ and $K\cdot G<0$. Adjunction gives genus zero and $K\cdot G=-2$. The generic fiber is therefore a smooth genus-zero curve over $\mathbb C(t)$. Its anticanonical system is a plane-conic embedding: after algebraic closure it is the degree-two embedding of $\mathbb P^1$, and the three sections and their unique quadratic relation descend by flat cohomology base change. [F2, F3, F4, step 4.1, step 4.2, construct, algebra]

**Construction step 4.4.** The conic-point supplier [F4] gives a section $\sigma:B\to S$. No fiber can be reducible. For a reducible connected fiber $G_b=\sum m_iC_i$, the equalities $G_b\cdot C_i=0$ and positive intersections between adjacent components give $C_i^2<0$ for every component. Since $K\cdot G_b=-2$, one component has $K\cdot C_i<0$, contradicting step 2.2. Hence a fiber has the form $mC$ with integral support. Its intersection with the section is $1=m(C\cdot\sigma)$, so $m=1$. Flat Euler-characteristic constancy gives $p_a(C)=0$, and [F2] makes it smooth rational. Thus every fiber is $\mathbb P^1$. [F2, F3, F4, step 2.2, step 4.3, algebra]

**Construction step 4.5.** Put $E=f_*\mathcal O_S(\sigma)$. Its fibers have dimension two and $H^1=0$, so [F3] makes $E$ a rank-two vector bundle with arbitrary base change and a surjective evaluation. It defines $S\to\mathbb P_B(E)$, fiberwise the degree-one isomorphism. The map is proper quasi-finite, hence finite; it is birational and the target is normal, hence it is an isomorphism. The pinned splitting theorem gives $E=\mathcal O(a_1)\oplus\mathcal O(a_2)$. Twist by the smaller degree and reorder to obtain $S\cong\mathbb F_a$ with $a\ge0$. The negative section has square $-a$, so minimality excludes $a=1$. [F3, F4, step 4.4, construct]

**Construction step 5.1.** If instead $\rho(S)=1$, step 1.3 gives a primitive positive Picard generator $H$ with $H^2=1$ and $K^2=9$. It is ample because an ample class is a positive multiple of it. Step 1.4 forces $K=-3H$. Riemann–Roch gives $h^0(H)\ge3$ and $H^2(H)=h^0(-4H)=0$. An effective member of $|H|$ is integral and reduced: a nontrivial effective decomposition would split the positive primitive Picard coefficient one. Adjunction makes it a smooth rational curve $C$, and $\mathcal O_C(H)$ has degree one. The restriction sequence and $H^1(\mathcal O_S)=0$ give $h^0(H)=1+h^0(\mathcal O_C(1))=3$ and generate $H$ at every point of $C$; its defining section generates away from $C$. Thus $|H|$ is basepoint-free and defines $S\to\mathbb P^2$. It is finite because $H$ is ample, and its degree is $H^2=1$. The finite birational morphism to normal $\mathbb P^2$ is an isomorphism. [F2, F3, F4, step 1.3, step 1.4, construct, algebra]

**Construction step 6.1.** Steps 4.5 and 5.1 classify the actual terminal surface of step 2.1 as the plane or a Hirzebruch surface. Reverse the constructed contractions. Each inverse is the point blowup already supplied by [F1], giving the stated presentation of the original $Y$. Each blowup raises Picard rank by one. The plane rank is one. For the ruled surface, the fiber degree of a line bundle is an integer $d$; twisting by the relative $\mathcal O(-d)$ makes that degree zero. The same rank-one cohomology/evaluation argument as step 4.5 identifies this twist with the pullback of a line bundle on $\mathbb P^1$. The relative degree and the base Picard generator therefore give exactly two independent Picard generators. The two base ranks give the asserted bound and exact count. [F1, F3, F4, step 1.2, step 2.1, step 4.5, step 5.1] ∎

#### Remarks

The cited Hacking–Keating passage states the factorization using surface MMP. The proof here supplies that geometric input locally from the pinned contraction, intersection, Riemann–Roch, cohomology and curve suppliers. The conic-point supplier is a separate explicit polynomial-coefficient construction. No factorization through a merely common roof is substituted for the final morphism from the original surface.

#### Linear and rank-two cases

A nonzero linear axis coefficient makes the central fibre smooth; flatness and the actual implicit theorem give a smooth family near it. Otherwise c,d have order at least two. The (C,D) Hessian of (1) along this axis is

    [ -2c2    b ]
    [   b   -2d2 ],

where c2,d2 are the quadratic axis coefficients. #### Rank one with both axis coefficients nonzero: ordinary pinch

Suppose c2*d2!=0 and b0²=4c2d2. Complete the square in C by actual parametric Morse splitting fixing (b,D). The residual plane function is divisible by D², because its critical section in C has C in (D) and the value at D=0 is zero. Write the exact central equation as

    X²-D² a(b,D)=0.

The coefficient a(b,0) is a unit multiple of b²-4c2d2. Its derivative in b at b0 is nonzero, since b0!=0. Therefore z=a(b,D) is an actual etale coordinate. #### Rank one with one vanishing quadratic coefficient: generalized conductor

Suppose c2!=0,d2=0,b0=0; the other case is symmetric. The central equation depends on b,D in its C-dependent part only through z=bD:

    c(C)-zC+d(D)=0.

Solve c'(C)=z by the ACTUAL implicit theorem, since c''(0)=2c2!=0. The resulting Legendre critical value is an actual one-variable function L(z)=z² times a unit. Parametric Morse in C, fixing z, yields

    X²-L(bD)-d(D)=0.

Choose the actual unit square root of L(z)/z², and replace b by b times that unit evaluated at bD. Its b derivative is nonzero. This gives EXACTLY

    X²-B²D²-d(D)=0.

If d is identically zero this is the reducible cross. Otherwise d(D)=D^m u(D), m>=3. Replace D by D'=D*u(D)^(1/m), and B by B'=B*D/D'; both changes are actual etale unit operations. The equation becomes

    X²-B'²D'²-D'^m=0,

which is f_n with n=m-2>=1. This proves the rank-one generalized-conductor identification for EVERY finite m, including the previously missing m>=4. The actual parameter-fixed inline construction 22 supplier then applies to the full family. Its eventual family resolution is the separately owned decreasing-n induction; the central coordinate calculation is not presented as that resolution.

If c2=d2=b0=0, (1) has central multiplicity exactly three because of bCD. This is the linear-variable cubic class, retaining all higher axis terms. Its actual family can have total order one, two or three according to its trait coefficients. Therefore it is incorrect to infer total order three solely from this central multiplicity.

### Inline construction 34

#### Cone conductors and actual ray straightening close the recursion class

Research local construction. The input is theta's proved normalization-component classification: a singular normal minimal-degree surface is a cone over a rational normal curve of degree d>=2. Its vertex germ is C[[u,v]]^mu_d for the scalar action, and its polarization has degree one on every ruling line. The conductor is an ACTUALLY reduced curve of polarization degree two.

An irreducible conductor conic cannot pass through the vertex. Project from that vertex. If the curve's projection is nonconstant, its image is the degree-d rational normal base curve. The elementary degree formula for projection of a curve through the centre gives

    2-mult_p(C)=e*deg(image)<=1.

This contradicts deg(image)=d>=2. If the projection is constant, the curve is contained in one ruling line and its reduced degree is one, not two. Therefore every conductor branch through the vertex is a ruling line. Reducedness and total degree two force precisely two DISTINCT ruling lines; a double line is unavailable. Choosing their endpoints as the two homogeneous coordinates on the rational normal base sends them to u=0 and v=0. The germ is the normal toric cone with its two invariant rays, exactly the monoid/conductor input of the Hilbert-basis-sail construction.

The actual conductor gluing need not initially be a torus-equivariant map of the two axis parameters, but it can be straightened without ramifying the TRAIT. The coarse parameter on one axis is a=u^d. An actual pointed axis isomorphism has form psi(a)=a*U(a), U(0) nonzero. On a pointed étale neighborhood take the unit root r(a)^d=U(a), choosing its nonzero residue root. The coordinate map on the index chart

    u -> u*r(u^d), v -> v

is invertible at the origin, commutes with the scalar mu_d action, and induces a -> psi(a) on the coarse axis. Hence it descends to an actual pointed étale coordinate change of the NORMALIZATION CONE, preserving the other ray. Both axis maps may be treated independently. The inverse uses the inverse axis isomorphism and its unit root. No spatial non-unit root, formal centre, or generic finite cover is substituted here.

The same argument for a smooth normalization plane has d=1. Thus, once the Gorenstein residue matching has supplied the cyclic pairing, every new hard cone/conductor point is an ACTUAL cyclic toric conductor pushout, after pointed étale changes fixing the current trait parameter t. Its filtered central tangent cone is therefore the one in inline construction 61, and its actual PBW point blowup/Jacobian budget is available. This closes the recursive CLASS, rather than assuming it from a picture of a subdivided fan.

The quotient-Jacobian ideal is invariant under these étale coordinate changes. In particular the integer certificate already decreased on the preceding actual point-blowup t-chart is not multiplied by a fresh ramification index. Lower double/pinch/hypersurface/CI branches may use their finitely many auxiliary trait extensions only on their finite EXIT constructions. This note does not assert a global descent on its own; theta's actual proper/étale patching supplier must be used at its literal interface.

### Inline construction 35

#### Non-CI cycle vertices: constant-term exclusion and PBW tangent cone

Research construction for the ORDINARY n-cycle face ring, n>=5. This does not identify every arbitrary toric monoid vertex with that ring, or classify every exceptional surface. All algebra below concerns the actual completed local ring; the resulting associated graded ring and point blowup are intrinsic to the original local scheme, so no formal modification is substituted for an actual one.

Let k=C, P=k[z1,...,zn], and let

    A0=P/(zi zj: i,j nonadjacent in the n-cycle).

Let S=k[[z1,...,zn,t]] and let A=S/I be a flat local deformation with A/tA the completion of A0. The generic fibre is smooth. Write m for the maximal ideal. Then t is regular on A.

#### 1. No relation has a first-order constant smoothing term

Flatness gives I/tI=I0. Choose actual completed generators fij=zi zj+t hij. The first-order deformation modulo t² sends the central relation zi zj to hij modulo (t,I0). For fixed j there are at least two distinct nonneighbors i,k, since n>=5. The central syzygy

    zk(zi zj)-zi(zk zj)=0

must lift, so in A0 its first-order image is zk hij-zi hkj=0. Taking spatial degree one gives zk cij-zi ckj=0, where cij is the constant term of hij. Distinct degree-one variables are linearly independent in A0, hence cij=ckj=0. Every nonedge occurs this way. Therefore every fij has TOTAL order at least two in (z,t). This is the actual negative-weight-minus-two vanishing; no unexplained deformation-rigidity theorem is used. Terms of order t² are allowed.

#### 2. The central resolution has pure shifts

The multigraded Koszul complex of A0 on the zi has the following elementary decomposition. At a squarefree multidegree W it identifies, up to the usual index shift, with the reduced simplicial cochain complex of the induced subgraph on W: a surviving squarefree monomial is exactly a face, and the Koszul differential deletes its vertex with the simplicial sign. At a nonsquarefree multidegree, contraction along a repeated variable gives an acyclic summand. Thus the Tor multidegrees are read directly from induced-subgraph reduced homology.

A proper induced subgraph of the cycle is a disjoint union of paths and isolated vertices. Its only possible reduced homology is H0, yielding internal degree j=i+1 in positive Tor degree i. The full cycle has a one-dimensional H1, yielding the single top term i=n-2,j=n. The empty multidegree is the degree-zero copy of k. Consequently the minimal free resolution over P has shifts

    0, 2, 3, ..., n-2, n.

It has length n-2 and its last free module has rank one. The depth formula makes A0 Cohen–Macaulay of dimension two, and the rank-one last term makes its canonical module cyclic, hence A0 is Gorenstein. The last shift n gives a-invariant zero. These facts also follow directly from the Koszul cochain calculation just given; no del Pezzo classification is involved.

#### 3. Actual lifted resolution and its initial complex

Take a minimal finite S-free resolution F of A. Tensoring it with S/(t) stays exact: the two-term resolution for S/(t) has higher Tor zero because t is A-regular. All differential entries are in the maximal ideal, and their reductions are in (z), so the reduced resolution is minimal. Its ranks and shifts are therefore the preceding central ones. Choose lifts of the homogeneous central bases. The degree shifts specify a filtration on each Fi.

For precision, the needed Gorenstein self-duality is an ordinary local homological construction. A has codimension n-2 and depth three: its regular element t and Cohen–Macaulay quotient give a regular sequence of length three, and dimension is three. The dual free complex has cohomology only in degree n-2. Reducing its top canonical module modulo t gives the central rank-one canonical module. Nakayama lifts its generator; regularity of t on this module and on A makes its annihilator exactly I, again by reduction and Nakayama. Hence the canonical module is A. The dual shifted complex is another minimal resolution of A. Lifting the identity on A to chain maps and cancelling their homotopies produces an isomorphism of the two minimal complexes (a map inducing an isomorphism modulo the maximal ideal is invertible). In particular the entries of the LAST differential generate I up to an invertible matrix and unit, because they correspond to the first differential under this duality.

The first differential has entries of order at least two by section1. The last therefore does also. Every intermediate differential has entries of order at least one simply by minimality. These are EXACTLY the orders required by the pure shifts: first and last jumps are two, every intervening jump is one. Thus their leading forms form a homogeneous free complex L over k[z,t]. Its reduction modulo t is the exact central homogeneous resolution.

This initial complex is exact in positive degrees, by a finite graded argument. Reduce a homogeneous cycle modulo t, lift a central boundary, and subtract it. The remainder is t times a cycle because each free module has no t-torsion. Divide by t and repeat. Each repetition lowers total degree by one, so the process terminates. The same argument proves that its degree-zero cokernel B has no t-torsion: if tv is a boundary, its preimage reduces modulo t to a central cycle, subtract its lifted boundary, and divide the remaining preimage by t. Therefore

    B/tB=A0,  t regular on B.

#### 4. Strictness: no extra initial relations

The complex L maps onto the actual associated graded complex, and its first image is generated by the leading quadratic fij. To show these generate the FULL initial ideal, write an actual relation as a combination of the fij. If the lowest filtration-degree contributions cancel, their coefficient vector is a cycle for the first initial differential. Exactness of L expresses this vector as an initial boundary from the next differential. Subtract the corresponding actual boundary in F; the represented relation does not change, while the coefficient expression's lowest degree strictly increases. For a fixed desired initial degree this requires only finitely many corrections. Thus every initial relation is generated by the initial fij. Completeness handles the irrelevant tail, without changing the finite leading term. Hence

    gr_m A = B,
    (gr_m A)/(t)=A0,
    t regular on gr_m A.

This is the promised PBW/normal-flatness assertion. It has not been inferred merely from t-regularity of A; the pure self-dual resolution is what supplies the strict initial complex.

#### 5. Geometric information obtained, and information not obtained

The cycle ring has Hilbert series (1+(n-2)u+u²)/(1-u)². Therefore

    Hilb(gr_m A)=(1+(n-2)u+u²)/(1-u)³.

Its resolution is Cohen–Macaulay and Gorenstein with last shift n in n+1 variables, giving a-invariant -1. Its Proj E is a degree-n Gorenstein surface, and its Cartier hyperplane t=0 is the actual cycle of n lines. In particular omega_E=O_E(-1) and K_E+(t=0)=0. It is reduced: a nilpotent reduces to zero in the reduced quotient A0, so is divisible by t; t-regularity keeps its quotient nilpotent, and descending degrees gives zero.

The actual maximal-ideal blowup is defined on the original local ring by its Rees algebra and has this E as its exceptional scheme. These statements prove the tangent-cone and boundary data needed for the proposed toric-vertex extraction. Normalization, log crepancy of the total modification, resolution of all exceptional component types, and their rational/toric vertex theory STILL require their explicit geometric constructions. In particular a Gorenstein surface of degree n with an n-gon hyperplane is not simply declared rational or ADE by this calculation.

For n=4 section1 fails, since every vertex has only one nonneighbor. The complete-intersection two-quadric branch must be treated by the separate component-blowup analysis. The argument also does not replace arbitrary cyclic quotient toric cones by ordinary planes; their lattice-index and gluing reductions remain exact preliminary supplier obligations.

### Inline construction 36

#### Flatification on an excellent normal complex base by admissible projective modifications

Research proof, 2026-10-28. This is the precise flatification needed for generic-entire projective domination. It proves an admissible **projective modification**, allowing finite normalizations after blowups. It does not claim the full arbitrary-ring single-blowup theorem of Stacks 0815.

#### 1. Statement and supplied local ingredients

Let S be an excellent normal integral Noetherian complex scheme, U a nonempty quasi-compact open, X->S a quasi-compact separated finite-presentation scheme, and F a coherent O_X-module. Suppose F_U is flat over U. Then there is a projective modification `b:S'->S`, an isomorphism over U, with S' normal integral, such that the strict transform F' is flat over S'.

Strict transform means the pullback modulo sections that vanish on U; after the boundary has been made Cartier with local equation a, this is the quotient by a-power torsion. All schemes and modules retain their actual nilpotent structures; the modification base itself is normal and integral. If F=O_X, the torsion is an ideal and its quotient is the actual algebra of the strict transform X'.

The proof uses the locally proved packets:

* `inline construction 25`, giving projectivity of a flat finite-presentation algebra with nonempty geometrically integral fibers as an infinite base module;
* `inline construction 50`, giving actual finite-projection source/base etale charts to a smooth affine target with geometrically integral fibers, including the closed support and its ambient etale lift;
* `inline construction 64`, Sections 2–4, giving product-ideal domination, etale norm-ideal patching and the Fitting-coefficient blowup.
* `inline construction 54`, giving the exact coherent Zariski ideal extension, recursive norm/product ideals on the current base, normal-factor lifts and finite refinement-tree join used in Section 2 below.

Existing source-surface finiteness/normalization interfaces apply here: finite-type schemes over an excellent complex base have finite normalization. Thus normalizing a blowup is finite over that blowup and remains projective. U is normal, so normalization changes no point or structure over U. These facts were already used explicitly for the ordinary stable-map source models; no normalization of an arbitrary nilpotent curve is being invoked.

#### 2. Normal modifications and patching

First blow up a coherent ideal defining the complement of U, then normalize. Its exceptional ideal is invertible Cartier; work on finitely many affine charts where it has a nonzero equation a. The new base is still normal integral and the map is projective and an isomorphism over U. Any subsequent normalized blowup retains these properties.

The etale norm-ideal patching proof applies with normalizations included. An etale chart over a normal integral base is normal and has finitely many disjoint integral components; use these components separately. The descended base ideals of that proof have support outside U. Normalize their product blowup. After etale pullback its normal integral components factor through every local blowup by the product-ideal Cartier argument. They also factor through each local normalization: a dominant morphism from a normal integral scheme to an integral scheme factors through the normalization in their common function field, because every element integral over a target affine algebra maps to an integral element of that normal source algebra. Here the modifications have the same function field on the dense U. Thus the common normalized product blowup dominates the local normalized blowups.

If a local flattening construction uses finitely many successive normalized blowups, apply this argument one stage at a time. A finite composite of projective modifications is projective. Flat strict transforms stay flat under these further base changes: the pulled-back module is flat over its new base, and the exceptional equation is a nonzerodivisor on it, so its further strict transform equals its ordinary pullback. This proves that finitely many local constructions can be patched and composed without losing previously obtained flatness.

The full stagewise formulas, including centers on intermediate etale refinements, are supplied in `inline construction 54`: extend a coherent ideal I from an actual quasi-compact open j:V->P by `ker(O_P->j_*(O_V/I))`; pull each next local center to an etale chart of the current P; descend it up to a factor with the norm/Fitting ideal; normalize the global product blowup; and lift its normal components uniquely through the next local normalizations. Its Sections 4–6 prove the finite-tree recursion, retained flatness and identity of the composite strict transform. Thus “stage by stage” here has an actual constructed global join.

#### 3. Universal injectivity and the exact strict-transform sequence

Let A be a Noetherian domain, B a flat finite-presentation A-algebra with nonempty geometrically integral fibers, and N a finite B-module. Suppose an actual map

    alpha:B^r -> N

is an isomorphism at the generic point pB of every fiber over Spec A. Then alpha is universally injective over A. For a finite A-module Q, a prime filtration and flatness of B give a filtration of `Q tensor B^r` with factors that are sums of `B/pB`. Each B/pB is a domain: it is flat over the domain A/p and injects into its integral generic fiber. Thus the associated primes of `Q tensor B^r` are among pB. The kernel of alpha tensor Q localizes to zero at all of them, since alpha itself is an isomorphism there. A nonzero finite submodule would have an associated prime of the containing finite module, a contradiction. Hence that kernel is zero. Finite Q suffice for all Q by filtered unions. This proves universal injectivity directly.

Put H=coker(alpha). We have a universally exact sequence

    0 -> B^r -> N -> H -> 0.                               (1)

Since B is A-flat, N is A-flat if and only if H is A-flat: if H is flat use extension of flat modules; in the other direction universal injectivity and the Tor sequence give flatness of H.

After any admissible base modification with exceptional equation c, let B',N_b,H_b be the ordinary pullbacks. Universal injectivity makes (1) exact after pullback and after quotienting by every base ideal `(c^k)`. Consequently, if `c^k n=alpha(v)`, then `v=c^k v'`: reduce the equality modulo c^k and use that injectivity. Thus the preimage in N_b of c-power torsion in H_b is exactly `alpha(B'^r)` plus c-power torsion in N_b. Since B'^r has no c-torsion, taking strict transforms gives the **exact** sequence

    0 -> B'^r -> N' -> H' -> 0.                            (2)

This proves the strict-transform sequence rather than assuming that arbitrary torsion quotients preserve exactness. The same argument works after normalizing the modification and on overlaps of exceptional frames.

#### 4. The local dimension-lowering construction

On a finite-projection chart write the smooth target as Spec B over Spec A, of relative dimension d, with nonempty geometrically integral fibers, and let N be the finite module obtained by finite pushforward of F. Its restriction over U is A-flat.

At the generic point pB of any fiber over U, N is free over B_pB. To check this, choose a residue-field basis and a surjection `B_pB^r->N_pB` by Nakayama. Its kernel stays exact modulo p by A-flatness of N, and is zero modulo the maximal ideal pB_pB; Nakayama kills that finite kernel. The free rank is locally constant as p varies over U: the freeness open on the smooth target maps openly to the base and meets the generic point of every integral fiber over that image. Since U is a nonempty open in the integral affine base A, it is connected; hence this rank is one fixed r.

Apply the Fitting-coefficient construction to N. Its hypothesis that B be projective as an A-module is now supplied by the separate local proof. We obtain a blowup supported outside U, followed if necessary by its finite normalization, such that N is locally free of rank r on an open V of the smooth target meeting every fiber. All target-base changes remain smooth with geometrically integral fibers.

Near any point s of the modified base choose r actual elements of N forming a basis at the generic point of its fiber. They define alpha:B^r->N. The open where alpha is an isomorphism contains that generic point; its image in the base is an open neighborhood of s because the target is smooth. Shrink the base to that image, and then to an affine neighborhood. Every fiber's generic point belongs to this isomorphism open, so Section 3 applies. Its cokernel H has fiber-support dimension at most d-1: its support misses the generic point of each integral d-dimensional fiber. Moreover H_U is flat by (1) and the original U-flatness of N.

Finitely many such base opens cover the quasi-compact modified base. This is the local dimension-lowering step. It is valid also for r=0, when N vanishes on the meeting open and H=N has lower-dimensional fiber support.

#### 5. Induction and the preserved specified open

Induct on the maximal dimension d of the geometric fiber supports of a coherent module. This integer is finite for the quasi-compact finite-presentation data. The negative-dimension case is the zero module and requires no modification.

Cover the source and base etale locally by the finite-projection charts. The charts whose maximal support dimension is already smaller than d are handled by induction. On a chart of dimension d, Section 4 gives the actual universally exact sequence with lower-dimensional H. Apply induction to H, over the same normal excellent base and the same specified U. Its normal admissible projective modification makes H' flat. Sequence (2), with its free flat term, then makes N' flat. Finite pushforward and closed-support immersion retain the same base modules, so this is exactly flatness of the original local strict-transform F'.

The finite-projection neighborhoods can be chosen to cover every point: as proved in that packet, closed points of every finite-type fiber suffice, their etale images are open and cover the generalizations, and quasi-compactness gives finitely many charts. Use Section 2 to dominate and patch these finitely many local modifications. Their pulled-back strict transforms remain flat, and flatness descends through the etale source and base coverings. This completes the induction.

Every center and every normalization is an isomorphism over the **given** U throughout. No step replaces U by a smaller generic open. The result is the asserted normal projective U-admissible modification and actual flat strict transform.

#### 6. Application to generic-entire projective domination

Take a projective closure Z over the trait of the projective generic surface, and normalize Z. Its generic fiber is the given smooth X and is unchanged. Form the integral closure of the graph of the generic isomorphism inside `Z times_S Y`. This graph G is proper over Z and is an isomorphism over the entire U=Z_eta.

Apply the proved theorem to O_G over the excellent normal integral base Z. It supplies a normal projective modification Z'->Z, identity over U, with flat strict graph G'->Z'. The source remains proper. Since the parameter is a nonzerodivisor on Z', flatness makes it a nonzerodivisor on `G' times_Z' G'`. Its closed diagonal is the entire product after inverting that parameter, hence already the entire product (its defining ideal would be parameter torsion). Thus G'->Z' is a proper monomorphism, hence finite; its finite flat algebra has generic rank one and therefore rank one everywhere on integral Z'. Its unit is a local basis, so that algebra is O_Z'. Consequently G'=Z'.

The induced `Z'->Y` is the required actual projective dominating model, isomorphic to Y on its **entire** generic pair. This auxiliary model need not be toroidal or log etale; it is used only as the projective support source in `inline construction 45`. The original target, its boundary and obstruction theory remain those on the actual logarithmic mapping stack.

This completes the geometric support prerequisite, subject to independent checking of these research proofs. It does not prove that the supported horizontal cone is exhausted by the claimed tropical contributions.

### Inline construction 37

#### Statement

Assume AC and let R=k[[t]], with k algebraically closed of characteristic zero. Let Y/R be a normal flat finite-presentation surface family with smooth generic fibre. Let D be the scheme-theoretic closure of its smooth generic outer curve. At a selected point p of D0 suppose A0=O_(Y0,p) is reduced Cohen-Macaulay of dimension two and embedding dimension at most three, the ACTUAL D0 is smooth or a split ordinary node, and its ideal I0 is identified with the canonical module of A0 by the supplied log-canonical coupon. These are actual central data; a generic-D condition alone is not a substitute.

Then D is an effective Cartier divisor near p, flat over R. After ONE t=s² and finitely many ordinary point blowups above p, there is a proper generic-isomorphism model whose total space, reduced central fibre and strict outer boundary are regular SNC on a neighbourhood of that strict boundary. The original t is retained as the recorded square of the actual old s. If D0 is already smooth, no extraction is needed. At a node write its actual trait equation xy=t^ell times a unit, ell>=1; at most ell successive potentially hard point extractions suffice. Every noncontinuing chart meeting D is already regular SNC. No new ramification is introduced at every extraction.

The strict boundary under an ambient point blowup is its ACTUAL point blowup by the Rees quotient. It is normal throughout the displayed nodal resolution and consequently remains the same strict boundary if an existing finite ambient normalization is performed. No normalization/Artin-base-change commutation is asserted.

The one-generator Cartier-lifting assertion is also valid for arbitrary flat local-Artin deformations of Y and D with this principal regular closed-fibre ideal; the generator is universally regular after Artin base change. It assumes no pre-existing Cartier lift.

For verification of the central node input: on a supplied toric-face PATH with its ACTUAL reduced endpoint outer ideal, the quotient by that ideal is k[x,y]/(xy). Low PATH degree one or two has the stated embedding bound. Extending the finite exit to an INITIAL higher-degree PATH requires the separate PATH-PBW/Ext supplier proving its noncontinuing endpoint charts enter this scope; that conclusion is not asserted here.

#### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The actual family, scheme-theoretic closure D, selected central algebra and canonical-module identification in the Statement.

[F1] [[thm-nonaffine-regular-local-ring-is-ufd]] supplies local factoriality of the smooth ambient presentations. [[thm-nakayama-lemma]] lifts one generator, and [[thm-krull-intersection-theorem]] gives separatedness for the local t-adic intersection. [[def-axiom-of-choice]] records inherited AC.

[F2] [[thm-serre-normality-criterion]] applies to the Cohen-Macaulay surface hypersurfaces with only isolated singular points below. [[thm-jacobian-criterion-smooth-morphism]] applies only to the ACTUAL presentations below. Hyperbolic Morse splitting is constructed by solving the two critical equations, translating, completing the square and adjoining specified unit roots; all are finite etale implicit operations fixing the indicated base coordinates.

[F3] The Rees quotient and strict-transform identities are proved directly below. The full chart calculations and the degree-one order correction are bound in inline construction 38. Its high-degree joining condition is not silently omitted by this candidate.

#### Proof

**Construction step 1.1.** For the PATH verification clause restrict to the two unglued outer rays. Their primitive nonconstant face monomials extend on the adjacent normal toric cone, vanish on its other ray and hence extend by zero on the other path components. Constants must agree along the connected path. Thus restriction is onto k[x] fibre_product_k k[y]=k[x,y]/(xy), with its ACTUAL endpoint kernel. This proves a scheme-theoretic node, not a guessed normalization. Degree-one and degree-two chain section rings have embedding dimensions two and three respectively. [given, construct]

**Construction step 2.1.** At the selected low-degree point a reduced Cohen-Macaulay two-dimensional local algebra of embedding dimension two is regular. In embedding dimension three, eliminate independent smooth ambient rows to obtain a reduced pure-height-one ideal in a regular three-dimensional local ring. By local factoriality each height-one prime is principal; the reduced ideal is their intersection, hence the product of distinct prime generators. It is a hypersurface. Its two-term free resolution computes its canonical module as a free rank-one module; the regular case is the length-zero calculation. The given identification makes I0=(f0) with f0 a NONZERODIVISOR, because the map A0->I0 is injective. [F1, F2, given, step 1.1, construct]

**Construction step 3.1.** Let I be the ideal of the actual strict closure D. It is t-saturated, so O_D has no t torsion and is flat over the DVR. Tensoring 0->I->O_Y->O_D->0 with k gives I/tI=I0. Lift f0 to f in I. The coherent quotient I/(f) vanishes modulo t and hence vanishes by Nakayama. If fr=0, reduction and regular f0 give r=t r1; regular t cancels and repeats this into every t^m. Krull intersection gives r=0. Thus D is an ACTUAL effective Cartier divisor, flat over R. [F1, step 2.1, algebra]

**Construction step 4.1.** The Artin clause is the same exact argument without an infinite intersection: flatness of Y and D gives I tensor k=I0, and Nakayama lifts f0 to one generator. The finite maximal-ideal filtration of O_Y has graded pieces equal to the closed-fibre algebra tensored with vector spaces. Multiplication by the lifted f induces multiplication by regular f0 on each piece, hence is injective. The argument survives every Artin base change. Actual local equations differ by units, so this is the Cartier line/section coupon, not merely a set-theoretic divisor. [step 2.1, step 3.1, algebra]

**Construction step 5.1.** If D0 is smooth, Cartier gives edim A0<=edim O_D0+1=2. Thus A0 is regular. Flat finite presentation in characteristic zero makes both Y/R and D/R smooth there. The relative differential of f is nonzero; choose a complementary parameter. The ACTUAL etale coordinates are (t,f,z), with central and outer support tf=0. This proves AMBIENT SNC. Smoothness of an arbitrary Weil D alone would not prove it. [F2, step 3.1, step 4.1, construct]

**Construction step 6.1.** At a node of D0, flat ideal lifting and the etale implicit theorem give one actual hypersurface in two spatial variables over R with central xy=0. Relative hyperbolic Morse FIXING t gives xy=h(t). Generic smoothness of D makes h nonzero, so h=t^ell u(t), ell>=1. Divide one spatial coordinate by its actual unit u, keeping t fixed. Thus D is ACTUALLY xy=t^ell and is normal: a surface hypersurface is Cohen-Macaulay and its only possible singular point is x=y=t=0, so the stated Serre criterion applies (ell=1 is smooth). Make the ONE initial t=s², so its exponent m=2ell is even. [F2, step 3.1, step 5.1, construct]

**Construction step 7.1.** Under an ambient point blowup the Rees map direct_sum m_Y^r -> direct_sum(m_Y O_D)^r is degreewise onto, giving a closed immersion of the actual point blowup of D. Its image is the strict generic closure, since the integral D has no component in the centre. For xy=s^m its x-chart is y=xY,s=xT,Y=x^(m-2)T^m; the y-chart is symmetric, and its s-chart is XY=s^(m-2), or XY=1 if m=2. These charts prove the blowup of D remains normal. [step 6.1, construct]

**Construction step 8.1.** If the ambient blowup is finitely normalized, its integral functions restrict generically to rational functions on this normal strict D and are integral over it, hence regular. This factors D through that normalization. It is a section of the finite base change, hence a closed immersion, and its image is exactly the strict generic closure. Thus D is unchanged, with its actual parameter. This is a trait calculation; no normalization commuting with arbitrary nonreduced bases is used. [step 7.1, algebra]

**Construction step 9.1.** First suppose the central ambient surface is regular. Its total family is smooth over R, with actual regular parameters x,y,s, and D is xy=s^m. Blow the ambient point.
