In its x-chart strict D is Y=x^(m-2)T^m, old s=xT. Translate Y by that function. The independent coordinates are x,T,Y_new; the central/outer support is xT Y_new=0, already SNC. The y-chart is symmetric. Only the s-chart repeats with D:XY=s^(m-2); for m=2 its central D is smooth and step 5.1 applies. The three-coordinate blowup Jacobian contributes x². Thus the point log discrepancy is 2+1-1-2=0: outer order is TWO here. [F2, step 6.1, step 7.1, step 8.1, construct]

**Construction step 10.1.** In embedding dimension three, the generator f0 is linear modulo the square of the maximal ideal: its nodal quotient has embedding dimension two. Lift it as the spatial coordinate z. The actual hypersurface ambient presentation restricts to a hyperbolic quadratic in x,y on z=0. Morse in x,y FIXING BOTH z and s gives Y:xy=Phi(z,s), D:z=0. Put p(z)=Phi(z,0), of order at least two or identically zero; do NOT replace it by its tangent cone. On D, Phi(0,s)=s^m u(s), hence exactly Phi=p(z)+s z A(z,s)+s^m u(s). [F2, step 2.1, step 3.1, step 6.1, step 9.1, construct]

**Construction step 11.1.** Blow the ambient point. In the x-chart the strict equation solves Y=p(xZ)/x²+TZ A(xZ,xT)+x^(m-2)T^m u(xT), with old s=xT and strict D:Z=0. Every displayed quotient is regular by ord p>=2. Thus the ambient coordinates x,Z,T prove this chart is already SNC with support xTZ=0. The y-chart is identical. The z-chart has NO strict D, since all its strict-boundary directions have projective z-coordinate zero. All noncontinuing outer charts are therefore proved, not assumed, log regular. [step 10.1, construct]

**Construction step 12.1.** The s-chart has exact equation XY=p(sZ)/s²+Z A(sZ,s)+s^(m-2)u(s), old s=s and strict D:Z=0. If A(0,0) is nonzero its central ambient surface is regular and step 9.1 applies. Otherwise p(sZ)/s² reduces to cZ² for ord p=2 and to zero for larger order or p=0; the same embedding-dimension-three class repeats. In BOTH cases the actual D exponent is m-2, still even. If m=2 then XY=u(0) on D0, hence D0 is smooth and step 5.1 gives ambient SNC there. [step 5.1, step 9.1, step 10.1, step 11.1, algebra]

**Construction step 13.1.** The four-coordinate blowup Jacobian contributes x³ and its hypersurface residue divides by the equation factor x², giving canonical factor x. Thus the degree-two extraction has hypersurface canonical discrepancy 3-2=1, old central order one and Cartier outer order one; its reduced-central log discrepancy is 1+1-1-1=0. Together with step 9.1 these computations retain the correct outer section and original parameter powers in every chart. At each continuing chart m strictly decreases by two, transitions to the regular-ambient case do not increase it, and every other outer chart was solved in step 11.1. Thus at most ell point extractions resolve the outer pair after the single square cover. [step 7.1, step 9.1, step 11.1, step 12.1, construct]

**Construction step 14.1.** The result is a finite composition of actual proper point blowups, generic identities, and any independently supplied finite normalizations compatible by step 8.1. The line/section Cartier coupons glue with their actual units. Off the strict boundary other interior singularities are left to the independent interior algorithm. At an INITIAL high-degree PATH point, one must separately prove its noncontinuing charts become toroidal or enter this low-degree scope; the abstract equation of D alone is not enough. For example t=xy+z² with D:z=0 has D:xy=t but central ambient A1. The square cover and the explicit charts above resolve that example. This proves exactly the public Statement without a generic-D-only or smooth-Weil-divisor shortcut. [step 1.1, step 3.1, step 4.1, step 8.1, step 13.1, construct] ∎

### Inline construction 38

#### Outer PATH boundary: actual node, Cartier lift, Rees restriction and low-degree finite exit

Research construction for the OUTER boundary bridge. It uses the ACTUAL central outer ideal, not only the generic boundary. This supplies the low-degree family exit after the separately owned PATH point-extraction algebra.

#### 1. The two endpoint rays give an actual NODE

Let the central local surface be a toric-face PATH: finitely many normal affine two-dimensional toric cones glued along their internal reduced invariant rays, with two unglued OUTER endpoint rays. Assume its outer ideal is the ACTUAL reduced endpoint kernel, as supplied by the canonical/log-family construction. This identification is an input; it is not deduced merely from a generic anticanonical divisor.

Restriction to the two outer rays gives a surjection

    O_Y0 -> k[x] fibre_product_k k[y] = k[x,y]/(xy).

Indeed each primitive nonconstant monomial on an endpoint ray extends on its adjacent normal toric component; it vanishes on that component's other ray and can be extended by zero on all other components. For a one-component path the same two face monomials vanish on the opposite ray. Constants must have one common value because the path is connected. This proves surjectivity and identifies its kernel with the actual reduced endpoint ideal. Thus the outer curve D0 is a NODE at every such endpoint meeting, with its full scheme algebra, rather than a guessed normalization.

At a point of just one outer ray away from the toric vertex the normal toric surface is regular along that invariant divisor and the outer curve is smooth. This is the elementary normal two-dimensional toric codimension-one chart, not a higher-valence vertex conclusion.

#### 2. Cartier at low degree, including the order distinction

Suppose the PATH algebra supplier identifies its actual central local ring as reduced Cohen-Macaulay of dimension two, with the low-degree tangent embedding data. Degree one has embedding dimension two and is regular. Degree two has embedding dimension three and is an actual hypersurface: eliminate independent smooth ambient rows, then its reduced height-one ideal in the regular three-dimensional local ring is principal (regular local rings are UFDs). Both are Gorenstein.

Assume the actual outer ideal I0 is identified with the dualizing/canonical module by the supplied log-canonical coupon. Since that module is free, I0=(f0) with f0 a NONZERODIVISOR. Its inclusion as an ideal makes regularity automatic: an isomorphism O_Y0->I0 sends 1 to f0 and is injective. Thus D0 is Cartier. The canonical-module identification is load bearing and is not replaced by an assertion that every boundary on a Gorenstein ring is principal.

There is an important grading/order distinction. For the degree-one toric plane, the full two-endpoint ideal is (xy), of maximal-ideal order TWO. For the degree-two conic cone k[x,y,z]/(xz-y²), the endpoint ideal is (y), of order ONE. The degree-two two-line-chain ring k[x,y,z]/(xz) has the same endpoint ideal (y). Therefore a point-extraction log-discrepancy argument using outer order one applies to degree two, not automatically to degree one.

#### 3. Lift the ONE generator to the ACTUAL total family

Let Y be flat over the trait and let D be the scheme-theoretic closure of its generic outer divisor. Its ideal I is t-saturated by definition, so O_D is t-torsion-free and hence flat over the DVR. Tensoring

    0 -> I -> O_Y -> O_D -> 0

with the residue field gives I/tI EXACTLY equal to I0. Lift f0 to f in I. The coherent quotient I/(f) has zero reduction modulo t, so Nakayama gives I=(f). Since O_Y is flat and f0 is regular, f is regular: a relation fr=0 reduces to r in (t), and cancellation of the regular t iterates this into every t^m; local Krull intersection gives r=0. Thus D is an actual effective Cartier divisor on Y, not only on Y0.

The same statement holds over every local Artin base when Y and D are flat. The ideal reduction is exact, Nakayama lifts one generator, and the flat maximal-ideal filtration makes the lifted generator regular, universally under Artin base change. It is the same nilpotent-retaining argument as the balanced-node completion lemma. Actual local generators differ by units on overlaps, so the Cartier line/section coupon and its prescribed tensor identifications glue etale-locally on a scheme or algebraic space.

#### 4. Smooth D0 plus Cartier forces the ambient pair to be log regular

If D0 is SMOOTH and Cartier in the two-dimensional Y0, then

    embedding_dimension(Y0) <= embedding_dimension(D0)+1 = 2.

Its dimension is two, so Y0 is regular. Flat finite presentation in characteristic zero makes Y/trait smooth at that point. Flatness and the smooth D0 likewise make D/trait smooth. Its regular equation has nonzero relative differential. Choose it and a complementary relative parameter: the actual etale chart is (t,f,z), with the central and outer support t f=0. Hence the AMBIENT pair is regular SNC, not just D as an abstract curve. This implication uses Cartier. A smooth Weil divisor on a singular threefold would not suffice.

#### 5. D itself has an ACTUAL A_l family equation

At a node of flat D0, finite-presentation flat ideal lifting and the etale implicit theorem give one actual hypersurface equation in two spatial variables over the trait, with central xy=0. Relative algebraic Morse in x,y fixes t and gives

    D: xy=h(t)=t^ell u(t), ell>=1, u a unit.

The function h is nonzero because the generic OUTER curve is smooth. Absorb the unit in x, keeping t fixed. This gives actual xy=t^ell, not merely an isomorphism of completions. It is a normal integral surface (smooth for ell=1, otherwise the elementary normal A_(ell-1) hypersurface).

Make ONE initial t=s². Then D is xy=s^m with m=2ell EVEN. All subsequent point extractions below use this same s; no new square root is taken at every step.

#### 6. Ambient point blowup restricts to the ACTUAL point blowup of D

For a closed point p on D, the Rees algebra map

    direct_sum m_Y^r -> direct_sum (m_Y O_D)^r

is degreewise SURJECTIVE. Thus Bl_m D is a closed subscheme of Bl_m Y, and the ideal on D is its actual maximal ideal. Since D is integral and not contained in the centre, its Rees algebra has no exceptional torsion; its closed image is exactly the strict closure of D away from p. This proves the asserted restriction, rather than assuming strict transform commutes with arbitrary base change.

For D:xy=s^m with even m>=2 the point blowup has x-chart y=xY,s=xT, hence Y=x^(m-2)T^m; the y-chart is symmetric. Its s-chart x=sX,y=sY is XY=s^(m-2). The first two charts are smooth with old s=xT; the last is normal A_(m-3), or XY=1 when m=2. Thus Bl_m D is normal throughout.

If the ambient blowup is subsequently normalized, D's strict transform is STILL this Bl_m D. The finite normalization is an isomorphism at its generic point, so its integral functions restrict to rational functions on D integral over D. Normality makes them regular. This gives a factorization D->ambient normalization; it is a section of the finite base change, hence a closed immersion. Its image is the strict generic closure. Thus ambient normalization has not silently replaced D by a different curve family or discarded a thickening.

#### 7. Complete degree-one ambient exit

When Y0 is regular and D0 is a node, Y is smooth over the trait. The actual equation of D can be put in the form xy=s^m in regular coordinates (x,y,s) on Y, with m even. Blow the ambient point. In the x-chart the strict boundary is

    Y=x^(m-2)T^m, old s=xT.

Translate the spatial coordinate Y by that displayed actual function. The three independent coordinates are x,T,Y_new, central support xT=0 and outer boundary Y_new=0. This is ALREADY regular SNC. The y-chart is symmetric. The s-chart is again a smooth ambient source with strict D:XY=s^(m-2). Only this continuing chart can need another outer extraction. If m=2 its central D is smooth, and section 4 gives the exit.

The extraction is log crepant: a point of a smooth threefold has canonical discrepancy two, old central order one and outer order two. Its reduced exceptional central coefficient is one, so the log discrepancy is 2+1-1-2=0. This is the degree-one correction to an outer-order-one formula.

#### 8. Complete degree-two ambient exit with outer equation retained

At degree two, D is Cartier and a node. Use its generator z as one spatial coordinate. The central hypersurface has a rank-two restriction in x,y on z=0. Actual relative Morse in (x,y), FIXING BOTH z and s, gives

    Y: xy=Phi(z,s),      D: z=0.

Write p(z)=Phi(z,0). Its order is at least TWO, or it is identically zero; it need not be exactly quadratic. In particular this retains actual toric A_k central surfaces whose tangent cone is two planes. On D, Phi(0,s)=s^m u(s), with m even. Hence EXACTLY

    Phi=p(z)+s z A(z,s)+s^m u(s).                    (1)

Blow the ambient point and take the strict hypersurface. In the x-chart y=xY,z=xZ,s=xT the equation solves Y EXACTLY as

    Y=p(xZ)/x²+TZ A(xZ,xT)+x^(m-2)T^m u(xT).

The quotient p(xZ)/x² is actual regular because ord p>=2. Thus the ambient coordinates are x,Z,T, old s=xT, and strict outer D is Z=0. This is regular SNC, regardless of A or u. The y-chart is symmetric. The z-chart has NO strict D: its homogeneous direction z is nonzero, whereas every strict-boundary blowup direction has z=0.

In the s-chart x=sX,y=sY,z=sZ we have

    XY=p(sZ)/s²+Z A(sZ,s)+s^(m-2)u(s), old s=s, D:Z=0.  (2)

If A(0,0)!=0, the central ambient surface is regular and the degree-one exit applies with the SAME remaining exponent m-2. If A(0,0)=0, p(sZ)/s² reduces to cZ² when ord p=2, and to zero when ord p>2 or p=0. Thus after this step the central equation is the quadratic or two-plane class, with exponent m-2. No claim that the original low-degree central germ was already its tangent cone has been made. If m=2 then D0 has XY=u(0)!=0, is smooth, and Cartier plus section 4 gives ambient SNC there. All other points away from the remaining boundary node are smooth or already the computed monomial charts. Any interior singularity disjoint from D belongs to the separate interior resolution algorithm; it is not an unresolved outer chart.

The point extraction has hypersurface canonical discrepancy 3-2=1, old central order one and Cartier outer order one. Thus its log discrepancy is 1+1-1-1=0. The actual original parameter powers and the strict outer coupon are retained in (1)-(2).

#### 9. Finite exit and EXACT joining condition

Starting in degree at most two, the single initial square cover makes m even. Every potentially hard CONTINUING chart in sections 7-8 reduces m by two. Every noncontinuing chart meeting D is ALREADY regular SNC; this was proved in ambient coordinates, not inferred only from smoothness of D. A transition from degree two to degree one does not increase m. Thus at most ell point extractions finish the outer pair, with no repeated ramification. The strict D identification survives each ambient normalization by section 6, so the exponent is the actual boundary exponent at every stage.

For an INITIAL high-degree PATH point, the separately owned PATH-PBW/Ext point-extraction supplier must prove its noncontinuing endpoint charts are toroidal or enter this low-degree class. The surface identity D:xy=t^ell by itself is NOT enough to assert that fact. For instance the smooth total threefold t=xy+z² with D:z=0 has D:xy=t, while its central ambient surface is A1 rather than SNC; its initial square cover and the explicit degree-two extraction above do resolve it. A proof which stops at D's abstract smoothness would miss this ambient issue.

Accordingly this file closes the ACTUAL low-degree Cartier/log-family exit, the Rees restriction and the finite exponent bound, and identifies the precise high-degree join expected from the PATH algebra supplier. It neither narrows the source to a generic-D-only assumption nor claims an arbitrary Weil boundary becomes Cartier without the central canonical-ideal identification.

### Inline construction 39

#### Outer paths: paired PBW, actual log-crepant extraction and finite boundary exit

Research construction for the fixed outer-path gap. The input is an ACTUAL flat pair (A,I)/C[[t]], with A/I flat, smooth generic surface/boundary, and central ring the exact toric-divisor PATH pushout. The endpoint ideal I0 is the actual reduced outer boundary kernel. The sail calculation gives gr(A0)=R(C,L), C a polarized rational chain of total degree d; gr(I0) is its endpoint canonical ideal and A0/I0 is the actual node. No Cartier property of I is assumed. The meaning of actual divisorial pushout is specified in `inline construction 11`. Merely knowing a normalizer fan is not this input.

#### 1. The outer ideal is the ACTUAL relative canonical module

The residue calculation gives I0=omega_A0. Both A and A/I are Cohen–Macaulay (lift the regular trait parameter), so the ideal I is maximal Cohen–Macaulay and t-flat. Let W=omega_A/R; W/tW=omega_A0. Canonical duality gives

    Hom_A0(W0,W0)=A0,        Ext_A0^j(W0,W0)=0 (j>0).

This can be computed without a representability theorem: choose a finite free resolution over the regular power-series ambient ring, dualize it to obtain W0, and dualize again; the resulting finite double complex has its only cohomology A0 in degree zero. The same computation is canonical-module duality on maximal Cohen–Macaulay modules.

Resolve W by free A-modules and apply Hom(-,I). Modulo t the complex computes Ext_A0(W0,I0). The long exact sequence for multiplication by t says that t surjects on Ext_A^1(W,I), since the special Ext¹ is zero. This Ext module is finite, so Nakayama makes it zero. Consequently Hom_A(W,I)→Hom_A0(W0,I0) is surjective. Lift the canonical isomorphism; its cokernel vanishes by Nakayama, and its kernel does also because I is t-flat and the special map is an isomorphism. Thus

    I ≅ omega_A/R

on the ACTUAL local ring, including arbitrary infinitesimal base changes. No formal isomorphism is silently algebraized. This supplies the total log-canonical-module identity from the flat pair and central residue calculation.

#### 2. The rational-chain resolutions and the first trait constants

For d≥3, R(C,L) has a pure TWO-LINEAR Cohen–Macaulay resolution: with d+1 spatial generators and codimension c=d-1 its shifts are

    0,2,3,...,d.

For a chain of d lines, the multigraded Koszul computation is the induced-forest computation from the cycle packet, now with no full-cycle H¹ term. Every nonzero higher Tor is in degree i+1. A polarized rational chain degenerates flatly to this line chain by the convex-weight degeneration of each rational normal component, fixing the two endpoints. Semicontinuity kills all off-diagonal Tor, and the unchanged Hilbert series fixes the remaining ranks. The type is d-1. The endpoint canonical module is generated in degree ONE; its dual pure resolution has shifts

    1,2,...,d-1,d+1.

The negative-degree-two deformation space is zero. For a line chain, apply a constant deformation to zi zj for a nonedge. A three-term syzygy zk(zi zj)-zi(zk zj)=0 makes qij zk-qkj zi=0 in degree one. Every nonedge of a chain with at least four vertices has an endpoint with a second nonneighbor, so its constant must vanish. The finite graded cotangent complex and the same flat degeneration prove the assertion for every polarized chain of degree d≥3. Thus actual defining equations have no term which is a nonzero scalar times t; every first equation has total order at least two. The minimal ambient has d+1 spatial variables plus the ACTUAL parameter t.

Lift the strict filtered central pure resolution to the total minimal free resolution. Its first map has order at least two and every other map order at least one. The associated graded complex modulo t is the exact central pure resolution. Finite graded t-division proves its exactness and t-regularity; the complete filtered-cycle elimination proves strictness. This is exactly the PBW argument of the cyclic carrier with no final quadratic jump. Hence gr(A)/(t)=R(C,L), and t is regular on gr(A).

#### 3. Paired strictness, not just PBW of the ambient ring

Use the actual canonical identification I=W. The dual resolution of A gives the resolution of I with the shifts above. The inclusion I→A is a chain map. At its first term it has order at least one because I is a proper ideal. At all middle terms the required order is zero. At its LAST term the required order is one and holds by duality of the canonical-ideal triangle

    I→A→A/I.

Indeed dualizing gives the same triangle, since I is canonical and A/I is Gorenstein with locally free relative canonical module. Thus the dual chain map again induces the inclusion I→A. Its degree-zero entries lie in I⊂m; these are the transpose of the last entries of the original map, up to minimal change of bases and homotopy. Minimal differentials have entries in m, so homotopy cannot create a constant last entry. This proves the required filtration bound; it is not an assumption that an arbitrary ideal inclusion is strict.

The associated graded mapping cone modulo t is the exact filtered central resolution of the ENDPOINT NODE. The same finite graded t-division and complete cycle elimination now give strictness of the PAIR. In particular

    gr(A/I)/(t)=gr(A0/I0),    t regular on gr(A/I),
    I intersect m^k = m^(k-1) I  (k≥1).

The last equality follows by lifting degree-one generators of gr(I) and successively removing leading terms. It is the exact Rees condition needed below. In particular a high-degree path cannot have REGULAR total outer surface: edim(A)=d+2, mu(I)=type(A)=d-1, so edim(A/I)≥3. Equivalently its node smoothing exponent is at least two.

#### 4. Actual extraction and log canonical module

Blow the actual maximal ideal. It is a vertical center, hence generic identity. PBW makes the extended Rees ring Cohen–Macaulay; its reduced graded fiber and generic normality give R1, hence the actual charts are normal. The parameter t has order one on every exceptional component, because it is regular on the graded ring. Thus the new central fiber is reduced.

On a chart with exceptional coordinate u, all spatial variables scale by u. The ambient relative volume scales by u^(d+1), while the top pure-resolution orientation scales by u^d. Therefore the NEW canonical module is generated by the OLD generators divided by u. The paired strictness identifies those generators with I/u and identifies its quotient with the ACTUAL Rees strict transform of A/I. Consequently

    omega_new/R = I_strictD.

This is the actual log-crepancy statement. It neither calls the non-Cartier old canonical module a line bundle nor uses a divisor discrepancy formula for an undefined pullback. Since the old central parameter has order one, reduced central support is also retained. Every chart keeps old t=uT exactly; no parameter unit is thrown away.

#### 5. Every non-trait chart is already an old solved block

Let E be the projective exceptional surface. Its hyperplane T=0 is the actual polarized rational CHAIN C, by PBW. At a smooth point of this hyperplane, E is regular: a smooth Cartier quotient of a two-dimensional Cohen–Macaulay ring has embedding dimension at most two. At a node, E has embedding dimension at most three and hence is an actual hypersurface XY=psi(T), by relative Morse fixing T. Lift the exceptional parameter u. Relative Morse fixing (u,T) gives

    XY=Phi(u,T),       old t=uT.

This is the actual two-base conic block already constructed. If E itself is regular while T=0 is nodal, Morse of the FUNCTION T fixing u instead gives old t=u(PQ+h(u)), the existing two-base monomial block. Thus ALL non-trait hyperplane nodes are handled, without assuming that finite central drawings give a family chart.

The strict outer divisor meets this hyperplane at exactly its two SMOOTH ENDPOINTS: the paired quotient modulo t is the endpoint node ring. At those endpoints E and the total chart are regular. The outer strict surface is the ordinary point blowup of its A_l surface and is regular in these non-trait charts, with old parameter xT. Its reduced central curve has two distinct branches. Therefore its equation f has a nonzero derivative in the third coordinate transverse to (u,T): otherwise restriction of uT would have a double tangent, not a node. The actual coordinates (u,T,f) make this a regular SNC triple. This is the missing ambient endpoint argument; regularity of the outer surface alone was not used to assert it.

#### 6. The continuing boundary chart has a finite integer decrease

The actual endpoint quotient is a node. Generic smoothness of the outer curve makes its actual total equation xy=t^l times a unit; absorb that unit. Make ONE initial t=s². The total outer surface is xy=s^(2l). Strict restriction of an ambient point blowup is its actual point blowup, by the Rees surjection and the paired strictness. The continuing s-chart has equation XY=s^(2l-2); the x/y charts are the regular side charts already treated. Normality of these A_l point blowups means ambient normalization does not alter the strict outer surface. These exact facts are in `inline construction 38`.

Thus every continuing HIGH-DEGREE boundary stage lowers the even node exponent by two. When the exponent is two, its trait chart has XY=1, so the outer central curve is smooth and no endpoint-type hard path remains there. Every side chart was already SNC or a solved interior conic block. New interior cyclic-conductor points are handled by the cyclic PBW quotient-Jacobian decrease; this introduces no new boundary restart. The exceptional component classification uses the same Hodge/complete-H/conductor-degree-two proof, with the OUTER strict divisor included among the unpaired degree-two boundary branches. Hard cone points have two toric rulings, whose actual ray identifications are straightened by the existing invariant unit-root lemma. Thus the recursive classes are again the exact path/cycle classes, not merely equal tangent fans.

Finally degree d≤2 paths are central hypersurfaces and their canonical endpoint ideal is principal. Flatness of A/I and Nakayama lift it to one ACTUAL generator of I. The separate low-degree outer lemma gives the full finite ambient exit with old parameter retained. Degree one has outer order two and is treated there with its correct discrepancy, not forced into the degree-one-ideal calculation above.

The resulting tree is finite: the boundary exponent bounds the high-degree path spine, each side exits as proved, and the interior cyclic branches have their strictly decreasing quotient-Jacobian budget. Only finitely many terminal A/D/pinch/root choices remain, so their auxiliary finite trait extensions may be combined after finiteness is established. This is the actual boundary-path bridge; it requires the stated genuine toric pushout and flat outer pair, not an arbitrary collection of normalizations.

### Inline construction 40

#### Actual PBW point blowup: crepancy and quotient-Jacobian decrease

Research construction. Input is the proved PBW/pure-resolution stage, not an arbitrary unverified toric central model. The polarized-cycle extension supplies the same data beyond an ordinary union of planes.

Let A be the actual normal local three-dimensional trait algebra, with trait parameter t, spatial embedding dimension n, relative dimension two, and smooth generic fibre. Assume the PBW construction has supplied the following data: A is Gorenstein; its complete ambient regular presentation has a strict filtered free resolution with shifts 0,2,3,...,n-2,n; gr_m A is reduced, Cohen–Macaulay and Gorenstein; its degree-one t is regular and gr_m A/(t) is the complete section ring of the specified polarized nodal genus-one curve. All first relation generators Fi have order two and their initial quadrics generate the FULL initial ideal. Put c=n-2. For an ordinary n-cycle with n>=5 these are the exact conclusions of inline construction 35. The generalized polarized-cycle proof must be bound at its actual scope before this lemma is consumed there.

#### The blowup is actual and its equations are strict

Take the maximal-ideal blowup of the ORIGINAL local scheme. This is relative Proj of its actual Rees algebra. The filtered resolution, with its exact initial complex, proves strictness of all its syzygy modules by the same finite leading-cycle subtraction as in the PBW proof. Homogenizing that resolution consequently gives the Rees presentation. Thus, on the t-chart, the strict equations are EXACTLY

    zi=t Zi,
    Fi(z,t)=t² Gi(Z,t),

with no missing embedded component or uncomputed t-saturation. On any other point-blowup chart replace t as scale by the selected spatial coordinate lambda; the same strict statement holds. This calculation can be checked after completion because completion is faithfully flat; it proves equality of the finitely generated actual chart ideals. It does not algebraize a formal choice of centre: the centre was the actual maximal ideal from the start.

The exceptional scheme is E=Proj gr_m A. The old parameter has generic order one along each exceptional component: its degree-one initial form is regular on gr_m A and is not zero on any component. In a non-t chart its exact expression is old t=lambda T. The reduced central divisor is therefore retained with its actual parameter product, not assigned from a picture.

#### Gorenstein charts, normality and the canonical generator

The extended Rees deformation has central fibre gr_m A and its parameter is a nonzerodivisor. Its Gorenstein property follows locally by lifting a generator of the canonical module through this regular parameter: reduce the dual finite free resolution to the Gorenstein initial complex, lift its top generator, and use Nakayama and regularity to see that its annihilator is the defining ideal. Localizing a degree-one chart coordinate makes the extended Rees algebra a Laurent extension of the degree-zero blowup chart, so the actual chart is Gorenstein. In particular it is S2.

Away from the exceptional scheme, normality is that of A. At each generic point of an exceptional component, the exceptional local ring is a field (gr_m A is reduced), and the exceptional parameter is regular. The threefold chart is therefore regular there. Every height-one point lies either there or outside the exceptional scheme. R1 plus S2 gives normality. This is a statement about the total blowup, not a false assertion that every central component is normal.

Crepancy is read from the same strict filtered resolution. In a t-chart the ambient regular top form scales as

    dz1 wedge ... wedge dzn wedge dt
      =t^n dZ1 wedge ... wedge dZn wedge dt.

The last free basis of the Gorenstein resolution has filtered shift n. In the homogenized dual resolution its canonical Ext generator therefore scales by the reciprocal t^n. The factors cancel EXACTLY. Its leading coefficient is a unit because the initial resolution's top canonical generator is a generator, not merely a section. Hence the pullback of an actual canonical generator of A extends as a nowhere-zero generator on the blowup chart. Every other scale chart has the identical cancellation lambda^n/lambda^n. This proves K_bl=pi^*K_A, without an unproved discrepancy classification or a negativity shortcut.

Since old t has order one along every exceptional component, adding the reduced central divisor preserves this equality of log canonical generators. The modification is log crepant for the CENTRAL trait boundary. An additional horizontal outer divisor, if present through the centre, requires its separately specified log-presentation data; this lemma does not silently include its multiplicity in the Gorenstein-cycle calculation.

#### The correct Jacobian budget is in the quotient ring

Let J_rel(A) be the ideal in A generated by the c by c minors of the relative Jacobian matrix (partial Fi/partial zj). It is the relative Fitting ideal controlling the smooth two-dimensional locus. Generic smoothness gives

    t^M in J_rel(A)

for a finite M. This certificate is IN A. Equivalently it is an ambient certificate modulo I, but its relation terms must be discarded only AFTER passing to the strict transform quotient. One must not claim that the ambient ideal (Fi,minors) has common factor t^c: Fi has factor only t² when c>2.

On the actual t-chart, differentiating Fi=t²Gi while holding t fixed gives

    pullback(partial Fi/partial zj)=t partial Gi/partial Zj.

Every c-minor consequently has factor t^c. Pull the certificate back to the actual quotient chart, where every Fi vanishes. It becomes

    t^M in t^c J_rel(A').

The chart is t-flat, so cancellation gives

    t^(M-c) in J_rel(A')

whenever the chart contains the point. If M<c, such a point is impossible. Thus every continuing t-chart consumes at least c=n-2 units of the finite quotient-Jacobian exponent. Actual pointed coordinate changes and elimination of a row with a unit Jacobian pivot preserve this Fitting ideal: block row/column operations leave a unit block and the smaller relative presentation. The certificate therefore survives all intermediate local embedding-dimension reductions.

On non-t charts the old parameter is lambda T, and the finite outer/model suppliers must be used with THAT exact product. The preceding integer decrease is not asserted along an arbitrary unrelated chart or after multiplying the parameter by a fresh ramification degree. It supplies finite continuation when the separately classified hard descendants remain in the t-chart of the PBW extraction, while the other descendants exit to the already finite lower model blocks.

#### Scope and use

This proves actual vertical point blowup, central log crepancy, and the strict quotient-minor decrease for every verified pure PBW Gorenstein cycle stage. The exceptional component normalization/conductor classification, polarized-cycle pure-resolution transfer, and the lower-degree/non-Gorenstein boundary cases must be supplied by their exact carriers. None is inferred merely from flatness, an unverified total monoid chart, or a central fan subdivision. No production write, source acceptance, or broad theorem readiness is asserted by this note.

### Inline construction 41

#### Polarized nodal cycles: degeneration, pure resolution and negative T1

Research mathematical construction for the higher-valence toric-face preliminary supplier. It proves the required GRADED algebra input. It does not itself turn that input into a PBW theorem for an arbitrary filtered total-family algebra.

Let C be a connected projective nodal cycle of rational curves over C. A single irreducible nodal rational curve and a two-component cycle with two nodes are allowed. Let L have strictly positive normalization degrees d_i, and set D=sum d_i>=3. Put

    A(C,L)=direct_sum_(m>=0) H0(C,L^m).

Then A is generated in degree one, with Hilbert series

    (1+(D-2)z+z²)/(1-z)².

It is Cohen-Macaulay Gorenstein of dimension two, with degree-zero canonical generator. Its minimal resolution over the polynomial ring on H0(L) is pure: beta_0,0=1; for 1<=i<=D-3 only beta_i,i+1 can be nonzero; and beta_(D-2),D=1. For D>=5 its negative graded cotangent space satisfies

    T1(A)_(-r)=0 for EVERY r>=2.

Here T1 means coker(Der(S,A)->Hom_A(I/I²,A)) for a degree-one polynomial presentation S->A, I its kernel. The exact elementary proofs follow.

#### 1. A concrete family degenerating to the ordinary D-gon

On each normalization component (P1,O(d_i),marked 0 and infinity), take its product with the auxiliary trait B=Spec C[[epsilon]]. Blow up the intersection of the CURRENT zero section with the current central end component d_i-1 times. Every centre is a smooth point of multiplicity one in the central fibre. Thus after each blowup the central fibre remains a reduced chain, and the zero section meets the NEW end component away from its other node.

After the first blowup twist the pulled-back line by -(d_i-1)E1. Its degrees are one on the old component and d_i-1 on E1. After the next blowup twist by -(d_i-2)E2: the preceding end has degree one and the new end degree d_i-2. Continue. The final central chain consists of d_i copies of P1, each of degree one. Its two end sections stay disjoint and smooth. The generic normalization component remains P1 with degree d_i.

Glue the zero and infinity sections of these component families cyclically, with specified isomorphisms of the endpoint line fibres. This is an actual finite nodal pushout: near each pair of smooth sections its local algebra is the fibre product of their two regular curve charts over O_B, with node equation xy=0. Its normalization exact sequence is

    0 -> O_family -> direct_sum O_component-families
       -> direct_sum O_node-sections -> 0.

The final map is the difference of the two restrictions and is surjective. Hence the pushout is flat over B and proper; the same exact construction glues the line bundles. Its special fibre is the ordinary D-gon with degree one on each component, and its generic fibre is a nodal cycle with the original component degrees.

Endpoint twists may change the GENERIC gluing scalar of L when compared with the original endpoint trivializations. This is not silently ignored. For a rational cycle, multidegree-fixed line bundles differ by one nonzero scalar, obtained by multiplying their node gluing constants around the cycle. Rescaling the coordinate of one P1 component, fixing its two endpoints, changes that scalar by a d_i-th power. After a finite extension of C((epsilon)) that scalar has a d_i-th root. Thus the generic POLARIZED cycle is isomorphic to the prescribed (C,L) after a finite field extension. All conclusions below are invariant under and descend from that faithfully flat field extension. No marking beyond the two nodal endpoints is being preserved by this auxiliary degeneration.

#### 2. Cohomology and an actual degree-one embedding

For any cycle with positive degrees and any m>=1, normalization gives

    0 -> L^m -> direct_sum O_P1(m d_i)
       -> direct_sum C_nodes -> 0.

Each component line has independently prescribed values at its two endpoints since m d_i>=1. The difference map to the nodes is therefore onto. H1 on the components is zero, so H1(C,L^m)=0 and h0(C,L^m)=mD. The same calculation applies to the expanded special chain and glues across the relative nodes. Actual cohomology and base change makes every positive graded piece a free B-module with these fibres. The degree-zero piece is O_B.

For completeness, L is very ample on every polarized cycle with D>=3. Its complete sections have independent node values plus the interior monomials on each component. For a cycle with at least three components this embeds each component as its rational normal curve in its endpoint/interior coordinate space; different spaces meet only at their specified endpoint, and their tangent directions there are distinct. For two components the same statement holds: if one has degree one, the other has degree at least two, and its rational normal curve meets the common endpoint line only at the two endpoints. For one nodal component, the normalization map is explicitly

    [s:t] -> [s^D+lambda t^D, s^(D-1)t, ..., s t^(D-1)],

with lambda!=0. Interior monomial ratios recover the parameter off the endpoints, and the two endpoint tangent directions are distinct because D>=3. It identifies exactly the prescribed endpoints, giving the nodal closed immersion.

These descriptions also prove relative projectivity of the pushout used above. The positive-degree glued line has a sufficiently high power separating points and length-two subschemes uniformly by the normalization evaluation calculation. Its finite free relative section module yields a fibrewise closed immersion; properness and the finite/Nakayama closed-immersion criterion make it an actual relative closed immersion. For L itself the explicit fibrewise description and base change give the same conclusion after restricting to the trait. Thus the construction is not an assumed projective pinching with no ample line.

#### 3. Relative section ring and degree-one generation

Let V=H0(L_family), free of rank D, and let R be the relative section ring. Every R_m is free with rank Dm for m>0. On the special ordinary D-gon, its degree-one coordinate ring is the Stanley-Reisner ring

    A_D=C[z1,...,zD]/I_cycle,

where a monomial survives precisely when its support is a face of the D-cycle. For D=3 the sole minimal nonface is the triple; for D>=4 the generators are the nonedge quadratics. In degree m>0 its monomial basis consists of D vertex powers and D(m-1) edge-interior monomials, exactly Dm. They give the full normalization-compatible section basis. Hence Sym^m V -> R_m is surjective modulo epsilon for every m. Its cokernel is a finite B-module; Nakayama makes it zero. Thus R is generated in degree one and is flat with the stated special and generic fibres. In particular A(C,L) is degree-one generated.

Set S_B=Sym_B V and I=ker(S_B->R). Each I_m is free over B and commutes with base change. For D>=4 the special ideal is generated by quadratics. The degreewise cokernel of S_B I_2->I has zero special fibre and is a finite B-module in every degree; Nakayama gives zero in each degree. Therefore the RELATIVE ideal, and hence the generic ideal, is generated by quadratics. This is a degreewise argument; it does not incorrectly assume epsilon lies in the Jacobson radical of the whole ungraded polynomial ring.

#### 4. Elementary Koszul computation for the ordinary D-gon

We compute its multigraded Koszul homology directly. In multidegree a with a_j>=2 for some j, insertion of the j-th wedge basis vector followed by division by z_j gives a contracting homotopy. The surviving-monomial condition is unchanged, since z_j remains in the support whether the wedge contains j or not. Thus all such nonsquarefree multidegrees are acyclic.

In squarefree multidegree W, a subset of the D vertices, the basis terms are indexed by I subset W for which W minus I is a face. The alternating Koszul differential is, after complementing I in W, the augmented simplicial cochain differential of the induced subgraph on W, with its degree reversed. Consequently the degree-(i,W) Koszul homology is the reduced cohomology in degree |W|-i-1 of that induced subgraph. This is a direct basis identification, not an unproved use of a general Stanley-Reisner formula.

A PROPER induced subgraph of a cycle is a forest of paths and isolated vertices, so its only possible reduced cohomology is degree zero. The FULL vertex set has one-dimensional reduced degree-one cohomology. The empty-set term gives beta_0,0=1. Therefore the only other Betti positions are j=i+1 for proper W, and the final (i,j)=(D-2,D), of dimension one. There is no homology for i>D-2. This proves the asserted pure shape for the special ring. Its Hilbert series follows from the degree count in section 3.

#### 5. Transfer of the pure resolution to every polarized cycle

Take the finite degree-j part of the relative Koszul complex R tensor exterior V. It is a bounded complex of finite FREE B-modules. If its special degree-i homology vanishes, the universal-coefficient exact sequence injects H_i/epsilon H_i into that zero vector space. Nakayama gives H_i=0, so the generic homology vanishes as well. Apply this separately in each degree. Every off-pure Betti position vanishes generically, including i>D-2. There is no assertion of uniform semicontinuity for an unspecified infinite complex.

The generic Hilbert series is the same as the special one. Thus the remaining Betti multiplicities are forced by

    sum (-1)^i beta_i,j z^j
       =(1+(D-2)z+z²)(1-z)^(D-2).

In particular the last coefficient forces beta_(D-2),D=1; there is no cancellation with any other permitted position. For 1<=i<=D-3 it gives

    beta_i,i+1 = [i(D-2-i)/(D-1)] binomial(D,i+1).

The finite graded free resolution has length D-2 in a polynomial ring of dimension D, while the ring dimension is two; hence it is Cohen-Macaulay (equivalently apply the elementary depth formula for a finite free resolution over a regular ring). Its last rank one gives type one. More explicitly, dualizing the pure resolution against S(-D) yields a cyclic canonical module with its generator in degree zero; the palindromic Hilbert numerator gives its Hilbert series equal to that of A. The natural surjection A onto that canonical module is therefore an isomorphism. This proves the stated Gorenstein property and a-invariant zero. These conclusions descend across the finite field extension in section 1.

#### 6. Negative weight minus two: explicit special syzygies

Assume D>=5. Let g_ij=z_i z_j for a nonedge {i,j}; these generate I_cycle. A degree-minus-two homomorphism I_cycle->A_D sends each g_ij to a scalar c_ij. Every vertex i has at least TWO nonneighbours. Given nonedge {i,j}, choose a different nonneighbour k of i. The ACTUAL linear syzygy

    z_k g_ij-z_j g_ik=0

forces c_ij z_k-c_ik z_j=0 in (A_D)_1. These distinct coordinate vectors are independent. Hence c_ij=c_ik=0. Applying this to every nonedge proves Hom(I_cycle,A_D)_(-2)=0. It follows in particular that T1(A_D)_(-2)=0. For r>=3 every quadratic generator would map to degree 2-r<0, so Hom and T1 vanish at those weights too.

The restriction D>=5 is ESSENTIAL. For D=4 each vertex has only one nonneighbour, the two quadrics form a complete intersection, and degree-minus-two deformations need not vanish. For D=3 the generator is cubic. These are exactly the separate small-degree algebra cases; they must not be swept into the high-degree statement.

#### 7. Transfer of the minus-two vanishing by ONE finite matrix

For the relative quadratic ideal set

    K3=ker(V tensor I_2 -> I_3).

The map is onto by quadratic generation, and all its modules are finite free over B. Hence K3 is finite free and commutes with base change. There is a natural finite-matrix map

    Phi: Hom_B(I_2,B) -> Hom_B(K3,R_1),

sending proposed constant values on quadratic generators to the values of their linear syzygies. Its SPECIAL fibre is injective by section 6. A full-rank minor is therefore a unit, so its GENERIC fibre is injective as well. Every degree-minus-two homomorphism I_generic->A_generic is in this kernel, hence is zero. For r>=3 quadratic generation again gives zero directly.

This proves T1(A(C,L))_(-r)=0 for all r>=2 and D>=5, with an explicit finite linear algebra argument. It does not replace cotangent cohomology by Tor semicontinuity or assume that arbitrary singular deformation functors are represented by the same matrix: only the stated Hom-space is bounded by this injective linear-syzygy map.

#### Exact use and limit

The actual positively polarized nodal cycle, its degree-one section ring, pure Gorenstein resolution and negative graded T1 vanishing are now proved. A consumer may apply them to a central associated graded ring only AFTER constructing an exact filtered toric-face identification gr A=R(C,L). Passing from these graded facts to an actual PBW/filtered-deformation presentation, saturation of a higher-valence component blowup, and a generic-fibre-preserving family fan lift requires its own argument. No such lift is asserted by the present graded calculation.

### Inline construction 42

#### Actual PBW for a nonhomogeneous toric-conductor cycle

Research construction. This supplies the exact filtered consumer join between the central Hilbert-basis computation, the polarized-curve graded calculation, and the actual point-blowup carrier. It does NOT infer initial-ideal strictness from flatness alone.

Let A0 be an actual complete local toric conductor pushout of the specified cyclic ray type. Assume its strict tangent-cone calculation has given

    gr_m0 A0 = R(C,L),

where C is a connected nodal rational genus-one cycle, L has positive integral component degrees, and total degree D>=5. This is the conclusion of inline construction 61 at its exact conductor-equalizer scope. Let A be a flat actual trait deformation over C[[t]] with A/tA=A0 and smooth generic fibre. Put n=D and c=D-2.

The exact graded input from inline construction 41 is: R(C,L) has a pure Gorenstein minimal resolution with shifts

    0,2,3,...,D-2,D;

its quadrics generate its ideal; and the constant-vector map from their degree-minus-two perturbations to the degree-one images of linear syzygies is INJECTIVE. The latter is the precise finite-matrix meaning of T1_-2=0. It is this injective map, not an unverified generic deformation slogan, that is used below.

#### Strict filtered central resolution

Choose actual lifts of the degree-one coordinate generators and of the initial quadratic relations of R(C,L). They form a standard basis: subtracting their lifted initial combinations raises the order of any relation, and completeness plus closedness of a finitely generated ideal gives equality. This follows also directly from the established equality gr A0=R(C,L).

Lift the graded free resolution to a strict filtered resolution of A0, inductively. Suppose its preceding differentials are already lifted with their specified shifts. For each chosen initial syzygy, an arbitrary lift has image of strictly higher order. The leading term of that error is a cycle for the preceding initial differential; exactness of the graded resolution expresses it as an initial boundary. Subtract a higher-order correction to the proposed lift. Its error order increases. Iterate; the corrections converge in the complete free module and give an exact actual syzygy with the prescribed leading term. All finite generating sets lift this way. Leading-cycle subtraction also proves exactness and strictness of the lifted module maps. At the last module any further kernel has zero associated graded and hence is zero. Every shift jump is positive, so every differential lies in the maximal ideal; this is a MINIMAL resolution. Thus no hidden cancellation changes its free ranks or required orders. This is the explicit filtered lifting, not an invocation of a homogeneous isomorphism of A0.

The central resolution ends in rank one and is Cohen–Macaulay, so A0 is Gorenstein. The same canonical-module lifting across the regular element t shows that A is Gorenstein. Choose its minimal ambient presentation S=C[[z1,...,zD,t]], with relation ideal I. Tensor its minimal free resolution with S/(t). This remains exact, since t is A-regular. Its reduction is the just-constructed central minimal resolution, and bases can be chosen lifting its strict filtered bases.

#### The first and last required orders

Take relation generators Fi lifting the central quadratic standard basis. They have form Fi=fi(z)+t hi(z,t). A first-order constant coefficient of hi would give a constant-vector perturbation of the initial quadrics. Lift each actual central syzygy across the flat deformation and extract the coefficient of t in SPATIAL degree one. Terms from higher spatial orders and from syzygy corrections multiplying central relations have spatial degree at least two; hence this extraction is exactly the graded linear-syzygy matrix applied to that constant vector. Its injectivity forces every constant coefficient to be zero. Consequently every Fi has total order at least two.

Gorenstein duality identifies the last free differential, up to invertible matrices and a unit, with the transpose of the first differential. Its entries therefore generate I and have total order at least two as well. Each middle map has total order at least one by minimality. These are precisely the orders required by the pure shifts: two at the first and last maps, one at every other map. Taking leading forms produces an actual homogeneous free complex L over C[z,t]. Its reduction modulo t is the exact graded resolution of R(C,L), because the chosen central lifts were strict filtered lifts.

#### Exact initial complex and exact initial ideal

A homogeneous cycle of L reduces modulo t to a boundary. Subtract its lifted boundary; the remainder is divisible by t and its quotient is again a cycle. Repeat. Total degree decreases, so this procedure terminates after finitely many divisions. Thus L is exact in positive degrees. The same argument applied to tv=d w proves that the degree-zero cokernel B is t-torsion-free: reduce w, subtract its lifted boundary and divide. Hence

    B/(t)=R(C,L), t regular on B.

Finally prove that this B is the FULL associated graded of A. Express an actual relation as a combination of the Fi. If its lowest coefficient contribution cancels, that initial coefficient vector is a cycle of L. Subtract the corresponding actual lifted boundary in the free resolution; this raises the lowest coefficient order without changing the represented relation. Repeat only until the desired finite leading degree is reached. Every actual leading relation is therefore generated by the initial Fi. Completeness handles its tail, and faithful flatness of completion transfers the equality to the actual finite local presentation. Thus

    gr_m A=B,
    (gr_m A)/(t)=R(C,L),
    t regular on gr_m A.

This proves the actual PBW assertion for the nonhomogeneous central toric quotient pushout. It is not a mere Hilbert-series comparison.

The same resolution gives gr_m A Cohen–Macaulay and Gorenstein with a-invariant -1. It is reduced: a nilpotent reduces to zero in the reduced quotient R(C,L), so is divisible by t; t-regularity makes its quotient nilpotent, and descending homogeneous degree gives zero. Its Hilbert series is

    (1+(D-2)u+u²)/(1-u)³.

The actual maximal-ideal blowup now has exactly the strict Rees charts and canonical-module cancellation established in inline construction 40. Its exceptional hyperplane is the ACTUAL polarized nodal cycle C, and its quotient-Jacobian exponent decreases by D-2 on a continuing t-chart. There is no need to assert a lift of a normalization fan or to take an unspecified spatial cover.

The input cyclic conductor equalizer, strict central tangent cone, and graded polarized-cycle matrices must be bound to their actual carriers. Degree D=3 or4 is not covered by the constant-perturbation injectivity premise; their hypersurface/general-two-quadric families require the exact low-degree constructions. No production installation, source acceptance, or full-theorem readiness is asserted.

### Inline construction 43

#### Projective rational-curve closure and an actual weak-degree-one (-1)-curve

Research prerequisite. Let R be a complete excellent DVR, K its fraction field. Let T/R be a smooth projective family of surfaces, with central H=-K_T nef and big, H²=1. Assume the generic surface is a smooth degree-one del Pezzo surface. The generic exceptional-curve existence was constructed in `inline construction 14`. After a finite trait extension choose such an integral generic curve E_K=P¹ with E_K²=-1. The following argument gives an ACTUAL smooth rational (-1)-curve on T0, not merely rationality of T0.

#### Complete local proof of connected special support

Let E be the schematic closure of E_K in T. The total T is regular. Its prime Weil divisor E is Cartier, integral and flat over R. It is projective. We prove that E0 is connected, including its potentially nonreduced structure, without importing an unproved Stein-factorization or formal-functions item.

Embed E in P^N_R. A finite graded S=R[x0,...,xN] module represents O_E. It has a finite graded free resolution: construct a minimal graded resolution using homogeneous generators modulo (s,x0,...,xN). The regular sequence (s,x0,...,xN) has a Koszul resolution of the residue field of length N+2 (exactness follows inductively from the non-zero-divisor property). Therefore Tor above N+2 vanishes. In the minimal resolution all differentials vanish after tensor with that residue field, so graded Nakayama forces the higher free modules to vanish. This proves the required finite resolution, not a hidden use of formal existence.

Sheafify and compute on projective space. Each O(k) has the usual monomial Cech computation: only degrees zero and N can contribute, and their nonzero cohomology groups are finite free over R with monomial bases; the same calculation holds after arbitrary R-base change. Applying this finite computation to the finite free resolution produces a bounded finite-free R-complex P computing RΓ(E,O_E). Because O_E is R-flat, the same derived construction after tensor with R/s^n computes RΓ(E_n,O_E_n).

For such a complex over the complete DVR,

    H⁰(P) = lim_n H⁰(P tensor R/s^n).

Here is an elementary verification: use the smallest-valuation nonzero differential entry to split off a two-term summand R --s^a--> R; the relations d²=0 eliminate the adjacent entries. Inductively the bounded complex is a direct sum of these summands and single free modules. The asserted inverse-limit equality is immediate for each summand, and hence for P.

If E0 were disconnected it would have a nontrivial idempotent. Idempotents lift uniquely through a nilpotent ideal; applying this on affine opens and using uniqueness to glue gives compatible idempotents of every O_E_n. The preceding equality gives an actual idempotent of H⁰(E,O_E). But E is integral and its generic fiber is P¹, so this ring embeds into H⁰(E_K,O)=K. It is a finite R-module by the same finite complex calculation. It is consequently integral over R; since R is integrally closed in K and contains the constants R, it equals R. It has no nontrivial idempotent. This contradiction proves connectedness of E0.

#### The actual (-1)-component

As a Cartier divisor E0 has H-degree one and square -1, by constancy of intersection numbers in the flat projective family. Decompose its effective cycle as C+Z, where H.C=1 and every component of Z has H-degree zero. Ampleness of the generic anticanonical polarization is not assumed on the central fiber. The coefficient of C is one, and it is the only positive-degree component.

Hodge gives C²≤(H.C)²/H²=1. Adjunction on the smooth central surface gives C²=2pa(C)-1≥-1, an odd integer. Thus C²=-1 or C²=1. In the latter case equality in Hodge forces C numerically equivalent to H. Hence C.Z=0, and, since distinct effective curves on a smooth surface have nonnegative local intersections, C is disjoint from the support of Z. Connectedness excludes Z≠0; Z=0 would contradict E0²=-1. Therefore C²=-1 and pa(C)=0. The integral curve C is smooth rational (normalization has genus zero and the nonnegative singularity-length correction must vanish). It is an actual contractible (-1)-curve.

The contraction is projective and preserves nefness and bigness of the anticanonical class: for every remaining curve A, the new anticanonical intersection equals H.A+C.A≥0, and its square becomes two. This proves existence of the first actual blowdown needed for a weak degree-one toric presentation.

#### Continuation boundary

After this contraction, a degree-two anticanonical double-plane model and its smooth deformation can be constructed by the separate degree-two linear-system supplier. For any degree-two ADE resolution with such a deformation, the same generic-curve closure has central positive-degree component C with H.C=1; Hodge now gives C²≤1/2, so adjunction forces C²=-1 without the connectedness argument. A second contraction reaches degree three; `inline construction 66` then constructs the actual rational pencil and finite vertical (-1)-contractions to F_n.

This file proves connectedness and the degree-one (-1)-curve. It does NOT claim that the degree-two anticanonical double-plane construction is automatic from terminology alone. Its precise linear-system supplier must be bound in a full toric-presentation assembly. It also does not resolve a nonnormal surrounding family merely from its central normalization.

#### Projective anticanonical specialization bypass: no smooth resolution family required

For the NORMAL weighted degree-one exceptional surfaces in this packet there is a shorter stronger route, which avoids the smooth-projective-resolution-family hypothesis of the first construction. Let Y be the actual normal weighted sextic with ADE singularities, H=-K_Y ample, H²=1, and q:S→Y its actual minimal resolution. Perturb its coefficients in the original PROJECTIVE weighted-sextic family, choose a generic exceptional curve by the explicit finite étale existence proof, and take its flat projective Hilbert closure E over a finite trait extension. Its central fundamental cycle is one prime A of H-degree one. Let C be its strict transform on S. Hodge and adjunction again give C²=-1 or +1.

Suppose C²=+1. Equality in Hodge gives C numerically equivalent to q*H. Every exceptional curve Z has H.Z=0, so C.Z=0; nonnegative local intersections show C is disjoint from the entire exceptional locus. Hence A avoids every singular point of Y. The Hilbert closure E is integral. Its special support has no isolated component: in its pure two-dimensional integral total space a minimal prime over the nonzero trait parameter has height one, and the excellent dimension formula gives a curve. Thus its entire special support is A and avoids the singular points. Near that support the ambient family is regular, since the central Y is smooth there. The prime divisor E is therefore Cartier on a neighborhood of its entire proper special support. Its special fiber has no embedded point and equals A scheme-theoretically. Constancy of the degree of its normal line bundle in this proper flat curve family gives A²=-1, contradicting C²=+1.

Consequently C²=-1 and pa(C)=0, yielding an ACTUAL smooth rational (-1)-curve on S. This proof tolerates all possible embedded points in a Hilbert limit until the support argument excludes them in the single hypothetical +1 case. It uses no nonschematic-target Hilbert theorem, no projectivity of a simultaneous resolution, and no unproved connectedness theorem.

After contracting C, the reduced anticanonical rational cycle remains reduced and nodal: an interior (-1)-curve meets it once at a smooth point, while a boundary (-1)-component performs an ordinary corner contraction. H¹(O)=0 persists. Its anticanonical square is two. Therefore the EXACT input of `inline construction 67` holds. That file constructs the anticanonical double plane and uses its PROJECTIVE Hilbert specialization plus Hodge to find the second (-1)-curve. This reaches degree three and closes the complete normal degree-one toric-presentation interface.

#### 2. Three elementary log contractions

Take an actual basic DF enhancement on C_W, with all the target/base coupons. The target-generator line has degree zero on every component contracted by q_i because its Q-image is a point. A contracted connected tree is assembled from the following elementary moves, as in the actual genus-zero ordinary stabilization graph construction.

**Unmarked tail.** It has one attaching node and no markings. The tree-cut degree formula gives slope zero in every target-generator coordinate. Its height equals the adjacent height. Contracting removes its unused node symbol; the surviving target ghost and its line/section pull back to the old ghost line/section.

**Unmarked bridge.** With two attaching nodes, the degree-zero formula makes the two signed slopes opposite with one common oriented vector c. If their smoothing symbols are rho_1,rho_2, then

    e_mid-e_left=c rho_1,
    e_right-e_mid=c rho_2,
    e_right-e_left=c(rho_1+rho_2).

The new node has smoothing symbol `rho_new=rho_1+rho_2`. The actual ordinary contraction plumbing identifies its smoothing line with the tensor product of the two old smoothing lines; its parameter and unit are their product, including their actual transition units. These are equalities in the universal contraction family and hence remain true after nilpotent base change. The target ghost line at this new node pulls back to the original lines on the two branches and bridge, with the same signed slope c.

**Once-marked tail.** It has one marking of vector contact a and one attaching node. The node slope is a and

    e_tail=e_adjacent+a rho.

The ordinary contraction transfers that marking to the attachment. In its universal marking chart the new marking parameter pulls back to a unit times the old marking parameter times the node-smoothing parameter. In the characteristic this says

    z_new -> z_old+rho.

Thus `e_adjacent+a z_new` pulls back to `e_tail+a z_old`. It is the old target ghost, including the actual unit of that plumbing identity. This proves the line/section pullback identity, rather than identifying only the geometric contact orders.

These three cases exhaust the unstable rational components after composition into Z_i. A component with two markings and a node has three special points and is not contracted. Collapse of the entire curve cannot occur because the total Z_i-degree is positive.
