#### 4. Cartier specialization and zero-section commute with proper support

Let i denote the regular embedding of the closed point into S. Use its refined base Gysin on H_beta even if the pulled-back parameter is a zero divisor on H_beta. Define

    V^h_0 = i^! V^h in A_d(H_beta,0)_Q.                     (3)

The zero-section/Cartier identity proved in the universal-cone packet gives

    V^h_0 = 0_(B_H,0)^! i^![C^h].                          (4)

One can check this directly in each common strict model: bundle pullback is bijective; refined Cartier Gysin commutes with this flat pullback; therefore it commutes with its inverse zero-section Gysin. For the vector-bundle-stack quotient, perform both constructions on the same full-rank approximation and affine torsor, use the scheme identity, and descend through the same injective comparisons. This is a global quotient comparison, not etale-local equality of final Chow classes.

Every construction in (1)–(4) has base H_beta or its proper special fiber. Rees blowups and rational-function graph closures used for the comparison remain over that base. In particular (4) is an identity in A(H_beta,0), and retains proper support throughout.

#### 5. Numerical specialization

Let a be the product of the actual evaluation pullbacks, cotangent-line classes and other operational insertions, of total codimension d. Pull them from the original mapping stack to H_beta; the domain/evaluation data here are those of its projection to M, not the domain of the possibly unstabilized Z-map. Operational compatibility gives

    i^!(a cap V^h) = a_0 cap V^h_0.

Push `a cap V^h`, a one-dimensional class on the proper S-stack H_beta, to S. Its pushforward is `n[S]`: its horizontal degree is n, and vertical one-cycles push to zero because their image has smaller dimension. Restricting to eta and using (2) identifies n with

    degree( a_eta cap [M_eta,beta]^vir ).

Proper/Cartier compatibility now gives

    degree( a_0 cap V^h_0 )
       = n
       = degree( a_eta cap [M_eta,beta]^vir ).               (5)

Thus numerical specialization has been proved on the proper support. No operation discards vertical components of the ambient intrinsic cone by an equality in a nonproper Chow group. Rather, the specialized cycle here is explicitly the specialization of the original generic cone, and its degree is computed on the proper stack carrying it.

The same proof covers point constraints as actual refined incidence operations. Choose the target point section through the relative smooth target locus (or through the relative smooth boundary locus for a boundary evaluation). Its regular embedding has codimension two in the surface family, or codimension one in the boundary family. Base-change that embedding along the actual evaluation and apply its refined Gysin, without assuming evaluation is flat. The incidence base is a closed substack of the proper H_beta and hence is proper. Cartier commutation and proper pushforward in the same global quotient construction give (5) on that incidence base. This formulation does not require every generic closed point to represent the same class in the Chow group of X, or a point class on the entire nonprojective total target to have been independently identified with an operational class. Cotangent-line powers are the actual operational Chern classes on the projection to M, and commute with these refined incidence operations. Every intermediate base stays a closed incidence space in the same proper support.

#### 6. Independence of the chosen proper quotient support

Suppose H_1 and H_2 are two such proper quasi-projective quotient supports, each generically isomorphic to M_eta,beta, with the original generic cone closed in their pulled-back bundle stacks. Form

    H_12 = H_1 times_M H_2

and take the scheme-theoretic closure of its common generic beta locus. Because the diagonal of M is finite, H_12 is finite over `H_1 times_S H_2`. A product of the supplied frame atlases is quasi-projective, and its finite pullback is quasi-projective; hence H_12 again has the required global quotient construction. It is proper over S. Both projections are proper and generically isomorphisms.

Close the same original generic cone in the pulled-back bundle stack on H_12. The induced proper map from that bundle stack to each bundle stack on H_i pushes the horizontal cone cycle to its horizontal cone cycle with degree one. Indeed this is an isomorphism on every generic cone component; closure adds no purely vertical irreducible component to this particular cycle. Multiplicities are the original generic multiplicities. Thus proper compatibility of zero-section Gysin gives

    p_i,* V^h_12 = V^h_i,
    p_i,0,* V^h_12,0 = V^h_i,0.                            (6)

These are proper-pushforward equalities on proper stacks. They identify the two supported specializations before passing to Chow of M. In particular their degrees and all proper operational insertions agree. The same argument compares different auxiliary frame/torsor strictifications via their common quotient comparisons. This supplies the support-preserving global descent that etale equality alone could not supply.

#### 7. Exact application boundary

The proper-support numerical step (1)–(6) is complete given the typed inputs of Section 1. The remaining inputs for application to the original non-Fano theorem are:

1. Construct the projective dominating Z->Y that is an isomorphism on the entire generic pair. A projective blowup over Y or an arbitrary Chow modification is not this assertion.
2. Identify the actual special horizontal cone operation (4), with its primitive smoothing orders, with the claimed finite toric vertex/gluing contributions. This requires exhaustiveness or vanishing of every other supported constrained type.
3. Retain the original outer boundary, contact distribution, markings, psi classes and coefficient class in that identification. The auxiliary model's polarization and possibly exceptional source components are support machinery only.

This proof does not certify the full special-fiber intrinsic cone of M, or put a POT on H_beta. It proves the invariant of the original generic projective pair equals the proper-supported specialization of its original cone. The global tropical comparison must compute exactly that supported specialization.

### Inline construction 46

#### Toric model of a rational anticanonical cycle from an explicit blowdown presentation

Research construction for possible corner-exceptional components. This reconstructs the elementary toric-model step and its boundary behavior. It assumes an ACTUAL finite blowdown presentation to P^2 or a Hirzebruch surface, which must be supplied for the component under consideration. It does not relabel the general rational-surface minimal-model theorem as proved by this packet.

#### Precise input and output

Let Y be a smooth projective rational surface with a reduced nodal anticanonical cycle C of rational curves (a single irreducible nodal rational curve is allowed). Supply a finite sequence of actual contractions of smooth (-1)-curves from Y to P^2 or F_n. Then after finitely many CORNER blowups of C there is an actual morphism to a smooth toric surface which is a sequence of INTERIOR boundary-point blowups in reverse. Consequently its relative vertex theory is computed by the already constructed toric/boundary-blowup formulas. No Fano or nef condition is imposed on Y or C.

#### 1. Every supplied contraction preserves the cycle in a controlled way

For a smooth (-1)-curve E not a component of C, adjunction gives K_Y.E=-1 and hence C.E=1. All intersections are nonnegative. Thus E meets C transversely at exactly one smooth point; it cannot pass through a node, where the total intersection would be at least two. Its contraction is the inverse of an interior boundary-point blowup. The image cycle is still anticanonical and nodal.

If E is a component of C, it meets its two neighbors at two distinct points, counted with the usual two edges when the same neighboring component occurs twice. Contracting E is a corner blowdown: two distinct branches are identified in the target, with distinct tangent directions given by the two distinct points on E. The resulting cycle remains nodal and anticanonical. A single nodal boundary component is not a smooth (-1)-curve and is never contracted by this clause.

These conclusions follow from the actual point-blowup canonical formula and intersection numbers. Thus the supplied blowdown presentation transports (Y,C) to a nodal rational anticanonical cycle on the minimal presentation surface, using only interior and corner operations.

#### 2. Ruled surface with two boundary sections

Suppose a ruled surface has boundary two smooth sections S_1,S_2 and r distinct fibers, forming a nodal cycle. Put k=S_1.S_2. Each section has valence two in the cycle, so k+r=2. At each section-intersection point, blow up that node (a corner blowup). The strict ruling fiber through it has self-intersection -1, is not a boundary component (SNC forbids that fiber from already being a third component at the node), and has total boundary intersection one. Contract that fiber as an interior blowdown.

This elementary transformation separates that pair of section branches and replaces their former intersection by a boundary fiber (the image of the new exceptional curve). Repeat at the finitely many section intersections. The result has two disjoint sections and exactly two boundary fibers. Two disjoint sections split the underlying rank-two bundle up to a line twist; choose base coordinate putting the two fibers at 0 and infinity and fiber coordinate putting the sections at 0 and infinity. These choices exhibit the actual standard toric action and full toric boundary. Thus this is a toric model obtained by corner blowups followed by interior blowdowns.

#### 3. P^2 cycles

A rational nodal anticanonical cycle on P^2 is one of: three lines in a triangle; a line and smooth conic meeting transversely twice; an irreducible nodal cubic. These are the possibilities by total degree three and rationality; a cuspidal cubic is excluded by the nodal hypothesis.

The triangle is already the full toric boundary. For line plus conic, blow up one node. Projection from that point identifies the new surface with F_1. The exceptional curve and the strict conic are two sections; the strict line is one boundary fiber, and the two sections meet once. Apply §2 once to get two disjoint sections and two boundary fibers, hence a toric surface.

For a nodal cubic, blow up its node. The result is F_1, with exceptional section and strict cubic section meeting at two distinct points. (The cubic class is 3H-2E and the ruling is H-E, so its ruling degree is one.) There is no boundary fiber. Apply §2 at these two section intersections. This gives the required toric model. All centers are actual boundary nodes and all contracted fibers are actual nonboundary (-1)-curves, so the construction is algebraic, not a birational picture alone.

#### 4. Remaining Hirzebruch cycles

Write F_n with negative section S, S^2=-n, and ruling fiber F. Then -K=2S+(n+2)F, so C.F=2. If the horizontal boundary has two components, each has ruling degree one and is a section. The other components are fibers, and §2 applies.

If it has one component B of ruling degree two, the cycle condition gives either:

(a) B is smooth rational and there is exactly one boundary fiber; or
(b) B is an irreducible nodal rational curve and there is no boundary fiber.

In (a), B=2S+(n+1)F, so B.S=1-n. Irreducibility and B!=S imply n<=1. For n=0, B has bidegree (2,1); switch the two rulings. B and the former boundary fiber become two sections meeting twice, covered by §2. For n=1, the negative section S is not in the boundary, B.S=0, and C.S=1. Contract S as an interior (-1)-curve. The resulting P^2 boundary is line plus conic, already handled.

In (b), B=2S+(n+2)F and B.S=2-n, so n<=2. For n=1 contract the negative section as an interior (-1)-curve; this gives the P^2 nodal cubic case. For n=0, blow up the boundary node. The two ruling fibers through that node have strict transforms of self-intersection -1. They are disjoint after the blowup and disjoint from the strict B: their original total intersection with B was two, fully accounted for by the two node branches. They each meet the new boundary exceptional curve once. Contract both as interior curves. The output is P^2 with the strict B a conic (self-intersection four) and the exceptional image a line (self-intersection one). Apply §3.

For n=2, blow up the node and contract the strict ruling fiber through it. This is an interior elementary transformation: the old negative section, disjoint from the node, now has self-intersection -1. Contract it as another interior curve. The image surface is P^2 and its nodal anticanonical cycle is covered by §3. These degree/intersection computations verify that every claimed contraction is the inverse of an allowed boundary-point blowup.

This exhausts the cycles on the supplied P^2/F_n presentation. No classification of arbitrary smooth rational surfaces was used beyond the explicitly supplied blowdown sequence.

#### 5. Lift the minimal presentation model back to Y

Corner blowups commute past an interior boundary blowup: the former center is a boundary node and is disjoint from the exceptional curve's unique boundary intersection, which is smooth. For two sequences of corner blowups take their common toric subdivision refinement at each node; its actual point blowups give a common corner refinement. These are finite two-dimensional fan refinements, not an inferred equality of cycles.

Use these commuting diagrams inductively through the supplied blowdown sequence. They produce a surface Y' obtained by corner blowups from Y, whose map to a corner refinement of the presentation model is a sequence of interior blowups. The latter presentation model has the toric model constructed in §§2-4. Corner refinements also commute past its interior blowdowns, since their centers are disjoint from the contracted curve's unique smooth boundary intersection. A corner refinement of a toric surface is toric. Thus Y' maps to a smooth toric surface by interior blowdowns, as claimed.

The actual relative theory is invariant under the corner/log modifications by the existing proper monoid-refinement cone operation, with its normal units and curve classes retained. Interior blowups are evaluated by the actual normal-cone/exceptional-cover formula. Therefore this construction supplies the needed standard local relative model for EVERY corner-exceptional component for which the required rational blowdown presentation is genuinely constructed.

#### Remaining interface

For a cubic exceptional component with an anticanonical triangle, an explicit contraction of six disjoint lines to P^2 would supply the input, with two centers on each toric boundary line. Other exceptional components require their own actual presentation. This packet does not assume all components of an arbitrary broad corner resolution are rational, nor invoke a general surface MMP theorem without reconstructing it. Those are the precise geometry obligations of the corner owner.

### Inline construction 47

#### Rational Gorenstein central points: explicit ADE forms and actual surrounding families

Research join, using TWO exact published root suppliers read in full: `thm-rational-gorenstein-normal-surface-singularity-resolved-by-point-blowups` and `lem-square-tangent-conic-blowup-singularities-controlled-by-a-cubic`. This fills the central-form identification needed after the Hodge/Leray argument for normal PBW exceptional components; it does not invoke ADE classification as an external theorem.

A nonregular rational Gorenstein completed complex surface ring has Hilbert function 2n+1 and three minimal generators by the published tangent-conic supplier. Therefore it is an actual/formal hypersurface with a nonzero quadratic initial form. Rank three gives A1. Rank two gives A-type by splitting its nondegenerate pair and taking the finite order of the remaining one-variable function; isolation follows from normality. A unit root makes its coefficient one.

For rank one, complete the square to x²+h(y,z), where h has order at least three. Its binary cubic h3 is NONZERO: the published square-conic lemma constructs precisely this nonzero cubic on the reduced exceptional line. If it has three distinct roots, weighted Jacobian division gives D4. If it has a double root and a distinct transverse root, split the transverse smooth branch by the actual/formal simple-root lemma, use it as coordinate z, and complete the other quadratic. The residual isolated one-variable term has finite order, giving z(y²+z^(n-2)), hence D_n. All unit removals and higher-weight eliminations are finite-contact algebraized below.

The only remaining case is a triple root; choose h3=y³. Formal Weierstrass preparation gives, up to a unit and a translation of y,

    x²+y³+A(z)y+C(z).

For completeness this preparation needs no source waiver: after removing the y-only unit, work successively modulo z^r. At each order divide the error in C[[y]] by y³; absorb the quotient into the unit and keep the three residues as the degree-two remainder. Completeness gives a monic cubic, and the y² coefficient is removed by translation in characteristic zero. The leading triple-cubic condition forces ord A≥3 and ord C≥4.

RATIONALITY excludes ord A≥4,ord C≥6. Indeed blow up the original singular point and take the z-chart x=zX,y=zY. The strict equation is

    X²+zY³+(A(z)/z)Y+C(z)/z².

If those two inequalities held, this successor would have square tangent X² and ZERO binary cubic in (Y,z). The published rational Gorenstein point-blowup theorem says that this ordinary blowup is normal, rational and Gorenstein at every successor. The published square-conic cubic lemma says its binary cubic is nonzero. This is a direct contradiction. No elliptic-cone classification or unresolved nonminimal Weierstrass assertion is being used.

The remaining cases are consequently exact:

* ord C=4: E6, weighted leading x²+y³+c z⁴.
* ord A=3,ord C≥5: E7, weighted leading x²+y³+a y z³.
* ord A≥4,ord C=5: E8, weighted leading x²+y³+c z⁵.

In each case the weighted Jacobian quotient has maximal weight below the equation degree (E6 residues 1,y,z,yz,z²,yz² after matching variables; E7 seven residues; E8 eight). Thus every higher-weight term is divided explicitly by the Jacobian and removed in successively increasing weight. This gives a formal contact equivalence to the displayed polynomial central form. The central Jacobian is m-primary. Truncate the equivalence until the actual error belongs to mJ² and apply the finite implicit matrix correction Y+Q(Y)=a, whose Jacobian is the identity. This is the ACTUAL étale finite-contact construction in `inline construction 49`, not an assertion that the original formal series algebraizes.

After this actual central equivalence, extend the selected actual coordinates to the surrounding trait family, keeping its parameter. Finite t-order Jacobian division into the explicit finite ADE quotient bases and the same finite implicit correction give an ACTUAL parameter-fixed polynomial ADE family. The generic smoothness certificate t^M in its relative contact ideal supplies the finite cutoff. The actual A ordered-root, D recursion, E6 line, E7 bitangent and E8 all-power-Hilbert blowups in this packet now apply after finite trait extension. They are generic identities. This closes the NORMAL-component surrounding-family join needed by the ordinary or polarized-cycle PBW point extraction.

No claim is made here about a NONNORMAL central component or about lifting a toric monoid subdivision; those are separate conductor/pushout/PBW constructions.

### Inline construction 48

#### Reducible cross: unit/thickness resolution and finite actual contact form

Research construction. The central cross is U²−V²W², equivalently the product of two smooth components tangent along a nodal conductor. The unit/thickness branch below has an explicit finite reduction to the already constructed toric-base conic model. The later general contact form is actual but is not itself a simultaneous resolution.

#### 1. Unit/thickness branch has a toric base

Assume the actual family equation is

    U²−V²W²=t^m epsilon,       m>=1,       epsilon a unit.

Make the single finite trait extension t=s². Adjoin a specified square root q of epsilon on an actual etale chart, and rescale U'=U/q,V'=V/q, leaving W and s fixed. The change has invertible Jacobian at the corner even when q depends on all variables. Its exact equation is

    U'²−V'²W²=s^(2m).

Set X=U'−V'W,Y=U'+V'W. The actual model is the pullback of the conic family

    XY=s^(2m),       V'W=(Y−X)/2.                            (1)

Here XY=s^(2m) is a toric surface over the actual trait, and the second equation is a conic over that base. Its axis-free discriminant curve X=Y splits into exactly the two actual sections

    X=Y=s^m,       X=Y=−s^m.

In the toric minimal resolution of XY=s^(2m), these meet the interior of the middle component at the distinct nonzero parameters +1 and −1, not any corner. Thus the proved toric-base-conic construction applies: all base corners are monomial, and the only nontrivial open-component charts are the primitive higher-double models at those two sections. Every toric base-centre ideal is an actual monomial ideal in X,Y,s; its pullback is Cartier on the generic fibre. The modification is therefore vertical and gives the identity on the entire base-changed generic fibre.

This does not use a spatial double cover or a global square root of epsilon along an independent conductor curve. The single unit root is an actual etale coordinate operation on the total chart, and its multiplier is retained in descent. Component labels and the two sections are kept, including their possible later Galois permutation.

For m=1 with regular total space, there is also the direct component-intersection blowup: in the equivalent form U(U−VW)=tG with G a unit, blow I=(U,U−VW)=(U,VW). Its VW-chart U=VW b has t=V²W²b(b−1)/G, a smooth normal-crossing chart. Its U-chart VW=Ua has t=U²(1−a)/G. Near a≠0 solve U=VW/a; near the only remaining a=0 singular point, VW=Ua is the toric node and 1−a,G are units. The prescribed toric base change/subdivision resolves it. This direct calculation preserves arbitrary unit G and does not assume a unit independent of U,V,W.

#### 2. Every generically smooth cross deformation has a finite actual contact form

Let F be an actual regular function over R=C[[t]] with F modulo t=U²−V²W² and smooth generic fibre. Its relative contact Jacobian ideal contains t^M for some finite M.

The central derivative ideal is (U,VW²,V²W). Every actual regular coefficient h has the exact decomposition

    h=c(V)+d(W)+b VW+2U A−2VW² B−2V²W C,                  (2)

where d(0)=0 and b is a scalar. Subtract the U=0 restriction and divide by U. On the remaining V,W-plane, subtract its two axis restrictions, allocating the common constant to c(V). The remainder is divisible by VW. Subtract the scalar value of that quotient at V=W=0; the rest is V times a regular function plus W times one. This gives (2) by finite algebraic divisions. The pure-axis functions are actual regular functions on etale axis charts; finitely many common etale refinements lift them independently to the ambient chart.

Run only N finite t-order elimination steps using (2), retaining its three residue types. The result is an actual etale equation F_N with

    F_N=G_N+t^N H,
    G_N=U²−V²W²+c_N(V,t)+d_N(W,t)+b_N(t)VW,              (3)

where c_N,d_N are finite polynomials in t with actual pure-axis coefficient functions, b_N is a finite polynomial in t, and all vanish modulo t.

As in the A/D/pinch lemma, J_{F_N} subset J_{G_N}+(t^N). For N>M this gives t^M∈J_{G_N} by a unit rearrangement. For N>=2M+1 the error lies in m J_{G_N}². The finite implicit contact-correction matrix therefore gives an exact actual etale contact equivalence between the original family and G_N, fixing t. No isolated central critical-locus hypothesis or formal algebraization is used.

#### 3. A finite numerical bound for continuing cross charts

If c_N(0,t), its V-derivative at zero and the W-derivative of d_N at zero were all zero, the origin would remain a singular point on every generic fibre of (3). Generic smoothness therefore makes at least one of those three one-variable coefficient series nonzero, with finite t-order.

Under a continuing conductor scaling U=s^r U1,V=s^a V1,W=s^b W1, with r=a+b, a constant coefficient loses 2r in its s-order, a linear-V coefficient loses a+2b, and a linear-W coefficient loses 2a+b. Thus an infinite branch of such continuing cross scalings is impossible. This is a numerical bound on that branch only, not a proof that every other chart is already a previously resolved singularity.

In fact unrestricted alternating conductor scalings can create exceptional E-type central points. A useful exact warning example is

    F=x²−v²w²+t(v³+w⁵)+t^M,       M>16.

For generic t the origin is not on F. A nonzero critical solution satisfies v=2w²/(3t), w=45t³/8, and its critical value is C t^16+t^M with C≠0; hence the generic fibre is smooth in this local branch. After t=s² and the scaling x=s^16X,v=s^10V,w=s^6W, the central equation is X²−V²W²+V³+W⁵, an E8 type (the mixed term has larger E8 weight). Therefore a claim that arbitrary cross conductor rescalings yield only D_n or A_n points would be false. This warning explains why that proposed reduction is not used. The actual E6/E7/E8 family constructions are given in inline constructions 12–14. The complete broad nonunit-cross reduction is the one-cover six-terminal-face argument in inline construction 57, using the normal form of inline construction 7 and the actual face inventory in inline constructions 8–9 and 55–59.

#### Scope

This construction supplies the unit/thickness branch and the actual finite arbitrary cross normal form. Its formerly separate semistable geometry is supplied in this item by inline construction 57 (finite one-cover terminal faces), together with the actual face-family and E-type constructions cited there. The broad corner/family assembly is inline construction 27, and the arbitrary cyclic/outer-path preliminary reduction is inline construction 1. Thus this bounded intermediate argument is not itself the broad proof; those explicit later inline constructions discharge that obligation without narrowing the theta theorem.

### Inline construction 49

#### Actual etale algebraization of a formal isolated smoothing normal form

Research construction closing the formal-to-actual gap for the D-type normal forms. This is a finite implicit-equation proof for an isolated relative hypersurface singularity, not an invocation of general Artin approximation. The ORIGINAL trait parameter is fixed throughout.

#### 1. Precise criterion

Let R=C[[s]] (or the finite complete trait extension in use). Let A be the local ring of a smooth three-dimensional relative ambient chart over R at its special point, etale over R[x_1,x_2,x_3]. Let F in A define a family whose generic hypersurface is smooth and whose special hypersurface has its only singularity at that point. Suppose its completed germ is formally contact-equivalent, with s FIXED, to an ACTUAL polynomial family

    G in R[x_1,x_2,x_3].

The coefficients of G may be arbitrary elements of the complete trait R. A formal unit and formal invertible spatial coordinate map give the completed equivalence. Then there is an ACTUAL common etale neighborhood on which F and G are contact-equivalent with s fixed. In particular an actual generic-isomorphism resolution of G transports etale-locally to F. One does not claim the formal coordinate series themselves algebraize; a NEW actual etale equivalence is constructed.

#### 2. Relative Tjurina ideal is primary at the special point

Put J_0=G and J_i=partial G/partial x_i for i=1,2,3. Let m=(s,x_1,x_2,x_3) after translating the chosen point. On the original ambient, the ideal (F,F_x1,F_x2,F_x3) has no generic zero because the generic fiber is smooth. On the special fiber its zero set consists only of the isolated singular point. Thus its radical in the local ring is m.

A formal contact equivalence which fixes s carries this relative Tjurina ideal to (G,G_x1,G_x2,G_x3): use the ordinary chain rule and the invertible spatial Jacobian, retaining the F term to account for derivatives of the unit. Consequently J=(J_0,...,J_3) is m-primary in the completed auxiliary ring. Faithful flatness of completion contracts ideal membership, so J is m-primary in the ACTUAL auxiliary local algebra as well. Choose r with m^r subset J. This is a finite Noetherian assertion, not a bound uniform over an unbounded moduli space.

Hence

    m^(2r+1) subset m J^2.                                               (1)

The inclusion is why the generic smoothness assumption matters. For a nonnormal central singular LINE, the relative Tjurina ideal need not be m-primary and this argument cannot be used there directly.

#### 3. Replace the formal equivalence by a finite approximation

Choose N>=2r+1. Approximate the formal unit and the formal s-fixed coordinate map by finite polynomials to order m^N. Their spatial derivative stays invertible at the special point, so the approximating coordinate map is etale there. If the original smooth ambient is itself only etale over affine space, take its ACTUAL etale fiber product under that approximating polynomial map, choosing the lift of the selected special point. Etale uniqueness of nilpotent lifts makes its completed coordinate map agree with the intended formal approximation to the same order. Thus no substitution into an unconstructed algebraic power series is being assumed.

On that ACTUAL common smooth ambient we obtain an actual regular function F_0 (the original F pulled back and multiplied by the approximating unit), such that

    h=F_0-G belongs to m^N subset m J^2.                                 (2)

Membership descends from completion by faithful flatness. Write it EXACTLY as

    h=sum_(i,j=0)^3 J_i J_j a_ij,  a_ij in m.                            (3)

Only finitely many actual coefficients a_ij are involved. This is possible by (1)-(2), rather than a choice of matching formal coefficients.

#### 4. A finite implicit system removes the entire remaining error

Introduce sixteen unknowns Y_ij. Put

    d_0=sum_j J_j Y_0j,
    d_i=sum_j J_j Y_ij  for i=1,2,3.

Evaluate the POLYNOMIAL G at x+d. Its exact finite Taylor expansion gives

    (1+d_0)G(x+d)-G(x)
       = sum_(i,j) J_i J_j [Y_ij+Q_ij(Y)],                              (4)

where the Q_ij are actual polynomials in Y with coefficients in the ambient local ring, and every Q_ij has Y-degree at least two. The linear terms are d_0 G and sum_i G_xi d_i. Every higher Taylor term has at least two d factors, hence contains two of the J_j; the mixed unit term has the same property. Assign those finitely many terms to their corresponding ordered pair (i,j). This proves (4) as an EXACT polynomial identity; no division by a vanishing J_i is performed, and no independence of the products J_i J_j is required.

Now solve the finite system

    Y_ij+Q_ij(Y)=a_ij  for all i,j.                                     (5)

At the special point a_ij=0, Y=0 is a solution. The Jacobian of its left side in the sixteen Y variables is the identity there, since all Q terms are quadratic. Therefore (5), with its Jacobian determinant inverted, defines an actual etale neighborhood of the ambient base and an actual solution Y_ij vanishing at the selected special point. This is the standard finite etale implicit-function construction: it is a finitely presented algebra with invertible Jacobian, not a formal infinite iteration being declared algebraic.

Substitute (5) into (4) and use (3). It gives

    F_0=(1+d_0)G(x+d).                                                    (6)

The factor 1+d_0 is a unit. Moreover J_j in m and Y_ij in m at the selected point, so d_i belongs to m^2. The spatial derivative of x->x+d is the identity modulo m; this map is etale there. The coordinate s has never changed. Equation (6) is therefore the required ACTUAL etale contact equivalence. Combining it with the finite approximating map from §3 proves the criterion in §1.

#### 5. Application to the supplied D4/Dn packets

The formal D4 construction in `inline construction 17` produces

    G=X^2+zY^2+2b(s)Y+c(z,s),

with b(s) in R and c a finite polynomial in z with coefficients in R. It is therefore an ACTUAL family over the complete trait, even though the original formal coordinate transformation need not be algebraic. The original generic fiber is smooth and the central D4 point is isolated. Sections 1-4 supply a NEW actual etale equivalence to G. The explicit finite D_n polynomial-family blowups in the corner-ladder packet, followed by the actual A-type root splitting, now transport to the original family on this etale neighborhood.

For completeness, that formal polynomial construction extends to every fixed D_n, n>=4, without a new approximation theorem. Put P_0=x^2+z y^2+z^(n-1). Its spatial Jacobian ideal is generated by 2x, 2zy and y^2+(n-1)z^(n-2). Exact monomial division leaves the n residue monomials

    1,z,...,z^(n-2),y.

Indeed remove x; every mixed zy monomial is in the ideal; replace y^2 by -(n-1)z^(n-2); and z^(n-1) is then a multiple of zy using z y^2. These replacements extend continuously to formal power series. Thus every error is an EXACT sum of a linear combination of those residue monomials and the three displayed Jacobian generators times formal coefficient functions.

At t-order j, add the residue coefficients to the polynomial normal-form coefficients and correct the spatial coordinates by t^j times those three coefficient functions. Their leading effect kills the Jacobian part of that order. All previously introduced normal-form coefficient terms are divisible by t, so their interaction with this correction has order at least j+1. Induction constructs an invertible t-fixed formal coordinate map and the exact polynomial family

    G=x^2+z y^2+2b(t)y+c(z,t),  deg_z c<=n-1,

with coefficients in R and the prescribed central leading z^(n-1). The transformations converge t-adically, hence in the full completed germ, and are the identity modulo t. This is the same explicit finite-residue procedure as the D4 packet, not an appeal to formal versality by name. Sections 1-4 then replace that formal equivalence by an ACTUAL etale one whenever the generic fiber is smooth and the central D_n point isolated. It does NOT automatically apply along an entire nonnormal conductor line. The explicit unit-pinch blowups remain necessary there; when their residual singularities become isolated normal D_n points, the criterion applies.

Actual local modifications are the identity away from the isolated bad point and preserve s. Their proper algebraic-space descent and global common toroidal-exterior refinement are the actual constructions in the adjacent packets. The direct projective-DF support/cone method supplies the numerical theory without a missing general Hom stack for that space.

This closes the FORMAL-to-ACTUAL isolated-normal-form gap at exactly its stated hypotheses. It does not certify remaining nonunit-conductor reductions or arbitrary corner cases which have not yet been brought to an isolated polynomial D-type form. No production content or acceptance record is changed.

### Inline construction 50

#### Relative finite-projection charts over a complex base

Research reconstruction, 2026-10-28. This supplies the geometric local reduction needed by the adjacent flatification algebra. It uses the actual imported Noether-normalization and isolated-fiber etale finite-neighborhood constructions, and proves the smooth geometrically integral chart selection in the complex setting. It does not assume arbitrary characteristic connected-component theorems.

#### 1. Spreading a geometrically integral smooth generic fiber

Let A be an integral finite-type complex algebra with fraction field K, and let V->Spec A be smooth of finite presentation, pure relative dimension d, with geometrically integral generic fiber. Then after one nonempty base shrinking its fibers are geometrically integral.

Here are the details needed in this proof. If d=0, the geometrically integral smooth generic fiber is Spec K. An affine generic neighborhood equal to Spec K spreads to an open isomorphic to an open of Spec A after clearing the finitely many coordinate equations and denominators. Its complement has empty generic fiber, and finitely many affine charts of that complement vanish after another denominator is inverted. Thus the assertion holds.

For d>0 choose a separating transcendence basis and a primitive element of the generic function field. The resulting primitive polynomial F is absolutely irreducible: geometric integrality says that the function field remains a domain after extending K algebraically, so the primitive polynomial remains irreducible. Its affine hypersurface has a dense open isomorphic to a dense open of V_K. Spread that isomorphism and both opens by clearing their finitely many equations and denominators. Homogenize F to degree D. The locus of reducible degree-D forms is closed in the projective coefficient space: it is the finite union, for `1<=e<D`, of the proper images of the multiplication maps from the projective spaces of degree-e and degree-(D-e) forms. Therefore, after shrinking Spec A, every F_s is absolutely irreducible.

The chosen open on the hypersurface stays nonempty on all fibers after shrinking. For example express it by finitely many principal open conditions; reduce their product modulo the single equation F with a fixed monomial order and invert its leading coefficient. A remainder that is nonzero generically has a coefficient that remains nonzero after another denominator is inverted. Thus the product does not vanish identically on any F_s. Its open is geometrically integral and nonempty.

It remains to exclude extra components of V_s outside this open. The complement has generic fiber dimension less than d. On each of its finitely many affine charts, generic Noether normalization gives a finite map to affine space of dimension less than d. Lift the normalizing coordinates and their monic equations and clear denominators in A. This makes that affine chart finite over the corresponding relative affine space after a base shrinking, so every fiber there has dimension less than d. Charts with empty generic fiber disappear after shrinking. Consequently the entire complement has fiber dimension less than d. Every component of a smooth relative-dimension-d fiber has dimension d. Hence each component meets the one geometrically integral open; there can only be one, and the reduced smooth fiber is geometrically integral. This proves the assertion.

The same argument works after replacing A by the coordinate ring of an integral reduced locally closed base stratum. Only finite-presentation equations, the actual imported Noether-normalization theorem, and projectivity of the polynomial multiplication maps were used.

#### 2. The fiber component through a smooth section is open

Let h:V->T be smooth of finite presentation over a finite-type complex base, with a section sigma. Let C be the union, over geometric fibers, of the connected component containing that section. This selection commutes with arbitrary field extension. Indeed the geometric components of a smooth fiber are disjoint, and the component containing a rational section point is fixed by the Galois action; if its ordinary connected component had several geometric components, this fixed one would descend to a proper nonempty open-and-closed subset of that connected component. Thus it is geometrically connected, and smoothness makes it geometrically integral.

C is constructible. By Noetherian induction on reduced closed subsets of T, it suffices to describe it over a dense open of an integral base. Over its generic field the selected component is a geometrically integral open-and-closed component of V_eta. On a finite affine cover its characteristic idempotents, their equations and their overlap equalities spread after clearing finitely many base denominators. This gives an open-and-closed V' over a dense base open; the section lies in V' after shrinking. Section 1 makes every fiber of V' geometrically integral after another shrinking. Thus C equals V' there. Induction on the closed complement gives a finite constructible stratification, proving the assertion. For a nonreduced base the topological assertion is obtained from its reduction.

C is also open in the complex analytic topology. If x and sigma(t) belong to the same fiber component, join them by a path in that smooth complex manifold. A compact path has a finite chain of smooth local product charts; make each fiber slice a connected polydisc and choose consecutive overlaps around points of the path. The analytic implicit-function theorem gives these charts even when the base is singular: a smooth algebraic morphism is locally etale over an affine-space projection, hence analytically a local product over the base. The finite adjacent overlaps and the neighborhoods of the two endpoints remain nonempty over a common analytic neighborhood of t, because these local smooth projections are open. Their connected fiber slices then join the nearby section point to every point in a neighborhood of x. Thus a neighborhood of x lies in C.

A constructible subset of a complex variety that is analytically open is Zariski open. To verify this, the Euclidean closure of any locally closed algebraic subset equals its Zariski closure: it contains a dense Zariski open in each irreducible closure, and that open is analytically dense by the identity theorem. The same holds for a finite union of locally closed subsets. Apply it to the constructible complement of the analytically open set. That complement is analytically closed and therefore Zariski closed. This proves that C is Zariski open.

The construction extends to any Noetherian complex algebra base used here. Descend the smooth finite-presentation family and its section to a finitely generated complex subalgebra. Smoothness descends after enlarging that subalgebra by the finitely many standard-smooth Jacobian identities, and the section descends by its finite coordinate equalities. Pull back the open just constructed. Since geometric-component selection commutes with field extension, that pullback is exactly the desired fiber-component open, not merely an open agreeing at some closed points. This also proves its arbitrary-base-change compatibility.

#### 3. A geometrically integral chart around a chosen smooth-fiber component

Suppose h:V->T is smooth of finite presentation and y is a point over t. Choose a connected component of the geometric fiber containing a selected lift of y. In the finite-projection application below the component is already geometrically integral over kappa(t): the selected point of the auxiliary smooth chart has rational residue field after the construction there. More generally one may make a finite separable residue extension first to define that component.

Choose a closed point z in this component, with residue field finite separable over kappa(t), which is possible in characteristic zero. Realize that residue extension by an etale base neighborhood, using a monic separable polynomial and its invertible derivative. Smoothness then supplies a section through z after a further etale base neighborhood: choose a standard smooth chart etale over affine space, lift the finitely many coordinates of z to the local base, and pull back that etale chart along the resulting affine-space section. This is an actual etale base change with a selected point and an actual section.

Apply Section 2 to the component through that section. Its open V^0 has geometrically integral nonempty fibers after restricting the base to the image of V^0. This image is open because h is smooth. The special fiber of V^0 is exactly the chosen component and thus still contains the selected lift of y. Finally choose an affine neighborhood of that point in V^0 and restrict the base to its open image, then to an affine neighborhood of the selected base point. The inverse image of that last affine base inside the affine source chart is affine. It is smooth, surjective, and has geometrically integral fibers (nonempty opens of the preceding integral fibers).

Thus the auxiliary smooth finite-projection target can be made affine with nonempty geometrically integral fibers by etale base change and open restriction. Etale residue extensions need not be elementary in this version; the ideal-patching proof permits all finite-presentation etale charts.

#### 4. Relative finite projection at closed fiber points

Let X->T be finite type between Noetherian complex schemes and F a coherent sheaf. Work on affine charts `T=Spec A`, `X=Spec B`. Put `Z=Spec(B/ann F)`. This is a closed finite-presentation support on which F is a finite module; on each fiber its topological support equals Z_t by the local Nakayama lemma. No scheme-theoretic reduction of F or of Z is made.

It suffices to construct charts near points x closed in their fiber. Every point of a finite-type fiber specializes to a closed point in an affine neighborhood; an etale neighborhood image containing that closed point is open and contains its generalizations. The resulting images therefore cover the entire source, and quasi-compactness gives a finite subcover.

Fix such an x over t, and let d be the dimension of a suitable affine neighborhood of its support in Z_t. Noether normalization gives a finite map from that affine fiber support to affine d-space over kappa(t). If x initially maps to a nonrational closed point there, let P_i be the monic minimal polynomial of the ith normalizing coordinate at that point and replace that coordinate by `P_i(t_i)`. The polynomial ring in the old coordinates is finite over the ring in these new coordinates, since each old t_i satisfies its monic polynomial `P_i(T)-new_i`. Their values at x are all zero. Thus the finite projection can be chosen to send x to the rational origin of affine d-space.

Lift these finitely many normalizing functions to the actual source algebra after shrinking the affine base at t and clearing denominators. The resulting morphism `Z->A^d_T` has an isolated selected fiber point x over `(t,0)`; quasi-finiteness at that point is the actual local finite-fiber criterion, and its open locus permits a source shrinking. Apply the imported `lem-etale-neighbourhood-isolated-fibre-point-finite` to this morphism. It gives an etale neighborhood W of `(t,0)` in affine d-space and an open Z' of the etale pullback of Z with a **finite** morphism `Z'->W`. W is smooth of relative dimension d over T, and its selected point has residue field kappa(t). The connected component of W_t containing that point is therefore geometrically integral: after geometric base change its disjoint smooth components are permuted by Galois, and the component containing this rational point is fixed, hence descends and cannot be a proper open-and-closed subset of the selected connected component.

Apply Section 3 to W, with the finite morphism pulled back throughout. In this application its selected point is already rational over kappa(t); take that point itself as z. The smooth-section base change can therefore be chosen elementary at t, so its selected residue field stays kappa(t). In particular the finite fiber over the selected point still has exactly the one source point supplied above. We now have an affine smooth target W' over an etale affine base T', with nonempty geometrically integral fibers of dimension d, and a finite source-support morphism `Z''->W'` still covering a lift of x.

The source etale neighborhood of Z can be extended to an etale neighborhood of X. First choose a standard etale presentation near its selected point. To retain finiteness of the map to W', remove from W' the proper closed image of the complement of that chosen neighborhood in Z''. That image misses the selected target point because its finite fiber has only the selected source point. After this shrinking the entire finite support lies in the standard presentation. In rings, lift its monic polynomial and localization elements from B/ann F to B, and invert the lifted derivative. The resulting etale B-algebra has exactly the prescribed quotient by ann F. This is literal base change of a monic equation, so works on all nilpotent bases. A further open restriction on the closed Z-neighborhood lifts to an open restriction of that ambient etale neighborhood by removing its closed complement. Thus F pulls back to the pushforward of its finite module on Z''. Finite pushforward to W' is a finite coherent module and preserves the base-flatness question, since its local sections are exactly the same underlying base modules.

This constructs the required etale source/base finite-projection chart. If one only needs to cover the locus where the maximal fiber-support dimension is d, use affine neighborhoods with no larger fiber-support dimension; each chosen chart has that maximal value and the preceding construction applies there.

#### 5. Interface to flatification

The smooth target algebra on each chart satisfies the exact hypotheses of `inline construction 25`. The original coherent module is, after its closed-support immersion and finite pushforward, a finite module over that projective base-module algebra. Thus the Fitting-coefficient blowup and universal-injectivity/dimension reduction can be performed there, and the etale norm-ideal patching descends them to the original base with the specified U preserved.

This file provides actual charts and includes the open/closed support and nilpotent equations. It supplies no virtual formula by itself. The final flatification argument must still state the dimension induction, its rank partition on U, and the exact strict-transform sequence.

### Inline construction 51

#### Finite continuing t-charts for the residual nonunit-cross family

Research only. This is a bounded termination supplier, not a claim that the other blowup charts have been resolved. Theta owns the global join; bmt_ladder owns the non-t charts.

Let R=C[[t]] (or its fixed finite extension), and let F(b,V,W,t)=0 be an actual algebraic/étale hypersurface with smooth generic fibre. The motivating residual chart after blowing a central component is

    F=t b²-bVW-g(V,W,t),

where finite cross contact normalization gives g=c(V,t)+d(W,t)+e(t)VW. Suppose F has total multiplicity exactly three at a central point. Its ordinary point blowup has a continuing t-chart

    (b,V,W)=t(B+a,V'+c,W'+d),
    F=t³ F'.

Here (a,c,d) is any fixed exceptional point, and the translation can be repeated at every subsequent stage. The parameter t is retained exactly. These are actual blowup charts; no formal algebraization is used.

Put J_F=(F,F_b,F_V,F_W) in the ambient local ring. Generic smoothness and noetherianity give t^M in J_F for some finite M. Relative differentiation, with t fixed, gives the exact identities

    pullback(F_b)=t² F'_B,
    pullback(F_V)=t² F'_(V'),
    pullback(F_W)=t² F'_(W'),
    pullback(F)=t³ F'.

Therefore the pulled-back Jacobian ideal is contained in t² J_F'. Pulling back a certificate for t^M and cancelling t² in the ambient domain gives

    t^(M-2) in J_F'

when M≥2. If M<2, the certificate would give 1 in (t) after cancellation, so no such t-chart point exists. Thus each continuing multiplicity-three t-chart consumes TWO units of a finite Jacobian exponent. Translations of the exceptional point do not alter this estimate. There are at most floor(M/2) consecutive such steps, and when the transformed certificate is 1 in J the central hypersurface is regular.

The same calculation applies to a weighted (2,1,1,1) chart of a multiplicity-four cross family: write (U,V,W)=(t²U',tV',tW') and F=t⁴F'. Then pullbacks of F_U,F_V,F_W are respectively t²F'_U,t³F'_V,t³F'_W, again giving J_F pullback contained in t²J_F'. This identifies the same finite invariant for the component-extraction plus ordinary-point cycle. A coordinate change or contact-unit multiplication preserves the Jacobian ideal modulo F; hence the exponent certificate survives the actual étale normalizations already supplied elsewhere.

For the motivating equation, the unshifted t-chart is explicitly

    F'=B²-BV'W'-g(tV',tW',t)/t³.

If g has order at least four, its special fibre is the reducible cross B(B-V'W')=0. Blowing one of these two actual central components recovers the cross cycle with updated function g(tV',tW',t)/t⁴. If g has a nonzero cubic term, the new central surface is the actual cubic

    B²-BV'W'-g3(V',W',1)=0;

its normal/nonnormal/reducible local branches require the separate cubic/conductor inventory. This note does not replace that inventory by an assertion that only A/D singularities occur.

The ordinary point blowup is crepant for the TOTAL hypersurface at a multiplicity-three point: the blowup of its smooth ambient fourfold has canonical discrepancy three, and the strict hypersurface subtracts its multiplicity three. For this specific residual family its exceptional cubic t b²-bVW-g3(V,W,t) is reduced. Indeed a repeated factor would be linear L. If L is independent of b, its square divides the leading b² coefficient t, impossible. If L depends on b, the remaining linear factor is independent of b and comparison of the b² coefficient forces that factor to be proportional to t; then the whole polynomial would be divisible by t, contradicting its -bVW term. The parameter consequently has order one along each generic exceptional component. The reduced central boundary is log crepant. This argument uses the exact absence of b in g; it does not assert reducedness for an arbitrary cubic deformation.

Other charts do not follow from this exponent estimate. For example the V-chart is

    t=Va, b=VC, W=VD,
    F/V³=aC²-CD-g(V,VD,Va)/V³,
    old parameter t=Va.

Hyperbolic relative Morse applies only where its actual (C,D) Hessian is invertible. Its degeneracy locus must be resolved by the separately owned family constructions. Once those branches are reduced to already finite blocks, the exponent above prevents an infinite unresolved t-axis branch; no appeal to a general threefold MMP or an unspecified termination theorem is made.

### Inline construction 52

#### Residual cross: actual V and W point-blowup family joins

Research construction of the NON-t chart outer joins. The continuing t-chart belongs to the separate relative-Jacobian termination construction. Exceptional interior singularities remain inputs to the cubic-surface inventory, rather than being assumed covered by this calculation.

#### Exact input and charts

Work over the actual trait with parameter t. Suppose an actual hypersurface germ has the exact finite contact form

    F=t b²-bVW-g(V,W,t),    g in (V,W,t)^3,
    g=c(V,t)+d(W,t)+e(t)VW.

Its total multiplicity is three. Blow up the closed point in the ambient smooth fourfold and take the strict transform. In the V-chart set

    V=u,  t=u a,  b=u c,  W=u d.

Since g has order at least three, h(u,d,a)=u^(-3)g(u,ud,ua) is an ACTUAL regular function on this blowup chart. The strict-transform equation and old parameter are exactly

    H=a c²-cd-h(u,d,a)=0,       t=u a.                 (1)

There is no formal substitution or approximate old parameter. The W-chart is obtained by interchanging V and W. The remaining b-chart and the continuing t-chart are separately owned constructions.

The point blowup is a generic-fibre isomorphism. Its canonical discrepancy on the hypersurface is zero: the ambient point discrepancy is three and F has order three. The exceptional valuation has t-order one. Thus the reduced central log discrepancy is zero at the generic reduced exceptional components. All multiplicities and any subsequent normalizations remain those of (1); no automatic SNC claim is made.

#### The entire old-component intersection is a two-base conic join

The exceptional divisor in this chart is u=0; the strict transform of the old central component is a=0. We treat their intersection u=a=0, including its possible singular point.

Write the homogeneous cubic g3 modulo higher order. Since g(V,W,0) is a sum of pure V and pure W functions, its degree-three part is A V³+D W³. Consequently

    h(0,d,0)=A+D d³,
    H(0,c,d,0)=-cd-A-Dd³.                            (2)

At a=0 the (c,d) Hessian of H is

    [ 0  -1 ]
    [ -1 -h_dd ],

whose determinant is -1, independent of h. If at a point of (2) one of H_c,H_d is nonzero, the implicit theorem eliminates that coordinate. The total space is smooth with independent coordinates u,a and one remaining spatial coordinate, and the old map t=u a is already a normal-crossing map.

The only point on (2) where both spatial derivatives vanish is c=d=0, and it belongs to (2) precisely if A=0. Indeed H_c=-d, and H_d=-c-3Dd² on that intersection. At this possible point the displayed Hessian is invertible. Actual relative algebraic Morse splitting in (c,d), FIXING both u and a, gives

    P Q=Phi(u,a),       old t=u a.                    (3)

This is exactly the existing inline construction 60 interface. It preserves the labelled old and exceptional divisors u=0 and a=0 and retains the actual old base parameter. In particular it is not a one-parameter Morse change allowed to mix t with spatial coordinates.

If Phi is nonzero, extract all axis factors u^m a^n, including either exponent zero, and apply that actual two-base construction with the remaining axis-free function. If Phi is a unit, (3) is smooth and t=u a is already SNC. Phi cannot vanish identically on a chart with smooth generic fibre: then the singular locus P=Q=0 is the two-dimensional base chart, and its restriction ua=t dominates the trait, contradicting generic smoothness. This argument concerns an actual etale chart of the flat hypersurface and hence cannot be evaded by treating it as a purely central formal chart.

Thus every point of the V-chart lying on u=a=0 has an actual regular-SNC family resolution supplied by either the displayed smooth coordinates or the existing two-base conic algorithm. The same statement holds for the W-chart. If A!=0, the V outer intersection has no Morse critical point at all; the corresponding possible W critical point is governed by D=0.

#### What remains on this chart

On u=0,a!=0, the old parameter t=u a is a unit times the exceptional coordinate. These are genuine interior points of the projective cubic exceptional surface

    t b²-bVW-g3(V,W,t)=0.

Their family singularities are not decided by the outer Hessian calculation. They must be assigned by the actual normal/nonnormal/reducible cubic inventory and the corresponding surrounding-family constructions. In particular the arbitrary coefficients involving t in g3 must be retained; one may not replace g3 by A V³+D W³ except on t=0.

Likewise the W-chart contributes the symmetric exceptional interior. The b-chart outer join and the continuing t-chart are separate suppliers. Together with these explicit boundaries, this note supplies all V/W OUTER family joins and nothing weaker is hidden behind a central-surface-only normalization.

### Inline construction 53

#### Smooth weighted degree-two cross component: actual cubic and toric presentation

This is the smooth exceptional-surface presentation branch only; an arbitrary cross blowup need not have a smooth exceptional surface. The surrounding family is handled by inline constructions 5, 51–52 and 57. Normal singular exceptions use the actual ADE family constructions 12–14 and the singularity inventories 68 and 70; their toric presentations use constructions 43 and 66–67. Nonnormal exceptions use the conductor branches of constructions 4, 23, 28 and 71, the actual general-pinch family modification in construction 72, and the finite-face assembly 57. Consequently this bounded smooth branch is not being substituted for those supplied family branches.

Let

    E={U²-V²W²-tau G_3(U,V,W,tau)=0} subset P(2,1,1,1),

where G_3 has weighted degree three and E is smooth and integral. Its boundary tau=0 is C_+ plus C_-, with U=+VW and U=-VW. Then E has an actual finite blowdown presentation to a Hirzebruch surface, preserving its rational nodal anticanonical cycle by the already constructed interior/corner rules. Consequently the existing toric-model and relative vertex suppliers apply. No del-Pezzo classification or count of 27/56 lines is used.

#### 1. The actual anticanonical line and the boundary minus-one curves

The weighted ambient singular point [1:0:0:0] is absent from E. Thus H=O_E(1) is an ample Cartier line. Projection to [V:W:tau] is finite of degree two: the equation is monic quadratic in the weight-two coordinate U. It follows that H²=2. Weighted residue adjunction gives K_E=O_E(4-(2+1+1+1))=-H; equivalently compute the residue form in the weight-one charts and its weight transition. This is the local hypersurface canonical calculation, not a classification of degree-two Fanos.

Each C_+ and C_- is the weighted graph of the polynomial ±VW on the ordinary line P1_[V:W]. It is a smooth P1 and H has degree one on it. Adjunction therefore gives C_+²=C_-²=-1. The two curves meet exactly at [V:W]=[1:0] and [0:1], and their intersection number is two, since H=C_++C_-. Their intersections are transverse. At each point smoothness of E makes the tau derivative nonzero (the remaining derivatives of U²-V²W² vanish there), and solving for tau gives the two distinct tangent branches U=+VW and U=-VW.

Contract C_+ by the actual projective minus-one contraction supplier to a smooth projective surface pi:E->Z. The image B of C_- is an irreducible rational nodal curve: its two transverse points on C_+ are identified, with distinct tangent directions. The canonical point-blowup formula gives

    pi*(-K_Z)=H+C_+,       (-K_Z)²=3,
    pi*B=C_-+2C_+=H+C_+.

For every curve A not equal to C_+, (H+C_+).A>0, and its square is three. Hence -K_Z is ample by the actual surface numerical criterion already proved in `lem-gts-curve-nef-ample-perturbation-by-nakai-construction`. In particular B is exactly an anticanonical nodal rational curve. This is the first actual contraction in the required presentation.

#### 2. Anticanonical embedding as a smooth cubic, proved rather than assumed

Put L=-K_Z. The weak del-Pezzo generation supplier applies with the identity contraction and L²=3, so L is globally generated. Smooth adjoint vanishing gives H^{>0}(O_Z)=0, and surface RR gives h0(L)=4 and

    chi(O_Z(nL))=1+(3/2)n(n+1).

The complete system maps Z to a nondegenerate integral surface Y in P3. It is finite: a positive-dimensional fibre would contain a curve of zero L-degree, impossible for ample L. Proper quasi-finite is finite, using the actual published root supplier at that literal scope. If d is its generic degree, intersection gives d deg(Y)=L²=3. A nondegenerate surface in P3 has degree at least two; degree one is a plane and would impose a linear relation on the chosen four independent sections. Therefore d=1 and Y is a cubic hypersurface.

A finite birational image is not silently assumed normal. Normality of Z embeds O_Y in phi_*O_Z; write the coherent quotient Q. The cubic hypersurface exact sequence gives

    chi(O_Y(n))=1+(3/2)n(n+1)=chi(O_Z(nL)).

Thus chi(Q(n)) is identically zero. A nonzero coherent sheaf on a projective scheme has positive leading coefficient in its Hilbert polynomial, so Q=0. A finite map is the relative spectrum of its pushed algebra, so phi is an isomorphism. Hence Z is an actual smooth cubic surface in P3 and its hyperplane class is L. This argument proves the needed embedding from the available local surface suppliers; it does not import an anticanonical-embedding classification.

#### 3. A line exists by one proper incidence and a finite Jacobian calculation

Let P19 parametrize homogeneous cubic equations in four variables and let Gr(2,4) parametrize lines in P3. The incidence I={(F,l):F restricts to zero on l} is projective over P19. Restriction to a fixed line gives all four cubic coefficients, so its kernel is a rank-sixteen vector bundle over the Grassmannian; I is its projectivization and has dimension nineteen.

At the Fermat cubic F=X_0³+X_1³+X_2³+X_3³, use the line

    (X_0,X_1,X_2,X_3)=(s,-s,t,-t).

In the four-dimensional affine graph chart of nearby lines, its two normal displacements are arbitrary linear forms l_1(s,t),l_2(s,t). Differentiating restriction of F gives

    (l_1,l_2) -> 3s² l_1+3t² l_2.

The four coefficients of s³,s²t,st²,t³ show that this is an isomorphism H0(O(1))²->H0(O(3)). Thus the four incidence equations have invertible Jacobian in the four line variables. The projection I->P19 has a nonempty open image near this actual point. Since its image is closed by projectivity and P19 is irreducible, it is surjective. Every cubic, in particular our smooth Z, contains an actual line. No enumeration or classification of lines is needed.

#### 4. The residual conic pencil is an actual generated system

Choose such a line l in Z. It is a smooth rational Cartier divisor and l²=-1 by adjunction. Planes through l form a pencil; subtracting l gives the system |L-l|. It is basepoint-free on **all** of Z. Indeed the two linear equations of l in P3 generate the ideal of l on Z, which is invertible because Z is smooth. Dividing their images by its local Cartier generator gives two sections of L-l which generate the unit ideal, including along l. Thus no unspecified gradient avoidance is used at a base point.

This defines f:Z->P1 whose fibres are residual plane conics. They are connected, including reducible or double conics. General Bertini gives a smooth generic conic. Its rational point over C(P1) is supplied by the explicit polynomial-coefficient/height calculation in `inline construction 4`: three degree-N polynomials give fewer homogeneous equations than unknowns for large N. That calculation proves the needed conic case directly and does not import a draft Tsen theorem. Properness extends the point to an actual section.

Now apply the constructed vertical contraction algorithm from that same file. A connected reducible fibre has negative component squares and K.fibre=-2; adjunction produces a smooth rational minus-one component. Contract it projectively over P1. Every contraction decreases numerical Picard rank, so finitely many give a smooth P1 fibration. Its section and actual cohomology/base-change identify it with P(E_2). The maximal-line-subbundle/RR calculation splits E_2 on P1, giving an actual Hirzebruch surface. Reversing these contractions is a finite ordinary point-blowup presentation of Z.

Combining with pi gives the requested finite actual sequence

    E -> Z=(smooth cubic in P3) -> ... -> F_n.

All intermediate surfaces are smooth projective. The anticanonical boundary starts as the two-component cycle C_++C_-, becomes the nodal curve B, and thereafter obeys the exact corner/interior contraction rules of `inline construction 46`. Its theorem applies to precisely such a supplied presentation and gives a toric model after corner blowups. Therefore its already constructed relative vertex comparison is available with the actual curve classes and normal units. No full-cross-family readiness is inferred from this bounded smooth-exceptional surface calculation.

#### Source and scope boundary

The minus-one contraction, weak del-Pezzo generation, numerical ampleness, RR and cohomology/base-change inputs are the actual local mathematical suppliers already installed in the physics workspace during the BMT work.
