### Inline construction 54

#### Stagewise domination of local normal admissible blowups

Constructive research supplement, 2026-10-28. This supplies the finite-sequence join in Section 2 of `inline construction 36`. It is a proof construction, not an independent audit or acceptance verdict. No production content or native run state is changed.

#### Exact data and conclusion

Let S be an excellent normal integral Noetherian complex scheme and U a nonempty quasi-compact open. Let S_i->S, for finitely many i, be quasi-compact etale morphisms of finite presentation covering the required base locus. Use their finitely many normal integral components separately. Suppose on each S_i an actual finite sequence is given:

    S_i^(0)=S_i,
    S_i^(k+1)=Normalization(Bl_(J_i,k)(S_i^(k))),

where J_i,k is a coherent ideal equal to O over U_i=U times_S S_i, and each local space is identified with U_i there. Allow finite Zariski or etale refinements at intermediate stages; the supplied construction is a finite refinement tree. Normalization is finite by excellence. The conclusion is a finite composite P->S of normalized admissible blowups, projective and an isomorphism over the **given** U, which etale locally dominates every completed local sequence. If the local sequences are given on all of each original S_i, there are compatible maps

    P times_S S_i -> S_i^(m_i)

over S_i. In the refinement-tree version the maps are on the corresponding finite etale covering refinements. This is sufficient for descent of the resulting flatness statements. The construction never replaces U by a smaller open.

#### 1. Keep a Cartier boundary through all stages

Choose a coherent ideal B defining S minus U. If U=S take B=O. Otherwise B is nonzero on the integral S. First take

    P_0=Normalization(Bl_B S).

The exceptional ideal B O_(P_0) is invertible and has a nonzerodivisor as generator. On each normalization chart this follows by pulling back the blowup's invertible ideal to a normal domain with the same generic field. Its zero set is exactly P_0 minus U, and normalization is an isomorphism on U because U is normal. This step is projective because the blowup is projective and its normalization finite.

At every subsequent stage P_k retain the pullback boundary B_k=B O_(P_k). It remains an invertible ideal with nonzero local equation a, and P_k minus U=V(B_k). Every etale chart of P_k is normal; its finitely many integral components are open and closed and all meet U. Indeed a component's image is a nonempty open of the integral P_k, so it contains the generic point. Thus a remains a nonzerodivisor on every such chart. This is the regular-generator condition needed in the product-ideal argument.

#### 2. An exact Zariski extension formula

Let P be a Noetherian scheme, j:V->P a quasi-compact open immersion, and I a coherent ideal of O_V with I|_(V intersect U)=O. Define

    Ext_V(I)=ker(O_P -> j_*(O_V/I)).                         (Z)

This is a coherent ideal. The pushforward is quasi-coherent: on an affine open of P, cover its quasi-compact intersection with V by finitely many principal affine opens; the sheaf condition expresses the pushforward module as the equalizer of their localization modules and the overlap maps, and localizing this finite diagram gives the corresponding diagram on every principal open. The kernel is therefore a quasi-coherent submodule of O_P; Noetherianity makes that ideal finite type and coherent. Exact restriction to V gives Ext_V(I)|_V=I. On U the quotient on V intersect U is zero, so Ext_V(I)|_U=O_U.

This construction is the needed actual extension; an arbitrary choice of generators on one open without an overlap argument would not suffice. If B_P is a finite ideal defining P minus U, then B_P^N is contained in Ext_V(I) for some N. Locally, write B_P=(b_1,...,b_t) and choose e_j with b_j^(e_j) in Ext_V(I), possible from the support inclusion. Then N=1+sum_j(e_j-1) works by the monomial pigeonhole argument. A finite affine cover and its largest exponent give one global N. Thus adjoining this power does not alter the ideal, even on V:

    Ext_V(I)+B_P^N=Ext_V(I).

In particular extension never introduces a centre on U. Formula (Z) works equally well for an affine subchart of an intermediate P_k, not merely an open of the original S.

#### 3. The local norm/Fitting factor used at a stage

Retain the exact one-step calculation proved in `inline construction 64`, Section 3; here are its rings and factor so that its recursive use is explicit. Let Spec A be an affine of P_k with B_k=(a), and let Spec B be an affine standard-etale open of an etale chart over it. Write B=C_h, with C=A[T]/(F) finite free of rank d and F monic; the selected branch has invertible derivative. Let J be the prescribed pulled-back next ideal on Spec B. Because J is unit on D(a), choose lifts of its finitely many generators to C, clearing h denominators, and include a^m for some m with a^m in J. Let K be the resulting ideal of C, so K B=J and a^m belongs to K. Set

    I=Fitt_0^A(C/K).

The d relations a^m e_j in a free rank-d presentation show a^(md) in I, so I is unit on D(a). At the selected branch the algebra C tensor_A B splits as B times C': factoring F(T)=(T-r)Q(T), the equality Q(r)=F'(r) in B^* gives the required comaximal factors. Hence

    (C/K) tensor_A B = (B/J) direct-sum N,
    I B = J * Fitt_0^B(N).                               (N)

The second identity follows from minors: zero-th Fitting ideals commute with base change and multiply on direct sums, and Fitt_0(B/J)=J. Both factors in (N) are unit on D(a). This descends J only **up to a complementary factor**; no actual descent of J is assumed. There are finitely many such standard-etale affine charts after using quasi-compactness and a finite affine cover of P_k.

#### 4. The recursive global stage

For fixed sequences, put m=max_i m_i and pad each shorter sequence by identity steps with ideal O. Inductively suppose P_k is normal integral, projective over S, identical on U, and maps

    phi_i,k:T_i,k=P_k times_S S_i -> S_i^(min(k,m_i))

have been constructed, equal to the given identity on U_i. At stage zero these maps are the projections to S_i. For k<m pull the actual next ideal back:

    Jbar_i,k = J_i,k O_(T_i,k)

using phi_i,k (or use O for a padded step). It is coherent, nonzero on each integral component and unit over U_i. The schemes T_i,k are quasi-compact etale over P_k, so Section 3 applies to finitely many standard-etale affine opens covering them. Denote the resulting base affine opens by V_k,l, the descended ideals by I_k,l, and the complementary factors by K_k,l. Extend each descended ideal by (Z):

    E_k,l=Ext_(V_k,l)(I_k,l),
    H_k=product_l E_k,l,
    P_(k+1)=Normalization(Bl_(H_k) P_k).                  (R)

Every E_k,l is unit over U and contains a power of B_k; therefore H_k is coherent and unit over U, and (R) is an admissible normal projective modification. The pullback of H_k is an invertible Cartier ideal. Each factor E_k,l becomes an invertible Cartier ideal: locally, if a finite product of ideals is generated by a nonzerodivisor c, write c as a sum of products of generators and choose a summand whose coefficient relative to c is a unit. Cancelling the complementary factors proves that each factor is principal with a regular generator. Repeating this two-factor argument proves the finite-product assertion. Its regularity is preserved after etale pullback.

On each standard-etale chart over P_(k+1), identity (N) now makes Jbar_i,k an invertible Cartier ideal by the same factor argument. These charts cover T_i,k+1; invertibility is local, so the ideal is invertible on all of that scheme. The blowup universal property consequently gives an actual map

    T_i,k+1 -> Bl_(J_i,k) S_i^(k).

It remains to lift to its normalization, rather than assuming that dominating a blowup dominates its normalization. Each integral component of T_i,k+1 is normal and is an isomorphism over the dense U_i with the corresponding component of S_i. Its map to the displayed blowup is dominant and has the same function field: all maps restrict to that identity on U_i. If A_0 is an affine ring of the blowup and b is in its normalization, the pulled-back b lies in that common function field and is integral over every normal local source ring containing A_0. It therefore belongs to each such source ring. These extensions agree in the common field and glue uniquely, proving the factorization

    phi_i,k+1:T_i,k+1 -> S_i^(k+1).

The new maps are compatible with the preceding ones because they are maps over S_i^(k) and agree with the specified identity on dense U_i. This completes the explicit recursive step. After at most m stages following P_0, P_m dominates all given local normalized sequences. Its finite composite map to S is projective and remains an isomorphism on U.

#### 5. Intermediate open refinements and finite construction trees

Local flatification often selects new affine or etale base neighborhoods after a blowup. This does not force any centre to be extended from the wrong base. At a stage, pull the chosen neighborhood of S_i^(k) back along phi_i,k. A Zariski neighborhood gives an open of T_i,k; an etale neighborhood gives an etale scheme over T_i,k. Either is etale over the **current** P_k. Refine these by the finitely many affine standard-etale charts used in Section 3. Thus every next ideal is first pulled back to an actual etale chart of P_k, and its norm ideal is extended by (Z) from an actual open of P_k. Formula (R) is then applied on that current base. No ideal on a later local stage is incorrectly treated as an ideal on S.

Because the given construction tree is finite, there is a maximum number of blowup edges in a path. Process all active next edges at a level together by (R), allowing identity ideals at completed branches. Replace the chart family by the finite covering refinements just described whenever the next edge requires them. This supplies an induction on that finite maximum depth and yields one normal projective modification with the required domination on the covering refinements. If two lifts on an overlap have the same local target, they agree: their equalizer is closed by separatedness of the target and contains the schematically dense inverse image of U; normal integral etale components have no extra component disjoint from U. A global map to one fixed local sequence can therefore be recovered whenever such a map is wanted. For descent of flatness the covering-refinement maps themselves already suffice.

#### 6. Preserve completed flatness and identify the composite strict transform

Let a coherent strict-transform module be flat over a completed local normal stage. Pulling it to any further base is flat. On the further normal integral base the pullback boundary has a nonzerodivisor equation c. Multiplication by c is injective on a flat base-module, since it is obtained by tensoring the injection O --c--> O. Thus it has no c-power torsion and its further strict transform is its ordinary pullback. Later global stages cannot destroy the flatness achieved on an earlier branch.

Successive strict transforms equal the strict transform for the composite modification. Locally write the boundary ideal as (a). The kernel removed in an earlier strict transform is a-power torsion, and its pullback is still killed locally by a power of the pulled-back a (finite generation supplies such a power). Quotienting it and then removing all remaining a-power torsion therefore gives precisely the quotient of the total ordinary pullback by all sections killed after inverting a. This is the composite strict transform. The same description holds on overlaps of boundary trivializations and after each finite normalization. Hence the local flat modules used at the end are the actual restrictions of one global strict-transform module, not unrelated replacements with the same generic restriction.

Flatness is local on source and base for the faithful etale covers in question. The final covering refinements therefore descend their flatness to the global strict transform. This proves the requested finite-sequence global join with the original U, its actual nilpotent module structures and the allowed finite normalizations retained.

#### Provenance and scope

This supplement uses the previously reconstructed one-step norm/Fitting factor (N), the elementary product-ideal calculation, the blowup universal property and finite normalization for the stated excellent Noetherian schemes. It adds the exact extension (Z), recursion (R), normal-factor lift and finite-tree bookkeeping that were previously abbreviated by “one stage at a time.” It does not assert the arbitrary-base single-blowup flatification theorem, a virtual specialization identity or any production acceptance.

### Inline construction 55

#### Complete even weighted terminal face of the tangential conductor

Research construction. Its family joins use the actual outer, higher-double, pinch and ADE suppliers already authored in this directory. The normal degree-one toric presentation additionally uses the explicit weak-degree-one line and weak-degree-two continuation supplier; it is not inferred from a classification citation.

Suppose an actual family has weighted order at least six in weights (x,v,w,s)=(3,2,1,1), and its weighted degree-six initial polynomial is

    P=X²-V³-V²W²
      +S²(alpha W⁴+beta V W²)
      +S⁴(gamma W²+delta V)+epsilon S⁶,

where the five parameters are not all zero. All remaining terms have weight greater than six. This is a statement about a terminal Newton face, and does not require that s be the original pre-extension parameter. Its weighted extraction is actual, generic identity, and crepant of discrepancy zero. The outer-chart supplier retains the exact current trait parameter s=uS and resolves those joins by the complete toric-base conic construction.

We classify every nonnormal exception by an explicit identity. In the branch convention P=X²-B6, its nonnormal factorization must be

    B6=L²Q,
    L=V+mu WS+nu S²,
    Q=V+W²-2mu WS-2nu S².

This follows from inline construction 71. The coefficient of V is 2lW²-3l², l=mu WS+nu S². Our displayed face has no VW³S term, forcing mu=0. Expanding then gives the complete identity

    (V+nu S²)²(V+W²-2nu S²)
      =V³+V²W²+2nu S²VW²-3nu²S⁴V
       +nu²S⁴W²-2nu³S⁶.

Therefore, with the PLUS parameter convention in P, nonnormality is EXACTLY

    alpha=0, beta=-2nu, delta=3nu²,
    gamma=-nu², epsilon=2nu³.

The all-zero parameters correspond to nu=0 and are excluded. Hence nu is nonzero in every nonnormal terminal case.

Normalize by X=(V+nu S²)A, V=A²-W²+2nu S². The normalization is P²_[A:W:S], its conductor is

    A²-W²+3nu S²=0,

and the strict outer boundary is S=0. Since nu is nonzero, this conductor is a SMOOTH conic; it meets the boundary line in two distinct transverse points. The central rational anticanonical cycle is line plus conic, with the explicit elementary toric presentation already constructed. Most importantly there is NO crossing of the conductor and hence no return to the tangential-conductor germ on this face.

The surrounding actual family is also covered by existing local constructions. At a conductor point with Q a unit, its two central branches split on a pointed étale neighborhood and the total equation is an actual higher-double family with the retained trait parameter. At Q=L=0, take W/S as an affine coordinate. The equation on L=0 has W²=3nu, so W is nonzero and gradients of L and Q are independent. Thus the exact central germ is the ORDINARY pinch X²=L²Q, not a tangential conductor. Its actual finite contact normal form and finite conductor-blowup family algorithm apply. At the two outer intersections the weighted outer-chart supplier retains the original parameter product; no change of trait is hidden in a central normalization. These joins have actual generic-isomorphism modifications and proper conductor gluing.

For every other parameter choice the exceptional surface is normal. Its interior singularities are precisely the finite ADE types supplied by inline construction 70, and their surrounding actual families are resolved by the constructed A/D/E6/E7/E8 algorithms. The sole outer nodal point has an invertible (X,V) Hessian. Relative Morse fixes S and gives YZ=h(S). Normality excludes h identically zero. The polynomial is even in S, so h has even positive order at least two: the point is A_m for a finite m>=1. The complete outer-family supplier handles this point with the exact parameter s=uS, including m=1.

On the smooth crepant exceptional resolution, H=pullback O(1) is nef and big, H²=1, and -K=H. The explicit connected Hilbert-closure/Hodge supplier inline construction 43 gives an actual (-1) curve. Its contraction is a smooth weak-degree-two surface. The exact anticanonical-cycle degree-two base-point-free/double-plane smoothing and generic bitangent-closure supplier gives a second actual (-1) curve, then the installed weak-degree-three line/conic construction yields a finite presentation to a ruled surface. Equivalently, if the outer A-chain has at least two components, one can contract the strict outer (-1) curve and a neighboring endpoint directly. Thus all normal cases have the required explicit toric-model presentation. The exact degree-two supplier is inline construction 67; bind its actual current bytes in the combined offer.

All nonzero faces in this five-parameter family are therefore terminal finite family blocks, using those explicit suppliers. The zero parameter face is the continuing high-weight tangential conductor and is governed by the separate finite Jacobian/descent termination argument.

### Inline construction 56

#### Tangential conductor: the mixed cubic-axis, tvw and t² face

Research construction. Use the ACTUAL contact form

    F=x²-v³-v²w²+c(w,t)+v[b0(t)+b1(t)w+b2(t)w²].

All deformation coefficients vanish modulo t. Assume the first-trait coefficient c1(w) has order at least three (including identically zero), and b0(t) is divisible by t². Define alpha as the w³ coefficient of c1, beta as the t coefficient of b1, and gamma as the t² coefficient of c(0,t). Suppose (alpha,beta,gamma)!=(0,0,0). This is the complete mixed Newton face, including its resonances; it is not restricted to pure tw³.

#### Exact finite extraction

Make ONE finite extension t=s³ and the actual normalized weighted blowup of weights (x,v,w,s)=(3,2,1,1). The strict exceptional surface is

    E: X²-V³-V²W²+S³(alpha W³+beta VW)+gamma S^6=0
       in P(3,2,1,1).                                (1)

Every unlisted actual term has strictly greater weight than six. In particular t²v and t²w have weights eight and seven, respectively. The normalized blowup of (x²,v³,w^6,s^6) constructs the operation projectively, and the s^6 generator makes it a generic-fibre isomorphism. The ambient discrepancy six equals the equation weight six; the exceptional old-s order is one. Hence the extraction is canonical- and reduced-central-log-crepant.

#### Nonnormality is impossible unless the whole face vanishes

Apply the EXACT square-factor restriction in inline construction 71. On S=1 the branch polynomial is

    B=V³+V²W²-beta VW-alpha W³-gamma.

If this double plane were nonnormal, the repeated weighted quadratic factor has the form L=V+aW+b. The quotient must be Q=V+W²-2aW-2b to match the V² coefficient. Matching the V coefficient in L²Q gives the polynomial identity

    2(aW+b)W²-3(aW+b)²=-beta W.

Its W³ coefficient gives a=0; its W² coefficient then gives b=0; consequently beta=0. The constant coefficient gives alpha=gamma=0. This contradicts the hypothesis. Thus E is normal for EVERY nonzero triple, including the boundary resonance below. This argument uses exact polynomial coefficients, not a generic-smoothness inference for the exceptional surface.

Interior singularities are therefore isolated and belong to the ACTUAL finite A/D/E6/E7/E8 inventory in inline construction 70. That proof uses the Weierstrass degree bounds to exclude nonminimal elliptic orders; no del Pezzo or ADE classification is being imported here. Each of the finite isolated interior points consumes the actual surrounding-family A/D/E resolver already constructed, after its recorded finite trait extension. The corresponding old outer boundary is disjoint from these points.

#### Exact boundary resonance: A2, A5 or A8

On S=0 the outer curve is X²=V²(W²+V), with its unique node p=[0:0:1:0]. At all other boundary points E is smooth. In W=1 write z=S³. The local equation is

    X²-V²-V³+alpha z+beta zV+gamma z²=0.

Its V-critical section solves 2V+3V²=beta z. Thus

    V=(beta/2)z-(3beta²/8)z²+O(z³).

At that section the residual function is

    alpha z+(gamma+beta²/4)z²-(beta³/8)z³+O(z^4).

Therefore:

* alpha!=0 gives order three in S, namely A2;
* alpha=0 and gamma+beta²/4!=0 gives order six, namely A5;
* alpha=0 and gamma=-beta²/4 gives beta!=0 (since the triple is nonzero), and order nine, namely A8.

There is NO missing infinite-order resonance. The cubic coefficient -beta³/8 is nonzero in the last row. Actual Morse splitting in (X,V) and an actual unit root give these central A forms.

E avoids ambient weighted quotient singularities exactly as in the simple- and quadratic-axis branches: W or S has weight one, and at W=S=0 the equation forces both coprime-weight X,V nonzero. Hence there is no further quotient branch in (1).

#### Surrounding family and fixed old parameter

In the w-chart write w=u,x=u³X,v=u²V,s=uS. The actual strict equation is

    X²-V³-V²+S³(alpha+beta V)+gamma S^6+uR=0,
    old s=uS.

At p, actual relative Morse splitting in (X,V), FIXING both u and S, gives PQ=Phi(u,S). The displayed critical-value calculation says Phi(0,S) is S³, S^6, or S^9 times a unit, according to the three rows above. The existing inline construction 60 construction supplies the finite actual regular-SNC family modification of this two-base chart. Away from p on the outer curve implicit smooth coordinates retain u,S, so old s=uS is already monomial. The exceptional interior points consume their actual A/D/E family suppliers. Further finite trait extensions required by interior isolated resolutions are followed by their supplied normalization and toric refinements on already SNC charts; SNC is not asserted to survive an arbitrary root extension unchanged.

Let T be the minimal crepant resolution of E. Put H=-K_T, with H²=1. The strict boundary D is a smooth P1 with H.D=1, hence D²=-1. Contract D, then contract C1, which becomes a (-1)-curve because n>=2. These are actual smooth contractions, yielding surfaces of anticanonical degree two and then three. The anticanonical line stays nef by its pullback formula H_new=H_old+C_contracted.

Generation is exact. The installed degree-one pencil on T has its unique base point outside the ADE exceptional locus, hence on D because the total boundary is a member. Thus the degree-two descended anticanonical line is generated and has three sections. The same sequence for C1 gives a generated degree-three line with four sections.

The constructed inline construction 66 then gives an actual finite contraction sequence to F_n: normal cubic image, actual line incidence, RR/Hodge square-zero conic pencil, actual section and vertical (-1)-contractions. Hence every T here has the required finite blowdown presentation, and its reduced nodal rational anticanonical cycle consumes the existing toric-model lemma. ### Inline construction 57

#### One fixed cover, six terminal faces, and finite tangential-conductor recurrence

Research construction. This is the finite-termination join for the exact actual family suppliers in this directory. It uses their proved local constructions; it does not replace unbound suppliers by a claim of readiness.

#### Input and budget

Let R=C[[t]] and let an actual pointed algebraic/étale family with smooth generic fibre have central germ

    f=x²-v³-v²w².

Use the actual finite contact supplier to put it EXACTLY in the form

    F=f+c(w,t)+v[b0(t)+b1(t)w+b2(t)w²],
    c(w,0)=b_i(0)=0.

Choose a finite relative-Jacobian certificate

    t^M in J_F=(F,F_x,F_v,F_w).

It exists because the generic hypersurface is smooth. All contact changes fix t and preserve this ideal modulo F. This finite certificate also bounds any continuing axis charts. The exact normal form below in fact makes every nonzero terminal face terminal without a hard conductor restart.

Make ONE fixed initial cover t=s^60. The number 60 is lcm(1,2,3,4,5,6); it is not chosen anew after a conductor recurrence. At each high-weight stage take the actual weighted point blowup of weights (3,2,1,1) on (x,v,w,s). Its discrepancy is zero when the hypersurface has weighted order six. Its outer charts are the exact old-s=uS charts already constructed. Along the smooth parts of its nonnormal conductor the ordinary higher-double/pinch family suppliers apply. The only continuing hard point of the high-weight central surface is its tangential-conductor point in the s-chart.

After r consecutive such s-charts at their origins, the exact substitutions are

    x=s^(3r) X, v=s^(2r) V, w=s^r W,
    F=s^(6r) F_r.

No intermediate ramification has occurred. The original contact normal form guarantees that an error monomial t^k of spatial weighted degree j contributes to F_r with s exponent

    a_r=60k-r(6-j).

Here j is the weight 2*deg_v+deg_w; the normal form uses only deg_v=0 or 1. Terms with j>=6 never cause a lower face. For j<6 put p=6-j in {1,...,6}. Since 60k is divisible by p, successive high-weight steps can first hit weight at most six ONLY at a_r=p, exactly weight six; they cannot jump to a lower weighted degree. Infinite actual axis series cause no problem: only the finitely many spatial weights j<6 are relevant, and each has a least nonzero trait order.

A terminal face occurs after finitely many high-weight steps. Otherwise c(0,t), the coefficient of w in c, and b0(t) would all vanish identically. Then the axis x=v=w=0 would be a point of the generic hypersurface with all relative derivatives zero, contrary to generic smoothness. At least one of these three coefficients supplies a monomial with j=0,1 or 2 and finite k, bounding the number of high-weight steps explicitly.

#### Exact six-face arithmetic

Let r be the number of prior high-weight steps at the first nonzero degree-six face. For every monomial in that face,

    60k=(r+1)(6-j).

Set q=60/gcd(60,r+1). Thus q divides every appearing p=6-j. Since at least one p lies in {1,...,6}, q is one of 1,2,3,4,5,6. The degree-six polynomial is therefore restricted to the following EXACT face (some displayed coefficients may vanish, but not all):

* q=6: only S⁶; the q6 constant-face supplier applies.
* q=5: only S⁵W; the simple-transverse axis-face supplier applies.
* q=4: S⁴W² and S⁴V; the quadratic-axis mixed face, or its tv-only A7 case, applies.
* q=3: S³W³, S³VW and S⁶; the mixed cubic-axis face applies.
* q=2: S²W⁴, S²VW², S⁴W², S⁴V and S⁶; the complete even-face supplier applies.

All coefficients and units are actual elements of R', and this equality follows from the actual base map (1); no artificial parametrization of a formal curve is substituted for it.

### Inline construction 61

#### Central toric conductor pushouts and their actual tangent cones

Research algebraic construction. This computes the tangent cone of a central pushout directly; it does not claim to lift a normalization fan subdivision to the deformed total family. It applies to a cycle of normal two-dimensional affine toric normalization germs whose conductor is exactly their two invariant boundary rays and whose ray gluing is the Gorenstein residue-compatible cyclic gluing. Smooth-conductor/pinch points are treated separately. The finite normalization and actual conductor equalizer are premises supplied by the preceding conductor construction.

#### The tangent cone of one normal toric surface

Let M be a rank-two lattice, sigma a strictly convex rational cone, B=C[[sigma intersect M]] its completed normal semigroup ring at the vertex, and m_B its maximal ideal. Put Pi=conv((sigma intersect M) minus {0}). The compact lower boundary of Pi is its sail. Its lattice points in angular order u0,...,ur form the Hilbert basis. Consecutive Hilbert-basis vectors have determinant one: otherwise the fundamental parallelogram contains another nonzero lattice point below the adjacent sail segment, contradicting that they are consecutive. The same argument proves generation, by subtracting a basis vector in the unimodular cone containing a given lattice point. This is the two-dimensional continued-fraction algorithm, expressed in the dual semigroup lattice.

Partition the sail into its maximal straight edges. For an edge F, let ell_F be its supporting functional with ell_F=1 on F. It is integral, because two consecutive Hilbert-basis points on F are a lattice basis; it takes positive integers on every nonzero point of sigma intersect M. The cones from the origin over the sail edges partition sigma. If m belongs to the cone over F, choose consecutive basis points ui,ui+1 on that edge whose rays contain m between them. Unimodularity writes m=a ui+b ui+1 with nonnegative INTEGERS a,b. Hence its maximal-ideal order is exactly

    ord_mB(chi^m)=a+b=ell_F(m)=min_G ell_G(m).

The lower bound is the displayed decomposition. The upper bound follows because every nonzero summand of any semigroup decomposition has ell_F at least one. Thus this is an exact maximal-ideal filtration computation, not an appeal to integral closure of ideal powers.

In the associated graded ring, products of monomials on a common sail-edge cone retain their product; products which cross a bend have strictly larger order and hence vanish in the proposed summed degree. Therefore

    gr_mB B = toric face ring of the chain of cones over the sail edges.

Each edge of lattice length d has its d+1 equally spaced lattice points at height one. Its semigroup algebra is the d-th Veronese cone over P1, whose projective image is the rational normal curve of degree d. Adjacent edges meet at the ray through their common endpoint, so their projective curves meet at one ordinary node, with no triple points. The toric boundary ray quotients are the endpoint variables, each of degree one. The Proj of gr_mB B is consequently an actual CHAIN of rational normal curves, with a positive integral degree on each component. This statement also includes a smooth normalization plane, whose sail is a single unit segment.

#### Gluing the filtered normalization rings

Take finitely many such normalization germs B_i. The conductor is their pair of boundary axes. The exact central ring A consists of tuples of functions whose restrictions agree under the specified paired axis maps. At the closed vertex the constants agree, and the Gorenstein residue condition makes the connected pairing graph a cycle, including the one- and two-vertex cases already classified. On associated gradeds the linear coefficients of the axis maps are nonzero constants; rescaling the endpoint coordinates removes them. Higher axis terms do not affect the following filtration, and can be matched by higher-order corrections.

Filter A by F^k A=A intersect direct_sum m_Bi^k. Its degree-k initial tuples are exactly the compatible degree-k tuples of the normalization graded rings. Necessity is immediate from restriction to the axes. For sufficiency, lift a compatible tuple and correct its higher axis discrepancies by actual higher series in the individual endpoint variables. The two axis restrictions on one normalization component can be changed independently after their common constant is fixed: the quotient by the reduced toric conductor is C[[u,v]]/(uv). Thus the corrections do not change the specified initial degree and produce an exact compatible tuple. It follows that

    gr_F A = R(C,L),

where C is the connected cycle obtained by gluing the endpoint curves of the preceding rational-normal chains, and L has the indicated positive degree d_i on each component. For k=0 the common constants give H0(C,O)=C, not one constant per normalization component. For positive k the compatible sections are exactly H0(C,L^k). Hence R(C,L) denotes the FULL section ring of this polarized nodal genus-one curve.

#### Degree-one generation when total degree D>=3

Let D=sum d_i. Normalization of the rational cycle shows H1(C,L^k)=0 and h0(C,L^k)=kD for k>=1: on each P1 the degree is nonnegative and evaluation at its two nodes is surjective, with the cyclic matching equations independent for positive total degree. The same normalization sequence gives omega_C=O_C, with generator given by opposite logarithmic differentials on consecutive components.

The bundle L is base-point-free. Choose a section u with D distinct smooth zeros, away from nodes, and choose v nonzero at those zeros. They span a generated pencil. The pencil trick gives surjectivity of W tensor H0(L^m)->H0(L^(m+1)) for m>=2, since H1(L^(m-1))=0. In degree two the pencil image has codimension one. This missing direction is supplied by a third product, as follows. Modulo u, values on its D zero points identify the image of H0(L) with one hyperplane defined by a residue relation with ALL coefficients nonzero. The missing degree-two functional is the corresponding nondegenerate diagonal quadratic form on those values (divide by the nonzero values of v). That quadratic form cannot vanish identically on a hyperplane of dimension D-1 when D>=3: a nondegenerate quadratic form on a D-dimensional complex vector space has maximal totally isotropic dimension floor(D/2)<D-1. Choose a section q whose value vector has nonzero quadratic value. Then q² supplies the missing degree-two direction. This proves that the full section ring R(C,L) is generated in degree one. The argument uses the actual residue pairing on the nodal curve, not a smooth-elliptic replacement.

Lift its finitely many degree-one generators to elements a_j of F^1 A. Every leading term of any element of F^k A is a degree-k polynomial in these generators. Subtract that polynomial in the actual a_j and raise its normalization order. Repeating gives convergence in the finite-normalization topology, which is the original complete local topology; equivalently use Artin–Rees for the finite A-module direct_sum B_i. The ideals (a_j)^k are closed. Therefore

    F^1 A=m_A=(a_j),
    F^k A=m_A^k,
    gr_mA A=R(C,L).

This is a strict tangent-cone statement for the actual CENTRAL ring. It does not assert Rees base change for a vertical total-family ideal.

#### Output and exact remaining interfaces

For D>=3 the associated graded central ring is the complete section ring of a positively polarized nodal rational cycle of total degree D. In particular its Hilbert function is h0=1 and h_k=kD for k>=1, giving

    Hilb(gr_m A)=(1+(D-2)u+u²)/(1-u)².

Its Cohen–Macaulay/Gorenstein and degree-one canonical-module statements can be read directly from H1(C,L^k)=0 for positive k, omega_C=O and Serre duality for negative k. The ring has a-invariant zero. Thus the general toric quotient boundary has been reduced, for tangent-cone purposes, to a POLARIZED NODAL CYCLE rather than assumed to be an ordinary union of planes.

To use this in the actual total-family PBW construction one must still supply the pure minimal-resolution and negative-weight-minus-two data for these polarized cycles, or prove them by an explicit degeneration to the ordinary D-gon. That is a finite central algebraic prerequisite, not an actual family fan-lift assertion. For D=3 the section ring is a nodal plane cubic hypersurface; for D=4 it is a genus-one complete intersection of two quadrics, which need not be the special ordinary four-plane cycle. Their exact actual family reductions must retain that distinction. Degree D=1 or 2 need the separate weighted/hypersurface small-degree classification; degree-one generation is NOT asserted for them.

All conclusions are conditional on the actual conductor equalizer and cyclic ray pairing specified at the start. An arbitrary collection of toric components with no such local gluing/duality premises is not relabelled as this ring. No production, acceptance, gate, or full broad-model assertion is made.

For D=3 the normalization degree partitions are 1+1+1, 2+1 and 3. Three planes give xyz=0 by the cyclic equalizer computation. A degree-two cone ac=u^b and a plane glued along their two conductor axes give

    u(ac-u^b)=0.

Indeed the normalization is the plane u=0 and the A_(b-1) surface ac=u^b. Their conductor restrictions are respectively ac=0 and u=0; the displayed hypersurface has exactly their compatible function equalizer. It has form zxy-x^p=0 with p=b+1>=3, after renaming.

For a single degree-three toric normalization cone write its four Hilbert-basis generators as B0,B1,B2,B3 in angular order. Consecutive unimodularity gives

    B0 B2=B1^b1,
    B1 B3=B2^b2,
    B0 B3=B1^(b1-1) B2^(b2-1),

with b1,b2>=2. These relations generate the normal semigroup algebra: reduce any monomial containing nonconsecutive generators by the displayed binomials; the remaining adjacent-generator monomials are independent by the corresponding unimodular lattice basis. Glue its endpoint axes and put z=B0+B3, x=B1, y=B2. Then

    zxy=x^(b1+1)+y^(b2+1).

This is the FULL equalizer. The endpoint product is already a monomial in x,y, so the normalizer is quadratic over the proposed subring. Also B3x=y^b2 and B0y=x^b1; multiplying by endpoint sums and using the endpoint product expresses every mixed endpoint monomial in z,x,y. Hence every element vanishing on the two axes lies in this subring, while every compatible pure-axis pair is a series in z modulo that conductor. No additional generator or finite supported extension is omitted.

Thus the uniform exact central form for D=3 is

    zxy=x^p+y^q,
    p,q>=3 or infinity,

where infinity means the term is absent. The partitions are respectively both exponents infinite, one infinite and one finite, or both finite. Arbitrary actual trait deformations of this hypersurface have form zxy-x^p-y^q+tH (after multiplying by an actual unit and retaining t). The complete finite family exit in inline construction 32 handles every H, including a unit or a trait-linear nonunit. It uses one cubic trait cover and a vertical blowup, with exact old-parameter products on every chart.

Together these computations supply all positive small degrees D<=3 in the cyclic toric-conductor class. D=4 is deliberately separate: its general equalizer is a two-quadric complete intersection and cannot be replaced by the special ordinary four-plane cycle without an actual preliminary construction. The missing-term four-generator CI exit has been authored separately, but its full central classification must be bound before using it universally.

### Inline construction 63

#### Finite resolution of the two-base corner block, with actual global centers

Research construction used by the linear-corner owner. The input is an ACTUAL smooth local threefold chart, with its OLD target parameter retained:

    tau = u^p (q W + u^m epsilon(u)),     p>=1, m>=1, epsilon(0)!=0.        (1)

The coefficient epsilon is an actual regular unit on the etale curve in u. The coincident case tau=u^p qW is already toric. This packet proves finite vertical/log-crepant resolution of (1), preserves the entire generic fiber, and provides a global-compatible center construction. It does not assert that every arbitrary corner has already been reduced to (1).

#### 1. Elementary local induction

Write Z=qW+u^m epsilon(u). Off u=q=W=0 the reduced divisor uZ=0 is already SNC: where q or W is a unit, Z is a smooth coordinate transverse to u; where Z is a unit only u occurs. Blow up the intersection branch (u,q). Its u-chart has q=u q_1, hence

    tau=u^(p+1) (q_1 W+u^(m-1) epsilon(u)).                              (2)

Its q-chart has u=q r, hence

    tau=q^(p+1) r^p (W+q^(m-1)r^m epsilon(qr)).                          (3)

The last factor of (3) is a smooth coordinate, its derivative in W being one. Thus (3) is an actual monomial normal-crossing chart. The only continuing chart is (2), in which m drops by ONE. For m=1, (2) has exponent zero: near the origin its bracket is a unit, while at a bracket zero q_1 and W are units and that bracket is transverse to u. Therefore it is SNC. This is a finite integer induction, not a claimed decrease of an unspecified resolution invariant.

The center is vertical. At its generic point it is the intersection of the two reduced components u=0 and Z=0, so the regular curve blowup has canonical discrepancy one and pulls that reduced boundary back with exceptional order two. Hence the pair modification is log crepant. At the origin the same coefficient identity follows by equality of Cartier divisors, whose coefficients are determined at the generic exceptional divisor; no additional divisor lies over the isolated origin. This direct form is useful whenever the branch q=0 extends as an actual global double curve.

#### 2. A center choice that uses only GLOBAL central components

One need not assume that the chosen branch q=0 globalizes. Blow up instead

    I=(u,Z),

the sum of the ideal sheaves of the two actual central components. This ideal is globally defined on the whole current target. Away from the bad point it is the ordinary toric blowup of their SNC intersection. Its two actual blowup charts are as follows.

On the u-chart, put Z=u a. Then

    qW=u(a-u^(m-1)epsilon(u)),
    tau=u^(p+1) a.

Rename a_0=a-u^(m-1)epsilon(u); its derivative in a is one. The chart is the TORIC ordinary node

    qW=u a_0,
    tau=u^(p+1)(a_0+u^(m-1)epsilon(u)).                                (4)

Resolve this isolated node by the small blowup (q,u). It is an isomorphism off the node because that ideal is Cartier there: where W is a unit use q=u a_0/W, and where a_0 is a unit use u=qW/a_0; the remaining non-Cartier point has q=W=u=a_0=0. Thus the isolated-point etale descent lemma applies without choosing a global branch curve.

In its u-chart q=u r, a_0=rW, so (4) becomes

    tau=u^(p+1)(rW+u^(m-1)epsilon(u)),                                (5)

the same continuing form with m reduced by one. In its q-chart u=q r, W=r a_0, so

    tau=q^(p+1)r^(p+1)(a_0+q^(m-1)r^(m-1)epsilon(qr)).                 (6)

The bracket has derivative one in a_0, so (6) is SNC. These are actual scheme equations; the small resolution is not a reduced or set-theoretic replacement.

On the Z-chart of the GLOBAL blowup, put u=Z b. The equation and old parameter are

    Z(1-Z^(m-1)b^m epsilon(Zb))=qW,
    tau=Z^(p+1)b^p.                                                     (7)

For m>=2 the bracket is a unit along Z=0. The implicit-function derivative in Z is one there. Thus Z is an etale function of q,W,b, equal to qW times a unit; (7) is a smooth SNC monomial chart, with its exact unit retained. Points with b=0 and Z!=0 are also smooth SNC, since q,W are then units.

For m=1, the only additional locus is where c=1-b epsilon(Zb) vanishes on Z=0. There b is a unit and dc/db is a unit. Use c as an etale coordinate to write

    Zc=qW,   tau=Z^(p+1)*unit.

Adjoin a (p+1)-st root of that unit etale-locally and rescale Z and c inversely. Then the old parameter is exactly a power of the toric coordinate Z. A finite base change and the explicit toric crossing subdivisions resolve it. No new non-toric block is introduced.

Thus each GLOBAL component-ideal blowup followed by its isolated toric-node small resolution either gives SNC/toroidal charts or reduces m by one. There are at most m continuing steps. The generic fiber is unchanged because every global ideal contains a component of tau=0 and the small node ideal is Cartier away from its central isolated point.

#### 3. Crepancy and component geometry

At the generic points of its curve center I is the ordinary intersection ideal of two reduced central divisors, hence its divisorial exceptional coefficients satisfy the log-crepant identity as in §1. Its exceptional fiber at the isolated singular-intersection point is only a P^1 (the two-generator complete-intersection ideal), so it has no further exceptional divisor there. The isolated ordinary-node small resolution is crepant: it is small between Gorenstein spaces and is an isomorphism at every codimension-one point. Consequently the global pair transformation preserves the actual logarithmic canonical divisor, not merely its numerical rank.

In the corner application the selected double-curve branches lie on the previously constructed rational exceptional/toric components; their normalizations are the actual boundary P^1 curves. The divisorial exceptional components are projective bundles over those curves. The displayed charts identify their toric crossings and their finite boundary-point modifications. Adjunction of the log-crepant identity gives their reduced anticanonical boundary cycles. Their ruled presentations and the explicit elementary boundary transformations in `inline construction 46` put them into the actual toric/boundary-blowup relative theory. No arbitrary positive-genus curve center is introduced in this construction.

For an application outside that corner geometry, rationality/presentation of the global center is an additional hypothesis; the local termination proof (1)-(7) itself does not assert it for arbitrary global curves.

#### 4. Algebraic-space gluing of the isolated choices

The only noncanonical local choices in §2 occur at isolated ordinary toric nodes. Choose a separated etale scheme neighborhood with exactly one preimage of that point. The local small modification is the identity away from that preimage. The diagonal in the double overlap is open and closed and contains the entire bad fiber; off it both modifications are identities. Thus the canonical overlap isomorphisms satisfy the cocycle and descend to a proper algebraic-space modification, as explicitly proved in `inline construction 29` §3. All ordinary global component blowups are schemes; the descent adds no unidentified centers or generic alteration.

The family owner has constructed the direct projective-DF support method for such a target, avoiding a claim that it is a scheme or that its full Hom stack has already been constructed. Pull the actual finite Cartier log data to a projective generic-iso model, form its proper finite enhancement stack, and close the ORIGINAL generic cone there. No POT on that support stack is asserted.

#### 5. Reduced semistability after the finite induction

The output has actual monomial parameter charts tau=unit*product_i x_i^(a_i) with at most three central branches and SNC outer divisor. Choose a common finite ramification degree divisible by all finitely many a_i and normalize. The local models are explicit saturated toric monoids for that base change. Their height-one slices have dimension at most two. A compatible finite subdivision through all relevant lattice points, followed by a pulling triangulation, makes every triangle empty with primitive edges and hence unimodular. The coned charts are regular and every central ray has height one. These are actual monomial blowup/root normalization charts; they retain all lengths and residual stabilizers and are the identity on the generic smooth family after the base extension.

Global compatibility uses the same finite monomial ideals on overlapping actual log charts, or a common refinement of their finite fans. Thus the result is a regular reduced SNC family, not merely an SNC support with forgotten multiplicities.
