### Inline construction 64

#### U-admissible flatification: reconstructed ideal algebra and exact remaining input

Research, 2026-10-27. This file proves the etale ideal-patching step and the Fitting-coefficient step under its exact module hypothesis. It records the complete reduction of the desired proper flatification to its remaining local algebra. It does **not** call a cited flatification theorem a local proof.

**Current successor, 2026-10-28:** `inline construction 25` supplies (P) and the universal-injectivity algebra. `inline construction 50` supplies the actual geometric charts. `inline construction 36` assembles the explicit dimension induction on an excellent normal integral complex base, allowing projective normalizations after blowups, and constructs generic-entire projective domination. The remaining-obligation descriptions below record the earlier reduction; they are discharged for that precise sufficient normal-base version by those successors. The full arbitrary-ring single-blowup theorem is still not asserted.

#### 1. Scope and actual source reading

The needed assertion is: for a proper finite-presentation `g:G->B` of Noetherian complex schemes, isomorphic over a specified quasi-compact open U in B, construct a U-admissible blowup `B'->B` whose strict transform G' is flat. In the application B is the integral projective closure of the generic surface, U is its entire generic fiber, and strict transform means closure of the generic graph, retaining its actual scheme structure.

Full on-line proofs actually read: Stacks tags 0815 (Theorem 38.30.7), 0814 (etale-local patching), 0813 and 0812 (norm/Fitting ideal descent), 0811 and 0810 (generic-fiber Fitting flattening), 057S (finite-projection reduction), 05I3 (universal injectivity reduction), 05FT and 05FS (projectivity of a flat algebra with geometrically integral fibers), and the completion/purity route 05DP, 05DM, 05DK, 059N, 059X. Reading a statement/proof that cites another result is not recorded as reconstructing that cited result. The imported local isolated-fiber supplier and elementary etale finite decomposition were also read at their actual full Statements and proofs.

The deepest extra input is not the blowup product identity. It is the purity/projectivity assertion in Section 5 below.

#### 2. Product-ideal domination, including its nonzerodivisor condition

Let A be a local ring and let J,K be finitely generated ideals such that `JK=(c)` with c a nonzerodivisor. Write `c=sum_l j_l k_l`. Since each product belongs to `(c)`, write `j_l k_l=c u_l`, where `sum_l u_l=1`. Some u_l is a unit. For this pair j,k we have `jk=c u`, so both j and k are nonzerodivisors. For any j' in J, `j'k=cv` for some v. Cancel k from `j'k=jk(u^-1 v)` to get `j'=j(u^-1v)`. Hence J=(j), and similarly K=(k). Both are invertible Cartier ideals.

It follows that blowing up a product of finitely many ideals makes each factor invertible, provided the total exceptional ideal is Cartier, as it is on the actual blowup charts. The universal property gives factorization through each individual blowup. This is not a claim that a product principal with a zero-divisor generator has the same consequence.

For a U-admissible blowup with exceptional local equation a, strict transform of a module is `pullback/module a-power torsion`. If that module is flat over the new base, a is a nonzerodivisor on the module whenever it is one on the base; hence every further strict transform is its ordinary pullback. Flatness is then retained by every further admissible blowup. Transitivity of strict transform follows by taking the quotient by sections killed on the common dense U: the two successive torsion quotients and the total torsion quotient have precisely that same universal property.

#### 3. Etale norm ideals: a complete descent calculation

Let A be Noetherian, a a nonzerodivisor, B an etale finite-presentation A-algebra, and J a finitely generated ideal of B with `V(J) subset V(aB)`. Near any chosen prime of B, a standard etale presentation writes `B=C_h`, with C finite free of rank d over A, of the form `A[T]/(F)` for F monic, and with etaleness at the selected h-localized branch. Lift finitely many generators of J to C, clearing h denominators. Since a lies in the radical of J, adjoin `a^m` to these generators for a suitable m. Let K be their generated ideal in C, so `K B=J` and `a^m in K`.

Put `I=Fitt_0^A(C/K)`. It is finite type. Multiplication by a^m kills C/K, whose underlying A-module is a quotient of the free rank-d module C. The presentation using the d relations `a^m e_i` shows `a^(md) in I`. Thus `V(I) subset V(a)`.

Base-change the finite algebra C to B. The multiplication/selected-branch map `C tensor_A B -> B` is the chosen etale section at the selected branch. Since the whole algebra is finite over B, that etale section is both open and closed. After the stated localization it splits the algebra as

    C tensor_A B = B times C'.

Consequently `(C/K) tensor_A B = (B/J) direct-sum N` for a finite B-module N. Fitting ideals commute with arbitrary base change and multiply on direct sums; applying their matrix minors gives

    I B = J times Fitt_0^B(N).

Thus, locally at each selected etale point, the prescribed ideal J is a factor of an ideal pulled back from A, with all ideals supported away from U=D(a). This proves the required ideal descent up to a complementary factor. It does not assume that J itself descends.

Now suppose finitely many etale base charts `B_i->B` and etale source charts cover a flatification problem, and each has an actual U_i-admissible blowup with flat strict transform. After first blowing up the boundary ideal, cover the base by affine opens on which its boundary is `(a)` Cartier. Apply the preceding calculation at each point of each etale base chart; quasi-compactness gives finitely many chart shrinkings and finitely many descended ideals I_l. Blow up their product on the affine base. After etale pullback each local flattening ideal is a factor of that product, hence becomes invertible by Section 2. The new base therefore factors through every specified local flattening blowup. Their flat strict transforms pull back to flat modules on the common cover. Flatness descends through the covering etale maps, both on source and on base.

For a Zariski cover of a quasi-compact base, first extend each finite ideal from its affine open, adjoining a power of the boundary ideal so that its zero set stays outside U; finite-presentation quasi-coherent ideal extension gives this directly by clearing the finitely many denominators on overlaps. Blow up the product of these extensions. The same factor argument applies. This proves the global etale/Zariski patching step with the specified U preserved.

#### 4. The Fitting-coefficient calculation under its exact hypothesis

For this reconstructed algebra calculation let A be a Noetherian **domain**, a nonzero, and A->B a flat finite-presentation algebra with nonempty geometrically integral fibers. Thus B is a domain: it injects into its integral generic fiber. In this section **assume in addition** that B is a projective A-module. Let N be a finite-presentation B-module whose localization at each generic fiber point above D(a) is free of a fixed rank r. The domain restriction suffices for this calculation; no general nonreduced-base version of the Fitting argument is claimed here.

Embed B as a direct summand of a free A-module F. Every element of B has finitely many coordinates in this embedding. Let J be `Fitt_r^B(N)`, and let I_0 be the ideal of A generated by the coordinates of finitely many generators of J. This ideal contains all coordinates of all elements of J: multiplication by any b in B is A-linear on the summand B, extends by the summand projection to an A-linear map of F, and therefore carries an I_0-valued coordinate vector to an I_0-valued coordinate vector. Thus `J subset I_0 B`, and I_0 is the actual coefficient ideal, not an arbitrary chosen approximation.

Set `I=a I_0`. At a prime p with a not in p, the generic fiber point pB lies outside V(J), because N has generic fiber rank r. Hence some element of J has nonzero image in `B tensor_A kappa(p)`. Its coordinate vector has a coordinate outside p, so `I_0` is not contained in p. Therefore `V(I)=V(a)`.

On a blowup chart `A'=A[I/c]`, with c one of the finite generators of I, write c=a c_0. The ideal `I_0 A'=(c_0)`, with c_0 a nonzerodivisor. Every element of `J B'`, `B'=B tensor_A A'`, is divisible by c_0, using its coordinate vector. For a selected generic fiber prime `p' B'`, at least one normalized generator `j/c_0` is nonzero modulo p'B': some original generator's coordinate equals the chosen c_0, so its normalized coordinate vector has a unit coordinate. That normalized element is a unit **in the localization at p'B'**. It need not be a unit everywhere on B'. This qualification is essential.

Thus `J B'_(p'B')=(c_0)` as an invertible nonzerodivisor ideal. Here is the required Fitting matrix calculation. Over the local domain at p'B', write a presentation with n generators and relation matrix Q. The generic rank of Q is n-r. Choose an `(n-r)`-square pivot block D whose determinant generates the ideal of all such minors; one exists because that ideal is principal in this local ring. Every minor obtained by replacing a pivot row with a complementary row is divisible by det(D). Cramer's rule therefore expresses the corresponding Schur-complement map from the n generators to the r complementary generators with coefficients **in the local ring**, not merely its fraction field. This map is the identity on the complementary r generators. Every relation column maps to zero: its Schur-complement coordinates vanish over the fraction field by generic rank, hence vanish in the domain. It gives a surjection `N -> B^r` with kernel precisely the torsion module, since it is a generic isomorphism. Inverting det(D) kills that kernel, so each of its finitely many generators is killed by a power of det(D). Here det(D) is c_0 times a unit. Since c_0 divides c, the torsion is also c-power torsion. Conversely the free quotient has no c-torsion. Thus the strict transform N' is free of rank r at p'B', and hence in an open neighborhood of that point. This proves the local matrix assertion without assuming that a pointwise rank condition alone gives freeness before taking strict transform.

This constructs a blowup making the module locally free on an open meeting every fiber. The additional projectivity hypothesis on B has been displayed rather than concealed. It is exactly the hypothesis discharged by Stacks 05FT in the published proof.

#### 5. Infinite-module input and its subsequent local reconstruction

The published route discharges the extra hypothesis by proving:

    A->B flat, finite presentation, nonempty geometrically integral fibers
    implies B is projective as an A-module.                    (P)

This is an infinite-module projectivity assertion, not the familiar statement that a finitely presented flat module is projective: B is generally infinite as an A-module. It cannot be replaced by that finite-module theorem.

The full proof of 05FS/05FT uses Noetherian induction on base ideals and generic freeness. After completion along a generic-freeness denominator a, it needs the following precise purity statement: for a finite module over a finite-type algebra, flat over the complete base, if every associated prime of every base-field fiber meets the completion locus, then the map to its a-adic completion is universally injective. Completion is Mittag-Leffler when the modulo-a module is projective. A pure submodule is then Mittag-Leffler, and a countably generated flat Mittag-Leffler module is projective. The algebra B is countably generated as an A-module by its monomials. Descending this projectivity along `A -> A[1/a] times Ahat` also needs its actual infinite-module descent criterion, not just finite-presentation descent.

The subsequent companion `inline construction 25` now reconstructs (P) locally: its Sections 2–6 prove the countable module criterion, pure completion using Artin–Rees and prime filtrations, nilpotent lifting, faithful-flat descent in the exact countable setting, and Noetherian induction. Its Section 7 also supplies the required universal-injectivity reduction at generic fiber points. Thus (P) is no longer merely cited. The imported finite-module rank-flattening lemma alone would not have supplied it.

#### 6. How the completed local inputs would imply the required proper flatification

For clarity, the rest of the intended induction is recorded with its dependencies visible. Etale locally near a point of maximal fiber-support dimension d, relative finite projection presents the coherent module as a finite pushforward from a closed support over a smooth affine base of relative dimension d with geometrically integral fibers. This uses the imported isolated-fiber finite-neighborhood supplier after local Noether normalization, together with the construction of a relative geometrically integral smooth chart. The full relative chart construction has also not been locally reconstructed here.

Use Section 4, once (P) is supplied, to make the pushed-forward module locally free on an open meeting every fiber. Choose a free rank-r submodule inducing a basis at the generic point of the chosen fiber. The purity/universal-injectivity lemma 05I3 makes the injection universally injective near that fiber. Its cokernel has strictly smaller fiber-support dimension. Strict transform retains the exact free-submodule sequence because the injection is universal and the removed exceptional torsion is entirely in the cokernel. Induct on d, with the zero module as the negative-dimension base case. Section 3 patches the finitely many local blowups without changing U. Over Noetherian schemes every coherent strict-transform module is already finitely presented, so the additional non-Noetherian finite-presentation step of 0815 is unnecessary for the present application.

When the module is O_G, its torsion quotient is an algebra and is the actual structure sheaf of the strict graph transform. Flatness of that sheaf is the needed flatness of G'. Theta's `inline construction 24` then proves that this flat strict graph is the blown-up projective base itself, yielding the required generic-entire projective dominating model.

The companion supplies (P) and the universal-injectivity reduction. The remaining geometric obligation is the relative finite-projection chart and its coherent-support reduction. The supported-cone proof can consume the resulting domination only after that construction and the displayed dimension induction are completed. This file does not yet certify U-admissible flatification or the full non-Fano theorem.

### Inline construction 65

#### Universal relative cone operation for the logarithmic map family

Research construction attached to the non-Fano comparison. The algebraic/basic-stack and log-prestable-base presentations are supplied by their separate family construction. The input here is the actual two-term **universally base-change-compatible** log-map POT, constructed by the fixed-log-domain Čech calculation, not a mere complex of the expected rank. The result constructs the relative virtual operation and its Cartier/proper compatibility needed for degeneration. No named virtual-pullback or virtual-pushforward theorem is used as an imported premise.

#### 1. Scheme-test operation and its strict model

Let f:M→B be of relative DM type and locally finite presentation, with E→L_{M/B} a perfect obstruction theory in degrees [−1,0]. Assume that for every finite-type scheme T→B the pulled-back map on M_T is its actual fixed-domain obstruction theory, compatible with all further base changes. This hypothesis holds for the log-map Čech construction: the pulled-back universal curve is flat nodal, its vector bundle is f*T_log, its derived pushforward commutes with base change, and local unit-lift discrepancies give precisely the same cocycle after every base restriction. Ghost maps are part of the prescribed log-domain datum, so there is no unproved ghost-lifting assertion in that statement.

For an integral scheme Z→B, embed a scheme presentation of M_Z as a closed subscheme of P smooth over Z. On an affine torsor replacement the relative POT can be put in the actual strict form

[F^∨→Ω_{P/Z}|M_Z] → [I/I²→Ω_{P/Z}|M_Z],

with the degree-zero map the identity and F^∨→I/I² surjective. Here F is a vector bundle on M_Z; it need not extend to P. The construction is the explicit affine strictification from the completed ordinary stable-map supplier: lift Ω→h⁰E into E⁰, split the resulting surjection E^{-1}⊕Ω→E⁰, take its projective kernel, and subtract the lift of the chain homotopy to make degree zero the identity. Projectivity on the affine replacement gives every lift; h^{-1}-surjectivity gives the conormal surjection. It does not assume a global strict ambient presentation exists before that replacement.

The actual cone embedding is

C_{M_Z/P}→F,

from Sym(F^∨)→gr_I O_P. Define

f_E^![Z]=0_F^![C_{M_Z/P}],                                                   (1)

with the ambient relative dimension included. If E has virtual rank r and Z has dimension k, the result has dimension k+r. For smooth f, F=0 and (1) is ordinary smooth pullback. This fixes its dimension and normalization.

No absolute smoothness of Z is needed here. For the fundamental cycle of each integral Z, the ambient P is smooth over an integral base and its components are integral after localization. Its Rees deformation has the expected pure dimension: every irreducible component of its central cone is a codimension-one component of the flat Rees family, so the cone cycle has dimension dim P, with its actual scheme multiplicities. This is the only purity point for which the ordinary-source argument used a smooth base. Arbitrary cycles on a reducible base are handled one integral cycle at a time.

#### 2. Independence is an actual cone comparison

Compare two relative embeddings using P×_Z Q. A map from the center to smooth Q extends étale locally to P; its conormal sequence identifies the extra normal-cone directions with the pulled-back relative tangent bundle of Q. Two choices differ by the corresponding derivation, exactly the tangent translation quotiented by the intrinsic cone. Triple products give the same cocycle because these derivations add. This constructs the actual intrinsic **relative** cone and its immersion in h¹/h⁰(E^∨), not just a rank comparison at points.

Changing the strict model adds or removes an acyclic vector-bundle pair; its cone/Gysin operation is unchanged by bundle homotopy and the zero-section identity. The published lemmas `lem-gysin-specialization-bivariant-and-base-change`, `lem-refined-gysin-commutation-and-composition`, and `lem-zero-section-gysin-and-excess-vector-subbundle` apply to these exact conormal surjections after **every** base change. Their hypotheses are not restricted to a smooth integral base. Thus (1) is independent of the affine torsor, embedding and strict model.

For global DM quotient charts use the completed ordinary source's global construction: framed stable-map spaces give GL-framed quotients, full-rank good pairs produce quasiprojective scheme approximations in a stable Chow range, and the incidence-complement affine torsors have injective Chow pullback by the projective-bundle/localization proof. Apply the same cone comparison on a common affine torsor for every complex being compared; push down through those injective comparisons and the stable range. If a base is presented by an Artin cone, do the same with its torus frame. The relative cone itself and its cycle glue by the preceding product-embedding cocycle. This is not an inference that equality of Chow classes on an étale atlas implies global equality.

#### 3. Rational equivalence and the bivariant identities

On the strict presentation, (1) is the composition of smooth pullback to P and the actual published operation c(M_Z,P,F). The latter is defined by its Rees cone and zero-section Gysin after every base restriction. Its published proof establishes proper, flat and Cartier compatibility through the induced proper maps of blowups and double-deformation Cartier intersections. In particular it does **not** identify the normal cone after a nonflat base change with an ordinary tensor product of the old normal cone.

Therefore these compatibilities hold for (1). To see rational equivalence explicitly, normalize the closure of the graph of a rational function on an integral test scheme. Its fibers over 0 and infinity represent its principal divisor. Apply the Rees construction to that proper family and use the commutation of the two Cartier intersections (the rational-function fiber and cone specialization). The resulting two virtual cycles differ by the divisor of that same family and hence are rationally equivalent. Proper compatibility descends this equality to the original test scheme. Consequently (1) extends linearly to an operation on its actual Chow group, not only on a chosen list of cycles.

For a proper map h:Z'→Z of test schemes the formula is

f_E^! h_* = h'_* f_{E'}^!;

for flat h it commutes with h*, and for a regular Cartier inclusion i it commutes with i^!. These statements follow in the strict model from the cited c operation and hence survive the common-torsor comparisons of §2. Universality of the actual log POT ensures the complexes after restriction are the obstruction theories just used, not merely quasi-isomorphic bundles with an unrelated map to a cotangent complex.

#### 4. Proper modifications of the logarithmic base

Let b:B'→B be a proper toric subdivision, or its smooth finite toric root-stack chart, on a log-prestable chart, and let M'=M×_B B'. In an integral chart the fan subdivision is proper and is an isomorphism over the dense torus, so b_*[B']=[B]. Its local projective construction is a subdivision by normalized monomial blowups; the chart description glues along faces. A finite toric root-stack chart is proper and is an isomorphism on the dense torus as a stack, so its fundamental-cycle pushforward is also one. This assertion is a rational-stack pushforward, not the coarse degree of the covering affine coordinate map. Formula (1) and proper compatibility consequently give

b'_*[M',E']^vir=[M,E]^vir.                                                  (2)

Here the virtual cycles mean the cone operation on the corresponding base fundamental cycles. The basic-map morphism to its log-domain base is strict. Consequently use the actual ordinary fiber product in this base change (and its associated pulled-back log data), retaining nilpotent multiplicities; do not replace it by the reduced normalization of its source. For example at a root t=r^n over t=0 the length-n fiber and its μ_n quotient must both be kept. The source map M'→M need not be an ordinary birational modification on a ghost stratum; (2) is derived from the **base** pushforward identity and the universal cone operation. This avoids the false shortcut that a log blowup of a log point has degree-one geometric fibers.

The construction is independent of the fan subdivision, since any two finite subdivisions have a common refinement and proper compatibility applies twice. For a fixed curve-class filtration, boundedness supplies only finitely many relevant types; the same argument then passes coefficientwise to the prescribed completion. No analytic convergence is asserted or needed.

#### 5. Cartier decomposition supplies the actual multiplicities

On an integral toric base chart Spec C[P], let the target smoothing parameter be t=uχ^ρ, u a unit. After a fan subdivision resolving the relevant chart, a toric boundary divisor indexed by a primitive ray ν has multiplicity

m_ν=ν(ρ)

in div(t). This is the discrete-valuation order of the monomial, not an assigned tropical weight. Hence in Chow

[t=0]=Σ_ν m_ν[D_ν].                                                        (3)

Apply the operation (1) to this exact Cartier cycle identity and use its Cartier compatibility. The virtual special fiber is the weighted sum of the corresponding virtual type fibers. Monodromy of a labelled type is removed by its actual finite marking cover; pushing that cover down divides by the type automorphism order. The weights m_ν retain every Kummer/base index. Higher boundary intersections are already included in the proper type compactifications and are not counted again as extra Cartier components.

The global equality is obtained from the globally defined relative cone and its zero-section class: the local Rees families are the restrictions of the same smoothing-parameter Cartier operation and the same intrinsic-cone comparison of §2. The toric divisor cycles and their type normalization maps glue before Gysin; their generic multiplicities are exactly the valuations in (3). Global quotient/affine-torsor comparison then gives the equality in the rational Chow group of the actual DM map stack. There is no appeal to an étale-local equality of final Chow classes.

Thus the log-map decomposition step is constructed from an actual universal obstruction theory and an actual Cartier divisor identity. It is not assumed as part of the phrase “toric degeneration”.

#### 6. Remaining application boundary

This file needs the separate algebraic family construction to supply: the basic-map DM stack, its proper finite-type pieces, log-prestable Artin-cone charts with their face maps, and the retained target smoothing parameter. The Čech obstruction construction supplies the universal POT. For the actual normal-cone model these are precisely the typed central-only and central-plus-outer interfaces requested from the family owner. They are not generic facts inferred from the abstract definition of a log-CY pair. Cutting/gluing the relative map POT and evaluating its vertex pieces are the next attached steps; (1)–(3) do not by themselves assert the full theta-period theorem.

#### Dependency/reading boundary

Actual published scheme suppliers used here are the two bivariant Gysin lemmas and zero-section/excess lemma named in §2. Their full Statements and the exact arbitrary-base-change construction were read. The ordinary stable-map supplier's Parts 16–19 were read at the strictification/global torsor/diagonal comparison interfaces, without reopening its resolved construction. The extension here is the universal relative log-map operation and its use on the toric Cartier base; it imports no external log-degeneration or virtual-pushforward theorem.

### Inline construction 66

#### A line on a weak degree-three surface gives an actual ruled presentation

Research construction. The point of this lemma is to remove the fixed ADE components of a residual plane pencil without assuming that this pencil is already free on a resolution.

#### Exact surface interface

Let Z be a smooth projective complex surface with H=-K_Z nef, H²=3, and H^1(O_Z)=H^2(O_Z)=0. Supply an actual smooth rational curve L with H.L=1 and L²=-1. Then there is an actual finite sequence of contractions of vertical smooth (-1)-curves from Z to a Hirzebruch surface. Neither a classification of weak del Pezzo surfaces nor an a priori basepoint-free residual plane pencil is used.

#### The movable part has square zero and no base points

Put D=H-L. Its intersections are D²=0 and H.D=2. Surface RR gives chi(O(D))=2. Moreover h²(D)=h⁰(K-D)=0, because the latter line has negative H-degree. Consequently h⁰(D)>=2.

Write the complete linear system as |D|=B+|M| with its entire effective fixed divisor B removed. The movable divisor M is nonzero, has h⁰(M)>=2, and M²>=0: choose two members without a common component. It has H.M>0, since a nonzero movable divisor of H-degree zero would lie in the negative-definite space perpendicular to H and have negative square. Set m=H.M; since H is nef and H.D=2, m is either one or two.

Hodge gives M²<=m²/3. RR parity gives M²-m even. If m=1, these require the integer M² to be odd, nonnegative and at most 1/3, an impossibility. Hence m=2 and M² is even, nonnegative and at most 4/3. Thus M²=0 and H.B=0. Every fixed component removed from the residual pencil has H-degree zero. This explicitly handles the ADE-root fixed part; it is not assumed absent.

Two general members of |M| have no common curve and total intersection zero. They therefore have no common point. The complete movable system is basepoint-free. Choose two members with disjoint support; their sections give an actual morphism f:Z->P1 with fibre divisor M.

Its generic fibre is smooth by generic smoothness. It is geometrically connected without invoking an unproved connected-fibres assertion. Over an algebraic closure of C(P1), all components of the smooth generic fibre are disjoint, and monodromy permutes them transitively because Z is integral. If their number is k, each has the same positive integral H-degree d, with kd=2. By adjunction on a component, whose self-intersection is zero in the fibre surface, 2g-2=-d; hence d is even. Thus d=2 and k=1. The generic fibre is a smooth genus-zero curve over C(P1).

For clarity the same computation can be made after the finite base extension separating components, on the smooth open part of the base; fibre adjunction there gives degree(omega)=-d. It does not require extending the separated components to divisors on the original surface.

#### A section and connected special fibres

The anticanonical linear system of a smooth genus-zero curve over C(P1) embeds it as a plane conic: RR gives three sections of its degree-two anticanonical line and no obstruction, and this can be verified after algebraic closure. The elementary polynomial coefficient count from inline construction 4 gives a C(P1)-point of this conic. Properness extends that rational point uniquely to a section S of f. In particular S.M=1.

Every special fibre is connected by Hodge and this section. Indeed, if F=A+B were a decomposition into disjoint nonzero effective divisors (including their fibre multiplicities), then A²=F.A=0 and B²=0. Since F²=0, Hodge implies that each of A and B is numerically a positive real multiple of F: the isotropic ray in F-perpendicular is unique. Positivity follows by intersecting with any ample divisor. Therefore the positive integers S.A and S.B sum to S.F=1, a contradiction.

This Hodge assertion follows directly from the signature (1,rho-1): the quotient of F-perpendicular by the line R F is negative definite. No Stein factorization supplier is hidden here.

#### Actual finite contractions

For a connected reducible fibre F=sum m_i A_i, each A_i² is negative, since F.A_i=0 and the connected fibre has another component meeting it. We have K_Z.F=-2. Some component A has K_Z.A<0. Adjunction says

    A²+K_Z.A=2p_a(A)-2 >= -2.

Both terms on the left are negative integers. Hence both equal -1 and p_a(A)=0. Thus A is an actual smooth rational (-1)-curve. Apply the installed projective minus-one contraction to A. Since A is vertical, f factors through the contraction: on a neighbourhood of its image regular functions pulled back from the base are constant on the contracted fibre and descend by the normal-target pushforward O identity. The section descends as well. The target is smooth and its canonical intersection with a fibre remains -2.

Repeat. Picard rank decreases at every step, so this process is finite. At its end no fibre is reducible. If a fibre is mA with integral support, the section forces m=1. Flatness of the map from the smooth surface to the nonsingular curve makes its arithmetic genus zero. Therefore A is smooth P1. The result is a smooth P1 fibration with section.

The explicit rank-two-bundle construction and splitting in inline construction 4 identify this surface with F_n: O(S) has degree one on every fibre, its direct image is locally free of rank two by cohomology and base change, evaluation gives the actual P1-bundle isomorphism, and maximal-degree line plus curve RR splits the bundle. This is an actual contraction sequence from Z, rather than a birational-rationality assertion.

#### Application to a normal degree-two weighted component

The separate quartic inventory supplies a crepant smooth resolution T of a NORMAL exceptional E and a strict labelled boundary (-1)-curve C. Its contraction T->Z gives H=-K_Z nef with H²=3. The pushforward of the original H_T has to be combined with C: pullback(H)=H_T+C, which is nef because its intersection with C is zero and its intersection with any other curve is nonnegative. Vanishing of H¹ and H² of O passes from the double-plane hypersurface to its rational ADE resolution and then across the smooth contraction.

To use the present lemma one still supplies an actual degree-one curve L on Z. One sufficient construction is the actual anticanonical cubic morphism: if |H| is generated with h⁰(H)=4, the image is a cubic and is birational, by degree multiplication 3 and nondegeneracy. Here is the direct-image detail in that polynomial argument. Every positive n has H^i(Z,nH)=0 for i>0 by nef-big adjoint vanishing. For n sufficiently large, Serre vanishing and Leray force R¹phi_*O_Z=0; R² is zero because every fibre has dimension at most one. Thus chi(phi_*O_Z(n))=chi(O_Z(nH))=1+3n(n+1)/2, exactly the cubic Hilbert polynomial. The quotient phi_*O_Z/O_Y is coherent and has zero Hilbert polynomial, hence is zero. Proper birational pushforward from normal Z is an integrally closed subring of the common rational-function field (regularity of an integral rational function is checked on Z). Therefore O_Y is normal. Proper line incidence supplies a line on every cubic (the full coefficient-derivative proof is in inline construction 53). Its strict transform on Z maps properly quasi-finitely birationally to P1; finite birationality to the normal P1 makes this an isomorphism. It is a smooth rational L with H.L=1, and adjunction gives L²=-1.

Generation at this join is constructive and needs no widening of the installed public statement. On T, H_T is the pullback of the ample O_E(1), so installed degree-two weak-del-Pezzo generation applies directly: H_T is generated and h⁰(H_T)=3. Nef-big adjoint vanishing gives H¹(H_T)=0. The exact sequence

    0 -> O_T(H_T) -> O_T(H_T+C) -> O_C -> 0

is exact with last term O_C because (H_T+C).C=0. Its restriction map on sections is onto. A section lifting 1 is nonzero everywhere on C. Away from C, the old H_T sections multiplied by the section of C generate H_T+C. Thus H_T+C is globally generated and has four sections. Since H_T+C is the pullback of H on the smooth contraction Z and f_*O_T=O_Z, the same is true of H on Z. Its nef-big vanishings also follow directly on T from 0->O_T(nH_T+(n-1)C)->O_T(n(H_T+C))->O_C->0 inductively, or from nef-big adjoint vanishing on Z. Hence the cubic construction above applies with the stated actual hypotheses.

Consequently T->Z followed by the constructed conic-fibre contractions is the required actual finite blowdown presentation for EVERY normal weighted degree-two exceptional surface covered by the quartic inventory. The existing rational-anticanonical-cycle toric-model construction then applies whenever the separately tracked labelled total-transform boundary is its supplied reduced nodal anticanonical cycle.

### Inline construction 67

#### Weak degree two with a rational anticanonical cycle: actual double plane and smoothing

Research supplier for the toric-presentation assembly. Input: a smooth projective surface S over C, H=-K_S nef and big, H²=2, H¹(O_S)=0, and an ACTUAL reduced connected anticanonical divisor D whose normalization consists of P¹ components and whose only singularities are ordinary nodes. A single irreducible rational nodal curve is allowed. This input is retained after the actual (-1)-contractions of the rational log Calabi–Yau cycles in this packet. This proof supplies the anticanonical linear system and the precise smoothing used by the next (-1)-curve argument.

#### The cycle gives the entire linear system

L=H|D has nonnegative component degrees and total degree two. It is globally generated and H¹(D,L^n)=0 for n>0. Here is the explicit cycle calculation. Contract every chain of degree-zero components in the normalization matching description of sections; on such a chain a section is constant up to its actual gluing units. After this operation either one P¹ component has degree two, with its two endpoints identified, or two components each have degree one, with their endpoints matched cyclically. On the degree-two component, sections are quadratics with one endpoint matching equation; evaluation at any third point is independent of the two endpoint evaluations (the three-point Vandermonde determinant), hence no basepoint occurs. On the two degree-one components, the two endpoint values determine each section and can be chosen freely. Matching transfers the same conclusion to the contracted zero chains and their nodes. The dualizing bundle of a nodal rational cycle is trivial, by the residue differential dz/z with opposite residues at each pair of branches. Duality, or the same normalization matching matrix, gives H¹(L^n)=0 since the dual has negative total degree and nonpositive component degrees. Also h⁰(L^n)=2n for n>0.

In 0→O_S→O_S(H)→L→0, H¹(O_S)=0 makes restriction surjective. The divisor section is nonzero outside D, while lifted sections generate on D. Thus H is globally generated and h⁰(H)=3. Induction in

    0→O_S((n-1)H)→O_S(nH)→L^n→0

gives H¹(O_S(nH))=0 for every n≥0 and

    h⁰(S,nH)=n(n+1)+1.

No general weak-del-Pezzo base-point-free theorem is being imported.

#### Exact section ring and normal double plane

The section ring of L on the cycle is generated by two elements of degree one and one element of degree two, with one degree-four relation. To verify this directly, the globally generated degree-two pencil maps the cycle to P¹; degree-zero chains have no H¹ and may be contracted in the normalization/gluing description. The resulting reduced genus-one nodal curve is a finite flat double cover of P¹. Its trace splits the rank-two algebra as O⊕M, and χ(O_D)=0 gives M=O(-2). The multiplication M²→O is a quartic section. This proves the stated generators and relation, including reducible cycles; it is not an elliptic-curve smoothness assertion.

Lift those generators to S and add the divisor section of D. The restriction exact sequences and their H¹ vanishing show inductively that these lifts generate the complete ring R=⊕H⁰(nH). There are three degree-one generators and one degree-two generator u. Their relation is monic quadratic in u of weighted degree four; trace completion makes it u²=f4(x0,x1,x2). The hypersurface has Hilbert series (1+t²)/(1-t)³, whose coefficients are exactly n(n+1)+1, so the surjection onto R is an isomorphism in every degree.

The complete section ring of a Cartier divisor on a smooth integral surface is normal: express its homogeneous pieces by the inequalities ord_C(f)+n ord_C(H)≥0 for every prime divisor C, and intersect the corresponding valuation rings in K(S)(t), together with the nonnegative grading condition. Intersections of integrally closed rings are integrally closed. Therefore

    Y=Proj R={u²=f4}⊂P(1,1,1,2)

is the ACTUAL normal anticanonical double plane. The morphism q:S→Y contracts precisely the H-degree-zero curves; it is birational and is an isomorphism on a dense open because Proj of the complete big section ring has function field K(S). Thus it is a resolution.

#### Its quartic singularities are exactly the needed rational ADE points

The weighted hypersurface exact sequence gives H¹(O_Y)=H²(O_Y)=0 by the monomial Cech computation for weighted projective space. The proper birational map has q_*O_S=O_Y by normality. Its higher direct image R¹q_*O_S is supported at the finitely many exceptional points. The Leray exact sequence, H¹(O_S)=0 and H²(O_Y)=0 force H⁰(R¹q_*O_S)=0, hence this sheaf is zero. Thus the actual resolution is rational at every singular point.

Here is the extra quartic case excluded by rationality. A multiplicity-four branch point of a plane quartic has, after translation, an exactly homogeneous binary quartic equation: there are no higher-degree terms. Normality forces that binary quartic to be squarefree. The weight-(2,1,1) blowup of its double-plane cone has exceptional curve X²=f4(Y,Z) in P(2,1,1), a smooth genus-one double cover of P¹ branched at four points. The resolution neighborhood is the total space of its negative line bundle. Projection and zero section split H¹(O_E) into H¹ of this neighborhood, so R¹ of the resolution is nonzero, a contradiction.

All lower-multiplicity normal quartic points have the explicit A/D/E6/E7 forms proved by the binary quadratic/cubic calculations in `inline construction 68`; those calculations do not use its special infinity restriction. For clarity the sole additional rank-one case is a triple tangent cubic y³ with quartic remainder. A nonzero z⁴ coefficient gives E6; otherwise a nonzero yz³ coefficient gives E7; if both vanish, the quartic is divisible by y² and the double plane is nonnormal. A double tangent and a transverse branch give finite D_n by actual branch factorization and completing the residual quadratic. Rank-two surface Hessian gives finite A-type. The finite contact lemma makes these exact actual étale normal forms. Thus no external ADE classification is concealed in the use of the double plane.

Exceptional curves on S have H.C=0. Hodge and adjunction make them smooth P¹ of square -2. There is no exceptional (-1)-curve. Consequently S is the minimal resolution of Y (uniqueness can equivalently be checked in the explicit A/D/E charts).

#### Projective Hilbert specialization and the next blowdown

Perturb f4 by a general quartic over a trait. Smooth quartics form a nonempty open coefficient locus, so choose a line not contained in its complement. This is an ACTUAL projective flat double-plane family with central Y and smooth generic member. Choose a generic H-degree-one exceptional curve by the actual bitangent existence proof. After finite trait extension its flat Hilbert closure exists on this PROJECTIVE family. Its central cycle has H-degree one; since H is ample on Y, that cycle is one reduced irreducible curve A with multiplicity one. Possible embedded points have no effect on this cycle argument and are not discarded as schemes.

Take the strict transform C of A on the ACTUAL resolution S. It is integral and H.C=1. Hodge gives C²≤1/2, while adjunction gives C²=2pa(C)-1≥-1. Thus C²=-1 and pa(C)=0: C is an ACTUAL smooth rational (-1)-curve. Contract it to reach weak degree three. `inline construction 66` then supplies the rational pencil and finite projective sequence of vertical (-1)-contractions to F_n.

This argument needs NO simultaneous-resolution family to be projective: the only Hilbert scheme used is that of the original projective double-plane family, and only its central fundamental cycle is pulled to the fixed smooth resolution S. It also needs no connectedness theorem for the Hilbert limit and makes no claim that its embedded points vanish. This closes the precise degree-two linear-system, cycle specialization and actual second-blowdown interface used by the degree-one and q2-face toric presentations.

### Inline construction 68

#### Weighted cross: the normal quartic exceptional inventory

Research construction, not a full resolution claim. This proves the bounded NORMAL exceptional-surface inventory for the weighted cross step. It identifies the nonnormal branches separately; it does not silently replace them by ADE points.

#### 1. Exact bounded exceptional equation

For the cross step with weights (2,1,1,1) on (U,V,W,tau), the reduced weight-four equation is

    E: U²=B(V,W,tau),   B=V²W²+tau G3(V,W,tau)

in P(2,1,1,1). Here G3 is a homogeneous cubic, and the coefficient of U² is a unit normalized to one. The surface avoids the singular weighted-projective vertex. On tau!=0 it is an ordinary double cover of the affine plane with branch polynomial f(v,w) of degree at most four. A point with U!=0 is smooth. Thus every interior singularity has equation x²=f(y,z), with f a translated polynomial of degree at most four.

If E is normal, its singular locus is finite. In particular f is reduced and has an isolated plane-curve singularity at each singular point: the equations x=f_y=f_z=0 give precisely the singular locus of the double cover.

#### 2. Multiplicity two and three

Write m=ord(f). If m=2 and the quadratic form has rank two, the double cover is A1. If it has rank one, actual parametric Morse splitting gives f=y²+h(z). Isolatedness forces h!=0. A finite root of its unit gives the equation x²+y²+z^n, namely A_(n-1). These are actual etale coordinate operations on the central algebraic germ. The finite-contact construction then transports an actual generically smooth trait deformation to its actual A-family form.

Suppose m=3. If the cubic tangent cone has three distinct factors, the germ is D4. One direct proof uses the homogeneous cubic: its derivative ideal contains every sufficiently high degree monomial, and all quartic terms lie in that ideal. Degree-increasing formal coordinate elimination gives a formal D4 equivalence; the finite implicit contact correction actualizes it. For this last step the central contact ideal is primary, so approximation to sufficiently high maximal-ideal order puts the error in m J². This is the central isolated version of the existing finite-contact lemma, not an appeal to convergence of a formal coordinate series.

If the cubic tangent cone is a double line and a distinct simple line, factor off the simple branch ACTUALLY as follows. In coordinates with tangent y²z, substitute z=yr, divide f(y,yr) by y³, and solve its equation for r near zero by the implicit-function theorem. The resulting z=y r(y) is a smooth algebraic-etale branch. Divide f by its smooth equation, use that equation as the coordinate z, and apply parametric Morse splitting to the remaining quadratic factor. Absorb its unit by a square root into x. The result is

    x²+z(y²+a(z)).

Here ord a>=2, and a is nonzero because the original singularity is isolated. Absorb its one-variable unit by compatible roots of units. The result is x²+zy²+z^(k+1), k=ord a, namely D_(k+2). This argument permits arbitrary finite k; it does not infer a uniform D index merely from the degree of the original polynomial.

If the tangent cone is a triple line, take it to be y³ and rescale its coefficient to one. The exact polynomial is

    f=y³+A z⁴+B yz³+C y²z²+D y³z+F y⁴.

If A!=0, the leading weighted equation is y³+A z⁴ with weights (4,3). Every other displayed monomial has weight greater than 12 and is removable by the derivative ideal (y²,z³). The elementary weighted elimination gives a formal E6 equivalence; central finite contact determinacy actualizes it as above.

If A=0 and B!=0, the leading weighted equation is y³+B yz³ with weights (3,2). The remaining monomials have weight greater than 9. The derivative ideal (3y²+Bz³,3Byz²) contains their weighted remainders: in particular z⁵ is in this ideal, as follows by multiplying the first generator by z² and subtracting a multiple of y times the second. Weighted elimination and finite contact correction give actual E7.

If A=B=0, f is divisible by y². The double cover then has a nonisolated singular locus. This case contradicts normality and must pass to the nonnormal branch rather than be named E8.

The weighted eliminations used here are elementary: at each degree express the error as derivatives times monomials of positive coordinate weight, perform that coordinate change, and continue. The relevant finite residue bases are E6: 1,z,z²,y,yz,yz²; E7: 1,z,z²,z³,z⁴,y,yz. There are no nonzero residue monomials of weight greater than the displayed leading equation. Consequently every successive higher-weight error is removable. Only a finite jet of the resulting formal equivalence is used by actual finite determinacy.

#### 3. Multiplicity four is impossible in the normal case

If m=4 at an interior point p, translating p makes f a homogeneous binary quartic, since its degree is at most four. Projectively, B is the union, with multiplicities, of four lines through p. Since p is not on tau=0, distinct such lines meet tau=0 at distinct points. But the restriction there is exactly V²W². There can therefore be only two distinct lines, each repeated twice. Thus B is a square up to a scalar, and U²=B is reducible and nonnormal. Multiplicity greater than four is impossible unless B is zero, which its restriction excludes.

It follows that a NORMAL weighted exceptional surface has only A, D, E6 and E7 singularities in the interior tau!=0. E8 is not an interior singularity of this quartic model. E8 may arise on different auxiliary weighted-degree-six models, and its family resolution cannot be inferred from this conclusion.

At either boundary intersection V=0 or W=0, use the other coordinate as one. The Hessian in (U,V), respectively (U,W), has rank two because of U²-V², respectively U²-W². Actual Morse splitting fixing tau gives PQ=h(tau). Normality forces h!=0. Hence these boundary singularities are A-type as well. The two labelled boundary curves are retained; the actual old-parameter join still uses the separate two-base conic construction.

After the actual crepant ADE resolutions, let T be the smooth projective central surface, H the pullback of O_E(1), and C the strict transform of either labelled boundary curve. Each original curve is a smooth P1 (it is the graph U=+VW or U=-VW over the line tau=0). Its strict transform is still a smooth P1: the explicit A-type resolution at its boundary points merely separates its smooth branches from the other component. We have K_T=-H and H.C=1. Adjunction therefore gives C²=-1. The installed actual projective minus-one contraction constructs T->Z, with Z smooth and K_Z²=3. Thus the singular normal degree-two branch supplies an actual first blowdown, not just rationality. Continuing from Z to a full presentation is an additional required construction; it is not asserted by this inventory.

#### 4. Exact nonnormal alternatives, still requiring their family joins

Over characteristic zero, normality of the double plane fails precisely when the branch quartic has a repeated irreducible factor. If C is such a factor, 2 deg C<=4. Hence deg C is one or two.

For deg C=2 the quartic is a scalar square C², so E is reducible. For deg C=1 write

    B=l² Q2.

The line l cannot be tau=0 because B restricts nontrivially there. Its restriction to tau=0 is proportional to V or W, and Q2 restricts to the square of the other coordinate. This is a finite-degree algebraic normal form, not a claim that the surrounding family is solved. At a transverse intersection of l=0 with a smooth Q2=0, the local central germ is the ordinary pinch x²=v²z, so the actual general-pinch family construction applies. A tangency of l to Q2 does NOT have independent coordinates (l,Q2); it requires a separate tangential-conductor construction. Singular or repeated Q2 likewise must be retained explicitly.

In the square case U²=C² gives two components; if C is smooth their local crossing is a higher-double type, whereas singular C creates a further cross. The degree bound alone is not a termination proof for repeated singular-conic crossings.

#### 5. Consumption and exact remaining obligation

The normal branch can consume actual A/D resolution, the actual E6 line blowup to D5, and the separately owned E7 reduction. Crepant central resolution and a finite blowdown presentation of the resulting rational anticanonical component must still be supplied by those constructions; this inventory is not a substitute for the surrounding threefold family modifications.

For repeated weighted cross steps the relative Jacobian power gives a finite bound on the continuing distinguished cross chart: if t^M lies in (F,F_U,F_V,F_W), after r weighted charts U=t^(2r)U_r,V=t^r V_r,W=t^r W_r and division by t^(4r), all original spatial derivatives are divisible by t^(2r). An identity for t^M is impossible when 2r>M, provided every weighted chart is indeed an exact transform with regular substituted coefficients. This bounds that continuing branch only. Nonnormal exceptional tangencies and secondary cross charts require an explicit reduction and a compatible global invariant before a full nonunit-cross resolution theorem can be claimed.

### Inline construction 69

#### Weighted degenerate-conductor extraction: actual outer charts and finite high-weight sequence

Research supplier, not a full resolution of all low-weight cases. Start with the ACTUAL contact normal form over a trait with parameter s,

    F=x²-v²w²-v³+c(w,s)+v B(w,s),
    B=b0(s)+b1(s)w+b2(s)w²,

where every deformation coefficient vanishes modulo s. Assign weights (x,v,w,s)=(3,2,1,1). Assume each deformation monomial has weighted order at least six. This can be arranged at the first extraction by ONE finite base change t=s^N with N≥6. The condition must be checked again at a later stage; this note does not insert an unproved repeated-ramification termination claim.

#### Actual weighted extraction

Use the weighted blowup of the actual coordinate ideal at the origin, equivalently Proj of the Rees algebra of the filtration by monomial weighted order. It is projective, and is the identity on the generic fiber because s is invertible there. The strict equation has weight six. Its exceptional surface is the weighted sextic

    E: X²=V²W²+V³-C6(W,S)-V B4(W,S)
    in P(3,2,1,1).

Its section S=0 is X²=V²(W²+V), a reduced irreducible nodal anticanonical curve. In particular the right side cannot be a global square (its restriction at S=0 is not a square), so E is irreducible and reduced. The sum-of-weights discrepancy is 3+2+1+1-1-6=0. The old parameter s has exceptional multiplicity one. Thus this is an actual crepant extraction, and, with reduced central support, it is log crepant. No spatial root cover is made.

#### Every outer chart is already solved

The weight-one w-chart is an ordinary scheme chart. Set

    w=u, x=u³X, v=u²V, s=uS.

Then

    X²-V²-V³+H(u,V,S)=0,      old s=uS,

where H is actual regular and divisible by S. On u=S=0 the curve X²-V²-V³=0 has exactly one singular point, X=V=0; its Hessian in (X,V) is nondegenerate. At each smooth point eliminate X or V, retaining the independent coordinates u,S; old s=uS is SNC. At the node relative algebraic Morse in (X,V), fixing (u,S), gives PQ=Phi(u,S), old s=uS. This is exactly `inline construction 60`, including its zero-thickness cases.

The weight-two v-chart has its standard μ2 presentation

    v=u², x=u³X, w=uW, s=uS,
    X²-W²-1+H(u,W,S)=0,      old s=uS.

The involution sends (u,X,W,S) to (-u,-X,-W,-S), and H vanishes at S=0. At u=S=0 the curve X²-W²=1 is smooth. The action is free there, because X,W cannot both vanish. Consequently the quotient is actually étale-locally a regular scheme there, with the invariant parameter uS and ordinary transverse central branches. No fixed quotient singularity is being dropped. The x-chart contributes no further outer point: its equation on u=S=0 is 1-V²W²-V³=0, forcing V≠0, so it overlaps the v-chart.

The remaining chart is the weight-one s-chart. It is the only place where a new interior problem can remain. Its exceptional surface is exactly the weighted degree-one surface in the separate normal/nonnormal DP1 inventory; the outer anticanonical node and its labels have already been handled by the w-chart.

#### Uniform Jacobian decrement, without further base change

On the s-chart set x=s³X,v=s²V,w=sW and write F=s⁶Fnew. The actual chain rule gives

    pull(F_x)=s³(Fnew)_X,
    pull(F_v)=s⁴(Fnew)_V,
    pull(F_w)=s⁵(Fnew)_W,
    pull(F)=s⁶Fnew.

For the relative contact ideal J=(F,F_x,F_v,F_w), pull(J) is contained in s³Jnew. Therefore an ACTUAL certificate s^M in J gives s^(M-3) in Jnew. This remains valid after any actual parameter-fixed invertible spatial coordinate change and at any finitely many recentered points of the new chart. Each continuing extraction satisfying the weight-six hypothesis decreases the integer certificate by at least three. Thus one fixed initial trait extension permits only finitely many such high-weight stages; no infinite sequence is hidden in their formal expansions.

For orientation, a deformation term s^a w^j updates on this chart to s^(a+j-6)W^j. An order-one nonunit coefficient cannot simply be treated by endless quadratic base changes: for x²-v²w²+v³+t w³+t^M, quadratic conductor extraction and exchanging v,w can reproduce the same pattern with exponent 2M-2. The weighted extraction avoids that claimed invariant. It does NOT by itself solve a later stage with deformation weight less than six.

#### Exact remaining interface

Normal interior surface points are actual ADE family blocks; the E6/E7/E8 and D/A constructions provide their simultaneous resolutions. Nonnormal weighted-sextic central normalization is supplied by `inline construction 71`; its family conductor crossing still needs its actual lift. A stage with some nonunit deformation term of weight less than six requires a separate finite terminal reduction. This file proves the extraction, every outer chart and the high-weight integer decrease, not that remaining terminal reduction.

### Inline construction 70

#### Normal weighted-degree-one interior: finite ADE inventory

Research proof for the ACTUAL weighted sextic exceptional surfaces of `inline construction 69`. This proves their normal interior points require only the actual A/D/E6/E7/E8 family suppliers already constructed. It uses neither a del Pezzo classification nor an abstract ADE simultaneous-resolution theorem.

Write the branch sextic in P(2_V,1_W,1_S) as

    B=V³+a2(W,S)V²+a4(W,S)V+a6(W,S),
    B(V,W,0)=V²(V+W²).

Here the a_i have their indicated homogeneous degree; the last restriction means a2(W,0)=W², a4(W,0)=a6(W,0)=0. On S=1, translating V by -a2/3 gives the exact ACTUAL polynomial Weierstrass equation

    X²=Z³+A(W)Z+C(W).

A has degree four with leading coefficient -1/3, while C has degree six with leading coefficient 2/27. This leading-coefficient information is essential.

#### Nonminimal orders would force nonnormality

If at some finite W=w0 one had ord A≥4 and ord C≥6, the degree bounds force the exact equalities

    A=-(W-w0)⁴/3,       C=2(W-w0)⁶/27.

Then 4A³+27C² is identically zero. The cubic is (Z-h)²(Z+2h), h=(W-w0)²/3. The double-plane surface is singular along X=Z-h=0 and hence is nonnormal. Therefore every NORMAL surface in this family has minimal orders at every interior point. This excludes simple elliptic and worse nonminimal Weierstrass cases directly, rather than appealing to a classification of Gorenstein del Pezzo surfaces.

#### All remaining singularities

At a singular point with Z-coordinate r≠0, the Z² Hessian coefficient is 6r≠0. Morse in (X,Z) leaves an isolated one-variable function, hence an A-type singularity. Normality makes its order finite. Move now to a singular point with r=0, W=w0, and put w=W-w0. Then A(0)=C(0)=0; set α=ord A, β=ord C, allowing infinity.

* β=1: the surface is smooth.
* α=1 and β≥2: the quadratic form in (X,Z,w) has rank three, giving A1.
* β=2 and α≥2: Morse in (X,w) leaves the nonzero cubic Z³, giving A2.
* α=2 and β≥3: the tangent cubic in (Z,w) is Z³+a w²Z+b w³ with a≠0. It cannot have a triple root, since its Z²w coefficient is zero. Three distinct roots give D4. A double root gives D_n for finite n: factor the transverse smooth branch by the actual henselian/étale simple-root lemma, make it a coordinate z, and complete the residual quadratic in its other coordinate. The isolated residual one-variable function has finite order and an actual étale unit root. This is exactly z(y²+z^(n-2)) up to a unit. The formal contact normalization, if used in completing the square, is algebraized by the finite m-primary contact matrix lemma.
* β=3 and α≥3: the tangent cubic Z³+b w³ has three distinct roots, giving D4.
* β=4 and α≥3: the weighted leading form is E6, X²-Z³-bw⁴, with weights (6,4,3). Every remaining term has larger weight.
* α=3 and β≥5: the weighted leading form is E7, X²-Z³-aZw³, with weights (9,6,4), or the same weights divided by the common factor on (Z,w). Every remaining term has larger weight.
* β=5 and α≥4: the weighted leading form is E8, X²-Z³-bw⁵, with weights (15,10,6). Every remaining term has larger weight.

These cases exhaust the minimal pairs; α≥4, β≥6 was excluded above. In the last three rows X may be assigned the integer weight making its square have the common degree (the displayed E7/E8 triples are twice the minimal fractional weights).

For precision about higher terms: the weighted Jacobian quotient bases for E6, E7, E8 have maximal weight strictly below the equation degree. Therefore every higher-weight term is in the Jacobian ideal with coordinate corrections of positive weight. Successive explicit division removes these terms in the completed local ring. The central Jacobian is m-primary; truncating sufficiently far puts the actual error in mJ², and the finite implicit system Y+Q(Y)=a with identity Jacobian gives an ACTUAL étale contact equivalence. This is the finite-contact supplier already constructed, not an invocation of Artin approximation. D4 and the finite double-root D_n cases use the same central procedure.

Apply the parameter-fixed finite trait normal form to the original surrounding family at each such point, then its ACTUAL A/D/E family resolution. These points lie away from the outer anticanonical curve, whose full family neighborhoods were handled separately in the weighted outer-chart packet. There are finitely many normal singularities because a normal surface has isolated singular locus here.

#### Limit

This closes the NORMAL interior inventory. It does not resolve the actual family along a NONNORMAL weighted sextic conductor, and does not supply the terminal reduction when the starting deformation has weight below six. Those are distinct remaining interfaces.

### Inline construction 71

#### Complete nonnormal weighted degree-one exceptional normalization

Research only. This is an exact CENTRAL-component construction, not a surrounding family lift or a termination claim. It supplies the nonnormal exceptional-component inventory in the weighted extraction of a tangential-conductor germ.

Use weights (X,v,w,t)=(3,2,1,1). Let

    E: X²=B6(v,w,t) in P(3,2,1,1),
    B6(v,w,0)=v²(v+w²).

The boundary is the actual hyperplane t=0. Assume E is nonnormal. The polynomial is irreducible: X²-B6 could factor only if B6 were a square, which is excluded by its specialization v²(v+w²). For a double cover in characteristic zero the codimension-one nonnormal locus occurs exactly when B6 has a repeated factor. This follows directly at the generic point of each branch divisor: over the corresponding DVR the equation X²=pi^m unit is regular/normal for m=0 or 1, and is nonnormal for m≥2; off the branch it is étale. Serre's criterion applies since the hypersurface is Cohen–Macaulay.

Write B6=L_d² Q_(6-2d), with Q squarefree and L homogeneous. No factor t divides L, since B6 at t=0 is nonzero. Thus L(v,w,0) is a nonzero homogeneous polynomial whose square divides v²(v+w²) in C[v,w]. The factors v and v+w² are distinct irreducibles, and the latter occurs only once. Therefore L(v,w,0)=c v. Its weight is two, so d=2. In particular there is no repeated factor of weighted degree one or three. Rescale constants and write EXACTLY

    L=v+alpha wt+beta t²,
    Q=v+w²+gamma wt+delta t²,
    B6=L² Q.

The weight-two polynomials with the displayed specializations have precisely these terms. This proves that every nonnormal E in this family is covered by this single four-parameter presentation; no classification of del Pezzo surfaces is used.

Adjoin A=X/L. Then A²=Q, and eliminate v:

    v=A²-w²-gamma wt-delta t².

The integral closure is the polynomial ring C[A,w,t], all variables of weight one. It is integral over the original graded ring because A satisfies its monic quadratic equation, and has the same fraction field because A=X/L. Being a polynomial ring it is normal, so it is the integral closure, not just a birational parametrization. Passing to Proj gives the actual finite normalization

    nu:P²_[A:w:t] -> E,
    X=L A,
    v=A²-w²-gamma wt-delta t².

The map includes the point w=t=0: there v=A² and X=A³, so no projective indeterminacy is present.

The conductor is also exact. In the double-cover notation R=C[v,w,t], the original ring is R plus L R A inside R plus R A. An element r+sA is in the conductor precisely when both it and its product by A lie in the original subring. This is equivalent to r and s both being divisible by L. Hence the conductor ideal upstairs is (L), and downstairs is (L,X). The projective conductor in P² is the conic

    C: A²-w²+(alpha-gamma)wt+(beta-delta)t²=0.

It is REDUCED, because its A²-w² part has rank two. It is either a smooth conic or two distinct lines. The strict transform of the outer boundary is D=(t=0), a line; it meets C at the two distinct points [A:w:t]=[1:1:0] and [-1:1:0]. At those points C is smooth and transverse to D, since C restricted to D has simple zeros. If C has two components, their crossing lies off D, again because the restriction to D has distinct roots. Thus D+C is a reduced simple-normal-crossing rational anticanonical cycle: line plus conic with two intersections, or a triangle. Its degree is three, so

    K_P²+D+C=0.

The elementary line/conic toric-model construction in inline construction 46 therefore applies directly to this normalization. It uses actual corner/interior blowups, with no del Pezzo classification and no unspecified rationality or contraction theorem.

The conductor map C -> (L=0) is the finite degree-two map induced by A²=Q|L. It can split when C is two lines. No numerical factor 1/2 is inferred for relative gluing: use the actual conductor correspondence and its diagonal, as specified in inline construction 4. This normalization computation does not by itself construct a modification of an arbitrary surrounding threefold family. That vertical family lift remains a separate prerequisite.

### Inline construction 72

#### General pinch families: finite conductor weight and actual isolated D-type tails

Constructive research supplement, 2026-10-28. Combine the actual finite-trait pinch contact normal form of the companion owner with the explicit blowups below. This removes the unit-coefficient restriction of `pinch-unit-family-actual-semistable-lift.md` at the ramified conductor points. No spatial cover or division of virtual degrees by a conductor-cover order is used.

#### 1. Actual normal-form input and finite weight

Use the ACTUAL etale presentation fixing the old trait parameter t from `finite-trait-pinch-contact-normal-form.md`:

    x²-Zv²+2b(t)v+c(Z,t)=0,                               (1)

where b is in tR, c(Z,0)=0, c has actual regular algebraic Z coefficients, and the generic fibre is smooth. The companion finite-elimination/contact argument, rather than a formal classification, supplies (1). Make one finite base change t=s². Write

    nu_b=ord_t b,       nu_c=ord_t c,

where nu_c is the first t-power with coefficient a nonzero germ of a function of Z, and use infinity if a function is identically zero. At least one is finite: if both vanish identically, the generic fibre is the same singular pinch surface. Set

    r_*=min(2nu_b,nu_c).                                 (2)

This is a finite positive integer. After r continuing conductor blowups the remaining chart will have exactly

    b_r=b(s²)/s^r,       c_r=c(Z,s²)/s^(2r).              (3)

For 0<=r<r_*, b_r is divisible by s and c_r by s², so the next displayed blowup is defined without any negative powers. In particular the original quadratic base change is not repeated and no increasing-exponent loop is hidden.

#### 2. The actual three-generator blowup at a continuing stage

Suppose the current equation is

    x²-Zv²+2s B(s)v+s² C(Z,s)=0.                         (4)

Blow up I=(x,v,s), the current central conductor ideal together with the base parameter. On the s chart x=sX,v=sV one obtains

    X²-ZV²+2B(s)V+C(Z,s)=0.                              (5)

This proves the recursion (3). On the v chart x=v a,s=v d the equation is

    a²-Z+2d B(vd)+d² C(Z,vd)=0,       s=vd.              (6)

On the x chart v=x a,s=x d it is

    1-Za²+2ad B(xd)+d² C(Z,xd)=0,     s=xd.              (7)

These are strict blowup charts of an integral local hypersurface. Every centre contains s, so every blowup is the identity on the entire generic fibre. The hypersurface multiplicity along the regular ambient centre (x,v,s) is two, and the ambient centre has codimension three. Canonical discrepancy is therefore (3-1)-2=0. The pullback of s has order one on the new exceptional surface and on the strict central component. This proves log crepancy at each stage.

#### 3. No unrecorded bad chart, and the exact terminal conic

Let beta=B(0), gamma(Z)=C(Z,0). The projective exceptional surface at this step is

    Q={X²-ZV²+2beta V W+gamma(Z)W²=0}
      in the P2 bundle [X:V:W] over the conductor line.   (8)

All its singular points lie in W!=0. Indeed W=0 gives X²=ZV² and V!=0, so the derivative in Z is -V², a unit. The strict old central component meets Q along this same smooth bisection W=0. In chart (6), at v=d=0 the derivative in Z is -1, making the old and exceptional central components the transverse axes v=0,d=0 of the regular total threefold. Equation (7) gives the same assertion on its overlap. Consequently every unresolved point is contained in the single chart (5), and is an interior point of Q, disjoint from the earlier central components.

If beta=0 and gamma is identically zero, (5) centrally is again the pinch surface. Its whole nonnormal locus lies in this continuing chart; the other charts are the smooth normalization-side charts just checked. This is exactly the case r+1<r_*.

At r+1=r_*, beta is nonzero or gamma is a nonzero function germ. If beta is nonzero, the conic (8) over Z=0 has determinant -beta² and is smooth. Thus the central surface and the total family are smooth over this local base point.

Otherwise beta=0 and gamma is nonzero. Let k=ord_(Z=0) gamma. The singular points of (8) are all seen in (5):

* k=0: its central surface is smooth near Z=0. At its potentially degenerate conic point the Z derivative is a unit.
* k=1: there are precisely the two points X=Z=0, V²=gamma'(0). The quadratic form in X,Z,V-V_0 has rank three, so these are A1 surface points.
* k=2: the only singular point is X=V=Z=0; the Hessian in X,Z has rank two and the remaining term has order four. This is the A3 chart, with an actual two-variable Morse presentation.
* k>=3: the same origin is the D_(k+1) surface germ X²-ZV²+Z^k times a unit. An actual root of the unit and the substitution Z'=Z times that root, together with the corresponding square scaling of X, give the standard central D-type polynomial.

These assertions follow by differentiating X²-ZV²+gamma(Z). In the last case the unit can be normalized explicitly: if gamma=Z^k epsilon(Z), choose alpha^(k-1)=epsilon; multiplying the equation by alpha and using Z'=alpha Z, X'=sqrt(alpha)X gives X'^2-Z'V²+Z'^k. All chosen roots are units with nonzero complex residue and are actual etale equations.

#### 4. Actual isolated-tail resolution and termination

The total generic fibre of (5) is smooth, because it is an open of the unchanged original generic fibre. Its remaining special singularities are isolated A/D points just listed. The exact finite-Jacobian division/contact argument of the companion normal-form construction supplies an **actual** etale polynomial D_n deformation presentation there; generic smoothness makes a power of s belong to the relative contact Jacobian, so its finite Taylor correction uses an etale implicit system, not an unproved formal approximation theorem. For A points the direct Morse/finite-branch construction suffices.

Apply `inline construction 6`: choose a squared root of P=b²-zc after finite trait extension and blow (z-a²,x+i(ay+beta)); one chart drops n by one and the other is smooth over the trait. The finite recursion ends in A3 and its actual ordered branch-section resolution. These modifications are small, projective locally and identity off their isolated closed point. Their central fibre resolves Q without adding divisorial components; the existing transverse central intersections W=0 remain unchanged. Proper etale descent of those isolated choices is the already constructed algebraic-space descent.

Take one common finite extension for the finitely many A/D tails. Such a later extension can change an already resolved crossing xy=s into xy=s^e; it is not simply called regular after base change. Normalize and apply the already constructed finite saturated toric subdivisions on those previously monomial charts. They introduce only the recorded ruled toric expansion components over their existing rational double curves. Outside the isolated tails and these toroidal crossings the family was smooth over the trait and remains so by base change. Thus at most r_* conductor blowups, finitely many isolated A/D small-resolution steps and these finite toroidal refinements give an actual regular reduced-SNC local family at every ramified pinch point. There is a single integer-decreasing nonisolated branch; the only non-toric branches at the terminal step are the explicitly finite isolated tail list. This proves termination and actual family lifting, not merely termination of a resolution of the central surface.

#### 5. Intrinsic centers, units and component geometry

The successive curve centers are the conductor ideals of the current nonnormal central components, added to (s). Conductors of their finite normalizations are coherent and intrinsic. Actual contact equivalences carry these ideals to (x,v,s), so their definitions agree on overlapping normal-form charts. They therefore give genuine projective global blowups where the conductor component is globally present; no choice of a local square root of a smoothing coefficient or of a spatial double cover must descend. Near the outer triangle/conductor meeting point the same intrinsic blowup must be analyzed with the old product parameter; that is the separate outer block, not a reason to discard its boundary.

The strict transform of each earlier nonnormal quadric is a ruled surface over the original conductor double cover C. In the cubic case C is the actual P1 constructed in the normalization packet. Its old adjacent section and its remaining conductor section are disjoint; later blowups normalize the latter. Hence these intermediate component surfaces are rational. The final surface (8), resolved at its isolated A/D points, is a conic bundle over the original conductor line L=P1. Its rationality follows from the direct three-polynomial coefficient/height argument in the unit-pinch packet, so no draft Tsen import is needed. Normal frames, the involution of C->L and every exceptional degree remain actual data.

Crepancy gives the reduced anticanonical boundary by adjunction once the outer block is included. The local construction does not replace that boundary by two independently labelled ends or divide a virtual count by the degree of C->L. Its actual regular-SNC target can instead enter the established projective-DF support/cone construction and the rational-cycle toric presentation with the complete boundary and class data.
